- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-006"></a>
### CPP-006: Interpolation overload resizes and then appends coordinates

**With preprocess=false, the pair-vector constructor of TransformationModelInterpolated puts N zero anchors in front of the N real anchors, so the model is built from x=[0,...,0,x1..xN].**  
- **Trigger:** Direct C++ API call: TransformationModelInterpolated(std::vector<std::pair<double,double>>{(1,10),(2,20),(3,30)}, params, false). There is no in-tree caller: TransformationModelLowess.cpp:183 uses the DataPoints constructor, which always preprocesses. pyOpenMS does not bind this overload.
- **Consequence:** Default cspline: CubicSpline2d accepts the non-decreasing x (cpp:33 only rejects descending pairs), then divides by zero-width intervals (CubicSpline2d.cpp:139-141, l=0 and mu=0/0), so every in-range evaluate() returns NaN. Linear interpolation interpolates towards a fake (0,0) point below x1. two-point-linear and four-point-linear extrapolation anchor at (0,0), which gives wrong slopes. If any anchor x is negative, cspline throws 'x vector is not sorted'. The <3-point check is skipped too. All of this happens silently on a public but unused constructor path.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/ANALYSIS/MAPMATCHING/TransformationModelInterpolated.cpp:224-233 at origin/develop 5082181714 (pair-vector constructor, preprocess == false branch): `x_.resize(data.size()); y_.resize(data.size()); for (const std::pair<double,double>& pair : data) { x_.push_back(pair.first); y_.push_back(pair.second); }`. The header (TransformationModelInterpolated.h:57) declares `bool preprocess` with no default. The out-of-line definition at cpp:212 adds `= true`, so callers in other translation units must pass false explicitly.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read the upstream code at origin/develop: the resize-then-push_back at cpp:226-231, and the CubicSpline2d init_ arithmetic at CubicSpline2d.cpp:121-158 with h[0]=h[1]=0. Nobody has executed it: the Rust report calls it source-reviewed, and Core TransformationModelInterpolated_test.cpp has no preprocess=false case at ci.5 or at dax/codex/p0-followups. A related defect is still present upstream (cpp:263-268) and in Core ci.5 and p0-followups: global-linear extrapolation in this same pair-vector constructor does `bloated_data.resize(x_.size())` followed by `emplace_back`, which adds N (0,0) points to the linear fit whether preprocess is true or false. The DataPoints constructor (cpp:339-342) passes `data` directly and does not have that problem.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): x_.reserve/y_.reserve now replace resize in the preprocess=false branch ('reserve, not resize: the loop below appends'), so only the real anchors are stored. The global-linear bloated_data resize+emplace_back in the same constructor was not fixed.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 1beb468 ([FIX] Address the first tranche of the Rust port's C++ findings), released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-007"></a>
### CPP-007: EMG tail expression overflows before its asymptotic branch

**In the middle branch, emg_point forms exp(z*z) and erfc(z) separately. Once z exceeds about 26.6, the first overflows to inf while the Gaussian factor and erfc underflow to 0, so a finite, near-zero EMG tail comes out as NaN.**  
- **Trigger:** A point about 37 sigma or more left of mu, e.g. emg_point(x=-40, h=1, mu=0, sigma=1, tau=1) (z=29). In a fit: PeakIntegrator with fit_EMG=true (PeakIntegrator.cpp:92, default false; used through EMGPreProcess_ at PeakIntegrator.h:937-942) or pyOpenMS EmgGradientDescent.fitEMGPeakModel (bind_misc.cpp:556). The initial sigma is mu*1e-2 (:563), so a window reaching more than about 0.37*mu left of the apex is enough: a Gaussian peak at RT 60 s (sd 5 s) sampled over 30-90 s gives mu=60, sigma=0.6, and a NaN loss term at x=30.
- **Consequence:** A NaN loss at iteration 1 breaks the loop before any parameter is recorded. The fit then returns DBL_MAX for h, mu, sigma and tau, and applyEstimatedParameters gives inf intensities that PeakIntegrator integrates without any error. If the NaN appears later, gradient descent stops early with a suboptimal fit. Both outcomes are silent wrong peak shapes or areas in an opt-in feature on valid input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/MATH/MISC/EmgGradientDescent.cpp:435-437 (emg_point) `else if (z <= 6.71e7) { return h * std::exp(-(1.0/2.0) * std::pow(((x - u)/s),2.0)) * (s/t) * std::sqrt(PI/2.0) * std::exp(std::pow((1.0/std::sqrt(2.0) * (s/t - (x - u)/s)),2.0)) * std::erfc(1.0/std::sqrt(2.0) * (s/t - (x - u)/s)); }`, with compute_z at :52 `(1.0 / std::sqrt(2.0)) * (sigma / tau - (x - mu) / sigma)`. Loss_function :248 sums emg_point. estimateEmgParameters :584 initialises `best_h = best_mu = best_sigma = best_tau = best_E = std::numeric_limits<double>::max()` and :635-639 breaks on a NaN/inf loss. fitEMGPeakModel :748-753 applies the best parameters.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — No C++ execution is recorded: the Rust report says no C++ comparison was run, and Core added no EMG test. In this verification, a numpy transcription of emg_point (IEEE semantics) gave finite values down to x=-36 and NaN from x=-37 (mu=0, sigma=tau=1). The same transcription with computeInitialMean on the 60 s peak gave init mu=60, sigma=0.6 and a NaN loss term for the first point; the DBL_MAX parameters evaluate to inf. The C++ probe could not be compiled locally because the Xcode licence is not accepted.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): A local erfcx_(z) computes exp(z*z)*erfc(z) directly below z=25 and uses the asymptotic series 1/(z*sqrt(pi))*(1 - 1/(2z^2) + 3/(4z^4) - 15/(8z^6)) above it. The middle branch is now `h*exp(-0.5*((x-u)/s)^2)*(s/t)*sqrt(PI/2)*erfcx_(z)`, so the tail underflows to 0 instead of NaN. The derivative expressions E_wrt_h/mu/sigma/tau (upstream :79, :124, :169, :215) are unchanged. They combine the exponents, so they overflow only at far larger distances (about |x-mu|/sigma > 700).
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 1beb468 (first tagged in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups, EmgGradientDescent.cpp:451)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-008"></a>
### CPP-008: ProForma modified ranges omit their residue annotations

**ProForma mass calculation silently drops modifications written on residues inside a modified range.**  
- **Trigger:** Valid but uncommon ProForma such as `(M[UNIMOD:35]A)[Formula:C]` or `(M[UNIMOD:35]A)[+1]`, passed to ProForma::getMonoWeight, tryGetMonoWeight, getMZ or canCalculateMass from C++ or pyOpenMS (bind_chemistry.cpp:1391-1415). No TOPP tool computes ProForma masses.
- **Consequence:** The mass is too low by the inner modification's mass (15.995 Da for oxidation) and no issue is reported. An unresolvable inner modification is not flagged either.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp. The parser stores the inner modifications (1164 `elem.modifications = parseModificationList_()`, 1170). resolveModifications range branch, 2115-2119: `for (auto& mod : range->modifications) resolveModification_(mod, '\0', ...)` only. calculateChainMass_, 1960-1968: `for (const auto& elem : range->elements) { ... mass += res->getMonoWeight(Residue::Internal); } for (const auto& mod : range->modifications) addModMass(mod);`. getMassCalculationIssues, 2523-2535, checks only residues and range->modifications.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed in this verification: Core's own post-fix ProFormaParser_test binary (built with 86f01c4's tests, /scratch/kohlbach/openms4-core-181dadf/build/bin) was run on dax with LD_PRELOAD of the pre-fix libOpenMS.so from core-sdk-82ce5b3. That library's ProForma.cpp matches origin/develop apart from two unrelated `group_count` lines (diffed), and it contains the pre-fix string 'Ambiguous region contains amino acids with different masses'. Line 2677 failed with `got 232.0881642404, expected 248.0830792404` (absolute 15.994915). A separate API probe against the same library printed `canCalculateMass=1 mass=232.0881642 expected=248.0830792`.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Range-element modifications are now resolved with the element's residue (ProForma.cpp:2205-2208 at ci.5), added in calculateChainMass_ (1990-1991) and checked in collectMassCalculationIssues_ (2667-2668).
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (fix), b2079fb (test), 86f01c4 (test switched to [Formula:C]); all in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/ProFormaParser_test.cpp, section 'regression: modifications inside ranges and ambiguous candidates', lines 2676-2677 at core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-009"></a>
### CPP-009: Ambiguous mass checks ignore modifications on candidates

**Mass validation for ambiguous regions ignores candidate modifications, so the returned mass depends on candidate order and silently drops unresolved candidate modifications.**  
- **Trigger:** An ambiguous region whose isobaric candidates carry modifications, which the upstream parser accepts: `(?I[+10]L)` compared with `(?LI[+10])`, or `(?I[UnknownMod999]L)`, passed to getMonoWeight, tryGetMonoWeight, canCalculateMass or getMZ (C++ or pyOpenMS).
- **Consequence:** canCalculateMass returns true. `(?I[+10]L)` gives 141.095 and `(?LI[+10])` gives 131.095, a 10 Da difference from candidate order alone. `(?I[UnknownMod999]L)` gives the unmodified mass with no issue.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp:2508-2522, getMassCalculationIssues: `std::set<double> masses; for (const auto& elem : region->elements) { ... if (res != nullptr) masses.insert(res->getMonoWeight(Residue::Internal)); ... } if (masses.size() > 1) issues.push_back({...AMBIGUOUS_REGION...});`. It never calls checkModificationForMass_ on elem.modifications. calculateChainMass_ 1953-1958 uses only `region->elements[0]` and its modifications. The parser attaches brackets to candidates (1137).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed in this verification: in the same run of Core's post-fix ProFormaParser_test against the pre-fix library (ProForma.cpp identical to develop), lines 2680, 2681 and 2683 failed (`got '1', expected '0'`). The API probe against the same library printed `(?I[+10]L) can=1 mass=141.0946294 ; (?LI[+10]) can=1 mass=131.0946294` and `(?I[UnknownMod999]L) can=1 mass=131.0946294 plain(?IL)=131.0946294`.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): collectMassCalculationIssues_ now checks every candidate's modifications (ProForma.cpp:2630 at ci.5), compares full candidate masses including modifications, and reports 'Ambiguous region contains candidates with different masses' (2656). 86f01c4 also reports an empty region (2623).
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (fix), 86f01c4 (empty region reported), test b2079fb; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/ProFormaParser_test.cpp, section 'regression: modifications inside ranges and ambiguous candidates', lines 2679-2683 at core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-010"></a>
### CPP-010: Integer mass decomposition can loop without progress

**IntegerMassDecomposer::getDecomposition loops forever for some decomposable masses because a witness with count 0 subtracts nothing.**  
- **Trigger:** Weights {10,16,25} at precision 1, then getDecomposition(73): exist(73) is true and the only decomposition is [0,3,1]. The call is reachable only by direct C++ use of the public header template. It has no in-tree caller (RealMassDecomposer uses getAllDecompositions and getNumberOfDecompositions), no pyOpenMS binding and no TOPP tool.
- **Consequence:** The call never returns (100% CPU hang) on valid input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/CHEMISTRY/MASSDECOMPOSITION/IMS/IntegerMassDecomposer.h:420-431, getDecomposition: `while (m != 0) { size_type i = witness_vector_.at(r).first; decomposition_value_type j = witness_vector_.at(r).second; decomposition.at(i) += j; if (m < j * alphabet_.getWeight(i)) break; m -= j * alphabet_.getWeight(i); r = m % alphabet_.getWeight(0); }`. Nothing guards j == 0. The witness is built in the gcd>1 second loop at 358-375, where `++counters[cur];` (360) runs once per block, outside `for (size_type r = 1; r < d; ++r)`, which stores `_witnessVector[cur] = std::make_pair(i, counters[cur]);` (367).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed in this verification: the verbatim origin/develop IntegerMassDecomposer.h, MassDecomposer.h and Weights.h (stubs only for config.h and Math::gcd) were compiled with g++ on dax in a temporary /tmp directory, removed afterwards. Mass 73 gave `exist(73)=1`, `getNumberOfDecompositions=1`, `all: 0 3 1`, then `getDecomposition` was killed by `timeout 5` (exit 124). Masses 41 and 20 returned normally. The Rust report also has an executed Python translation (tools/probes/ims_witness_source_oracle.py) that shows witness[3]=(2,0).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The fix only removes the symptom. getDecomposition now breaks when j == 0 (IntegerMassDecomposer.h:426 at ci.5), so the hang is gone, but the witness-counter root cause is untouched. The same probe compiled against the ci.5 header returns `one: 0 0 0` for mass 73: no error, and not a decomposition of 73 (the correct one is [0,3,1]). A hang has become a silently wrong result.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Test:** none (IntegerMassDecomposer_test.cpp getDecomposition section, lines 81-85, is still `// TODO`)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-012"></a>
### CPP-012: Smoothed area accumulation uses raw peak intensities

**computeSmoothedPeakArea takes the smoothed intensity only for the first point and the raw intensities for every other trapezoid, so it returns roughly the raw area rather than the documented smoothed area.**  
- **Trigger:** Any call to MassTrace::computeSmoothedPeakArea (C++ or pyOpenMS) on a trace with smoothed intensities set, e.g. after ElutionPeakDetection. A trace without smoothed intensities reads smoothed_intensities_[0] of an empty vector.
- **Consequence:** Silently wrong peak area: filtering uses smoothed values but accumulation uses raw ones. On the upstream test fixture the result is 70303689.05 instead of 70322464.78, and it diverges further when the smoothed and raw scales differ. Calling it on an unsmoothed trace is undefined behaviour (typically a null read and crash). Reach is limited to direct API users, because getIntensity(true) uses computeFwhmAreaSmooth.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/MassTrace.cpp:66 `double int_before = smoothed_intensities_[0];` (no empty guard), :70 `if (smoothed_intensities_[i] > 0.0)`, :73 `peak_area += (int_before + trace_peaks_[i].getIntensity())/2 * rt_diff;`, :75 `int_before = trace_peaks_[i].getIntensity();`; MassTrace.h:246 `/// Sum all non-negative (smoothed!) intensities in the mass trace`; MassTrace_test.cpp:495 `TEST_REAL_SIMILAR(peak_area, 70303689.0475001)`. Public via pyOpenMS bind_kernel.cpp:462; no in-tree library or TOPP caller.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 181dadf changed MassTrace_test.cpp 'double computeSmoothedPeakArea() const' to expect 70322464.7770001, with the comment that 70303689.0475 was the old mixed result; upstream's own test still pins 70303689.0475001. I replayed both expressions in Python on the fixture (RT and intensities at MassTrace_test.cpp:53-80, smoothed values at :391-398, float32 raw intensities): 70303689.04750012 for the upstream expression and 70322464.77700011 for the smoothed trapezoid, matching both expectations. No failing C++ run of the new test on pre-fix code is recorded; the Rust report did expression evaluation only. There is no empty-trace test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): computeSmoothedPeakArea uses smoothed_intensities_ for both ends of every trapezoid and for the carried value, and returns 0 when smoothed_intensities_ is empty.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (MassTrace.cpp fix), 181dadf (test expectation); both in core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MassTrace_test.cpp, section 'double computeSmoothedPeakArea() const' (expected value 70322464.7770001; empty case untested)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-015"></a>
### CPP-015: Cross-link mass depends on endpoint traversal order

**When the label-only cross-link endpoint comes before the endpoint that carries the linker chemistry, ProForma mass calculation leaves out the linker mass.**  
- **Trigger:** Valid ProForma with the label-only endpoint first, inter-chain `K[#XL1]//K[+138.06807961#XL1]` or intra-chain `EMEVTK[#XL1]SESPEK[+138.06807961#XL1]`, passed to getMonoWeight, tryGetMonoWeight or getMZ for Peptidoform or PeptidoformIon (C++ or pyOpenMS). No TOPP tool.
- **Consequence:** The precursor mass and m/z are silently 138.068 Da too low for DSS. The same molecule written chemistry-first gets the correct mass, and no issue is raised.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp:1929-1941, addModMass: `if (!mod.alternatives.empty() && mod.alternatives[0].second.has_value()) { const auto& label = ...; if (label.type == Label::Type::CROSSLINK) { if (counted_crosslinks.contains(label.identifier)) return; counted_crosslinks.insert(label.identifier); } } auto [has_mass, mod_mass] = getModificationMass_(mod);`. A label-only bracket parses to an empty InfoTag plus a label (1206), and getModificationMass_ returns {true, 0.0} for an InfoTag (1910).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed in this verification: Core's post-fix ProFormaParser_test run against the pre-fix library failed at line 2703 with `got 292.2110568932, expected 430.2791365032` (absolute 138.06807961). The API probe against the same library printed `label-first=292.2110569 chemistry-first=430.2791369` and, intra-chain, `label-first can=1 mass=1392.64427 ; chemistry-first can=1 mass=1530.71235`.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): addModMass (ProForma.cpp:1945 at ci.5) now finds the CROSSLINK label on any alternative. Only an endpoint with resolved_mod != nullptr or carriesChemistry_ claims the label. counted_crosslinks becomes a map of label to mass, and conflicting endpoint masses are logged.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (fix), test b2079fb; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/ProFormaParser_test.cpp, section 'regression: cross-link mass does not depend on endpoint order', lines 2698-2705 at core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (inter-chain only)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-016"></a>
### CPP-016: Count-only mzML loading can still decode peak arrays

**When MzMLFile::loadSize runs with an MS-level or RT filter, it skips counting any spectrum that has no scan start time, and it fully decodes that spectrum's peak arrays into a throwaway map instead.**  
- **Trigger:** Call MzMLFile::loadSize(filename, scount, ccount) with PeakFileOptions filters set (MS levels or RT range) on an mzML whose spectra pass the filter but have no MS:1000016 scan start time. This is valid, but uncommon. Adding malformed base64 or numpress data to such a spectrum makes the count-only call throw. Only the public C++ API reaches it: nothing in the tree calls MzMLFile::loadSize, and pyOpenMS binds only FeatureXMLFile::loadSize.
- **Consequence:** scount is lower than the number of spectra load() returns with the same options, because load() keeps spectra that have no RT. These spectra are decoded and stored in memory although only a count was asked for. Corrupt binary data in one of them turns a count query into a ParseError.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:2341-2351 (origin/develop 5082181714): only the scan-start-time cvParam counts in count mode, `if (load_detail_ == XMLHandler::LD_COUNTS_WITHOPTIONS) { skip_spectrum_ = true; ++scan_count_; }`. onEndElement :1398-1427 has no load_detail_ gate: `if (!skip_spectrum_) { ... if (options_.getFillData()) { tmp.data = std::move(bin_data_); } spectrum_data_.push_back(std::move(tmp)); if (spectrum_data_.size() >= options_.getMaxDataPoolSize()) { populateSpectraWithData_(); } }`. Decode errors become ParseError at :239-243. Count mode is chosen in MzMLFile.cpp:100-103 (`if (options_.hasFilters()) handler.setLoadDetail(LD_COUNTS_WITHOPTIONS)`), and getCounts returns scan_count_ at :187. ms-mapping.xml:121 `scan_may` makes scan start time optional.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Code reading of the count-mode branches at MzMLHandler.cpp:1398-1438 and 2327-2352. The Rust report says it claims no C++ runtime reproduction. The MzMLFile_test loadSize section on Core (ci.5 and p0-followups, MzMLFile_test.cpp:274-303) uses only MzMLFile_1.mzML, whose spectra all carry an RT, so no test exercises this case.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): In onEndElement, a spectrum still unskipped in LD_COUNTS_WITHOPTIONS mode with no RT set is now counted and skipped before the enqueue and decode path: `if (!skip_spectrum_ && load_detail_ == XMLHandler::LD_COUNTS_WITHOPTIONS && !rt_set_) { skip_spectrum_ = true; ++scan_count_; }` (ci.5 MzMLHandler.cpp:1431-1435).
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, kept through ci.4 and ci.5 and on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-017"></a>
### CPP-017: The chromatogram skip option activates a global callback guard

**Loading any valid mzML with skip_chromatograms=true silently drops the file description, instrument, run and all spectrum metadata and peaks until </chromatogramList>, returning empty shell spectra with no error. It is P1 rather than P0 because no TOPP tool, util or pyOpenMS binding sets the option; only C++ API users hit it.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): origin/develop (edc7aa4) src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:149 `skip_chromatogram_ = options_.getSkipChromatograms();` (in setOptions, called before every parse by MzMLFile.cpp:150/187/205), and the generic start guard at :870-873 `if (skip_spectrum_ || skip_chromatogram_) { return; }` (same guard in onCharacters :803). The flag is only cleared at `</chromatogramList>` (:1495); the `</chromatogram>` branch at :1473-1476 clears it only `if (!options_.getSkipChromatograms())`. The `</sourceFileList>` handler (:1499-1509) copies from source_files_, which the swallowed <sourceFile> starts never filled. `</spectrum>` (:1396-1429) is not guarded, so it pushes spectra whose content was all swallowed.
- **Evidence:** test-backed
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): skip_chromatograms no longer suppresses the file description and every spectrum;
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 core 1beb468, src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp: setOptions no longer sets skip_chromatogram_; the <chromatogram> start tag sets it and </chromatogram> clears it. Test in the same commit (MzMLFile_test.cpp).
- **Test:** OpenMS4 core 4f5c86f src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MzMLFile_test.cpp, section `void testSkipChromatograms()` (lines 1303-1323, assertions added in 1beb468). Line 1315 `TEST_NOT_EQUAL(pm.getExperimentalSettings().getSourceFiles().size(), 0)` fails on pre-fix code: MzMLFile_1.mzML has a 5-entry sourceFileList, and every <sourceFile> start element is swallowed by the guard. The pm.size() checks (1314, 1322) may still pass pre-fix, because the unguarded </spectrum> pushes empty shell spectra. The upstream test at origin/develop only asserts chromatogram counts, so the defect passes CI.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-021"></a>
### CPP-021: CV XML formatting ignores the value's actual unit

**Through the C++ API, a value in minutes for a term allowing seconds and minutes is written as seconds without conversion, and any unit on a unitless term dereferences an empty set's begin() (undefined behaviour). Current upstream writers are coincidentally correct: MzIdentMLHandler passes retention time in UO:0000010 and psi-ms.obo MS:1000894's lexically first allowed unit is UO:0000010. The other DataValue callers never carry units, so there is no file-driven reach.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/ControlledVocabulary.cpp:127-130 `if (value.hasUnit()) { std::string un = *(this->units.begin()); s += "\" unitAccession=\"" + un + "\" unitCvRef=\"" + StringUtils::prefix(un, 2);`. The value's own unit (getUnit/getUnitType) is never read, and begin() of an empty std::set is dereferenced when the term has no allowed units.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): the unit written is the value's own unit;
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 core 1beb468, src/openms/source/FORMAT/ControlledVocabulary.cpp: write the DataValue's own UO/MS unit accession (escaped), or no unit for an unknown ontology.
- **Test:** The ControlledVocabulary_test toXMLString(DataValue) section (lines 232-240) is identical at 4f5c86f and origin/develop and tests only a unitless value, so the fix has no regression test. The Rust log claims no C++ run. The line reads the term's first allowed unit and never the value's.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-028"></a>
### CPP-028: Recognized software metadata can throw during mzML writing

**If a Software carries a meta value whose key is an exact PSI-MS term name (e.g. "completion time", MS:1000747), storing mzML throws std::out_of_range from the header writer. It should write a cvParam or fall back to a userParam instead, so valid data cannot be written. The trigger is uncommon.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:3787 `writeUserParam_(os, software, 3, "/mzML/Software/cvParam/@accession", validator);`. At 3666-3669 a key found by `cv_.checkAndGetTermByName(*key)` goes to `validateCV_(*c, path, validator)`, which at 3595 calls `validator.SemanticValidator::locateTerm(path, sc)`. src/openms/source/FORMAT/VALIDATORS/SemanticValidator.cpp:538 `const vector<CVMappingRule>& rules = rules_.at(path);`. rules_ is keyed by mapping element paths (SemanticValidator.cpp:39), and share/OpenMS/MAPPING/ms-mapping.xml:94 only defines `/mzML/softwareList/software/cvParam/@accession`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): software metadata is validated against the mapping's own path, and locateTerm reports an unmapped path instead of throwing std::out_of_range
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 1beb468 (MzMLHandler.cpp path corrected to /mzML/softwareList/software/cvParam/@accession; SemanticValidator::locateTerm uses find() and returns false for unmapped paths)
- **Test:** No test at 4f5c86f (MzMLFile_test, MSDataWritingConsumer_test, SemanticValidator_test) stores a Software meta value named after a PSI-MS term. The Rust log claims no execution. The static chain is complete: std::map::at on a key absent from the mapping throws std::out_of_range, and the result is cached only after locateTerm returns.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-029"></a>
### CPP-029: Annotation-only brackets pass conversion checks but fail conversion

**For valid ProForma with an INFO annotation (`M[INFO:note]`), the checker reports no issues and isRepresentableAsAASequence returns true. Strict conversion then throws ConversionError, so valid, representable input is refused and the two APIs contradict each other. It is uncommon and API-only.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp:2196-2201 raises UNRESOLVED_MOD only `if (carriesChemistry_(mod))`, and isChemistryTag_ at 1809 excludes InfoTag. isRepresentableAsAASequence at 2268-2271 is `getAASequenceConversionIssues(pf).empty()`. The attachment at 2310-2313, `if (mod.resolved_mod != nullptr) resolved.push_back(mod.resolved_mod); else if (policy == ConversionPolicy::FAIL_ON_LOSS) throw Exception::ConversionError(... "Unresolved modification at position " ...)`, throws for every null handle. The default policy is FAIL_ON_LOSS (ProForma.h:826). Upstream ProForma.cpp matches core-v4.0.0-ci.2 except for an unused group_count counter.
- **Evidence:** test-backed
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The attachment throw now uses the same predicate: policy == FAIL_ON_LOSS && carriesChemistry_(mod) (2399).
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 181dadf (ProForma.cpp toAASequence: `else if (policy == ConversionPolicy::FAIL_ON_LOSS && carriesChemistry_(mod))`)
- **Test:** ProFormaParser_test.cpp, section "regression: conversion preserves annotations and residue positions" (added in b2079fb; line 2690 at 4f5c86f): `TEST_EQUAL(ProForma::toAASequence(ProForma::parse("M[INFO:note]")), AASequence::fromString("M"))`. On the pre-fix (= upstream) attachment loop this throws ConversionError.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-030"></a>
### CPP-030: Strict AASequence conversion silently drops terminal crosslinks

**A peptidoform parsed from text with a terminal cross-link label converts under FAIL_ON_LOSS to a plain linear sequence. isRepresentableAsAASequence also reports true. The same link on a residue is rejected, so terminal cross-links are lost silently on valid but uncommon input.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp:2231-2240 (N-term) and 2253-2262 (C-term) check only `carriesChemistry_(mod)` and `hasGenuineAlternatives_(mod)` and never inspect labels. By contrast, the residue loop at 2208-2214 pushes `ConversionIssueType::CROSS_LINK` when `label->type == Label::Type::CROSSLINK`. Terminal attachment at 2340-2343 applies only `if (mod.resolved_mod != nullptr)`, so a label-only `[#XL1]` terminus is discarded without an issue.
- **Evidence:** test-backed
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Both terminal loops now push a CROSS_LINK issue when crossLinkLabel_(mod) is non-null (2322-2323, 2346-2347).
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 181dadf (ProForma.cpp collectConversionIssues_: both terminal loops push CROSS_LINK when crossLinkLabel_(mod) != nullptr)
- **Test:** ProFormaParser_test.cpp, section "regression: conversion preserves annotations and residue positions" (b2079fb; line 2691 at 4f5c86f): `TEST_EXCEPTION(Exception::ConversionError, ProForma::toAASequence(ProForma::parse("[#XL1]-PEPTIDE")))`. On pre-fix (= upstream) code no issue is collected and PEPTIDE is returned without an exception. The fix is still present at 4f5c86f (ProForma.cpp:2334, 2358) and was not part of the ci.4 cross-link generator revert.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-032"></a>
### CPP-032: CV mapping namespace stripping mishandles path segments

**With strip_namespaces=true, any unprefixed segment (including `@accession`) makes a valid mapping file fail with ParseError, and the message prints only a fragment of the path. A prefixed attribute such as `@p:accession` becomes `accession`, so rules silently never match. The option cannot load any real mapping correctly. No in-tree caller uses it.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/CVMappingFile.cpp:83-98 `StringUtils::split(*it, ':', split); if (split.empty()) { element_path += "/" + *it; } else { if (split.size() == 2) { element_path += "/" + split[1]; } else { fatalError(LOAD, std::string("Cannot parse namespaces of path: '") + element_path + "'"); } }`. src/openms/include/OpenMS/DATASTRUCTURES/StringUtils.h:605-608 returns the whole string as one element when there is no delimiter (`substrings.push_back(s); return false;`), so split is never empty. The file is identical to OpenMS 4 core-v4.0.0-ci.2.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): CVMappingFile.cpp:96 now keeps any segment with fewer than 2 ':'-parts unchanged.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 181dadf (CVMappingFile.cpp: `if (split.size() < 2)` keeps plain segments, a leading '@' is retained when stripping the prefix, and the error reports the original path)
- **Test:** CVMappingFile_test.cpp at 4f5c86f has only the strip_namespaces=false load section, and no other test passes true. The Rust log claims no execution. The split contract is explicit in the header, so the reading is certain.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-034"></a>
### CPP-034: CV reference bulk assignment can invalidate its input iterator

**`m.setCVReferences(m.getCVReferences())` appends to the vector it is iterating. Because end() is re-read on every pass, the loop runs until a reallocation leaves `it` dangling, which is a heap use-after-free read. The only in-tree caller (CVMappingFile.cpp:38) passes a separate member, and pyOpenMS copies through Python lists, so only direct C++ self-aliasing reaches it: memory safety via API misuse, P1.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/DATASTRUCTURES/CVMappings.cpp:68-75 `for (vector<CVReference>::const_iterator it = cv_references.begin(); it != cv_references.end(); ++it) { cv_references_[it->getIdentifier()] = *it; cv_references_vector_.push_back(*it); }` and :77-80 `const vector<CVReference>& CVMappings::getCVReferences() const { return cv_references_vector_; }`
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The loop now runs over a local copy of the input (CVMappings.cpp:73), with a comment about the aliasing.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 181dadf src/openms/source/DATASTRUCTURES/CVMappings.cpp (iterate a local copy)
- **Test:** No CVMappings_test change between core-v4.0.0-ci.2 and 4f5c86f. CVMappings_test.cpp at 4f5c86f has no self-aliasing case. The log claims no execution.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-038"></a>
### CPP-038: ProForma crosslink spectra count resolved linker mass twice

**With standard ProForma crosslink notation and a mass-delta linker (e.g. the upstream test input `PEPK[+138.068#XL1]IDE//ANOK[#XL1]THER`, ProFormaParser_test.cpp:3109), the linker is attached as a residue modification and added again as cross_linker_mass. Precursor and linked-fragment m/z come out D/z too heavy; CV-named linkers double-count too once the caller has run resolveModifications. This is a silent wrong theoretical spectrum from valid input, but ProForma crosslink spectrum generation is an uncommon, API-only path: P1.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp:2822-2834: `auto [alpha_found, alpha_pos, alpha_mass, alpha_label] = findCrossLink(alpha_pf);` ... `AASequence alpha_seq = toAASequence(alpha_pf, ConversionPolicy::BEST_EFFORT);` ... `double linker_mass = (alpha_mass > 0.001) ? alpha_mass : beta_mass;` ... `crosslink.cross_linker_mass = linker_mass;`. toAASequence resolves internally (:2276 `resolveModifications(pf_copy);`) and attaches every resolved modification (:2310 `if (mod.resolved_mod != nullptr) resolved.push_back(mod.resolved_mod);`, :2317 `seq.setModification(seq_pos, resolved[0]);`). The XLMS backend then adds the linker again (TheoreticalSpectrumGeneratorXLMS.cpp:920 `double precursor_mass = alpha.getMonoWeight() + crosslink.cross_linker_mass;`, also :967).
- **Evidence:** test-backed
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): generateSpectrum now resolves copies of both chains, locates each endpoint with findCrossLink (which returns a CrossLinkEndpoint_ struct), and erases the selected linker bracket from each copy before BEST_EFFORT conversion (2949-2966).
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 181dadf src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp (CrossLinkEndpoint_, linker bracket removed from resolved chain copies) + 86f01c4 (reject lossy chemistry, regression test)
- **Test:** ProFormaParser_test.cpp, section "regression: cross-linked spectra reject unrepresentable chemistry" (added in 86f01c4, after the 181dadf fix). It generates spectra for `M[UNIMOD:35]AK[+138.068#XL1]//PEPK[#XL1]IDE/2` with ion types "M" and requires both [M+H] peaks to match ProForma::getMZ within 1e-8. On the pre-fix code, K[+138.068] resolves through getBestModificationByDiffMonoMass to Xlink:DSS[138] (unimod record 1898, 138.06808) and is also passed as cross_linker_mass. The precursor lands about 69 Th too high and the test fails.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-041"></a>
### CPP-041: The mzML header writer does not escape several string attributes

**A `"`, `&` or `<` in these strings is written raw into a double-quoted attribute, so the mzML is not well-formed and cannot be reloaded. The reader unescapes these same fields from valid input (MzMLHandler.cpp:1142 externalSpectrumID, :1251 software version, :1547 fraction identifier, :2458/:2462 checksums), so converting a valid file that uses &amp; there silently produces a broken file. Such characters are uncommon in these fields: P1.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:3765 `os << "\t\t<software id=\"" << id << "\" version=\"" << software.getVersion() << "\" >\n";`; :3797/:3801 `value=\"" << source_file.getChecksum() << "\"`; :5226 `value=\"" << exp.getFractionIdentifier() << "\"`; :5400 `os << "externalSpectrumID=\"" << ac.getIdentifier() << "\"";`. The non-standard data array names are also raw (`value=\"" << array.getName() << "\"`) at :5579, :5602, :5794-5795, :5977 and :6002. The neighbouring :3793 uses writeXMLAttribute_.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): All of these sites now go through writeXMLAttribute_: software version (MzMLHandler.cpp:3800), SHA-1 and MD5 checksum text (:3832, :3836), fraction identifier (:5268), externalSpectrumID (:5481) and the non-standard array names in the spectrum and chromatogra…
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 181dadf src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp (writeXMLAttribute_ at 3815, 3847, 3851, 5273, 5486, 5633, 5656, 5849, 6032, 6057 in 4f5c86f)
- **Test:** At 4f5c86f, neither MzMLFile_test.cpp nor MSDataWritingConsumer_test.cpp tests quote, ampersand or less-than characters in these fields; the fix in 181dadf has no test. The log claims no execution.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-043"></a>
### CPP-043: XLMS precursor isotope companions omit charge normalization

**The isotope companions of the intact, -H2O and -NH3 precursor peaks are placed at charged mass + 1.00335/z, about z times the precursor m/z. The real companion is missing. OpenPepXL always adds these peaks, but its default deisotoping of high-resolution spectra removes the experimental isotope anyway, so scores change only in non-deisotoped (low-resolution or deisotope=false) searches or when the misplaced peak hits an unrelated fragment.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/TheoreticalSpectrumGeneratorXLMS.cpp:609 `double mono_pos = precursor_mass + (Constants::PROTON_MASS_U * static_cast<double>(charge));`, :610 `p.setMZ(mono_pos / static_cast<double>(charge));`, :623 `double pos = mono_pos + (Constants::C13C12_MASSDIFF_U / static_cast<double>(charge));`. The -H2O companion (:653) and the -NH3 companion (:682) repeat the pattern. The function is called with maxcharge at :389 and :925. SimpleTSGXLMS.cpp:398 already has the correct `(mono_pos + Constants::C13C12_MASSDIFF_U) / charge`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Real. Fixed in core-v4.0.0-ci.3 and reverted for ci.4 together with CPP-042, so that ci.4 restores the ci.2 cross-link spectra exactly; reverting it with the suffix losses was a scoping decision, not a finding that the fix was wrong
- **Commits:** open. Fixed in 181dadf (TheoreticalSpectrumGeneratorXLMS.cpp:623/653/682) and reverted in 23944b6 for ci.4 as a scoping decision.
- **Test:** The Rust log says 'No C++ spectrum execution is claimed'. There is no class test at 4f5c86f; the fix in 181dadf had none and was reverted in 23944b6. The 3 OpenPepXL regression-output changes cannot be attributed to this fix rather than the suffix-loss fixes.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-047"></a>
### CPP-047: ProForma XLMS link positions ignore preceding flattened sections

**For valid ProForma text such as '(PE)[+1]PK[+138.068#XL1]IDE//PEPK[#XL1]IDE', the range is not counted, so the alpha link is reported at position 1 instead of 3. TheoreticalSpectrumGeneratorXLMS then silently uses wrong linked/unlinked fragment boundaries and K-link eligibility. Cross-link ProForma with a range or ambiguous region before the link site is valid but uncommon, and only the ProForma API reaches this code.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp:2019-2043: findCrossLink increments `++position;` (:2040) only inside the `std::get_if<SequenceElement>` branch. toAASequence (:2290-2295) emits `region->elements[0]` for a non-empty AmbiguousRegion and every residue of `range->elements`. generateSpectrum (:2822-2834) copies alpha_pos/beta_pos into `crosslink.cross_link_position`. The two-chain issue check (:2079-2092) only compares labels. Ranges come from plain text, e.g. ProFormaParser_test.cpp:335 `ProForma::parse("PROT(EOSFORMS)[+19.0523]ISK")`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): findCrossLink now counts positions exactly as toAASequence emits residues: +1 per SequenceElement, +1 per non-empty AmbiguousRegion, +elements.size() per ModifiedRange (2060-2108).
- **Commits:** 181dadf ProForma.cpp findCrossLink now counts +1 per non-empty AmbiguousRegion and +elements.size() per ModifiedRange; 86f01c4 additionally converts with FAIL_ON_LOSS.
- **Test:** The Rust log says this was triggered through the public AST, 'not a claimed parsed-text or executed C++ spectrum result'. At 4f5c86f the ProFormaParser_test section 'regression: cross-linked spectra reject unrepresentable chemistry' (86f01c4) covers rejection of lossy chains. No test checks link-position counting after a range or ambiguous region.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-053"></a>
### CPP-053: Chromatogram array promotion mismatches values and their physical role

**Case 1: a chromatogram with [time, intensity, pressure/flow] is loaded with the canonical intensities as peaks, but relabelled as pressure/flow, and the physical array is silently dropped. OpenMS's own writer creates this case, since it emits a supplemental FloatDataArray named 'pressure array' under its CV term (MzMLHandler.cpp:5778-5790). Case 2: with [time, pressure, flow], the pressure values are later written as flow. Multi-array chromatograms are valid but uncommon, so P1.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:634-643 loops `for (auto& array : input_data) { ... const auto it = types.find(array.meta.getName()); if (it != types.end()) { inp_chromatogram.setMetaValue("mzml intensity array", it->second); array.meta.setName("intensity array"); } }` over every array. MzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:273-277 computeDataProperties_ returns the first array named 'intensity array'. MzMLHandler.cpp:696 skips arrays named 'intensity array' when it creates supplemental arrays. :5619 writes the peak intensities under the stored 'mzml intensity array' role.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): populateChromatogramsWithData_ (MzMLHandler.cpp:648-668) promotes a physical array only when no canonical 'intensity array' is present, and then only the first one in document order.
- **Commits:** 181dadf src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp populateChromatogramsWithData_ (promote only when no canonical intensity array exists, first match only)
- **Test:** No test at 4f5c86f loads a chromatogram with several candidate arrays. The MzMLFile_test Thermo section (:1455-1497) has a single pressure array only. The log's value example comes from reading the code and was not executed.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-056"></a>
### CPP-056: Synthetic mzML scans omit spectrum ion mobility

**Writing mzML silently drops the spectrum-level drift time, 1/K0, CCS or FAIMS CV of any spectrum without acquisition records. FileConverter reaches this on Bruker DDA-PASEF .d input: BrukerTimsFile.cpp:2630-2634 and :2773-2777 set VSSC drift time on MS2 spectra and never add an Acquisition. In that path the precursor keeps the same 1/K0 (writePrecursor_ :4607). Unique mobility is lost only for less common in-memory, pyOpenMS or IMDataConverter spectra, so P1.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:5407-5442 writes the spectrum mobility cvParams (FAIMS, ms, VSSC, CCS) only inside `if (j == 0)` of the `for (Size j = 0; j < spec.getAcquisitionInfo().size(); ++j)` loop. The fallback at :5469-5473, `if (spec.getAcquisitionInfo().empty()) { os << "\t\t\t\t\t<scan>\n"; os << ... name=\"scan start time\" ...`, writes RT, zoom scan and scan windows but no mobility term. The reader only takes spectrum mobility from cvParams (:2309).
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The first-scan RT and mobility output (including the FAIMS signed-value rule and the rule that an unset drift time is not written) is now a single local lambda, write_first_scan_rt_and_mobility (MzMLHandler.cpp:5438).
- **Commits:** 181dadf src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp (write_first_scan_rt_and_mobility lambda shared by real and synthetic scans)
- **Test:** No class test at 4f5c86f stores a spectrum with drift time and empty AcquisitionInfo. MzMLFile_test and MSDataWritingConsumer_test changes in the range do not touch drift time. The log is based on reading the code.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-058"></a>
### CPP-058: Nonnumeric DataValue casts read an inactive union member

**Casting a String or list DataValue to a floating type reads the inactive double member instead of throwing the documented ConversionError. This is undefined behaviour. In practice the pointer bits come back as a number; it does not crash or corrupt memory. From input files, it is reached only by schema-valid but unusual mzML: a spectrum without scan start time and with an untyped 'elution time (seconds)' userParam, which silently gets a garbage RT. Elsewhere it needs API casts of non-numeric metadata, so P1.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/DATASTRUCTURES/DataValue.cpp:466-478 `DataValue::operator double() const { if (value_type_ == EMPTY_VALUE) { throw ... } else if (value_type_ == INT_VALUE) { return double(data_.ssize_); } return data_.dou_; }`. The same pattern is in long double (:452-464) and float (:480-492). Reach from input: MzMLHandler.cpp:1401-1403 `if (!rt_set_ && spec_.metaValueExists("elution time (seconds)")) { spec_.setRT(spec_.getMetaValue("elution time (seconds)")); }`. An untyped userParam becomes a String through XMLHandler.h:171-174 `else { data_value = DataValue(value); }` and is stored on the spectrum at MzMLHandler.cpp:3488-3490.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Each of the three operators gets `else if (value_type_ != DOUBLE_VALUE) throw Exception::ConversionError(__FILE__, __LINE__, OPENMS_PRETTY_FUNCTION, "Could not convert non-numeric DataValue of type '" + NamesOfDataType[value_type_] + "' and value '" + this->t…
- **Commits:** 181dadf src/openms/source/DATASTRUCTURES/DataValue.cpp + DataValue.h (throw ConversionError for any type other than INT_VALUE/DOUBLE_VALUE)
- **Test:** DataValue_test.cpp is unchanged in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..4f5c86f. Its floating ConversionError checks (:1004-1016) cover only EMPTY_VALUE. The log states no execution or sanitizer run.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-061"></a>
### CPP-061: SpectrumHelper::makePeakPositionUnique discards the whole spectrum record

**Any spectrum or chromatogram passed through the public template silently loses all its metadata, which is data loss on valid input. The only in-tree caller (FeatureFinderMultiplexAlgorithm.cpp:480) passes a metadata-free local chromatogram, and the function is not bound in pyOpenMS, so no tool output is affected. That keeps it below P0.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/SpectrumHelper.h:158 `PeakContainerT p_new;` (default-constructed, only merged peaks pushed) and :196 `std::swap(p_new, p);`, so p loses RT, MS level, native ID, precursors and meta values. The warning at :150 mentions only data arrays.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): p_new now starts as a copy of p followed by `p_new.clear(false)`.
- **Commits:** packages/core 181dadf: SpectrumHelper.h `PeakContainerT p_new = p; p_new.clear(false);`
- **Test:** SpectrumHelper_test.cpp section 'makePeakPositionUnique' at 4f5c86f checks only intensities and sizes, never metadata. The log claims no execution. The default construction followed by the swap is unambiguous in the source.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-066"></a>
### CPP-066: BinnedSpectrum default construction leaves a null bin matrix

**A null-pointer dereference follows from calling getBinIntensity (or any functor using getBins()) on a default-constructed BinnedSpectrum; from Python, `BinnedSpectrum().getBinIntensity(100.0)` segfaults the interpreter. Such an object has no bin layout, and no in-tree code reads one (ClusterHierarchical, TargetedSpectraExtractor and MetaboTargetedAssay all use the detailed constructor), so this is memory-safety only through API misuse.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/BinnedSpectrum.h:93 `BinnedSpectrum() {};` with :172 `SparseVectorType* bins_ {nullptr};`. src/openms/source/KERNEL/BinnedSpectrum.cpp:191-194 `float BinnedSpectrum::getBinIntensity(double mz) { return bins_->coeffRef(getBinIndex(mz)); }` (bin_size_ is 0, so getBinIndex also converts floor(mz/0) to an index). Exposed in src/pyOpenMS/bindings/bind_kernel.cpp:211 `.def(nb::init<>())` and :218 getBinIntensity.
- **Evidence:** test-backed
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): getBinIntensity returns 0 without a bin layout or beyond the last bin; the first fix still converted floor(mz / 0) to an index, which aborted a Debug build.
- **Commits:** packages/core 181dadf: BinnedSpectrum.cpp default ctor allocates empty bins, getBinIntensity guards empty/no-layout/out-of-range; test in BinnedSpectrum_test.cpp
- **Test:** packages/core 4f5c86f src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/BinnedSpectrum_test.cpp, section START_SECTION((BinnedSpectrum())), added in 181dadf: calls empty.getBinIntensity(100.0), getBinIntensity(0.0) and getBins()->nonZeros() on a default-constructed object. Pre-fix code dereferences the null bins_, after a UB float-to-index conversion, and crashes.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-072"></a>
### CPP-072: DRange::united of two empty ranges returns the universal range

**Uniting two empty DRanges gives min = DBL_MAX and max = -DBL_MAX per dimension, which normalize_ swaps into [-DBL_MAX, DBL_MAX]: a non-empty universal range instead of an empty one.**  
- **Trigger:** DRange<D>::united where both operands are empty, e.g. `DRange<1>().united(DRange<1>())`, or an accumulation loop `bbox = bbox.united(r)` that starts from a default (empty) range and meets an empty r before any non-empty one. Reachable from the C++ API and from pyOpenMS DRange1.united / DRange2.united (bind_format.cpp:1720-1721, :1773). git grep finds no caller in src/openms, src/topp or src/openswathalgo.
- **Consequence:** The result claims to contain every coordinate (isEmpty() false). Any later union in an accumulation stays universal, and containment or intersection tests against it silently succeed. This is a wrong result on valid but uncommon input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/DATASTRUCTURES/DRange.h:178-195 united() has no empty check: :189 `united_min[i] = min_[i] < other_min[i] ? min_[i] : other_min[i];`, :190 `united_max[i] = max_[i] > other_max[i] ? max_[i] : other_max[i];`, :192 `united_range.setMinMax(united_min, united_max);`. DIntervalBase.h:142-147 setMinMax calls normalize_(), and :346-348 `if (min_[i] > max_[i]) { std::swap(min_[i], max_[i]); }`. The empty sentinel is :368-369 (maxPositive, minNegative), and isEmpty() at :216-219 is `*this == empty`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Arithmetic follows directly from the code. The Rust report claims no C++ run. The Core triage ledger mentions a clang++ mock of the template hierarchy printing min0=-1.79769e+308, max0=1.79769e+308, but that mock is not the real header and not a class test. DRange_test.cpp:362-376 unites only non-empty ranges and is unchanged in Core.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): united() now begins with `if (this->isEmpty() && other_range.isEmpty()) return DRange<D>::empty;` (ci.5 DRange.h:185), plus an @note that uniting two empty ranges gives an empty range. When one operand is non-empty the result was already correct and is unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3, in ci.5; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-077"></a>
### CPP-077: rasterizeIMFrame multiplies the bin counts without an overflow check

**rasterizeIMFrame multiplies im_bins by mz_bins without an overflow check, so a product that wraps size_t produces a small total_pixels while pixel indices are computed from the unwrapped counts and are written out of bounds.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ call `spec.rasterizeIMFrame(buf, 1ull<<33, 1ull<<33, ...)`, or any im_bins and mz_bins whose product exceeds SIZE_MAX, with a buffer sized by the wrapped product, on a spectrum with in-range peaks. pyOpenMS cannot reach it, because bind_spectrum.cpp:602-603 takes the bin counts from the numpy array's shape, which matches a real allocation. There are no TOPP or GUI callers at develop.
- **Consequence:** A heap out-of-bounds write. Only bin counts no real buffer can have reach it, so it is memory safety through C++ API misuse.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.cpp:895 `const Size total_pixels = im_bins * mz_bins;` (unchecked), :898 `std::fill(output, output + total_pixels, 0.0f);`, :951 `const Size pixel_idx = static_cast<Size>(mz_bin) * im_bins + static_cast<Size>(im_bin);`. The only bin-count checks are the zero checks at :872-881. The same unchecked product is at MSExperiment.cpp:297 in rasterizeRTMZ.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream code read at 5082181714. There is no execution or sanitizer run: the Rust report says source review, and MSSpectrum_rasterizeIMFrame_test.cpp is unchanged in ci.2..p0-followups. Its InvalidValue sections at :209 and :220 are the older zero-bin tests. Two related gaps: (1) upstream :931 lets NaN m/z or IM values through, since every comparison with NaN is false, into the UB Int64 cast at :937-938, and there is no clamp for negative indices. Core's 181dadf fixed that too, with a std::isfinite filter and clamps below 0. (2) rasterizeRTMZ's identical unchecked `rt_bins * mz_bins` is still in Core (dax/codex/p0-followups MSExperiment.cpp:298).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): MSSpectrum.cpp:959 at ci.5 throws Exception::InvalidValue when `im_bins > std::numeric_limits<Size>::max() / sizeof(float) / mz_bins`, before any write. The same commit rejects non-finite ranges and scales, skips non-finite peaks and clamps negative bin indices to 0.
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (codex/cpp-review-completion; also on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-080"></a>
### CPP-080: sortByPositionPresorted trusts is_sorted and feeds std::inplace_merge an unsorted range

**sortByPositionPresorted trusts each chunk's is_sorted flag, and TheoreticalSpectrumGenerator flags its isotope-with-losses chunk as sorted when it is not, so std::inplace_merge returns an unsorted theoretical spectrum.**  
- **Trigger:** TheoreticalSpectrumGenerator::getSpectrum with isotope_model=coarse (max_isotope>=2, default 2) or fine, add_losses=true and add_metainfo=true (sort_by_position defaults to true), for a peptide whose ion contains both an H2O-loss (D/E/S/T) and an NH3-loss (K/N/Q/R) residue, e.g. y2 'EK' of PEPTIDEK. Reached from the TOPPView 'Generate theoretical spectrum' dialog (TheoreticalSpectrumGenerationDialog.cpp:238 forces add_metainfo, :61 add_losses, :155-172 coarse/fine), pyOpenMS, and any C++ caller passing a false is_sorted claim.
- **Consequence:** The H2O-loss cluster (M-18.0106, M-17.007) is emitted before the NH3-loss peak (M-17.0265), so the chunk is unsorted. inplace_merge keeps the relative order of elements from one input range, so the returned spectrum is silently unsorted even though sort_by_position was requested. Consumers that binary-search (findNearest, MZBegin/MZEnd) or assume order get wrong answers; SpectrumAlignment is not silent, since SpectrumAlignment.h:64-66 throws IllegalArgument on unsorted input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.cpp:419-422 `if (!chunks[i].is_sorted) { std::stable_sort(select_indices.begin() + chunks[i].start, ...); }`, :433 `std::inplace_merge(... chunks[first].start, ... chunks[mid].end, ... chunks[last].end, comparePos)`; :405-408 whole-spectrum stable_sort only when no data arrays. Producer: src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/TheoreticalSpectrumGenerator.cpp:875-881 and :978-983 `addLosses_(spectrum, ion, ...)` for every ion then `chunks.add(true);`; addLosses_ :470 `std::set<std::string> losses;` iterated at :484, and with isotopes :524-532 emits each loss's whole isotope cluster; :210-220 add_metainfo pushes the ion-name/charge arrays before :222 `if (sort_by_position_) spectrum.sortByPositionPresorted(chunks.getChunks());`. ResidueDB.cpp:154-221: D/E/S/T lose H2O, K/N/Q/R lose NH3; EmpiricalFormula::toString (EmpiricalFormula.cpp:258-266) gives "H2O1" < "H3N1".
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Traced from upstream sources: data arrays exist before the sort only with add_metainfo (TSG.cpp:210-222), which selects the merge branch; the loss chunk ordering follows from the std::set<std::string> order and the isotope emission loop; the out-of-order pair is by mass arithmetic (-18.0106+1.0034 > -17.0265; a 15N shift of 0.997 also exceeds 0.984). Upstream TSG tests combining isotope_model with add_losses (TheoreticalSpectrumGenerator_test.cpp:562-579, :749-804) leave add_metainfo at false, so the whole-spectrum stable_sort hides it. No C++ run is recorded; the Rust report is source review; no Core regression test was added.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): In the merge branch a chunk claiming to be sorted is checked with std::is_sorted using the same comparator and stable-sorted if the claim is false; the single-chunk shortcut also requires isSorted(). TSG still sets the wrong flag, but the merge now gets sorted inputs.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-081"></a>
### CPP-081: Chunks::add can record a chunk whose start exceeds its end

**If a spectrum loses peaks between two add() calls, the recorded chunk has start > end, or start past the end. When data arrays are present, sortByPositionPresorted then builds iterators past the vector and passes inverted ranges to stable_sort/inplace_merge, which is undefined behaviour. The only in-tree user, TheoreticalSpectrumGenerator::getSpectrum, only appends peaks, so this needs API misuse: P1.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.h:80-83 `void add(bool is_sorted) { chunks_.emplace_back((chunks_.empty() ? 0 : chunks_.back().end), spec_.size(), is_sorted); }` with no size check; consumed unchecked at src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.cpp:421 `std::stable_sort(select_indices.begin() + chunks[i].start, select_indices.begin() + chunks[i].end, comparePos);` and :433 `std::inplace_merge(select_indices.begin() + chunks[first].start, ... + chunks[mid].end, ... + chunks[last].end, comparePos);`
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): MSSpectrum.h:87-101: `add()` now hoists the start into a local and throws Exception::Precondition (OPENMS_PRETTY_FUNCTION, message carrying both sizes) when `spec_.size() < start`, so an inverted or past-the-end chunk can never be recorded;
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 core 181dadf: src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.h (Chunks::add throws Precondition) + MSSpectrum.cpp sortByPositionPresorted chunk-tiling validation
- **Test:** No OpenMS4 test exercises add() after the spectrum shrinks. The only Chunks test (MSSpectrum_test.cpp sortByPositionPresorted section, line 895) predates the fix and only appends. The log reports no execution.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-084"></a>
### CPP-084: mergePeaks leaves the destination's data arrays mis-sized, so a later sort or select throws

**Merging two valid, sorted chromatograms where the destination has peak-parallel data arrays and the other adds new RTs leaves the object violating its own array-size invariant. The next select(), sort(lambda) or permuting sortByIntensity() then throws Exception::Precondition on valid input, and the arrays are silently misaligned before that. By the rubric this is an exception on valid, uncommon input (P1), though reach is C++-API-only and the exception is clean rather than a crash.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSChromatogram.cpp:548-553 `temp.resize(size() + other.size()); auto new_end = setSumSimilarUnion(...); ContainerType::assign(temp.begin(), new_end);` never touches float_data_arrays_/string_data_arrays_/integer_data_arrays_. checkDataArraySizes_ (:355-372) throws Precondition on a non-empty mismatched array and runs from selectUnchecked (:399) and sort() (MSChromatogram.h:245). The header note at MSChromatogram.h:472 only says the metadata 'is not guaranteed to be correct after merging'.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): MSChromatogram.cpp:565-569: clear the three peak-parallel arrays inside mergePeaks, with a comment saying why (they describe the pre-merge peaks and the size mismatch would make the chromatogram's own sort()/select() throw), mirroring clear() at MSChromatogra…
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 core 181dadf: src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSChromatogram.cpp (clear the three data arrays in mergePeaks) + MSChromatogram.h @note
- **Test:** No OpenMS4 test covers data arrays after mergePeaks (MSChromatogram_test.cpp is unchanged between ci.2 and 4f5c86f). The log reports no execution. The log's trigger, sortByPosition(), does not throw upstream: it returns early when isSorted() (MSChromatogram.cpp:167), and a merge of sorted inputs is sorted. select(), sort(lambda) and a sortByIntensity() that has to permute do throw.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-090"></a>
### CPP-090: The same comparator mutates its arguments, so hits are sorted only where the sort happens to compare

**Hits are sorted only inside the std::sort comparator, so a feature with exactly one PeptideIdentification (never compared) keeps its hits unsorted although sortPeptideIdentifications() promises sorted identifications.**  
- **Trigger:** A Feature carrying exactly one PeptideIdentification whose hits are not in score order (featureXML keeps file order; IDScoreSwitcher does not re-sort), processed by FeatureLinkerUnlabeledQT with use_identifications=true. With two or more identifications every element is compared at least once and gets sorted.
- **Consequence:** QTClusterFinder takes a non-best hit as hits[0]: the min_score_ test and the sequence/charge key used to collect RTs for the ID-based RT tolerance bins (QTClusterFinder.cpp:110-119, 138-232) are computed from the wrong hit, so the estimated linking tolerances, and hence the linking, can differ. No crash.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/BaseFeature.cpp:125-127 'std::sort(peptides_.rbegin(),peptides_.rend(), [](PeptideIdentification& p1, PeptideIdentification& p2) {p1.sort();p2.sort();'. Consumer QTClusterFinder.cpp:106-115 'feat.sortPeptideIdentifications(); auto& hits = pepIDs[0].getHits(); ... hits[0].getScore() > min_score_ ... const std::string key = pepIDs[0].getHits()[0].getSequence().toString() + "/" + feat.getCharge();'. No hit sort precedes it there (the pep.sort() loop at :400-403 runs later, in run_internal_). PIPECHO/Impl.cpp:71-76 sorts each identification's hits before the call, so it is not affected.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Argument from develop source only. std::sort performs no comparison on a one-element range, so p.sort() never runs. No Core test covers a single identification with unsorted hits: the BaseFeature_test sortPeptideIdentifications section uses three identifications, which the old comparator also sorts. The Rust report and cpp-p0-walkthrough.md claim no execution.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): BaseFeature.cpp (ci.5 :126-131): every identification's hits are sorted in a separate pass before std::sort, and the comparator (:147) takes const PeptideIdentification&, so it can no longer modify its arguments.
- **Commits:** 181dadf [FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5; first released in 4f5c86f/ci.4); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** none (BaseFeature_test.cpp sortPeptideIdentifications section, changed in b6dd412, runs the code with three identifications and passes on pre-fix code as well)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-091"></a>
### CPP-091: Mixed isHigherScoreBetter flags make the sort comparator asymmetric

**When a feature's identifications disagree on isHigherScoreBetter, the comparator can return true for both comp(A,B) and comp(B,A), violating std::sort's strict weak ordering (undefined behaviour).**  
- **Trigger:** sortPeptideIdentifications() on a feature with at least two identifications that have hits and opposite orientations, e.g. A (higher-better, best score 0.2) and B (lower-better, best score 0.5). Reachable via FeatureLinkerUnlabeledQT use_identifications=true (QTClusterFinder.cpp:106) or PIPECHO (Impl.cpp:76) on featureXML where IDs from different engines were mapped without ConsensusID. This violates the documented same-score-type precondition.
- **Consequence:** Undefined behaviour in std::sort. On the few identifications a feature normally carries, the practical result is an arbitrary order, which decides which identification QTClusterFinder treats as best. Out-of-range access in the library sort is possible in principle on longer ranges but was not demonstrated.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/BaseFeature.cpp:136-142 'if (p1.isHigherScoreBetter()) { return p1.getHits()[0].getScore() < p2.getHits()[0].getScore(); } else { return p1.getHits()[0].getScore() > p2.getHits()[0].getScore(); }'. The direction comes from the left operand only. BaseFeature.h:164 only documents the assumption: 'sorts PeptideIdentifications, assuming they have the same scoreType.' Nothing checks it.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Asymmetry shown from the source by the A/B example. No C++ test mixes orientations. The hit-less ids[2] (higher_score_better=false) added in b6dd412 never reaches the direction branch. cpp-high-priority-unverified.md lists it as static-only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): BaseFeature.cpp (ci.5 :132-147): orientation is read once from the first identification with hits (default true) and captured by value, so every comparison uses one direction. Mixed-engine identifications are now ordered consistently, but by that first identification's direction rather than rejected (the Rust port returns an error instead).
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5; released first in 4f5c86f/ci.4); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-096"></a>
### CPP-096: Feature::getConvexHull() is a const method that lazily mutates the object and hands out a mutable reference

**getConvexHull() is const but lazily rewrites the cached hull and returns a non-const reference, so concurrent calls on the same Feature race, and a caller holding a const Feature can modify the cache.**  
- **Trigger:** (1) Two threads making the first getConvexHull() call on the same Feature after setConvexHulls() or a non-const getConvexHulls(), which includes every Feature loaded from featureXML. Both write convex_hull_'s containers unsynchronised. (2) f.getConvexHull().clear() on a const Feature& compiles. In-tree, the OpenMP use at FeatureFinderAlgorithmPicked.cpp:595/805 is on a loop-local Feature. The only write through the reference is FeatureFinderCentroided.cpp:366 'ft.getConvexHull().expandToBoundingBox();', which the non-const getConvexHulls() call on :367 immediately marks dirty again, so it has no effect.
- **Consequence:** A data race on std::vector members, which can corrupt the heap, in user C++/OpenMP code that shares a Feature across threads. Through the mutable reference, a cached hull can disagree with convex_hulls_ until the next invalidation. No upstream code path triggers either, and pyOpenMS returns a copy (bind_kernel.cpp:3930) under the GIL.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/Feature.h:99 'ConvexHull2D& getConvexHull() const;' over :176 'mutable bool convex_hulls_modified_{};' and :179 'mutable ConvexHull2D convex_hull_;'. src/openms/source/KERNEL/Feature.cpp:93-135 recomputes when dirty ('convex_hull_ = convex_hulls_[0];' :101, 'convex_hulls_modified_ = false;' :132, 'return convex_hull_;' :135). The non-const getConvexHulls() and setConvexHulls() set the flag (:83, :89).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source review only. The Core test change (b6dd412, Feature_test.cpp) only renames the section to the const signature; there is no concurrency or const-mutation test, and no sanitizer run is recorded.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): Feature.h:103 and Feature.cpp:93 now return 'const ConvexHull2D&', so mutating through the result no longer compiles. That half is fixed; TOPP's FeatureFinderCentroided call had to be removed on its compatibility branch. The lazy mutable cache and its race remain: Core only added a @note that the method writes the cache and must not be called concurrently on one feature. The review table records the item as fixed, but the race part is documented, not fixed.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (return type) and b6dd412 (test section rename), both in core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Test:** none (Feature_test.cpp section renamed only)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-105"></a>
### CPP-105: IndexedMzMLHandler copy constructor silently drops both native-id maps

**Looking up any spectrum or chromatogram by native id on a copied IndexedMzMLHandler or OnDiscMSExperiment throws IllegalArgument ('Could not find spectrum id') even though the id exists, while index-based access still works. The class docs recommend per-thread copies (OnDiscMSExperiment.h:61-67), so this is an exception on valid use. No TOPP tool combines a copy with native-id lookup (TICCalculator's firstprivate(map) uses getSpectrumById).**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:76-88: the copy constructor initialises filename_, spectra_offsets_, chromatograms_offsets_, index_offset_, spectra_before_chroms_, filestream_, parsing_success_ and skip_xml_checks_, but not spectra_native_ids_ or chromatograms_native_ids_ (declared at IndexedMzMLHandler.h:60 and :64). The lookups at IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:255 (`if (!spectra_native_ids_.contains(id))`) and :288 then throw IllegalArgument.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Added spectra_native_ids_(source.spectra_native_ids_) and chromatograms_native_ids_(source.chromatograms_native_ids_) to the copy constructor in declaration order, with a comment saying why.
- **Commits:** core 181dadf src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp (both maps added to the copy initialiser list)
- **Test:** No regression test. The core copy-constructor section (IndexedMzMLFile_test.cpp:50-72) compares index-based access only, and OnDiscMSExperiment_test.cpp `[EXTRA] Test copy constructor copies options` checks options only. Neither file changed in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..4f5c86f, and the log reports source review only.
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-106"></a>
### CPP-106: IndexedMzMLHandler::openFile accumulates index state instead of replacing it

**When a second valid indexed mzML is opened on the same handler, getNrSpectra() returns the sum of both files. The first file's offsets and native ids (emplace keeps the old key) are then read against the new stream, so records come back wrong, or a negative span throws std::bad_array_new_length. The API supports reopening, but it is uncommon, which makes this P1; no test at 4f5c86f opens two indexed files in a row.**  
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop edc7aa4cbf (13 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:37-46 appends with no clear(): `spectra_native_ids_.emplace(off.first, spectra_offsets_.size()); spectra_offsets_.push_back(off.second);` (same for chromatograms). openFile at :92-101 only does `filestream_.close(); filename_ = filename; filestream_.open(filename); parseFooter_();`. The early return at :27-31 also leaves the old offsets in place.
- **Evidence:** code-reading
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): parseFooter_ now starts by clearing both offset vectors and both native-id maps, and resets index_offset_ to -1, spectra_before_chroms_ to true and parsing_success_ to false.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 core 181dadf, src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp (parseFooter_ clears offsets and maps and resets index_offset_, spectra_before_chroms_, parsing_success_)
- **Confirmed by:** ranking classifier plus two adversarial skeptics

<a id="cpp-115"></a>
### CPP-115: ConsensusMap::appendRows pairs column headers by position, not by column index

**appendRows pairs the merged column headers with rhs's headers by position instead of by map index, so when the two maps have different column keys it adds the size of an unrelated column, renames the wrong header, and doubles the size of a column that only rhs has.**  
- **Trigger:** ConsensusMap::appendRows (FileMerger -append_method append_rows at FileMerger.cpp:324, or pyOpenMS appendRows) on two maps whose column-index sets differ, e.g. this {0:"a"} and rhs {1:"b"}: header 0 becomes "mergedConsensusXMLFile" with size a+b, and header 1 keeps "b". For this {0} and rhs {0,1}, column 1 gets size 2*b. Maps with the same keys, the use FileMerger documents ('inputs come from the same MS run'), are merged correctly.
- **Consequence:** Wrong column-header sizes and lost original filenames are written to the output consensusXML. Downstream code that reads the header size or filename, such as the ConsensusMapNormalizer reference-map choice or run attribution, then works with wrong values. There is no diagnostic.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/ConsensusMap.cpp:82 'column_description_.insert(rhs.column_description_.begin(), rhs.column_description_.end());' then :88-91 'for (; it != column_description_.end() && it2 != rhs.column_description_.end(); ++it, ++it2) { getColumnHeaders()[it->first].filename = "mergedConsensusXMLFile"; getColumnHeaders()[it->first].size = it->second.size + it2->second.size; }'
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Argument from the code. The pre-existing ConsensusMap_test.cpp appendRows section (Core dax/codex/p0-followups:174-213) does append headers {0:m1} and {1:m2}, but it only checks getColumnHeaders().size()==2, which passes before and after the fix. The Rust report is source review; its Rust test copies the positional behaviour in Rust and is not a C++ run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The insert-then-lockstep loop is replaced by one loop over rhs's headers. Each key is looked up in column_description_: a key only rhs has is copied as it is, and a key in both maps gets its size summed and its filename set to mergedConsensusXMLFile.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** none (ConsensusMap_test.cpp 'appendRows' section does not assert header filenames or sizes)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-116"></a>
### CPP-116: ConsensusMap::setPrimaryMSRunPath writes by position through a default-inserting map

**setPrimaryMSRunPath writes path i into column key i through operator[], so on a map whose column keys are not 0..n-1 it creates new empty columns and leaves the real columns with their old filenames.**  
- **Trigger:** ConsensusMap::setPrimaryMSRunPath(StringList), or its MSExperiment overload, on a map with sparse column keys, e.g. headers {5} and one path. The count check passes, column 0 is created with the path, and column 5 keeps its stale filename. Reached from C++ or pyOpenMS (bind_kernel.cpp:3449-3450). The in-tree TOPP callers (FeatureLinkerUnlabeled.cpp:310, FeatureFinderMultiplex.cpp:387, MassTraceExtractor.cpp:230/234) pass maps keyed 0..n-1 or empty maps.
- **Consequence:** An extra, empty column appears and the real column keeps the wrong run path, so features are attributed to the wrong or an unknown MS run when the map is stored or exported. There is no diagnostic.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/ConsensusMap.cpp:518 'else if (!column_description_.empty() && s.size() != column_description_.size())' (count check only), then :525 'Size i(0);' and :534 'column_description_[i].filename = p;' (std::map::operator[] default-inserts)
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Argument from the code. No Core or upstream class test calls ConsensusMap::setPrimaryMSRunPath on sparse keys. The Rust report is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The existing column keys are first collected in index order, and path i is written to column_indices[i]. The running index is used only past the end, which the count check limits to a map with no headers, so that case still gains one column per path.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-143"></a>
