### CPP-143: Negative CHEMMOD identifiers fail modification-cell parsing

**MzTabModification::fromCellString splits on every '-', so a CHEMMOD identifier with a negative mass ('8-CHEMMOD:-18.010565' or position-less 'CHEMMOD:-18.010565') throws ConversionError.**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabFile::load (MzTabFile.cpp:976, 1154, 1317 for PRT/PEP/PSM modifications) via FileInfo or pyOpenMS MzTabFile.load, on an mzTab carrying a non-UNIMOD negative-mass modification, including files OpenMS's own MzTab export writes for such a modification (extractModificationList -> getModificationIdentifier_). A hyphen in a position parameter's text triggers the same throw.
- **Consequence:** Loading a valid mzTab aborts with ConversionError (no try/catch in MzTabFile::load), so OpenMS cannot read back its own output for that modification.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTab.cpp:131 `if (!StringUtils::hasSubstring(lower, "-"))`, else :140 `StringUtils::split(ss, "-", fields);` and :142-144 `if (fields.size() != 2) { throw Exception::ConversionError(... "Can't convert to MzTabModification from '") + s);`; :157 `StringUtils::toInt32(position_fields[i])` is strict (StringUtils.cpp:240-269). The exporter writes :1528 `MzTabString("CHEMMOD:" + StringUtils::toStr(r.getDiffMonoMass()))`, used for PSM/peptide modifications at :2558, :2586, :2601.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — '8-CHEMMOD:-18.010565' splits into three fields and hits the size check; 'CHEMMOD:-18.010565' splits into two and toInt32("CHEMMOD:") throws. Rust log: 'no C++ execution'; the Core ledger used a Python simulation; no Core test contains a negative CHEMMOD (git grep 'CHEMMOD:-' in ci.5 and p0-followups tests finds nothing).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The position/identifier separator is now the first '-' outside parameter brackets and quotes that follows only digits, '|' and spaces; any other leading character means there is no position list and the whole trimmed cell is the identifier, so signed CHEMMOD masses and hyphens inside parameters parse.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-144"></a>
### CPP-144: Quoted commas split a MzTab modification-list entry

**A comma inside a quoted name or value of a modification's position parameter is not protected, so the modifications cell is split in the middle of that parameter.**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabFile::load of PRT/PEP/PSM modifications (MzTabFile.cpp:976, 1154, 1317) via FileInfo or pyOpenMS, for a cell such as `8[MS,MS:1001876,"a, b",0.9]-UNIMOD:21` (mzTab requires quoting names containing commas). OpenMS's exporter only writes the 'false localization rate' parameter, so input comes from third-party files, or from a store after the CPP-145 fix starts quoting bare commas.
- **Consequence:** In practice the load aborts: the first fragment becomes a bogus identifier (e.g. `8[,,"blabla`) and the second fragment's position toInt32 or '-' split throws ConversionError, so a valid file cannot be read; contrived cells could be silently mis-split.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTab.cpp:263 (MzTabModificationList::fromCellString) `if (ss[pos] == ',' && !in_quotes && in_param_bracket)` replaces only unquoted in-bracket commas with BEL, then :271 `StringUtils::split(ss, ",", fields);`. The function's own comment at :234 gives the case `8[,,"blabla, [bla]",v]`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Traced the scan on the comment's own example: the quoted comma stays a comma, the split yields `8[\a\a"blabla` and ` [bla]"\av]-mod:123`, and the second throws in toInt32(" "). Rust log: 'no C++ execution'; no Core test covers it.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The protection condition is now `ss[pos] == ',' && (in_param_bracket || in_quotes)`, so commas inside a parameter or inside quoted text are replaced before the split and restored afterwards.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-145"></a>
### CPP-145: MzTab parameter rendering fails to quote a bare comma

**MzTabParameter::toCellString quotes a name or value only if it contains ", ", so a bare comma (e.g. 'N,N-dimethyl') is written unquoted and the cell gains a fifth field.**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabFile::store / MzTabMFile::store of any MzTabParameter cell (metadata, search_engine, score types, modification parameters) whose name or value has a comma not followed by a space: a load-then-store of a file with a quoted bare-comma name (fromCellString strips the quotes), or C++/pyOpenMS setName/setValue. Upstream exporters build parameters with fromCellString from fixed strings or UniMod titles, which contain no bare commas.
- **Consequence:** The written mzTab is malformed for that cell: OpenMS's own MzTabFile::load throws ConversionError on it and spec readers split the parameter wrongly.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTabBase.cpp:337 `if (StringUtils::hasSubstring(name_, ", "))` and :348 `if (StringUtils::hasSubstring(value_, ", "))` in MzTabParameter::toCellString; fromCellString (:362-409) splits on every unquoted comma and throws at :406-408 unless `fields.size() == 4`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Direct comparison of the writer's ", " test against the reader's any-comma split. Rust log: 'no C++ execution'; no Core test round-trips a bare-comma parameter.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Both checks use the char overload `hasSubstring(name_, ',')` / `hasSubstring(value_, ',')`, so any comma causes quoting.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-146"></a>
### CPP-146: MzTab score-by-run header order differs from row order

**MzTabFile writes search_engine_score[i]_ms_run[j] headers run-major but the row cells score-major, so with two or more score types and two or more runs the off-diagonal values sit under the wrong score/run column.**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabFile::store(filename, MzTab) from C++ or pyOpenMS with a section row holding at least two score types and two ms_runs, e.g. MzTabFile.load of a multi-score third-party mzTab followed by store. Upstream exporters fill only score index 1 (MzTab.cpp:806, 859, 984, 1162; AccurateMassSearchEngine.cpp:1042), where both orders agree; the protein header also renumbers runs 1..N regardless of stored keys.
- **Consequence:** Silent wrong result in the written file: scores are attributed to another score type/run (header and cell counts still match, so nothing fails).
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTabFile.cpp:2037-2042 (generateMzTabProteinHeader_) outer `for (Size i = 0; i != reference_row.search_engine_score_ms_run.begin()->second.size(); ++i)` over runs, inner over score types, pushing `search_engine_score[<score>]_ms_run[i + 1]`; the protein row :2119-2125 walks `row.search_engine_score_ms_run` (MzTab.h:191 `search_engine_score[index1]_ms_run[index2]`, score major) score first, run second. Peptide header :2231-2236 (`for i < search_ms_runs` / `for j < n_search_engine_scores`) against row :2334-2337; the small molecule, nucleic acid and oligonucleotide generators have the same nesting.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Loop nesting of header vs row generators compared; loader (MzTabFile.cpp:750-751, 952) confirms the map is keyed score then run. Rust log: 'no C++ execution'; no Core test writes a multi-score multi-run row.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): All header generators now loop score-major like the rows; the protein header iterates the reference row's own map and emits the stored run keys instead of 1..N.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-147"></a>
### CPP-147: MzTab PSM optional columns are dropped on load

**The mzTab reader recognises a PSM optional column only if its header cell is literally "opt_", so every real opt_<context>_<name> PSM column is silently dropped on load.**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabFile::load of any mzTab 1.0 file whose PSH line has opt_ columns, e.g. OpenMS's own opt_global_cv_MS:1002217_decoy_peptide and opt_global_cv_MS:1000889_peptidoform_sequence (MzTab.cpp:2261-2262). Reached via pyOpenMS MzTabFile.load and FileInfo (src/openms/source/FORMAT/FileInfo.cpp:1479, which only counts rows).
- **Consequence:** Read-side data loss: the PSM rows load without any optional-column values (decoy flag, peptidoform sequence, user columns). No value is wrong and nothing is written back, but a pyOpenMS load, or a load followed by store, loses those columns silently.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTabFile.cpp:1287 (origin/develop 5082181714) in the PSH parser: `else if (cells[i] == "opt_") { psm_custom_opt_columns[cells[i]] = i; }`. The PSM row loop at 1335-1341 walks only psm_custom_opt_columns, so row.opt_ stays empty. PRH/PEH/SMH use `StringUtils::hasPrefix(cells[i], "opt_")` (819, 1119, 1459). Related: load() trims each line in place at :229 (`StringUtils::trim(s)`, StringUtils.h:395 strips trailing tabs), and the PRT/PEP/SML opt_ reads `s.fromCellString(cells[it->second]);` (1014, 1191, 1536) are not bounds-checked.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Upstream line confirmed by reading origin/develop. Core test MzTabFile_test.cpp '[EXTRA] optional PSM columns retain trailing empty cells as null' (b6dd412, in ci.5) appends opt_global_present/opt_global_empty to PSH and asserts that 'kept' is loaded. That assertion cannot pass on the pre-fix `== "opt_"` code, but I found no recorded run of the test failing on pre-fix code (docs/cpp-high-priority-unverified.md argues it would fail). The Rust report entry is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): PSM opt_ columns are now registered by prefix, like the other three sections. All four sections bounds-check opt_ cells, because a row whose trailing empty cells load() trims away would otherwise read past `cells`. The prefix fix alone would have extended that out-of-bounds read to PSM rows. Not upstreamed: the 2026-09-14 p0 patch set explicitly left out the CPP-147 hunks.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5): the PSH branch uses hasPrefix(cells[i], "opt_"), and `if (it->second < cells.size())` guards were added in the PRT, PEP, PSM and SML opt_ loops. Test b6dd412 (ci.5). Kept through the later reader reworks on dax/codex/p0-followups (5cf84fd, b34c9a2, 0ea39d3, 86c8d4e).
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MzTabFile_test.cpp, section '[EXTRA] optional PSM columns retain trailing empty cells as null' (b6dd412)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-151"></a>
### CPP-151: MzTab-M exporter changes metadata keys before lookup

**The mzTab-M exporter rewrites meta-value keys containing spaces to underscores before it looks them up, so those optional columns are written with every value null.**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabM::exportFeatureMapToMzTabM on a FeatureMap whose features, observation matches or identified compounds carry a meta value whose key contains a space, followed by MzTabMFile::store. Reached via TOPP AccurateMassSearch with featureXML input and mzTab-M output (AccurateMassSearchEngine.cpp:889, AccurateMassSearch.cpp:206) or pyOpenMS MzTabM.exportFeatureMapToMzTabM + MzTabMFile.store.
- **Consequence:** Silent data loss when writing mzTab-M: a column opt_global_a_b is created, but 'a_b' is looked up instead of 'a b', so every row holds null. OpenMS's own AccurateMassSearch keys contain no spaces, so it takes user or third-party meta keys to trigger.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTabM.cpp:108, 118 and 137 in getFeatureMapMetaValues_: `std::transform(keys.begin(), keys.end(), keys.begin(), [&](std::string& s) { return StringUtils::substitute(s, ' ', '_'); });`, and the same for obsm_keys and compound_keys. StringUtils::substitute(std::string&, char, char) (StringUtils.h:477) modifies in place. addMetaInfoToOptionalColumns (:78-93) then calls `meta.metaValueExists(key)` with the substituted key, and MetaInfo::exists returns false for an unregistered name (MetaInfo.cpp:129-137). Called at :561, 614, 615 and 651.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Traced on origin/develop: the in-place substitute, then metaValueExists on the rewritten key, then MetaInfoRegistry::getIndex returning -1 for an unknown name. Neither Core (ci.2..p0-followups) nor upstream has a test that exports a meta key with a space. The Rust report entry is source review, not executed.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The collected keys are kept raw, so the lookup uses the stored key. Only the column name is space-substituted, inside addMetaInfoToOptionalColumns.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5), src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTabM.cpp: the three std::transform substitutions were removed and a comment added. Unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-153"></a>
### CPP-153: pepXML drops a uniquely resolved undeclared modification

**When an undeclared mod_aminoacid_mass matches exactly one ModificationsDB entry, the pepXML reader neither adds that modification nor registers an unknown one, so it is silently dropped.**  
- **Trigger:** PepXMLFile::load of a search_hit whose mod_aminoacid_mass is not declared in search_summary (within 0.002 Da on a matching residue), has no id attribute, and whose diff mass matches exactly one ModificationsDB modification (e.g. pepXML from converters that omit refinement or unexpected mods from the header). Via IDFileConverter, CometAdapter, MSFraggerAdapter or pyOpenMS.
- **Consequence:** Silent wrong result: the PeptideHit sequence lacks the modification (wrong peptidoform and mass), with no warning. Two or more matches do keep mods[0]; zero matches register an unknown mod.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/PepXMLFile.cpp:1743-1753: `if (!mods.empty()) { if (mods.size() > 1) { warning(LOAD, ... " Using " + mods[0]->getFullId()); current_modifications_.emplace_back(mods[0], modification_position - 1); } } else { // still nothing found, register as unknown as last resort ... }`. It is reached only after lookupAddFromHeader_ fails for fixed and variable header mods (:1700, :1704) and the optional PSI-MOD id lookup fails (:1708-1719).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Control flow read on origin/develop. Neither Core nor upstream has a test for an undeclared mod with a unique DB match (the Core PepXMLFile_test.cpp additions in 181dadf and e4dcc47 cover terminal fixed mods only). The Rust report entry is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): `current_modifications_.emplace_back(mods[0], modification_position - 1)` moved out of the size>1 block, so one match or several both add mods[0]. The warning still fires only on ambiguity.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5), PepXMLFile.cpp onStartElement; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-154"></a>
### CPP-154: pepXML fixed protein C-terminal modification misses terminal branch

**When pepXML declares a fixed protein-terminal modification, the loader never applies a protein C-terminal one as a terminal modification and applies a protein N-terminal one to every peptide, whatever its position in the protein.**  
- **Trigger:** A pepXML search_summary with a fixed (variable="N") terminal_modification or aminoacid_modification with protein_terminus c/n/Y. Examples are Comet runs with fixed 'Protein N-term'/'Protein C-term' mods (CometAdapter.cpp:592-596 writes add_Nterm_protein/add_Cterm_protein) and MSFraggerAdapter add_nterm_protein/add_cterm_protein (MSFraggerAdapter.cpp:714-718, 817-818). The file is then loaded by the adapters, IDFileConverter or pyOpenMS PepXMLFile.load.
- **Consequence:** Every peptide without an explicit N-terminal modification gets the fixed protein N-terminal modification, which shifts its mass. A fixed protein C-terminal modification without an amino acid is dropped from hits that carry no explicit mod_cterm_mass. With an amino acid it is put on every matching residue of every peptide. Sequences and masses are wrong, and nothing warns.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/PepXMLFile.cpp:2134-2135 `else if (mod.getRegisteredMod()->getTermSpecificity() == ResidueModification::C_TERM || mod.getRegisteredMod()->getTermSpecificity() == ResidueModification::PROTEIN_N_TERM)`, so PROTEIN_C_TERM (set at :213-217) falls to the `else // go through the sequence` loop at :2148-2164, which matches `StringUtils::hasSubstring(mod.getAminoAcid(), r->getOneLetterCode())`. The N-term branch at :2126-2132 sets PROTEIN_N_TERM fixed mods with no protein-boundary check. The warning at :2143-2145 says 'N-terminus'. Flanks are stored raw from peptide_prev_aa/peptide_next_aa at :1319-1326, so pepXML boundaries are '-'. The file is byte-identical to Core core-v4.0.0-ci.2.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core test PepXMLFile_test.cpp '[EXTRA] fixed protein-terminal modifications require terminal evidence' (181dadf) loads three hits carrying fixed protein N-term Acetyl and protein C-term Amidated, with no modification_info. Only the '-'-flanked hits may carry them. By code reading, pre-fix code puts Acetyl on all three hits and Amidated on none, so the checks fail. No failing run on pre-fix code is recorded: e4dcc47 has no failure log, the Rust report says 'not executed', and the recorded Core test runs are post-fix. Fixed protein-terminal mods are valid but uncommon, hence P1 rather than P0.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Before either terminal branch, a fixed PROTEIN_N_TERM/PROTEIN_C_TERM modification is now skipped unless some PeptideEvidence has aa_before '['/'-' (N) or aa_after ']'/'-' (C). The C-term branch tests PROTEIN_C_TERM and the warning says C-terminus. Explicit per-hit modifications are unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (PROTEIN_C_TERM in the C-term branch, protein-boundary gate, warning text) + e4dcc47 (gate also accepts pepXML '-'); in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5 and unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/PepXMLFile_test.cpp, section '[EXTRA] fixed protein-terminal modifications require terminal evidence' (line 507 at ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-155"></a>
### CPP-155: qcML loses units across its own store/load

**The qcML writer stores units under unitRef/unitAcc attributes, but the reader (and the qcML schema) use unitCvRef/unitAccession, so every unit is lost on a store/load round trip.**  
- **Trigger:** Storing a qcML whose quality parameters or attachments carry units. The units come from external qcML files passed through QCShrinker, QCMerger, QCEmbedder, QCExporter or QCExtractor, or from the C++/pyOpenMS API. No OpenMS producer sets units itself.
- **Consequence:** The written file carries units under non-schema attribute names, and reloading it with OpenMS gives empty units. Unit information is lost with no warning.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/QcMLFile.cpp:88-95 (QualityParameter::toXMLString) `s += " unitRef=\"" + unitRef + "\"";` / `s += " unitAcc=\"" + unitAcc + "\"";`. Attachment::toXMLString at :199-206 does the same. The reader at :825-826 (qualityParameter) and :854-855 (attachment) reads `optionalAttributeAsString_(qp_.unitAcc, attributes, "unitAccession"); optionalAttributeAsString_(qp_.unitRef, attributes, "unitCvRef");`. The file is byte-identical to Core core-v4.0.0-ci.2.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — docs/port-resumption-2026-09-13/inherited-core-review.json records a scratch harness run against pre-fix Core HEAD, whose QcMLFile.cpp is byte-identical to upstream develop: 'a stored unit came back empty'. The harness itself is not retained. Core regression checks in b2079fb assert unitCvRef/unitAccession after store/load/store. There is no recorded failing run of those tests on pre-fix code. The input is valid but uncommon, so P1.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The writer emits unitCvRef/unitAccession (QcMLFile.cpp:93-97, 205-209 at ci.5). The reader prefers the schema names and falls back to the legacy unitAcc/unitRef, so files written by older OpenMS keep their units.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (fix), b2079fb (tests), in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/QcMLFile_test.cpp, sections '[QcMLFile::QualityParameter] std::string toXMLString(UInt indentation_level) const' and '[QcMLFile::Attachment] std::string toXMLString(UInt indentation_level) const' (b2079fb)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-156"></a>
### CPP-156: qcML table writer discards its normalized row copy

**The qcML table writer replaces spaces in a copy of each row but then writes the original row, so a cell containing a space becomes two cells when the file is read back.**  
- **Trigger:** Any qcML attachment table with a cell containing a space. Sources: QCEmbedder CSV tables, API or pyOpenMS tables, and QCCalculator's own id-settings row when the database path, db_version or taxonomy contains a space (QcMLFile.cpp:1498-1502), or consensus native IDs such as 'controllerType=0 controllerNumber=1 scan=N' (:1955).
- **Consequence:** The written tableRowValues has more tokens than columns. On reload every later value in that row shifts under the wrong column, and exported tables are misaligned with no warning.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/QcMLFile.cpp:236-242: `std::vector<std::string> copy_row = *it; for (std::string& sit : copy_row) { StringUtils::substitute(sit, std::string(" "), std::string("_")); } s += StringUtils::trimmed(ListUtils::concatenate(*it, " "));`. The unmodified row is written. The reader splits rows on spaces at :882 `StringUtils::split(s, " ", row_);`.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — docs/port-resumption-2026-09-13/inherited-core-review.json records a harness run against pre-fix Core HEAD (identical to upstream develop): row {"hello world","x"} reloads as 3 cells. The harness is not retained. Core test b2079fb checks that exportAttachment contains 'hello_world\tx' after a round trip. There is no recorded failing test run on pre-fix code. Cells with spaces are valid but uncommon in OpenMS-produced tables, so P1.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The writer serialises copy_row (QcMLFile.cpp:248 at ci.5), so spaces in cells become underscores the way column types already did. Empty cells and other whitespace are not addressed.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (fix), b2079fb (test), in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/QcMLFile_test.cpp, section '[QcMLFile::Attachment] std::string toXMLString(UInt indentation_level) const' (b2079fb)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-158"></a>
### CPP-158: qcML map2csv emits misaligned rows when a column is missing

**map2csv takes its column list from the first row only and writes neither a value nor a separator for a missing cell, so later values shift under the wrong header and columns absent from the first row are dropped.**  
- **Trigger:** QcMLFile::map2csv on a table whose rows have different keys, called directly (pyOpenMS map2csv) or through exportIDstats (QCExtractor 'set id' export, QCExtractor.cpp:136), whose 'id' and 'ms2' rows are filled from different CV terms. No active OpenMS tool writes those set parameters, so real input is uncommon.
- **Consequence:** The CSV is silently misaligned. For {id:{A,B}, ms2:{B,C}} the output is 'qp\tA\tB\t\nid\t1\t2\t\nms2\t3\t\n': the ms2 value for B appears under A, and C is lost.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/QcMLFile.cpp:686-689 `for (... it = cvs_table.begin()->second.begin(); it != cvs_table.begin()->second.end(); ++it) { cols.push_back(it->first); }`. At :704-709 `if (found != it->second.end()) { ret += found->second; ret += separator; } //TODO else throw error`. exportIDstats (:733-740) fills row 'id' from QC:0000043-47 and row 'ms2' from QC:0000053-57.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — docs/port-resumption-2026-09-13/inherited-core-review.json records a harness run against pre-fix Core HEAD (identical to upstream develop): {id:{A,B}, ms2:{B,C}} 'gives a misaligned table'. The harness is not retained. Core test b2079fb asserts the union-header output. There is no recorded failing test run on pre-fix code. This is a wrong result on valid but uncommon input, so P1.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The header is the union of all row keys, collected in a std::set. Every column always gets its separator, and a missing cell is left empty (QcMLFile.cpp:697-731 at ci.5). Tables whose rows share keys are unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (fix), b2079fb (test), in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/QcMLFile_test.cpp, section 'std::string map2csv(const std::map<std::string, std::map<std::string, std::string> > &cvs_table, const std::string &separator) const' (b2079fb)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-159"></a>
### CPP-159: qcML writer and reader disagree on set-member CV accession

**A qcML file that OpenMS stores with sets loses all set members when it is loaded again, because the writer marks members as QC:0000005 records and the reader only accepts MS:1000577.**  
- **Trigger:** Register a set with member runs (for example QCMerger -setname, or QcMLFile::registerSet/merge in C++ or pyOpenMS), store the qcML, load it, and call collectSetParameter. Files that use MS:1000577 member records instead get members from every earlier set (names_ never cleared), keyed by file name rather than run ID, and a member's value leaks into the next value-less parameter.
- **Consequence:** After load, setQualityQPs_members_ is empty for every set, so collectSetParameter (bound in pyOpenMS) returns nothing. The QC:0000005 records survive a later store only as ordinary set parameters, so the file text is not lost, but the membership the API exposes is wrong. No active upstream tool reads membership (the collectSetParameter calls in QCMerger.cpp:130,160 are commented out).
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/QcMLFile.cpp:2083-2086 store() writes each set member as `qp.id = *kt; qp.name = "set name"; qp.cvRef = "QC"; qp.cvAcc = "QC:0000005";`, but the reader at :841-843 only does `if (qp_.cvAcc == "MS:1000577") { names_.insert(qp_.value); }`, and :945-948 `if (!(qp_.cvAcc == "MS:1000577" && parent_tag == "setQuality")) { qps_.push_back(qp_); qp_ = QualityParameter(); }` keeps QC:0000005 as a plain set parameter (and skips the qp_ reset after a skipped MS:1000577 member). The setQuality start (:865-872) never clears names_ (QcMLFile.h:201), and :984 `registerSet(run_id_, name_, names_)`. Core ci.2 QcMLFile.cpp is byte-identical to origin/develop.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The inherited Core review ledger (OpenMS4-tests/docs/port-resumption-2026-09-13/inherited-core-review.json, CPP-159) records a harness run against the pre-fix code: members come back empty, and a value-less set parameter after an MS:1000577 member inherits "fileA.mzML". That code is identical to origin/develop. Core regression test b2079fb then checks store/load membership. No recorded run of that test on pre-fix code; the Rust report is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): onStartElement treats QC:0000005 under setQuality as a member (adds qp_.id, the run ID), maps legacy MS:1000577 file names to run IDs through run_Name_ID_map_, clears names_ at each setQuality start. onEndElement drops both member forms from the parameter list, so store() does not write them twice, and always resets qp_.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4, ci.5); test b2079fb
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/QcMLFile_test.cpp, section 'void store(const std::string &filename) const' (b2079fb): sets s1={r1,r2}, s2={r2} survive store/load, collectSetParameter returns 2 and 1 values.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-160"></a>
### CPP-160: qcML TIC slump percentage truncates before multiplication

**The TIC and RIC slump percentages in qcML are computed with integer division before multiplication, so they come out as 0 for any run with more than 100 spectra and are wrong whenever the spectrum count does not divide 100.**  
- **Trigger:** QCCalculator (or QcMLFile::collectQCData from C++/pyOpenMS) on any ordinary run with more than 100 spectra; QC:0000057 is always computed, QC:0000023 when the mzML carries a TIC chromatogram. Example: 200 spectra, 100 below 10k gives 0 instead of 50.
- **Consequence:** Silent wrong QC summary values (percentage of TIC/RIC slumps reported as 0) in qcML for essentially every real run. It is a QC metric, not an identification or quantification result, hence P1 rather than P0. The empty-experiment division by zero is not reachable on its own because collectQCData dereferences exp.begin() earlier.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/QcMLFile.cpp:1297 (QC:0000023 TIC slump) `qp.value =StringUtils::toStr((100 / exp.size()) * below_10k);` and :1371 (QC:0000057 RIC slump) the same expression; both operands are Size, so the integer division runs first. The only caller is FileHandler.cpp:1822 (`qcmlfile.collectQCData(...)`, QCCalculator qcML output).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The inherited Core review ledger (inherited-core-review.json, CPP-160) records a harness run against the pre-fix code, identical to origin/develop: 200 MS1 spectra with 100 TIC points and 100 spectra below 10k gave tic slump=0, ric slump=0. The Core regression test (b2079fb) expects "50" for both; no recorded run of that test on pre-fix code. The Rust report is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Both sites compute `exp.empty() ? Size(0) : 100 * below_10k / exp.size()`: multiply first, guard empty, and keep the integer percentage string so the QCCalculator reference output (4 spectra) is unchanged. The TIC numerator still counts TIC chromatogram points against the spectrum count, as before.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4, ci.5); test b2079fb
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/QcMLFile_test.cpp, section 'regression: slump percentage for more than one hundred spectra' (b2079fb)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-161"></a>
### CPP-161: Percolator loader requires FileName through an unchecked map lookup

**PercolatorInfile::load throws std::out_of_range on the first data row of any PIN that has no FileName column, although the header documents that column as optional.**  
- **Trigger:** PercolatorInfile::load on a PIN without a FileName column that otherwise has the columns load() requires (SpecId, Label, ScanNr, ExpMass, CalcMass, retentiontime, Peptide, Proteins, score column). An example is OpenMS's own store() output: it never writes FileName, and PercolatorAdapter adds retentiontime and score when description_of_correct != 0 and -generic_feature_set is set. SageAdapter is unaffected because Sage writes FileName. pyOpenMS does not bind load.
- **Consequence:** The load aborts with an undeclared std::out_of_range instead of returning identifications or the documented ParseError. There is no silent loss. No shipped tool reaches it.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/PercolatorInfile.cpp:222-223 `std::string raw_file_name("UNKNOWN"); unordered_map<std::string, size_t> map_filename_to_idx;`. The map is filled only inside `if (file_name_column_index >= 0)` at :245-253. :273 then runs unconditionally: `pids.back().setMetaValue(Constants::UserParam::ID_MERGE_INDEX, map_filename_to_idx.at(raw_file_name));`. PercolatorInfile.h:47 documents the FileName column as optional ('If a filename column is encountered ...').
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The path is unambiguous from the code: an empty map, then .at("UNKNOWN"). No Core regression test covers a FileName-less PIN; the only load test reads sage.pin, which has FileName. The Rust report marks the item 'not executed'. The input is valid per the header but uncommon, so P1 (exception on valid input). Note that standard Percolator PINs lacking retentiontime still hit a .at() out_of_range at :274 even after this fix.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): ID_MERGE_INDEX is set only when a FileName column exists (PercolatorInfile.cpp:279-282 at ci.5). FileName-less PINs now load without a merge index, and filenames is left untouched. Other required columns still use .at().
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5 and unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-162"></a>
### CPP-162: Percolator enzyme features use unmapped protein-terminal markers

**For PSMs at a protein terminus, the enzN/enzC Percolator features are computed from OpenMS's '['/']' markers before those become '-', so the features wrongly say the terminus is not an enzymatic cleavage site.**  
- **Trigger:** PercolatorAdapter (in-process training via stampPinFeaturesOnHits at PercolatorAdapter.cpp:983, or a .pin written at :1162), ProSE (ProSE.cpp:615) or pyOpenMS PercolatorInfile.store. It needs PeptideIndexer-annotated hits whose first evidence starts at a protein N-terminus or ends at a protein C-terminus, and any specific enzyme (default trypsin; 'no_enzyme' is unaffected).
- **Consequence:** Protein N-terminal peptides get enzN=0. Protein C-terminal peptides not ending in a cleavage residue get enzC=0. The Peptide column of the same row shows '-'. The SVM sees these valid tryptic PSMs as non-enzymatic and scores them down. Target and decoy PSMs at protein termini are affected alike, so FDR control holds, but sensitivity for this small class of PSMs drops a little.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/PercolatorInfile.cpp:521-522 read aa_before/aa_after from the first PeptideEvidence. :524 `const bool enzN = isEnz_(aa_before, StringUtils::prefix(unmodified_sequence, 1)[0], enz);` and :526 compute enzC from those raw flanks. The normalisation `aa_before = aa_before == '[' ? '-' : aa_before;` and `aa_after = aa_after == ']' ? '-' : aa_after;` comes only after, at :534-535. isEnz_ :625 (trypsin) `return ((n == 'K' || n == 'R') && c != 'P') || n == '-' || c == '-';`. PeptideEvidence.cpp:18-19 sets N_TERMINAL_AA '[' and C_TERMINAL_AA ']', and PeptideIndexing.cpp:127-128 sets them at protein termini.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The ordering in stampPinFeaturesOnHits is plain from the code. No Core test exercises terminal markers: PercolatorInfile_test uses 'K'/'S' flanks, and none was added through dax/codex/p0-followups. The Rust report says 'not executed' and deliberately reproduces the old behaviour. The input is common, but the effect is a biased feature for a few percent of PSMs with FDR still valid, not a plainly wrong final result, so P1 rather than P0.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The '['/']' to '-' normalisation now uses PeptideEvidence::N_TERMINAL_AA/C_TERMINAL_AA and runs right after the flanks are read, before enzN/enzC (PercolatorInfile.cpp:544-545 at ci.5). The later duplicate normalisation was removed, so the Peptide string output is unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5 and unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-163"></a>
### CPP-163: Percolator writer and loader disagree on trailing protein-list width

**PercolatorInfile::store writes a multi-protein PSM as extra tab-separated protein fields, as the PIN format specifies, but load() rejects any row wider than the header and only splits the Proteins field on ';'.**  
- **Trigger:** PercolatorInfile::load on a spec-layout PIN (including OpenMS store() output that carries the columns load() requires) where at least one PSM lists two or more proteins in tab-separated trailing fields. Sage PINs join accessions with ';' in one field (sage.pin), so SageAdapter is unaffected. pyOpenMS does not bind load.
- **Consequence:** load() throws ParseError ('does not have the same number of columns as the pin_header') on the first shared-peptide row, so valid files, including OpenMS's own output, cannot be read back. The error is clean and nothing is lost silently. No shipped tool reaches it.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/PercolatorInfile.cpp:549 (writer) `stamp_meta_value("Proteins", ListUtils::concatenate(proteins, "\t"));`, joined into the row with tabs by preparePin_ at :595 `txt.addLine(ListUtils::concatenate(feats, "\t"));`. Reader :239-242 `if (row.size() != pin_header.size()) { throw Exception::ParseError(...) }`, then :288 `const std::string& sProteins = row[to_idx.at("Proteins")];` and :313 `StringUtils::split(sProteins, ';', accessions);`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The writer/reader width mismatch is direct from :549/:595 against :239. Percolator_subprocess_parity_test notes that Proteins 'embeds tabs and inflates the split count'. No store-to-load round-trip test with a multi-protein PSM exists in Core (through dax/codex/p0-followups), and the Rust report says 'not executed'. Rejecting valid input is treated as P1 (failure on valid input), though tool reach is nil.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): When the header's last column is 'Proteins' (proteins_trailer, PercolatorInfile.cpp:176 at ci.5), rows may be wider than the header. Accessions are collected from every field from Proteins to the end of the row, and each field is still split on ';' for Sage. Shorter rows, and wider rows without a Proteins trailer, still throw ParseError.
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5 and unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-167"></a>
### CPP-167: mzIdentML writer places C-terminal modification at last-residue location

**The mzIdentML writer puts a peptide's C-terminal modification at location N (the last residue) instead of the C-terminus position N+1, and writes a beta-peptide C-terminal cross-link at N+2.**  
- **Trigger:** Storing mzIdentML (MzIdentMLFile::store, IDFileConverter, FileHandler, OpenPepXL cross-link output, pyOpenMS) for a peptide hit with a C-terminal modification (e.g. Amidated) or a C_TERM beta cross-link.
- **Consequence:** The file silently places the modification on the last residue. OpenMS's own loader then attaches it to that residue, or drops it with a warning when the residue does not allow it. Spec-following consumers misplace it too. A C_TERM beta cross-link at N+2 lies outside the peptide. The store raises no error.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzIdentMLHandler.cpp:1366 `p += "\t\t<Modification location=\"" + StringUtils::toStr(hit.getSequence().size()) + "\">\n";`, :1761 `StringUtils::toStr(peptide_sequence.size())` (XL-MS writer), and :1939 `StringUtils::toStr(peptide_sequence.size() + 2)` (beta C_TERM cross-link). MzIdentMLFile.cpp:49 stores with this SAX handler, and :38 loads with MzIdentMLDOMHandler. The DOM loader at MzIdentMLDOMHandler.cpp:2903 reads `index == aas.size() + 1` as C-terminal, and otherwise :2913 calls `aas.setModification(index - 1, cv.getName())` with a warn-and-drop catch (:2917-2919). MzIdentMLDOMHandler.h:131 documents 'peptide_length + 1 = C-terminus'.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading of origin/develop; MzIdentMLHandler.cpp is identical to core-v4.0.0-ci.2. The Rust report says 'confirmed by source review; not executed'. No Core round-trip test with a C-terminal modification exists at ci.5 or p0-followups. The existing MzIdentMLFile_test.cpp:143-148 checks load-time inference for location-less mods only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): writePeptideHit and writeXLMSPeptideHit now write size()+1 for the C-terminal modification. The beta C_TERM cross-link writes size()+1 instead of size()+2. The unused SAX reader check in handleCVParam_ now also treats size()+1 as C-terminal.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-172"></a>
### CPP-172: Streaming mzML consumer references header entries declared only for first record

**The streaming mzML writer builds its header from the first spectrum only, but later spectra still reference source-file and data-processing ids (sf_sp_<s>, dp_sp_<s>, dp_sp_<s>_bi_<m>) that the header never declared, so the file is schema-invalid and those metadata are lost on reload.**  
- **Trigger:** Stream through MSDataWritingConsumer (PlainMSDataWritingConsumer, the low-memory modes of TOPP tools, pyOpenMS) where a spectrum after the first has any of: its own SourceFile; a DataProcessing vector that is not pointer-identical to the first spectrum's, even if equal in value (e.g. spectra built in memory, each with its own DataProcessingPtr); or float/integer/string arrays carrying processing. Spectra read by MzMLFile::transform share processing pointers and usually have no per-spectrum source file, so ordinary mzML-to-mzML streaming does not trigger it.
- **Consequence:** The mzML output has dangling IDREFs, so it is schema-invalid. On reload OpenMS logs a warning and drops the source file, and it silently gives the spectrum an empty processing history. This is silent metadata loss (not peak data) when writing a standard format, on uncommon valid input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/DATAACCESS/MSDataWritingConsumer.cpp:76-83 writes the header once from a one-spectrum map: `MapType dummy; dummy = settings_; dummy.addSpectrum(scpy); ... Internal::MzMLHandler::writeHeader_(ofs_, dummy, dps_, *validator_);`. MzMLHandler.cpp:4962-4966 declares `sf_sp_<i>` only for spectra in that map, :5060-5074 fills dps only from it, and :5185-5197 declares `dp_sp_<s>_bi_<m>` only for its float arrays. writeSpectrum_ then emits `sourceFileRef="sf_sp_" << s` for any non-default source file (:5252-5255). For a history that is not pointer-equal to dps[0] it falls back to `dp_ref_num = s` and emits `dataProcessingRef="dp_sp_" << dp_ref_num` (:5258-5272). Array refs `dp_sp_<s>_bi_<m>` are emitted at :5567, :5597 and :5806. On read, :899-906 warns 'unregistered source file reference' and drops the source file, and :920 `processing_[data_processing_ref]` silently default-inserts an empty history.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Upstream code read at 5082181714, lines above. Core regression tests assert that the dangling references are absent: MSDataWritingConsumer_test.cpp section '[EXTRA] later spectra only reference what the header written from the first spectrum declares' checks no sf_sp_1/dp_sp_1/dp_sp_2 and that the history reads back. No failing run on pre-fix code is recorded. The test file was disabled before 181dadf/b6dd412 (see CPP-188), and the Rust report and the kimi follow-up state no C++ execution.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): After the header is written, consumeSpectrum rewrites each later spectrum to reference only what the header declares. A value-equal history is mapped to the first spectrum's pointers. A different history is replaced by the first spectrum's history. A per-spectrum source file and array processing (float, integer, string) are cleared. Later chromatograms' array processing is cleared too. A one-time warning is logged and the limitation is documented in MSDataWritingConsumer.h. The output is now valid, but it still omits these details, and a different later history is recorded as the first spectrum's.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (spectrum source file, spectrum processing, float-array processing), 63e332c (integer/string arrays and chromatogram arrays), test registration b6dd412; all in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MSDataWritingConsumer_test.cpp, sections '[EXTRA] later spectra only reference what the header written from the first spectrum declares' and 'regression: streamed arrays reference only declared processing records'
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-174"></a>
### CPP-174: mzXML precursor value and window width depend on SAX chunking

**When mzXML precursorMz text reaches the handler in several SAX chunks, the precursor m/z is parsed from the last chunk only, the isolation window is halved once per chunk, the m/z range filter tests a fragment, and split comments keep only their last chunk.**  
- **Trigger:** Loading an mzXML file (MzXMLFile::load, FileHandler, FileConverter, pyOpenMS) whose <precursorMz> or <comment> text is split by an XML comment, CDATA section, processing instruction or character or entity reference, e.g. `<precursorMz windowWideness="10">12<!-- x -->3.<![CDATA[45]]></precursorMz>`.
- **Consequence:** A silently wrong precursor m/z (45 instead of 123.45) and isolation offsets (1.25 instead of 5). A precursor-m/z range filter can drop or keep the wrong scan. Scan and instrument comments are truncated. Common mzXML writers emit precursor text in one chunk, so this input is valid but uncommon.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzXMLHandler.cpp:567-588. Each characters() chunk inside precursorMz runs :570 `double mz_pos = asDouble_(transcoded_chars);`, then :572 setMZ(mz_pos), then :577-578 `setIsolationWindowLowerOffset(0.5 * window_width); setIsolationWindowUpperOffset(0.5 * window_width);`, then :581-587 tests the precursor m/z range on that chunk and pops the spectrum. Comments are overwritten per chunk at :597 `exp_->getInstrument().setMetaValue("#comment", transcoded_chars);` and :605 `spectrum_data_.back().spectrum.setComment(transcoded_chars);`. The windowWideness attribute sets the lower offset at :219-221. SAX2HandlerAdapter.h:55-57 forwards characters() unbuffered. The file is identical to core-v4.0.0-ci.2.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — docs/port-resumption-2026-09-13/inherited-core-review.json (CPP-174) records a scratch run of the pre-fix handler. Its MzXMLHandler.cpp is byte-identical to origin/develop. The split precursorMz gave m/z 45 with offsets 1.25/1.25, the split scan comment gave ' Comment', and the split instrument comment kept only 'Comment'. b2079fb then added a regression test that expects 123.45, 5.0/5.0 and the full comments.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): onCharacters appends precursorMz chunks to the scan's char_rest_ (ci.5 :626-630). The value is parsed once in a new </precursorMz> branch of onEndElement, which calls setMZ, halves the window once and applies the range filter. An already buffered peaks payload is decoded first if a non-conforming file puts <peaks> before <precursorMz>. Comment chunks are accumulated instead of overwritten.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (fix) and b2079fb (test), both in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MzXMLFile_test.cpp, section 'regression : SAX chunk boundaries and mismatched peak counts' (b2079fb)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-177"></a>
### CPP-177: Linear transformation accepts symmetric_regression but never uses it

**Setting symmetric_regression=true on the linear transformation model changes nothing: the model is always an ordinary least-squares fit of y on x, although the parameter description and class docs promise a regression of y-x on y+x.**  
- **Trigger:** Any TransformationModelLinear fit from 3 or more non-collinear points with symmetric_regression=true. Examples: MapAligner* with model:type linear and model:linear:symmetric_regression true, or OpenSwathAnalyzer / OpenSwathChromatogramExtractor with -model:symmetric_regression.
- **Consequence:** The user asks for symmetric regression, usually because both axes are noisy, and silently gets the y-on-x OLS slope, which is attenuated. On the 4-point data of the unit test ((0,1),(1,2),(1,4),(2,2)), the documented y-x on y+x regression gives a slope of about 1.92, but the model returns slope 0.5 and intercept 1.75. On highly correlated RT data the difference is small. Every in-tree caller (OpenSwathWorkflow.cpp:1513, OpenSwathRTNormalizer.cpp:289, CalibrationWorkflow.cpp:671, etc.) forces false, so only users who set this non-default option are affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/ANALYSIS/MAPMATCHING/TransformationModelLinear.cpp:34 `symmetric_ = params_.getValue("symmetric_regression") == "true";` is the only read of symmetric_. The fit at :68-80 always uses `gte::ApprHeightLine2<double>()` on (x,y). :142-143 still documents `"Perform linear regression on 'y - x' vs. 'y + x', instead of on 'y' vs. 'x'."`, and TransformationModelLinear.h:24-25 says symmetric regression is performed depending on the parameter. The option is exposed as a user flag in src/topp/OpenSwathAnalyzer.cpp:98 and OpenSwathChromatogramExtractor.cpp:175, and in the MapAligner model:linear section (src/tests/topp/MapAlignerIdentification_parameters.ini:31, advanced="false").
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Read the upstream code: symmetric_ is stored at cpp:34 and read nowhere else (git grep origin/develop). An existing test runs this path. Upstream and Core TransformationModelLinear_test.cpp:79-103 set `p_in.setValue("symmetric_regression", "true")` and assert slope 0.5 and intercept 1.75. Those are exactly the OLS y-on-x values for that data (computed by hand), so the green test pins the ignored-flag behaviour. There is no recorded run in which a symmetric-regression expectation fails.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): The parameter is still accepted but documented as ignored. The description now reads 'Currently ignored (accepted for compatibility): the model is always fitted by regressing y on x, not y - x on y + x', and the header says the fit is ordinary regression of y on x. The fit itself is unchanged, so existing parameter files keep their results.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (TransformationModelLinear.cpp: parameter description and comment) and 9b56872 (TransformationModelLinear.h class doc and member doc), both in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** none (the existing TransformationModelLinear_test.cpp getParameters section, lines 79-103, only pins the OLS result with the flag set)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-178"></a>
### CPP-178: MSstats missing design pair silently uses sample0

**When a consensus column's (file, label) pair is not in the experimental design, storeLFQ and storeISO look it up with std::map::operator[], which inserts 0, so the intensities are written under sample row 0's Condition and BioReplicate, fraction 0 and (LFQ) run 0 without any error.**  
- **Trigger:** MSstatsConverter or pyOpenMS MSstatsFile.storeLFQ with an LFQ consensusXML whose column headers carry channel_id (e.g. 0, as FeatureFinderMultiplexAlgorithm.cpp:1071 writes) against a label-1 design; or storeISO with a TMT design that omits a channel present in the consensusXML.
- **Consequence:** Silent wrong MSstats input: rows from other files or channels are attributed to the first sample's condition and replicate (for LFQ with channel_id 0 every row collapses into run 0 and is then merged by the summarization), corrupting the downstream statistical comparison.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/MSstatsFile.cpp:439-447 (storeLFQ) `const unsigned sample_idx = path_label_to_sample[tpl1]; const unsigned fraction = path_label_to_fraction[tpl1]; ... const unsigned run = run_map[tpl2]; const unsigned openms_fractiongroup = path_label_to_fractiongroup[tpl1];` on non-const std::maps; storeISO :700-705 `const unsigned sample = path_label_to_sample[tpl1];` likewise. The LFQ label is the raw 0-based `cf_labels.push_back(Int(column.getMetaValue("channel_id")));` (:108), while ConsensusMap.cpp:815-817 adds 1. The guards do not catch it: getNumberOfLabels() returns only the maximum label (ExperimentalDesign.cpp:652-660) and the subset check compares basenames only (:302).
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core test sets channel_id=2 on a single-label design and expects Exception::MissingInformation from storeLFQ; on pre-fix code the operator[] path writes a CSV instead, argued from code, with no recorded failing run. storeISO is fixed but not tested. The Rust report rates the reachability as unconfirmed; the upstream channel_id 0-based convention shows a concrete path.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): storeLFQ and storeISO look the pair up with find() in path_label_to_sample and throw Exception::MissingInformation naming the file and label when it is missing; the fraction, fraction-group and run lookups use at().
- **Commits:** packages/core 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5), src/openms/source/FORMAT/MSstatsFile.cpp; test in b2079fb (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MSstatsFile_test.cpp section storeLFQ (channel_id=2 expects MissingInformation); storeISO not covered
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-180"></a>
### CPP-180: MSstats aggregation collapses equal intensities at distinct times

**With the sum or mean retention-time summarization, MSstatsFile::storeLFQ drops every feature whose intensity exactly equals one already seen for the same peptide ion and run, so the written Intensity is too low (sum) or wrong (mean).**  
- **Trigger:** MSstatsConverter -method LFQ -retention_time_summarization_method sum|mean, or pyOpenMS/C++ MSstatsFile.storeLFQ with "sum"/"mean", on a consensusXML where one peptide ion in one run has features at distinct RTs with bit-identical intensities (e.g. 10, 10, 20; zero intensities are the likeliest real tie). Defaults are unaffected: MSstatsConverter defaults to max (MSstatsConverter.cpp:108-109) and ProteomicsLFQ passes "max" (ProteomicsLFQ.cpp:2802-2804). storeISO forces manual (MSstatsFile.cpp:544-550), so it is unaffected.
- **Consequence:** Silent wrong quantity in the MSstats input: 10+10+20 is written as 30 instead of 40 (sum), and 15 instead of 13.33 (mean); a 0,0,100 set gives mean 50 instead of 33.3.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MSstatsFile.cpp:146-160 `set<MSstatsFile::Coordinate> retention_times{}; set<MSstatsFile::Intensity> intensities{};` ... `retention_times.insert(get<1>(p)); intensities.insert(get<0>(p));`; :187-194 `intensity = meanIntensity_(intensities);` / `intensity = sumIntensity_(intensities);`; include/OpenMS/FORMAT/MSstatsFile.h:137-150 sum and `sumIntensity_(intensities) / intensities.size()` over a `std::set<IntensityType>`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core regression test added in b2079fb (after the 181dadf fix): MSstatsFile_test.cpp storeLFQ section builds three features at RT 10/20/30 with intensities 10, 10, 20 and expects 40 (sum) and 40/3 (mean) in row[9]; the pre-fix std::set would give 30 and 15. No recorded run of that test against pre-fix code. The Core ledger's scratchpad check ran a copy of the loop, not OpenMS itself; the Rust log states no C++ execution.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): constructFile_ now collects intensities in a vector, one entry per distinct retention time, and computes sum and mean with std::accumulate; the same commit also rejects unknown summarization method names and design (file,label) pairs missing from the design instead of writing zeros or row 0.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (released in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); test b2079fb (core-v4.0.0-ci.3+)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MSstatsFile_test.cpp, section storeLFQ (sum and mean with tied intensities 10,10,20)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-181"></a>
### CPP-181: SqliteConnector permits copying an owned database handle

**Copying a SqliteConnector, or an OSWFile that holds one, gives two objects the same SQLite handle, which both destructors close.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ code copy-constructs or copy-assigns a SqliteConnector or OSWFile. No upstream TOPP tool, GUI, test or binding does: neither class is bound in pyOpenMS, and OpenSwathOSWWriter holds its connector through unique_ptr.
- **Consequence:** A double sqlite3_close_v2 on the same connection, which is a use-after-free of the sqlite3 object; copy assignment also leaks the target's original handle. This is memory safety reachable only through API misuse, so P1.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/SqliteConnector.h:62-63 declares `~SqliteConnector();` with no deleted or user-defined copy operations, over the raw member `void* db_ = nullptr;` (:132). SqliteConnector.cpp:25 has the destructor call `sqlite3_close_v2(static_cast<sqlite3*>(db_));`. OSWFile.h:55-56 `OSWFile(const OSWFile& rhs) = default; OSWFile& operator=(const OSWFile& rhs) = default;` over `SqliteConnector conn_;` (:180).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The Rust report is pinned source review with no C++ run or sanitizer. There is no Core test: SqliteConnector_test.cpp has not changed since 70c8251, and no is_copy_constructible check exists. The fix is a compile-time deletion.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): SqliteConnector(const SqliteConnector&) and operator= are deleted. OSWFile's defaulted copy operations became implicitly deleted, and clang's warning about that broke TOPP's macOS x64 Homebrew cask build, so ac41cc1 deletes them explicitly.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 1beb468 (SqliteConnector copy operations deleted; core-v4.0.0-ci.3+) and ac41cc1 (OSWFile copy operations explicitly deleted; core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-190"></a>
### CPP-190: Default handler writing reads an uninitialized SQL batch size

**An MzMLSqliteHandler that writes without a prior setConfig() compares against an uninitialized sql_batch_size_.**  
- **Trigger:** Direct MzMLSqliteHandler use (C++ or pyOpenMS): construct a handler, createTables(), then writeExperiment, writeSpectra or writeChromatograms without calling setConfig. The upstream class test's writeExperiment and writeSpectra sections do exactly this.
- **Consequence:** Reading an indeterminate int is undefined behaviour, and in practice the flush threshold is arbitrary. A small or negative value flushes after every record, which is harmless. A large value never flushes: every blob is kept in memory and bound into one INSERT, which exceeds SQLite's bound-parameter limit (32766 by default) and throws on valid data beyond about 16k records. The file-level APIs (SqMassFile, MSDataSqlConsumer) are not affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteHandler.h:238 `int sql_batch_size_;` has no initializer. The only assignment is setConfig at h:121-127 (`sql_batch_size_ = sql_batch_size;`). The constructor init list in MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:269-277 ends at `write_full_meta_(true)`. The value is read at cpp:1263 and :1474 `if (sql_it > sql_batch_size_)`. pyOpenMS bind_format.cpp:2921-2924 exposes the constructor and writeExperiment/writeSpectra/writeChromatograms without requiring setConfig. SqMassFile.cpp:25/32/40 and MSDataSqlConsumer.cpp:25 do call setConfig. The file is identical to core-v4.0.0-ci.2.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Code reading only. The Rust report says no executed C++ reproduction or sanitizer run. No MSan or valgrind run is recorded. The class-test sections that write without setConfig pass without detecting the read, and no Core test was added for it.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The constructor init list now has `sql_batch_size_(500)`, matching setConfig's default, and the default is documented on @param sql_batch_size. Positive values are still not validated. An oversized setConfig value fails at prepare and is rolled back (see CPP-194).
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-192"></a>
### CPP-192: Blob hydration assigns objects by SQL row encounter order instead of record identity

**sqMass reading attaches data blobs to spectra and chromatograms in SQL row order instead of by record ID, so the pairing depends on each query's plan.**  
- **Trigger:** Reading a valid .sqMass file (SqMassFile::load/transform, SpectrumAccessSqMass in OpenSwathWorkflow or SwathFile, MzMLSqliteHandler read functions, pyOpenMS) whose DATA rows are not stored in the same order as the SPECTRUM or CHROMATOGRAM rows. Examples are files from a non-OpenMS writer or files edited after writing. OpenMS's own writers insert both in ascending ID order.
- **Consequence:** If the native IDs differ, a valid file is refused with a false 'Native id ... does not match' IllegalArgument. If the native IDs coincide (e.g. empty or duplicated), the peaks silently land on the wrong spectrum or chromatogram.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:130-135 `if (!sql_container_map.contains(id_orig)) { Size tmp = sql_container_map.size(); sql_container_map[id_orig] = tmp; }` assigns the k-th distinct SQL id to container k. :145-148 throws 'Native id for spectrum / chromatogram does not match' on a mismatch. The data queries have no ORDER BY: :515-523 `INNER JOIN DATA ON CHROMATOGRAM.ID = DATA.CHROMATOGRAM_ID " ";"`, :541-550, :563-571, :588-597. The metadata queries prepareChroms_ (:631-635) and prepareSpectra_ (:753-757) end in `select_sql += ";";`. The file is identical to core-v4.0.0-ci.2.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Code reading. The Rust report says no executed C++ reproduction. Its retained SQLite EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN observations (SCAN DATA, then SEARCH SPECTRUM via autoindex) support only the row-order mechanism. No dedicated Core regression test exists. The p0-followups section '[EXTRA] reading rejects data arrays of different length and duplicated array roles' stores record 1's DATA at rowids 1001/2001, so its readExperiment check incidentally relies on ORDER BY. It was written for CPP-191, and no failing run without ORDER BY is recorded.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Every metadata and data read query now ends in ORDER BY SPECTRUM.ID or ORDER BY CHROMATOGRAM.ID (ci.5 cpp:553, 580, 601, 627, 665, 793). The ordering requirement is documented above populateContainer_sub_.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** none dedicated. dax/codex/p0-followups MzMLSqliteHandler_test.cpp '[EXTRA] reading rejects data arrays of different length and duplicated array roles' covers it only incidentally.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-193"></a>
### CPP-193: Spectrum/chromatogram IDs and peptide sequence remain unescaped SQL values

**Spectrum and chromatogram native IDs and precursor peptide_sequence values are pasted between apostrophes into SQL text without escaping, so an apostrophe breaks the sqMass write and crafted text can run its own SQL.**  
- **Trigger:** Write a sqMass file (SqMassFile::store, FileConverter to .sqMass, OpenSwathWorkflow -out_chrom .sqMass via MSDataSqlConsumer, pyOpenMS) where a native ID or peptide_sequence contains an apostrophe. Example: a metabolite library whose compound id such as 5'-AMP becomes peptide_sequence (ChromatogramExtractor.h:330 `prec.setMetaValue("peptide_sequence",std::string(comp->id))`) or a transition id used as chromatogram native ID.
- **Consequence:** sqlite3_exec fails and throws IllegalArgument inside the metadata transaction. The SPECTRUM/CHROMATOGRAM/PRECURSOR/PRODUCT rows of the batch are rolled back when the connection closes, while the DATA blobs were already autocommitted (see CPP-194). The tool aborts and leaves a partial sqMass file. A crafted ID such as `x'); DROP TABLE RUN; --` executes arbitrary SQL against the output database.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop 5082181714 src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1169-1170 `run_id_ << ",'" << spec.getNativeID() << "'," <<`; :1204 `"," << activation_method << ",'" << pepseq << "'" << "); "`; :1405 `... << run_id_ << ",'" << chrom.getNativeID() << "'); "`; :1425 same for chromatogram pepseq. The batches run through SqliteConnector.cpp:134 `sqlite3_exec(db, statement.c_str(), ...)`, which executes several statements. Only the RUN insert (:897-900) is parameterized, a fix from upstream #9734.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream source read at the lines quoted. Apostrophes are neither escaped nor bound, and sqlite3_exec accepts multiple statements. The Rust report (CPP-193) records a source review only, with no C++ run. Upstream's MzMLSqliteHandler_test injection section covers only the loaded file path (RUN row). No Core test adds an apostrophe native ID or sequence.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): In writeSpectra_/writeChromatograms_, native IDs and peptide sequences are quoted with StringUtils::quote(s, '\'', QuotingMethod::DOUBLE), which doubles apostrophes and wraps the value as an SQL string literal (ci.5 MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1300, 1331, 1565, 1579). Values are still interpolated rather than bound.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf "[FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation" (codex/cpp-review-completion, merged bb8f5b8; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-196"></a>
### CPP-196: SWATH selection stops at a matching chromatogram precursor NULL ID

**readSpectraForWindow stops reading at the first matching PRECURSOR row that belongs to a chromatogram (NULL SPECTRUM_ID), returning no spectra or only a prefix of the spectra for that SWATH window.**  
- **Trigger:** A sqMass file with a chromatogram whose precursor m/z lies within 0.01 of a SWATH window centre, stored before the matching spectrum precursor rows (the writeExperiment order), read with SwathFile::loadSqMass (SwathFile.cpp:352-356) in OpenSwathWorkflow or directly via MzMLSqliteSwathHandler. TIC/BPC chromatograms have precursor m/z 0, so the case is uncommon.
- **Consequence:** The SWATH map for that window silently gets no spectra or only some of them. OpenSwath then extracts empty or truncated chromatograms and loses or distorts the quantification of every analyte in that window, with no error.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp:92-95 `"SELECT " "SPECTRUM_ID " "FROM PRECURSOR " "WHERE ISOLATION_TARGET BETWEEN "` has no SPECTRUM_ID filter, and :101 `while (sqlite3_column_type( stmt, 0 ) != SQLITE_NULL)` treats a NULL SPECTRUM_ID as the end of the results. MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1418/:1429 writes a PRECURSOR row with CHROMATOGRAM_ID only for every chromatogram, and writeExperiment writes chromatograms before spectra (:883-884). The file is byte-identical to the source the Rust probe compiled.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Rust report CPP-196: an adapted C++ probe on kim (GCC 13.3, SQLite 3.45.1) compiled the pinned MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp and SqliteConnector.cpp, which are identical to origin/develop (diff checked). /Users/kohlbach/Claude/OpenMS/OpenMS4-Exploration/oracle/sqlite-swath-s1-probe/result.log (sha256 792d2506...) records `CASE CPP196_chromatogram_first count=0 ids=`, `CASE CPP196_chromatogram_middle count=1 ids=1,` and `CASE CPP196_no_null_control count=2 ids=1,2,`. This was adapted execution, not a class test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The query now filters `WHERE SPECTRUM_ID IS NOT NULL AND ISOLATION_TARGET BETWEEN ...` (ci.5 MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp:106). All three accessors iterate with Sql::nextRow, which throws on a failed step, instead of the NULL sentinel, and finalize the statement through a StatementGuard. The header notes that chromatogram precursors are skipped.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion, merged bb8f5b8; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** none (MzMLSqliteSwathHandler_test.cpp unchanged from core-v4.0.0-ci.2 through dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-200"></a>
### CPP-200: SqMassFile transform issues an extra empty selected-read batch

**SqMassFile::transform issues one extra read with an empty index list, which aborts the conversion when a file holds exactly 500, 1000, ... spectra or chromatograms, and fails the precondition in Debug builds whenever a record type is absent.**  
- **Trigger:** Release build: a sqMass file with a spectrum or chromatogram count that is a nonzero multiple of 500, streamed through FileConverter sqMass to CHROMPARQUET (FileConverter.cpp:1033), SqMassFile::convertToXICParquet, OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher sqMass to sqMass with -process_lowmemory (OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher.cpp:176), or C++ transform. Debug build: any file with zero spectra or zero chromatograms, such as chromatogram-only OpenSwath output.
- **Consequence:** The final empty batch reads every record, the size check throws Exception::IllegalArgument, and the conversion aborts after the spectra were consumed, before any chromatograms (or after the chromatograms). The input is valid but has an uncommon count. With a zero count in release builds the empty read returns nothing and does no harm.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/SqMassFile.cpp:52 `for (size_t batch_idx = 0; batch_idx <= (sql_mass.getNrSpectra() / batch_size); batch_idx++)` and :74 the same for chromatograms, so the last batch has idx_start == idx_end and empty indices. MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:392/:417 `OPENMS_PRECONDITION(!indices.empty(), ...)` only applies in Debug (src/openms/CMakeLists.txt:26-30, Macros.h:60,91). prepareSpectra_ :753 and prepareChroms_ :631 add the WHERE clause only `if (!indices.empty())`, so an empty list selects every record, and :398 / :423 `if (indices.size() != exp.size())` throws IllegalArgument.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The Rust report and the Core ledger record source review only, with no C++ run. The Core SqMassFile_test [EXTRA_TRANSFORM_META] section transforms MzMLSqliteHandler_1.mzML (spectrumList count 2, chromatogramList count 1), so it never reaches the empty batch. No test covers 0 or multiples of 500.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Counts are read once and reused for setExpectedSize; both loops run `for (Size idx_start = 0; idx_start < count; idx_start += batch_size)` with Size arithmetic, so no empty batch is issued for zero or exact multiples.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4, ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-204"></a>
### CPP-204: MSDataSqlConsumer owning raw pointer leaks during failed construction and can be shallow-copied

**MSDataSqlConsumer owns its handler through a raw pointer, so a copied consumer deletes the handler twice (use-after-free and double free), and a constructor that throws after allocating the handler leaks it.**  
- **Trigger:** Copy: `MSDataSqlConsumer b(a);` or assignment, then destroy both; the second destructor flushes through and deletes the freed handler. Leak: construct with an output path SQLite cannot open, or with buffer_size < 0.
- **Consequence:** Copying causes heap corruption or a crash (undefined behavior). The failed-construction path leaks a small handler object. No in-tree code copies a consumer, and pyOpenMS exposes no copy, so the memory-safety half needs C++ API misuse.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/DATAACCESS/MSDataSqlConsumer.h:94 `OpenMS::Internal::MzMLSqliteHandler * handler_;`, with no user-declared or deleted copy constructor or assignment in the class (h:34-103), so the implicit ones are generated. MSDataSqlConsumer.cpp:18 `handler_(new OpenMS::Internal::MzMLSqliteHandler(filename, run_id) )`, then :22-26 `spectra_.reserve(flush_after_); ... handler_->createTables();`. Both can throw: SqliteConnector.cpp:53 throws SqlOperationFailed when the database cannot be opened, and reserve throws std::length_error for a negative size. The only `delete handler_;` is in the destructor (cpp:42).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Argument from the header and source only. No leak checker, sanitizer run or copy probe is recorded, and none is claimed in the Rust report. The deleted copy in Core is a compile-time property with no runtime test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): handler_ became std::unique_ptr<MzMLSqliteHandler> (MSDataSqlConsumer.h:134, <memory> included), so a fully built member is released when the constructor body throws. The copy constructor and copy assignment are `= delete` (h:62-64), and the destructor no longer deletes by hand.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** none (SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp '[EXTRA] reading spectra written by MSDataSqlConsumer' makes the constructor throw for buffer size -1 but does not check for a leak; the copy prohibition is compile-time only)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-207"></a>
### CPP-207: SpectrumAccessSqMass unchecked view positions permit out-of-bounds access

**SpectrumAccessSqMass reads outside its subset index vector when given a negative position in the nested constructor, or an out-of-range or negative id on a subset view in getSpectrumById or getSpectrumMetaById.**  
- **Trigger:** Build a subset view, e.g. SpectrumAccessSqMass(handler, {1}), then call getSpectrumById(1) or getSpectrumById(-1), or construct SpectrumAccessSqMass(subset, {-1}). Reachable from the C++ API and from pyOpenMS (bind_format.cpp binds the (handler, indices) constructor and getSpectrumById). OpenSwathWorkflow only passes in-range positions: SwathFile.cpp:356-366 builds the views, and positions come from getSpectraByRT/getNrSpectra.
- **Consequence:** Undefined behaviour: an out-of-bounds read of std::vector<int>. The nested constructor silently stores a garbage id. In the getters the garbage id goes into the SQL IN list, where it usually matches no row, so readSpectra throws IllegalArgument (MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:398-402). If it matches a row, an unrelated spectrum comes back silently. A full view (empty sidx_) is not affected: bad ids reach SQL and throw.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/ANALYSIS/OPENSWATH/DATAACCESS/SpectrumAccessSqMass.cpp:43-45 `if (indices[k] >= (int)sp.sidx_.size()) throw ...; sidx_.push_back( sp.sidx_[ indices[k] ] );` has no check for negative positions. :75 `indices.push_back(sidx_[id]);` (getSpectrumById) and :106 (getSpectrumMetaById) index a subset view with no bounds check. The header (SpectrumAccessSqMass.h:106) promises IllegalArgument for out-of-range entries, and the upstream test SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp:216 notes '(out of bounds access!)'.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core added regression tests in 181dadf (SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp at ci.5): nested constructor with indices3=[-1] on a one-element view must throw IllegalArgument (lines 106-108), and getSpectrumById/getSpectrumMetaById with 1 and -1 on a one-element view must throw (lines 143-144, 165-166). These tests did not exist at core-v4.0.0-ci.2. On pre-fix code the nested-constructor case would not throw; the getter cases would probably still pass by chance, because the handler rejects the garbage id. No recorded pre-fix failure run, and no sanitizer run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): A file-local storageIndex(sidx, id) throws IllegalArgument when id < 0 or id >= sidx.size() on a subset view, and getSpectrumById/getSpectrumMetaById now use it. The nested constructor also rejects indices[k] < 0. Header docs now list the throws.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf ([FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation), released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp: sections SpectrumAccessSqMass(const SpectrumAccessSqMass& sp, const std::vector<int>& indices) (lines 106-108), getSpectrumById (lines 142-144), getSpectrumMetaById (lines 165-166)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-208"></a>
### CPP-208: SpectrumAccessSqMass bulk read does not preserve the configured view order or duplicates

**For a subset view whose ids are not in ascending order, SpectrumAccessSqMass::getAllSpectra returns the spectra in SQL id order, so position k no longer matches getSpectrumById(k). A view that repeats an id throws, although the per-position getters and getNrSpectra accept it.**  
- **Trigger:** SpectrumAccessSqMass(handler, {1,0}) or (handler, {1,0,1}), then getAllSpectra. This is also reached through SpectrumAccessOpenMSInMemory(ISpectrumAccess&) (SpectrumAccessOpenMSInMemory.cpp:20-23), which OpenSwathWorkflow uses to load maps into memory. In-tree views from readSpectraForWindow/readMS1Spectra (MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp:65-67, 92-97) are in table order, and in practice ascending and unique, so the default workflow does not trigger it. pyOpenMS does not bind getAllSpectra.
- **Consequence:** A non-ascending view gets silently reordered spectra and metadata in bulk reads, e.g. an in-memory copy whose positions disagree with the on-disk accessor. A view with a repeated id throws IllegalArgument instead of returning the configured view.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): SpectrumAccessSqMass.cpp:137 `handler_.readSpectra(tmp_spectra, sidx_, false);` passes the configured view unchanged. MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:753-757 builds `WHERE SPECTRUM.ID IN (...)` with no ORDER BY, so each distinct id comes back once in scan order. :398-402 `if (indices.size() != exp.size()) { throw Exception::IllegalArgument(... "Illegal spectral indices detected "`. Meanwhile getSpectrumById/getSpectrumMetaById honour the position (`sidx_[id]`, :75/:106), and getNrSpectra returns sidx_.size() (:199).
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 181dadf added a getAllSpectra block for the view [1,0,1] (SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp lines 277-306 at ci.5). It expects sizes 19800/19914/19800 and compares each position with getSpectrumMetaById and getSpectrumById. Pre-fix, readSpectra would get 2 rows for 3 ids and throw. The block did not exist at ci.2, and no pre-fix failure run is recorded. The OpenMS 4 ledger records a read-only sqlite3 probe on SqliteMassFile_1.sqMass: `IN (1,0)` returns ids 0,1 and `IN (1,1)` returns one row. That probe shows the SQL mechanism, not the C++ failure.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): getAllSpectra now sorts and deduplicates sidx_, reads each distinct id once, and rebuilds the configured view with lower_bound (repeats included) whenever the unique ids differ from sidx_. The header now says views may repeat ids in any order and every accessor honours that order.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf, released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
