- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp, section void getAllSpectra(...), block 'a view in reverse order and with a repeated spectrum' (lines 277-306)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-212"></a>
### CPP-212: OpenSwath drift filtering dereferences missing or misaligned mobility arrays

**ISpectrumAccess::filterByDrift dereferences a null drift-time array when the spectrum has no ion-mobility data, and reads past the end of the intensity or drift arrays when they are shorter than the m/z array.**  
- **Trigger:** (a) Any drift-filtered access on spectra without IM arrays: the C++ API ISpectrumAccess::getSpectrumById(id, drift_start, drift_end) or getMultipleSpectra(RT, n, drift_start, drift_end) on a SpectrumAccessSqMass (ISpectrumAccess.cpp:94-100), or SpectrumAddition::addUpSpectra with an im_range (SpectrumAddition.cpp:188). (b) OpenSwathWorkflow with an IM library and MS1 spectra lacking IM arrays, use_ms1_ion_mobility=true (default) and Scoring:spectrum_addition_method=resample (non-default). The path is calculatePrecursorDIAScores (OpenSwathScoring.cpp:326-336), then fetchSpectrumSwath (:736-749), then mergeSpectraForScoring_ (:106-111), then filterByDrift (:97). SONAR multi-map scoring with a non-empty IM range also reaches it (:681/690/771/785). (c) Arrays of unequal length, which need malformed data.
- **Consequence:** A null shared_ptr dereference crashes the process (segfault; formally undefined behaviour) on valid but uncommon data or configuration. Mismatched lengths cause an out-of-bounds heap read. I did not establish whether the mzML/OpenSwath loaders can deliver shorter IM arrays.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openswathalgo/include/OpenMS/OPENSWATHALGO/DATAACCESS/ISpectrumAccess.h:85-92: every guard is commented out (`//OPENMS_PRECONDITION(input->getDriftTimeArray() != nullptr, ...)`, `//throw Exception::NullPointer(...)`). :98 `OpenSwath::BinaryDataArrayPtr im_arr = input->getDriftTimeArray();` can be null (DataStructures.h:267 `return BinaryDataArrayPtr(); // return null`). :102 `auto im_it = im_arr->data.cbegin();` and :108 `im_arr_out->description = im_arr->description;` dereference it unchecked. The loop at :110-121 advances int_it and im_it once per m/z element with no length check. SpectrumAccessSqMass::getSpectrumById (SpectrumAccessSqMass.cpp:83-93) only ever sets the m/z and intensity arrays.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Traced the upstream code at origin/develop: the header guards, the nullable getter, the SqMass spectrum construction and the OpenSwathScoring call chain, with parameter defaults from MRMFeatureFinderScoring.cpp:99,118. No Core test exercises filterByDrift (git grep over src/tests at dax/codex/p0-followups finds nothing), and the Rust report says no unsafe execution was attempted.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): filterByDrift now throws std::invalid_argument for a null input, a missing m/z or intensity array, a missing drift-time array, or arrays of unequal length. getSpectrumById(id, drift_start, drift_end) and getMultipleSpectra(..., drift) document the throw. DataStructures.h getDriftTimeArray skips null array pointers.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (ISpectrumAccess.h, DataStructures.h), released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-214"></a>
### CPP-214: MSDataSqlConsumer full metadata discards supplied experimental settings and addRun suppresses accumulated snapshot

**With full_meta=true the sqMass consumer discards the experimental settings it is given (sample, instrument, source files, contacts), and after addRun() it never writes the accumulated per-record metadata snapshot.**  
- **Trigger:** MSDataSqlConsumer(full_meta=true), the constructor default, fed by MzMLFile::transform with a first pass or by a direct setExperimentalSettings call from C++ or pyOpenMS. The addRun half needs C++ API use of addRun with full_meta=true. OpenSwathWorkflow uses full_meta=false (OpenSwathBase.cpp:357), and OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher's low-memory path skips the first pass.
- **Consequence:** A reloaded sqMass has default experimental settings and no error is reported. After addRun its snapshot holds only the filename, so reading falls back to the SQL columns. This is silent metadata loss in an OpenMS-specific format.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): MSDataSqlConsumer.cpp:107 `void MSDataSqlConsumer::setExperimentalSettings(const ExperimentalSettings& /* exp */) {;}`, although MzMLFile.cpp:230 `consumer->setExperimentalSettings(experimental_settings);` supplies them. addRun at :49-52 does `MSExperiment meta; meta.setLoadedFilePath(filename); handler_->writeRunLevelInformation(meta, full_meta_); wrote_any_run_ = true;`, and the destructor at :36-40 writes peak_meta_ only `if (!wrote_any_run_)`. The handler snapshot copies settings from the passed experiment, `static_cast<ExperimentalSettings &>(meta) = exp;` (MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:910), and it inserts the RUN row each time (:897), so it cannot be called a second time.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — A Core regression test covers the settings half: SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp '[EXTRA] reading spectra written by MSDataSqlConsumer' (lines ~396-420) calls setExperimentalSettings with a sample name and full_meta=true, then checks getSample().getName() after readExperiment. The pre-fix no-op would fail it, but no failing run is recorded. The addRun half is code-reading only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): When full_meta_ is set, setExperimentalSettings copies the settings into peak_meta_ with static_cast<ExperimentalSettings&> (cpp:138-146), so the final snapshot carries them. The addRun snapshot suppression was not changed. It is documented in the addRun doc comment in MSDataSqlConsumer.h: with full_meta the snapshot holds only the filename, and later record metadata is not stored.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp, section '[EXTRA] reading spectra written by MSDataSqlConsumer' (full-meta settings check; the addRun half is untested)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-218"></a>
### CPP-218: Positive-accuracy Numpress encoding destroys one- and two-point coordinate arrays

**Lossy linear Numpress with a positive target accuracy gets a fixed point of 0 for one- or two-value arrays, so the m/z or RT values are stored as 0 and decode as NaN (0/0), with no error because the writers skip the accuracy check.**  
- **Trigger:** Write a 1- or 2-point chromatogram with lossy compression to .sqMass (MzMLSqliteHandler::writeChromatograms always uses accuracy 0.05) or .xic (MSChromatogramParquetConsumer, lossy hard-coded), e.g. via OpenSwathWorkflow -out_chrom (OpenSwathBase.cpp:358-359 `lossy_compression = true`), OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher (lossy default true) or SqMassFile with use_lossy_numpress; or a 1- or 2-peak spectrum with lossy compression and positive linear_fp_mass_acc (MSDataSqlConsumer default 1e-4).
- **Consequence:** Silent data loss: the stored coordinate array reloads as NaN. mzML writing is protected by its default error-tolerance check; sqMass and .xic are not. Short arrays are an edge case in OpenSwath output.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/MSNUMPRESS/MSNumpress.cpp:242-245 `if (dataSize < 3) { return 0; // we just encode the first two points as floats }`, but encodeLinear :326 `ints[1] = static_cast<long long>(data[0] * fixedPoint + 0.5);` and decodeLinear :443 `result[0] = ints[1] / fixedPoint;`. MSNumpressCoder.cpp:133-138 falls back only `if (fixedPoint < 0.0)`. MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1079-1081 `numpressErrorTolerance = -1.0; // skip check, faster` with `linear_fp_mass_acc = linear_abs_mass_acc_;`, and :1320-1322 the same with `linear_fp_mass_acc = 0.05;` for chromatogram RT. MSChromatogramParquetConsumer.cpp:443 `const bool use_lossy_compression = true;` and :461-463 the same config.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Rust report CPP-218: a standalone driver compiling the exact upstream-equivalent MSNumpress.cpp (rev bc9cc12, same code as origin/develop apart from CRLF) with GCC 13.3 ran 42 cases; 12 short positive-accuracy cases decoded to NaN and 4 short all-zero arrays got an infinite factor. The Core ledger (cpp-findings.json) records a separate UBSan driver: 14 of 24 short cases broke before the fix, 24/24 exact after. Core also has a regression test; no recorded failure run of that test on pre-fix code.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): optimalLinearFixedPointMass returns 0 only for dataSize == 0; optimalLinearFixedPoint uses a bound of 1 when a single value or both of two values are 0, avoiding the infinite factor. The fix is in the codec, so sqMass spectra and chromatograms and the Parquet consumer all benefit.
- **Commits:** packages/core 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5), src/openms/source/FORMAT/MSNUMPRESS/MSNumpress.cpp
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp section '[EXTRA] reading spectra written by MSDataSqlConsumer' (one- and two-peak spectra, lossy, accuracy 1e-4, expects m/z 100/101); no short-chromatogram test
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-219"></a>
### CPP-219: Chromatogram precursor reload drops supplemental activation metadata

**Reloading an mzML chromatogram precursor drops the supplemental activation cvParams the writer emitted, and with them the EThcD/ETciD method.**  
- **Trigger:** Store with MzMLFile a chromatogram whose precursor has ETD+EThcD (or ETciD) plus the meta value 'supplemental beam-type collision-induced dissociation' / 'supplemental collision-induced dissociation' / 'supplemental collision energy', then load it. Loading any mzML whose chromatogram precursor carries MS:1002678/1002679/1002680 has the same effect.
- **Consequence:** The supplemental meta values are lost. The combined EThcD/ETciD method is lost as well, because the writer replaced MS:1002631/MS:1003182 with the supplemental term, so the reloaded precursor shows only ETD. In release builds the only trace is a debug-level 'Unhandled cvParam' message. The data loss happens on valid but uncommon input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp. Only the spectrum activation branch has the route, :1990-2005: `else if (accession == "MS:1002679" || accession == "MS:1002678" || accession == "MS:1002680") { spec_.getPrecursors().back().setMetaValue(cv_.getTerm(accession).name, termValue); ... ETciD / EThcD }`. The chromatogram branch (:2033-2154) has no such case and ends at :2150-2153 with `warning(LOAD,std::string("Unhandled cvParam '") + accession ...)`. The writer is shared: :5932 `writePrecursor_(os, chromatogram.getPrecursor(), validator);`. Inside it, :4707-4716 skip MS:1003182/MS:1002631 when `metaValueExists("supplemental ... dissociation")`, and :4736 writeUserParam_ writes those meta values as cvParams. That works because ms-mapping.xml:140-145 allows children of MS:1000044 and MS:1000510 at the activation path, and psi-ms.obo has MS:1002678 is_a MS:1000422 and MS:1002680 is_a MS:1000510. XMLHandler.cpp:105-110 sends warning() only to the debug log in release builds.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core has a round-trip regression test. It checks that the stored buffer contains MS:1002678 and MS:1002680, then checks the reloaded meta values and the EThcD count. No run of that test against the pre-fix reader is recorded: the 56f5f29 commit message and the review docs give no failure log, and the earlier 'fails on the pre-fix reader' was inferred. The writer-side behaviour was confirmed by reading upstream code and the CV mapping.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The chromatogram activation branch gains the same MS:1002679/1002678/1002680 case as the spectrum branch. It sets the meta value from the CV term name and inserts ETciD (MS:1002679) or EThcD (MS:1002678).
- **Commits:** 56f5f29 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MzMLFile_test.cpp section '[EXTRA] chromatogram precursors keep supplemental activation' (ci.5 :1522-1550). It covers MS:1002678 and MS:1002680, not MS:1002679.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-220"></a>
### CPP-220: Negative initial linear-Numpress coordinates can wrap during sqMass writing

**In lossy sqMass (and .xic) writing, a negative first or second m/z or RT value is quantized to a negative integer whose low 32 bits are stored and decoded as unsigned, so the whole array reloads shifted by 2^32/fixedPoint without any error.**  
- **Trigger:** Store a chromatogram with RT [-100,-99,-98] (or any array whose first two values are negative) with lossy compression. For RT, fixed point is 10 (accuracy 0.05): -999 is stored as 4294966297 and the first RT reloads as about 4.29e8, and later values shift by the same amount because the decoder extrapolates from the wrapped starts.
- **Consequence:** Silent wrong RT (or m/z) values in the written file. Negative leading RTs are valid but uncommon (e.g. shifted or transformed RT axes); negative m/z is not physical.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1111-1113 and :1351-1353 `if (use_lossy_compression_) { MSNumpressCoder().encodeNPRaw(data_to_encode, uncompressed_str, npconfig_mz);` with no domain check and :1079/:1320 `numpressErrorTolerance = -1.0; // skip check, faster`. MSNumpress.cpp:326-330 `ints[1] = static_cast<long long>(data[0] * fixedPoint + 0.5); ... result[8+i] = (ints[1] >> (i*8)) & 0xff;` keeps the low 32 bits; decodeLinear :411 `unsigned int init` and :438-443 `ints[1] = ints[1] | ((0xff & (init = data[8+i])) << (i*8));` zero-extends. MSChromatogramParquetConsumer.cpp:456-472 has the same unchecked encoding.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core regression test stores [-100,-99,-98] lossy and requires an exact reload. The pre-fix failure value is from hand arithmetic (verified here: optimalLinearFixedPoint gives 2147483647, so the 0.05 accuracy factor 10 is used, and -999 wraps), not from a recorded failing run; the Rust report explicitly has no executed C++ reproduction for this trigger.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): New linearNumpressCanStore_ (true when data[0] and data[1] are >= 0); writeSpectra and writeChromatograms store an array that fails it with zlib only (compression code 1) and record the choice per array. Residual: the .xic path in MSChromatogramParquetConsumer.cpp (still :443-471 on dax/codex/p0-followups) keeps the unchecked lossy encoding, and the raw codec's domain is unchanged.
- **Commits:** packages/core 56f5f29 '[FIX,TEST] Resolve the Rust port's findings CPP-219 to CPP-229' (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5), src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SqMassFile_test.cpp section '[EXTRA_NEGATIVE_LINEAR] void store(const std::string& filename, MapType& map)'
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-224"></a>
### CPP-224: Gumbel maximum-likelihood fitting reads beyond a short weight vector

**GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter::fitWeighted walks the weight vector alongside the values without a length check, so a shorter weight vector is read past its end on every objective evaluation.**  
- **Trigger:** `GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter().fitWeighted({1.0, 2.0}, {1.0})`, or any weight vector shorter than x. A longer weight vector is silently truncated.
- **Consequence:** A heap out-of-bounds read, which is undefined behaviour, and fitted parameters driven by garbage memory. Only API misuse reaches it: the only in-tree caller, PosteriorErrorProbabilityModel::fitGumbelGauss (PosteriorErrorProbabilityModel.cpp:160), passes incorrect_posteriors resized to x_scores.size() (:521-524 via fillLogDensitiesGumbel :467-470). fitGumbelGauss itself has no production caller (only PosteriorErrorProbabilityModel_test.cpp:274), and neither function is bound in pyOpenMS.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/MATH/STATISTICS/GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter.cpp:58-63 `auto wit = m_weights.cbegin(); for (auto it = m_data.cbegin(); it != m_data.cend(); ++it, ++wit) { double diff = (*it - x(0)) / sigma; fvec(0) += *wit * (-logsigma - diff - exp(-diff)); }`. fitWeighted :74-82 builds `GumbelDistributionFunctor functor (x, w);` and runs LM with no x.size()==w.size() check. The header GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter.h:63-72 states no length requirement.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 56f5f29 section '[EXTRA] fitWeighted rejects a weight vector of another length' calls fitWeighted({1.0, 2.0}, {1.0}) and ({1.0, 2.0}, {1.0, 1.0, 1.0}) and expects IllegalArgument, which the old code never throws. No recorded failing run and no sanitizer run: the Rust report says the out-of-bounds read was not executed, and an ASan probe in this verification could not be compiled locally (Xcode licence).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): fitWeighted throws Exception::IllegalArgument when x.size() != w.size(), and also for non-finite values or non-finite or negative weights, before any iteration. The header documents one finite, non-negative weight per value.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 56f5f29 (first tagged in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups, GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter.cpp:51)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter_test.cpp section '[EXTRA] fitWeighted rejects a weight vector of another length'
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-227"></a>
### CPP-227: Download filename selection does not prevent concurrent overwrite

**Network::downloadFile checks that a file name is free and later opens it with a truncating std::ofstream, so a concurrent download or another process creating the same name in between gets its file truncated and overwritten, breaking the documented no-overwrite promise.**  
- **Trigger:** Two Network::downloadFile calls (threads or processes) into the same folder whose URLs have the same basename, both finishing the fs::exists probe in saveFileName_ before either opens the file; or any other creator of that path in the gap between the probe (after the transfer completed) and the ofstream open.
- **Consequence:** The second writer truncates and replaces the first file; both calls log success for the same path, so one download is silently lost. Reach is narrow: public C++ API only, no in-tree library/TOPP caller (only Network_test), no pyOpenMS binding, and the race window is short.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/SYSTEM/Network.cpp:40 `if (!fs::exists(dest / basename)) return basename;`, :44 `while (fs::exists(dest / (basename + "." + std::to_string(i))))`, then :60-61 `std::string filename = folder + "/" + saveFileName_(url, folder); std::ofstream ofs(filename, std::ios::binary);` (truncating open, no exclusive create), against src/openms/include/OpenMS/SYSTEM/Network.h:36-38 '... until an unused name is found — existing files are never overwritten.'
- **Evidence:** code-reading — TOCTOU sequence is evident from source; Network.cpp at core-v4.0.0-ci.2 is identical to develop. The Rust report says the interleaving is source-derived, not executed. Core's Network_test.cpp (also on dax/codex/p0-followups) has only a single file:// download section; no concurrent test exists.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Network.cpp replaces saveFileName_ with createUnusedFile_, which reserves each candidate name via std::fopen(filename, "wbx") (exclusive create, fails with EEXIST for any existing entry including dangling links), retries the next suffix only on EEXIST, and writes through the reserved FILE* with fwrite/fclose error checks; Network.h documents that the guarantee holds for concurrent downloads.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 56f5f29 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; no later change on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-228"></a>
### CPP-228: Squaring Gumbel negative log likelihood can change the optimum

**The maximum-likelihood Gumbel fitter hands the negative log-likelihood to a least-squares solver, which minimises its square. When the NLL at the true optimum is negative (fitted scale below about exp(-(1+gamma)) = 0.21 in data units), the solver settles on the NLL=0 contour and returns a wrong location and scale.**  
- **Trigger:** `GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter(FitResult(1.0, 2.0)).fitWeighted({0.10, 0.12, 0.15, 0.11, 0.13}, {1,1,1,1,1})`: NLL is +9.03 at the start and -13.39 at the MLE a=0.11379, b=0.01413. The same happens for any narrow-scale weighted sample, e.g. scores in PosteriorErrorProbabilityModel::fitGumbelGauss (:160) whose incorrect-score scale is below about 0.21.
- **Consequence:** The fit silently returns a wrong location and scale with a success status. Levenberg-Marquardt cannot raise NLL^2 from 81.5 to 179, so it stops on the zero contour far from the MLE. Reach is C++ only: fitWeighted and fitGumbelGauss, which has no production caller (only PosteriorErrorProbabilityModel_test.cpp:274; the header @todo at PosteriorErrorProbabilityModel.h:39 says it is not exposed). No TOPP tool or pyOpenMS binding reaches it.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/MATH/STATISTICS/GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter.cpp:62-66 `fvec(0) += *wit * (-logsigma - diff - exp(-diff)); } double foo = -fvec(0); fvec(0) = foo; fvec(1) = 0.0;` (Functor(2,2), residuals [NLL, 0]), minimised at :80-82 by `Eigen::LevenbergMarquardt<Eigen::NumericalDiff<GumbelDistributionFunctor>,double> lm(numDiff); ... lm.minimize(x_init);`, which minimises NLL^2. The upstream test GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter_test.cpp:70-91 only uses b=0.8, where the NLL stays positive.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 56f5f29 section '[EXTRA] fitWeighted finds the maximum-likelihood estimate of a narrow sample' expects a=0.11378962, b=0.01413100 from start (1,2). No failing run of the old code is recorded. Supporting numbers: scratchpad b200/gumbel_check.py confirms NLL -13.39 and zero gradient at that estimate. In this verification, MINPACK LM (scipy least_squares method='lm') on the same [NLL, 0] residual from (1,2) ended at a=-0.1159, b=0.2773 with NLL about 0. That is an emulation, not Eigen's C++ LM: the C++ probe could not be compiled locally (Xcode licence).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The Eigen LM objective is replaced by the Gumbel likelihood equations. The scale is the root of g(b) = b - mean + tilted_mean(b), found by bisection after bracketing; the location is a = -b*log(sum w e^{-x/b} / sum w), computed with log-sum-exp so small b neither overflows nor underflows. It throws UnableToFit when fewer than two distinct values carry weight, and returns the initial parameters when the total weight is zero (including empty input).
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 56f5f29 (first tagged in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter_test.cpp section '[EXTRA] fitWeighted finds the maximum-likelihood estimate of a narrow sample'
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-01"></a>
### X-01: .oms consensus ratios stored under the last handle's id and loaded into a local copy, so every ratio was lost

**The .oms writer stores consensus-feature ratios under the id of the feature's last handle, and the loader appends the feature before filling ratios into its local copy, so every ratio is lost on a round trip.**  
- **Trigger:** Storing a ConsensusMap whose ConsensusFeatures carry Ratio entries to .oms (OMSFile::store / FileHandler) and loading it back. No upstream library code or TOPP tool produces ratios (addRatio/setRatios have no callers besides the pyOpenMS bindings at bind_kernel.cpp:3826-3827), so only C++ or pyOpenMS users who set ratios hit it.
- **Consequence:** Silent data loss: the loaded ConsensusMap has no ratios. Either half alone loses them: the loader discards what it reads, and for a feature with handles the stored rows sit under a handle id the loader never queries.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): Store, src/openms/source/FORMAT/OMSFileStore.cpp:1546-1571: `storeBaseFeature_(feat, feature_id, -1); int parent_id = feature_id;` (1549-1550), then `storeBaseFeature_(BaseFeature(handle), ++feature_id, parent_id);` per handle (1553), then `query_ratio->bind(":feature_id", feature_id);` (1561), i.e. the last handle's id. Load, src/openms/source/FORMAT/OMSFileLoad.cpp:1035-1055: `ConsensusFeature feature(...); consensus.push_back(feature);` (1036) comes before the ratio loop, which queries `WHERE feature_id = :id` with the consensus id (1039) and fills the local `feature.getRatios()[ratio_index]` (1048).
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Both halves traced on origin/develop. Core commit 181dadf adds to OMSFile_test.cpp two ratios on consensus[1] in the store section and round-trip checks (getRatios().size()==2, values and refs) in the load section. I found no recorded run of this test failing on pre-fix code, only the explanatory test comment.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Ratios are stored under the consensus feature's own id, and the feature is appended only after its ratios are filled. The handle rows still attach to consensus.back().
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5): OMSFileStore.cpp binds :feature_id to parent_id; OMSFileLoad.cpp moves consensus.push_back(feature) after the ratio loop. Unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/OMSFile_test.cpp, sections 'void store(const std::string& filename, const ConsensusMap& consensus)' (adds ratios) and 'void load(const std::string& filename, ConsensusMap& consensus)' (checks them) (181dadf)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-02"></a>
### X-02: SimpleTSGXLMS divides charge-two suffix neutral losses by the charge twice (sibling of CPP-042)

**For linear x/y/z ions at charge 2 or higher, SimpleTSGXLMS passes the m/z instead of the charged mass to the loss helper, which divides by the charge again. Water and ammonia loss peaks land at (M/z - L)/z instead of (M - L)/z.**  
- **Trigger:** Any OpenPepXL search with default parameters: neutral losses are on and the main-score linear spectra are generated up to charge 2. Every charge-2 y ion containing D/E/S/T (water loss) or K/N/Q/R (ammonia loss) is affected; x and z ions too when enabled. Also reachable from pyOpenMS through OpenPepXLAlgorithm.run. Worked example: y1 of S at z=2 has its intact peak at 53.5286 and a water-loss peak at 17.7590 instead of 44.5233.
- **Consequence:** Silently wrong theoretical spectra in the OpenPepXL main score (match-odds) and in the linear-peak pre-filter, which drops candidates with fewer than 2 matched linear peaks (OpenPepXLAlgorithm.cpp:496-504). Identification scores, candidate survival and fragment annotations are distorted on ordinary cross-link data, with no error.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/SimpleTSGXLMS.cpp:226 `double pos(mono_weight / charge);` then :230 `addLosses_(spectrum, pos, charge, backward_losses[i]);`. The helper at :460 does `spectrum.emplace_back((mono_weight - loss_H2O_) / charge, charge);` (:465 does the same for NH3). The prefix branch passes the charged mass (:197 `addLosses_(spectrum, mono_weight, charge, forward_losses[i]);`). Reach: OpenPepXLAlgorithm.cpp:102 `ions:neutral_losses` defaults to "true" and :365 passes it to specGen_mainscore. :471 and :475 call `specGen_mainscore.getLinearIonSpectrum(..., 2, ...)`, which loops z = 2..1 (SimpleTSGXLMS.cpp:121). The SimpleTSGXLMS.cpp blob is byte-identical at origin/develop and core-v4.0.0-ci.2 (cae8ac7).
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core commit 9b56872 added SimpleTSGXLMS_test.cpp section '[EXTRA] charge-two suffix losses use neutral masses'. For PEPTIDES it checks that each charge-2 peak has a charge-1 counterpart at 2*mz - proton. By algebra the buggy code fails it for loss peaks, but no recorded run of that test on pre-fix code was found. The only observed effect: against ci.3 the installed TOPP_OpenPepXL_1 regression outputs changed (3 output diffs, per 23944b6). That run combined this fix with CPP-042 and CPP-043, so it does not isolate X-02.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (reverted): The ci.3 fix passed mono_weight instead of pos to addLosses_ in the suffix branch, matching the prefix branch and all other loss calls. It was reverted because it changes OpenPepXL reference outputs, which need a reviewed update. The finding is open in Core.
- **Commits:** 9b56872 (SimpleTSGXLMS.cpp:230 plus test, shipped in core-v4.0.0-ci.3). Reverted by 23944b6 (core-v4.0.0-ci.4) together with CPP-042 and CPP-043 to restore ci.2 OpenPepXL output. Not in ci.5 or dax/codex/p0-followups: SimpleTSGXLMS.cpp still has `addLosses_(spectrum, pos, ...)`. Re-land alone decided 2026-09-14 (docs/project-state.md backlog, docs/cpp-p0-walkthrough.md CPP-042).
- **Test:** none now. SimpleTSGXLMS_test.cpp '[EXTRA] charge-two suffix losses use neutral masses' was added in 9b56872 and removed in 23944b6.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute; severity re-checked by a skeptic: P0 to P1

<a id="x-03"></a>
### X-03: SqMassFile::transform rebuilds chromatograms and spectra from the SQL tables alone, so low-memory sqMass-to-sqMass conversion loses settings such as the SRM chromatogram type

**SqMassFile::transform streams spectra and chromatograms rebuilt from the SQL tables only, so settings that load() restores from the full-meta record, such as the SRM chromatogram type, spectrum type and data processing, are lost.**  
- **Trigger:** Stream a sqMass file written with full meta through SqMassFile::transform: OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher sqMass to sqMass with -process_lowmemory (upstream OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher.cpp:173-176 feeds a PlainMSDataWritingConsumer), or any C++ consumer. pyOpenMS does not bind transform, and the CHROMPARQUET consumer does not use these fields.
- **Consequence:** Silent metadata loss in the streamed output: SRM chromatograms come out as mass chromatograms (the ChromatogramSettings default), and spectra lose their centroid/profile type, data processing and other settings, while an in-memory load of the same file keeps them. It happens only on the uncommon low-memory path.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/SqMassFile.cpp:46-47 reads the full-meta settings `sql_mass.readExperiment(experimental_settings, true); consumer->setExperimentalSettings(experimental_settings);` but :63-66 and :85-88 pass `tmp_spectra[k]` / `tmp_chroms[k]` from readSpectra/readChromatograms straight to the consumer. Those records come from SQL alone: MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:612-629 prepareChroms_ selects only native ID, precursor and product, and the schema at :990 `CREATE TABLE CHROMATOGRAM(ID, RUN_ID, NATIVE_ID)` and :967 SPECTRUM (MSLEVEL, RETENTION_TIME, SCAN_POLARITY, NATIVE_ID) has no type or data processing. load() instead restores full settings from RUN_EXTRA (:313-318 `f.loadBuffer(uncompressed, exp)`); write_full_meta defaults to true (SqMassFile.h:44).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Core commit 23944b6 records that the new OpenSwath sqMass cacher test found the lost SRM chromatogram type (also in docs/cpp-issues-review.md:59). The spectrum-settings half was found by review of the ci.4 candidate (4f5c86f). Both halves are covered by SqMassFile_test. Upstream code is identical to Core ci.2 at these lines.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): transform copies ChromatogramSettings/SpectrumSettings from the full-meta record for the same index when the native IDs match. RT, MS level and drift time still come from SQL.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 23944b6 (chromatograms) and 4f5c86f (spectra), both in core-v4.0.0-ci.4 and ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SqMassFile_test.cpp, section [EXTRA_TRANSFORM_META] void transform(...) (added 23944b6, extended 4f5c86f): SRM chromatogram type and CENTROID spectrum type survive transform into MSDataStoringConsumer and a round trip through MSDataSqlConsumer and load().
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-04"></a>
### X-04: OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher low-memory sqMass conversions shift the MSDataSqlConsumer constructor arguments and write mzML under a .sqMass name

**In -process_lowmemory mode OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher shifts the sqMass writer's arguments (batch size becomes run id, full_meta becomes buffer size, lossy flag becomes full_meta, mass accuracy becomes the lossy flag), and its sqMass-to-sqMass path writes indexed mzML into a file named .sqMass.**  
- **Trigger:** OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher -in x.mzML -out y.sqMass -process_lowmemory (advanced flag): run_id=500 (lowmem_batchsize), flush_after=1 or 0, full_meta:=lossy_compression, lossy_compression:=(mass_acc != 0) which is true for the default -1.0, linear accuracy falls back to 1e-4. OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher -in x.sqMass -out y.sqMass -process_lowmemory writes mzML.
- **Consequence:** mzML->sqMass: numpress lossy compression at 1e-4 Th is applied even with -lossy_compression false (unless -lossy_mass_accuracy 0), -lossy_mass_accuracy is ignored, -full_meta follows the lossy option, the RUN id is the batch size (500) instead of 0 as the in-memory path stores, and every spectrum is flushed individually. This is silent precision/metadata loss in the sqMass output. sqMass->sqMass: output is mzML under a .sqMass name, which fails loudly when later read as SQLite.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/topp/OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher.cpp:163 `MSDataSqlConsumer consumer(out, batchSize, full_meta, lossy_compression, mass_acc);` against MSDataSqlConsumer.h:55 `MSDataSqlConsumer(const std::string& sql_filename, UInt64 run_id, int buffer_size = 500, bool full_meta = true, bool lossy_compression=false, double linear_mass_acc=1e-4);`. Lines 171-176 (sqMass->sqMass, low memory) `PlainMSDataWritingConsumer consumer(out); consumer.getOptions().setWriteIndex(true); SqMassFile f; f.transform(in, &consumer, true, true);`. Upstream TOPP tests exercise -process_lowmemory only for mzML->cached mzML (tests/topp/CMakeLists.txt:2030).
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Code reading of upstream OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher.cpp:102-176, MSDataSqlConsumer.h:55 / .cpp:16-27,85, MzMLSqliteHandler.h:121-127, SqMassFile.cpp:31. OpenMS4 test-data 0bfba7a adds TOPP_OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher_test_6 (low-memory mzML->sqMass->mzML) and test_7 (low-memory sqMass->sqMass->mzML), both compared with test 4's in-memory reference. No recorded run of these tests against the pre-fix cacher was found; the tests were run after the fix, where test_7 exposed a separate SqMassFile::transform chromatogram-type loss (cpp-issues-review.md:59). P1: valid input, but reachable only through an advanced flag.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Both low-memory branches now construct `MSDataSqlConsumer consumer(out, 0, batchSize, full_meta, lossy_compression, mass_acc);`, run id 0 as the in-memory conversion stores; the sqMass->sqMass branch uses the SQL consumer instead of PlainMSDataWritingConsumer.
- **Commits:** openswath package: 8313cad (branch fix/sqmass-cacher-arguments) = 9bc2205 (codex/package-split), '[FIX] Pass the run id to the low-memory sqMass writers'; test-data package: 0bfba7a (codex/package-split, fix/sqmass-cacher-arguments). Not in any release tag (openswath-v1.0.0-ci.2 does not contain it). Not a Core commit.
- **Test:** packages/test-data/topp/CMakeLists.txt TOPP_OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher_test_6_{step1,step2,out1} and TOPP_OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher_test_7_{step1,step2,out1} (0bfba7a)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-05"></a>
### X-05: IDFilter in-silico digestion passes end - start as the peptide length although evidence ends are inclusive, so every length is one short

**IDFilter's in-silico digestion filter passes end - start as the peptide length although evidence ends are inclusive, so it checks the peptide minus its last residue and gets C-terminal specificity and missed cleavages wrong.**  
- **Trigger:** TOPP IDFilter -in_silico_digestion:fasta on correctly indexed identifications, e.g. PeptideIndexer output. Under the default full specificity, every fully specific peptide whose second-to-last residue is not itself a cleavage site is affected: nearly every tryptic peptide, including protein C-terminal ones.
- **Consequence:** Silent wrong result. Correct evidences of fully specific peptides fail the C-terminal check and are stripped from their hits, leaving them without protein references. Under semi specificity, peptides that are specific only at the C terminus are wrongly removed, a fragment ending just after a cleavage site is wrongly accepted, and a missed cleavage at the true penultimate site is not counted. Single-residue evidences (length 0) are always rejected. Traced example with inclusive coordinates: IALSRPNVEVVALNDPFITNDYAAYMFK at 0..27 in the IDFilter_12 BSA2 protein is rejected with length 27 and accepted with length 28. The C-terminal peptide EWATYTCVLGFHVYVPVR at 28..45 behaves the same way.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/PROCESSING/ID/IDFilter.h:459 (the code is in the header's DigestionFilter, not IDFilter.cpp) `return digestion_.isValidProduct(AASequence::fromString(accession_resolver_.getValue(evidence).sequence), evidence.getStart(), evidence.getEnd() - evidence.getStart(), ignore_missed_cleavages_, methionine_cleavage_);`. Ends are inclusive: PeptideEvidence.h:88 'position of the last AA', and PeptideIndexing.cpp:668 `PeptideEvidence(accession, pe.position, pe.position + (int)it_hit->getSequence().size() - 1, ...)`. isValidProduct_ treats the length as exclusive: EnzymaticDigestion.cpp:243 `const int end = pos + length; // past-the-end index`, and C-terminal specificity at :279-286 tests `end == seq_size` or `end` in cleavage_positions. Reached from TOPP IDFilter.cpp:513-518 (-in_silico_digestion:fasta, default specificity full).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Established from the quoted code and the inclusive-end contract. A standalone replica of tokenize_ and the SPEC_FULL branch of isValidProduct_ with the Trypsin regex (scratchpad/x05probe/probe.cpp, built and run under ASan) printed 0 for (0, 27-0) and 1 for (0, 28), and 0/1 likewise for the C-terminal peptide. OpenMS itself was not run. The TOPP fixtures IDFilter_12..16 use 1-based-looking coordinates (start="1" with aa_before="-"), which hide the error. Their references strip all evidences under Trypsin full (12, 13) and keep some under Trypsin/P or semi (14-16). No class test covers DigestionFilter: IDFilter_test only exercises PeptideDigestionFilter. Rated P1 rather than P0 because the in-silico digestion option is an uncommon IDFilter mode.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Not fixed. The length is still end - start in Core. After Core's CPP-045 fix, 0..0 evidences now reach this call with length 0 and are rejected.
- **Commits:** none; listed as open in OpenMS4-tests docs/cpp-issues-review.md:62 ('five TOPP references were produced with the current length and need analysis before it changes'). IDFilter.h:459 is unchanged at core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-06"></a>
### X-06: A full-metadata sqMass file whose native ids lack '=' cannot be read back: the mzML snapshot renames them to spectrum=<index> while the SQL tables keep the original

**A default full-metadata sqMass file cannot be loaded back if any spectrum's native ID lacks '=': the mzML snapshot renames all spectra to spectrum=<index>, the SQL table keeps the original IDs, and the load throws on the mismatch.**  
- **Trigger:** SqMassFile::store (or MzMLSqliteHandler::writeExperiment with full metadata) of an MSExperiment in which at least one spectrum has a native ID without '=', then SqMassFile::load. Examples: a spectrum built through the API or pyOpenMS with an empty native ID, or IDs like 'S1'. FileConverter to sqMass hits it with such input.
- **Consequence:** load() throws IllegalArgument ('... does not match: <original> != spectrum=0') on a file OpenMS wrote from valid data, and the X-10 leak follows. The data is still in the file: readSpectra, SpectrumAccessSqMass, or a load with write_full_meta=false still work. The standard load fails on valid but uncommon input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop. MzMLHandler.cpp:4787-4801: if any spectrum's native ID lacks '=' (`if (!StringUtils::has(exp[s_idx].getNativeID(), '=')) { renew_native_ids = true;`), then at :5242-5245 `native_id = std::string("spectrum=") + s;` renames every spectrum. MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:913-928 (writeRunLevelInformation) stores the full-metadata snapshot through `MzMLFile().storeBuffer(output, meta)`, so the snapshot gets the renamed IDs, while writeSpectra :1167-1169 inserts `spec.getNativeID()` unchanged into SPECTRUM.NATIVE_ID. readExperiment :285-321 loads the snapshot (`f.loadBuffer(uncompressed, exp)`), and populateContainer_sub_ :145-149 then throws `IllegalArgument("Native id for spectrum / chromatogram does not match: " ...)`. SqMassFile.h:44 defaults `write_full_meta{true}`, and SqMassFile.cpp:25-26 passes it to readExperiment.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Traced through upstream source only. No probe or failing test is recorded. Core tests always give sqMass spectra '='-containing IDs (for example MzMLSqliteHandler_test.cpp:682 `setNativeID("spectrum=" + ...)`), so they never exercise it. docs/cpp-issues-review.md lists it as open.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Not fixed. Listed under 'Findings outside the log' in docs/cpp-issues-review.md as open.
- **Commits:** none (the code is unchanged in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups: MzMLHandler.cpp:4846 renew_native_ids, MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:184 mismatch throw)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-09"></a>
### X-09: StringUtils::skipNonWhitespace(string_view) returns int, so removeWhitespaces can write before the buffer for strings over 2 GiB

**removeWhitespaces stores a 64-bit prefix length in an int, so a leading non-whitespace run of 2^31 to 2^32-1 bytes gives a negative start and the loop reads, and can write, before the string buffer.**  
- **Trigger:** Any string whose first whitespace (or end) is at an offset in [2^31, 2^32), passed to StringUtils::removeWhitespaces. From files, the realistic case is an mzML binaryDataArray whose base64 text is 2 to 4 GiB on one line (about 1.6 to 3.2 GiB of encoded data, e.g. more than 200 M uncompressed 64-bit values), loaded with default options. An OBO line or mzXML peaks payload of that size also works. Runs of 4 GiB or more wrap to a non-negative start and behave correctly.
- **Consequence:** Under C++23 (cxx_std_23), start = L - 2^32, which is negative. `it` and `dest` then point up to 2 GiB before the heap buffer. The loop reads that memory, most likely a SIGSEGV. If the memory is mapped and holds a space, tab, CR or LF byte, has_spaces becomes true and `*dest = *it` writes before the buffer, which corrupts memory. The string is also shifted. This is a memory-safety defect reachable from an input file, but only one with a single text node larger than 2 GiB.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/DATASTRUCTURES/StringUtils.h:251-255 `inline int skipNonWhitespace(const std::string_view& data) { auto pos = skipNonWhitespace(data.data(), data.data() + data.size()); return static_cast<int>(pos - data.data()); }` and :526-528 `int start = skipNonWhitespace(std::string_view(s.data(), s.size())); auto it = s.cbegin() + start; auto dest = s.begin() + start;`. Reached by default mzML loading at MzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:147 `StringUtils::removeWhitespaces(bindata.base64);` (skip_xml_checks_ defaults to false, PeakFileOptions.h:240), and also by ControlledVocabulary.cpp:174 (every OBO line), MzXMLHandler.cpp:1160 and MzDataHandler.cpp:480.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Argument from the header code and its callers only. No probe, test or recorded run exists: Core's StringUtils_test removeWhitespaces section (line 523) uses only small strings. The defect was noted while reviewing the P0 follow-ups and has not been executed.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Not fixed. The fix is to return size_t or ptrdiff_t from skipNonWhitespace/skipWhitespace(string_view) and to keep start as size_t in removeWhitespaces.
- **Commits:** No Core commit. StringUtils.h:251-255 and :526 are unchanged at core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups. Recorded as a backlog item in OpenMS4-tests docs/project-state.md:177-179 (commit 7e2fe579dd): Core's Base64 no longer calls it, but the mzML handler's default whitespace stripping still does.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute; severity re-checked by a skeptic: P0 to P1

<a id="x-12"></a>
### X-12: Activation-method name tables are indexed without bounds checks (Precursor, FileInfo, mzXML writer, RangeUtils, IsobaricChannelExtractor); an out-of-range enum set through the API reads out of bounds

**The activation-method name tables are indexed straight from the enum value, so a precursor holding SIZE_OF_ACTIVATIONMETHOD or another out-of-range value makes the name lookups read outside the static std::string arrays.**  
- **Trigger:** API use: in C++, insert static_cast<Precursor::ActivationMethod>(n) with n outside 0..18. In pyOpenMS, `p.setActivationMethods({Precursor.ActivationMethod.SIZE_OF_ACTIVATIONMETHOD}); p.getActivationMethodsAsString()`. Writing such a spectrum to mzXML, running FileInfo statistics on it, RangeUtils activation filtering, IsobaricChannelExtractor or Ms2SpectrumStats has the same effect. The only input-file route (negative sqMass ACTIVATION_METHOD codes) is the separate CPP-199.
- **Consequence:** Out-of-bounds read of a static std::string, one past the end for the sentinel and further for larger values: a crash, garbage, or a wrong method name in text output and mzXML.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/METADATA/Precursor.cpp:124 `am.push_back(NamesOfActivationMethod[static_cast<size_t>(m)]);` and :135 (short names), with no range check. The arrays have SIZE_OF_ACTIVATIONMETHOD (19) entries (Precursor.h:84-85). activationMethodToString/ToShortString (:164, :173) reject only m == SIZE_OF_ACTIVATIONMETHOD. Other unchecked indexing: FileInfo.cpp:1631-1632, :1657, :1840; HANDLERS/MzXMLHandler.cpp:1021 `NamesOfActivationMethodShort[static_cast<size_t>(*(precursor.getActivationMethods().begin()))]`; KERNEL/RangeUtils.h:331; ANALYSIS/QUANTITATION/IsobaricChannelExtractor.h:249; QC/Ms2SpectrumStats.cpp:66. pyOpenMS exposes the sentinel as a settable value: bind_kernel.cpp:2506 `.value("SIZE_OF_ACTIVATIONMETHOD", ...)`, together with setActivationMethods (:2452) and getActivationMethodsAsString (:2444).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Unchecked indexing is visible at every listed site. A search for int-to-ActivationMethod conversions at origin/develop finds only MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:712/:856 (CPP-199), the MzXMLHandler reader (:233, always in range from std::find) and PrecursorVisualizer loops bounded by SIZE_OF_ACTIVATIONMETHOD. Once CPP-199 is set aside, the bad value can only arrive through the API, which the rubric rates P1. Not executed.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Not fixed. Core validates only the sqMass reader (0 <= value < SIZE_OF_ACTIVATIONMETHOD, CPP-199). Precursor's name accessors and the other consumers still index without a check.
- **Commits:** none; recorded as a backlog item in OpenMS4-tests docs/project-state.md:185-187 ('Pre-existing defects found while reviewing the P0 follow-ups', not fixed). Precursor.cpp:118-138 is unchanged at core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups. The file route is closed separately by CPP-199's reader check in 181dadf.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-13"></a>
### X-13: FileConverter passes exp.size() (the spectrum count) as the peak limit n of MapConversion::convert

**FileConverter converting spectra (mzML, mzXML, MGF, ...) to consensusXML keeps only as many peaks as the file has spectra, silently dropping all other peaks.**  
- **Trigger:** FileConverter -in run.mzML -out run.consensusXML (or -out_type consensusparquet) on any peak file whose MS1 peak count exceeds its spectrum count, i.e. practically every real run. get2DData (MSExperiment.h:175) takes only MS1 peaks, so e.g. 30,000 spectra with 10^7 MS1 peaks yield 30,000 consensus features. No upstream TOPP test covers spectra->consensusXML.
- **Consequence:** Silent truncation of the output to the N most intense MS1 peaks (N = number of spectra), with no warning, while the featureXML path keeps everything. On MS2-only input (e.g. MGF) the same call site additionally reaches the separate out-of-bounds read CPP-111 upstream (n = spectrum count > empty MS1 point vector); that part is a different finding, fixed in Core 181dadf.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/topp/FileConverter.cpp:894 `MapConversion::convert(0, exp, cm, exp.size());` (peak data to consensusXML/consensusparquet). exp.size() is the spectrum count, but n is the peak limit: ConversionHelper.h:58-59 `@param[in] n Maximum number of peaks to copy`, and ConversionHelper.cpp:24-27,35-44 clamps n to getSize() then keeps the n most intense points via partial_sort. MSExperiment.cpp:744 getSize() `returns the total number of peaks`. The sibling featureXML branch (FileConverter.cpp:846-866) keeps every peak and warns; the consensusXML branch neither keeps all nor warns.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read upstream FileConverter.cpp:846-894, ConversionHelper.h:39-64 and ConversionHelper.cpp:13-49, MSExperiment.cpp:744-750, MSExperiment.h:171-188. Line introduced by 60a2c97dd1 ('move conversion of ConsensusMap to own class'). No executed probe or Core test; OpenMS4 docs (project-state.md backlog, cpp-p0-walkthrough.md CPP-111 concerns) flag it only as 'looks unintended'. Classified P1 rather than P0 because converting raw peaks to consensusXML is an uncommon, inherently lossy path. Process note: one `git log -S` on the dax partial clone lazily fetched missing blobs (git auto-packed); no refs, worktree or branches were changed.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Left open; recorded in OpenMS4-tests/docs/project-state.md as a pre-existing defect found while reviewing the P0 follow-ups.
- **Commits:** none. topp package (codex/package-split and origin/codex/core-compatibility) src/FileConverter.cpp:894 still reads `MapConversion::convert(0, exp, cm, exp.size());`. Core only fixed the adjacent clamp (CPP-111, 181dadf, core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5), which makes MS2-only input yield an empty map instead of an OOB read.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

## P2 (39)

<a id="cpp-002"></a>
### CPP-002: Fractional seconds overflow signed int during normalization

**When a non-digit such as a space or sign follows the dot, sscanf's %d still reads a large integer but zero fraction digits are counted, so `millisecond *= 10` overflows a signed int (undefined behaviour).**  
- **Trigger:** DateTime::set("2000-01-01T00:00:00. 2147483647") (also with a "+00:00" suffix), or DateTime::fromString with the same text and format "yyyy-MM-ddThh:mm:ss.zzz". Reachable from files: the protXML program_details time attribute goes straight to set() (ProtXMLFile.cpp:88-89), and so do .oms date_time strings (OMSFileLoad.cpp:360). Also reachable from pyOpenMS DateTime.set. The mzML/idXML/featureXML readers do not reach these branches.
- **Consequence:** Signed-overflow UB on malformed timestamps. In practice the millisecond wraps to garbage, and it aborts under a trapping sanitizer. The same code also misreads valid timestamps with more than 3 fraction digits: ".123456" gives 456 ms instead of 123, and ".0001" gives 1 ms. That error is millisecond-level with no scientific effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop 5082181714 src/openms/source/DATASTRUCTURES/DateTime.cpp:222 `sscanf(stripped.c_str(), "%d-%d-%dT%d:%d:%d.%d", ..., &millisecond)` then :229-231 `for (size_t i = dot_pos + 1; i < stripped.size() && std::isdigit(...); ++i) ++frac_digits; for (int i = frac_digits; i < 3; ++i) millisecond *= 10; millisecond %= 1000;`. The same code is at :247/:254-256 (set, no timezone) and :578/:585-587 (fromString "yyyy-MM-ddThh:mm:ss.zzz").
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The Rust port ran an unmodified-source UBSan probe (OpenMS4-R/tests/data/datetime_cpp_issues.json, source 82ce5b3, whose lines 230/255/586 are identical to upstream). All three inputs aborted with return code -6 and "runtime error: signed integer overflow: 2147483647 * 10 cannot be represented in type 'int'" at DateTime.cpp:230, :255 and :586. No Core test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): A new static parseMillis_(date) reads at most three fraction digits from the text after the first '.' and scales to milliseconds without overflow. All three branches now use it instead of the sscanf value. The overflow and the >3-digit misread are both gone. The UB in sscanf %d itself, for out-of-range digits, is not addressed.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..ci.5; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Test:** none (DateTime_test.cpp unchanged ci.2..ci.5 and on p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-003"></a>
### CPP-003: Configuration repair is computed and discarded

**When OpenMS.ini has an old or missing version tag, getSystemParameters logs that it fills in missing defaults, builds the repaired Param, and then returns the unrepaired one.**  
- **Trigger:** A readable OpenMS.ini in the config dir whose 'version' differs from the running version (or is absent) and which lacks one of the default keys (home_dir, temp_dir, id_db_dir, threads); then any call of SystemSettings::getSystemParameters, e.g. via findDatabase from SearchEngineBase::getDBFilename (SearchEngineBase.cpp:95-97) or PeptideIndexer.cpp:140-151 when the given database path is not directly readable, or from pyOpenMS.
- **Consequence:** Missing keys stay missing despite the warning. getTempDirectory/getUserDirectory guard with p.exists() (SystemSettings.cpp:37,57) so they are unaffected; findDatabase throws ElementNotFound('id_db_dir') before File::find runs, so the user gets an uncaught element-not-found error (search-engine adapters) or a bare usage printout with ILLEGAL_PARAMETERS (PeptideIndexer catch(...)) instead of the intended 'Input database not found' message. API callers reading e.g. 'threads' also get ElementNotFound. No computed result changes.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop (5082181714) src/openms/source/SYSTEM/SystemSettings.cpp:154-161 (develop's home of what Core calls FileConfig.cpp File::getSystemParameters): `OPENMS_LOG_WARN << "Updating missing/wrong entries in '" << filename << "' with defaults!\n"; Param p_new = getSystemParameterDefaults_(); p.setValue("version", VersionInfo::getVersion()); p_new.update(p); // no new version is stored } } return p;`. The merged p_new is never used. Concrete consumer: :77-81 `Param sys_p = getSystemParameters(); ... File::find(db_name, ListUtils::toStringList<std::string>(sys_p.getValue("id_db_dir")))`, where Param::getValue -> getEntry_ throws Exception::ElementNotFound on a missing key (Param.cpp:1692-1697). The pyOpenMS binding src/pyOpenMS/bindings/bind_misc.cpp:998 even documents the result as 'completed with defaults where entries are missing'.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Discarded p_new is plain in source and identical in Core at core-v4.0.0-ci.2. The Rust report states no C++ runtime reproduction (its Rust test mirrors the source behaviour). Core's File_test.cpp 'static Param getSystemParameters()' section (still unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups, lines 526-530) only checks non-empty and current version on the default path, so it does not exercise the stale-ini path and would pass pre-fix.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): src/openms/source/SYSTEM/FileConfig.cpp: after `p_new.update(p);` the line `p = p_new; // the repaired parameters are the result; the file itself is not rewritten` replaces the comment, so the repaired tree is returned; the ini file is still not rewritten.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; no later change on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Test:** none (File_test.cpp getSystemParameters section does not cover a stale or version-less OpenMS.ini)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-020"></a>
### CPP-020: Formula interning can separate mass validation from calculation

**tryGetMonoWeight validates a second, more fully resolved copy of the peptidoform but calculates the mass from the first copy, so it can return a mass that omits an unresolved modification without reporting any issue.**  
- **Trigger:** Only a hand-built Peptidoform AST: a residue with a NamedMod whose name equals the FullId of a Formula entry that a later FormulaTag in the same call interns, e.g. M with NamedMod "M[Formula:Cl101]" followed by M with FormulaTag "Cl101", on the first call in a process. It is not reachable from ProForma text, because parseNamedMod_ stops at ':' (1263-1283). fromAASequence cannot produce it either: it writes getId() (2425-2428), which is empty for interned formula entries. getMonoWeight and the PeptidoformIon overloads validate before they resolve the calculated copy, so they are not affected.
- **Consequence:** The first tryGetMonoWeight returns MM plus one formula delta with zero issues. The second call on the same AST returns MM plus two deltas, so the result depends on process-global database state.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp:2641-2650, tryGetMonoWeight(pf): `Peptidoform pf_copy = pf; resolveModifications(pf_copy); issues_out = getMassCalculationIssues(pf_copy); ... return calculateChainMass_(pf_copy, counted_crosslinks);`. getMassCalculationIssues copies and resolves again (2493-2494: `Peptidoform pf_copy = pf; resolveModifications(pf_copy);`). resolveFormulaTag_ interns entries into ModificationsDB as a side effect (1664 `full_id = site + "[Formula:" + ef.toString() + "]"`, 1701 `mod_db->addModification(...)`), and NamedMod resolution looks names up by FullId (1792 `searchModificationsFast(arg.name, ...)`).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed in this verification: an API probe against the pre-fix Core library (ProForma.cpp identical to develop) built the AST above and printed `first tryGetMonoWeight issues=0`, `first = 3811.945656` (MM 280.0915351 + Cl101 3531.854121), `second tryGetMonoWeight issues=0`, `second = 7343.799776`. The Rust report claims no C++ run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The validation body moved into collectMassCalculationIssues_(pf_resolved) (ProForma.cpp:2601 at ci.5). getMonoWeight, tryGetMonoWeight (2805: 'validate the copy that is calculated') and the ion overloads (resolveChainsForMass_) now validate and calculate the same resolved copy. No regression test was added.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-031"></a>
### CPP-031: An empty ambiguous region shifts the conversion attachment index

**An empty ambiguous region `(?)` still advances toAASequence's attachment cursor, so a following modification is attached one residue too far to the right or throws IndexOverflow.**  
- **Trigger:** Malformed ProForma with an empty `(?)` region followed by a modified residue, converted with BEST_EFFORT or DROP_UNLOCALISED: pyOpenMS toAASequence, or peptidoform columns read by QPXFile.cpp:2019 (warns about the AMBIGUOUS_REGION issue, then converts) and FeatureMapArrowIO.cpp:1664 (silent). FAIL_ON_LOSS throws ConversionError earlier.
- **Consequence:** `(?)M[UNIMOD:35]` throws IndexOverflow instead of a clean conversion error (the file readers catch it and fall back to the plain sequence). `(?)M[UNIMOD:35]A` returns silently with Met oxidation attached to residue 2 (A), printed as `MM(Oxidation)`, mono 236.083, so the sequence string no longer matches its mass.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp: residue emission 2290-2293 `if (!region->elements.empty()) unmod_seq += region->elements[0].amino_acid;`, but the attachment cursor at 2335 does `else if (std::holds_alternative<AmbiguousRegion>(section)) seq_pos++;` unconditionally. parseAmbiguousRegion_ (1119-1145) has no empty check, unlike parseModifiedRange_ (1176 'Empty range is not allowed'). AASequence.cpp:1444-1446 throws `Exception::IndexOverflow` when index >= size. ResidueDB::getModifiedResidue checks the modification's origin only with OPENMS_PRECONDITION (ResidueDB.cpp:456).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed in this verification: an API probe against the pre-fix Core library (ProForma.cpp and AASequence.cpp identical to develop) printed `(?)M[UNIMOD:35] threw IndexOverflow: the given index was too large: 1 (size = 1)`, `(?)M[UNIMOD:35]A -> MM(Oxidation) mono=236.0830792` and `FAIL_ON_LOSS threw ConversionError`. In the run of Core's post-fix test binary against the same library, the regression section stopped at line 2690 on a different finding and never reached this finding's assertion at 2693.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): toAASequence advances seq_pos only for a non-empty region (ProForma.cpp:2436-2437 at ci.5), matching the emission rule, and findCrossLink counts positions the same way. 86f01c4 makes collectMassCalculationIssues_ report 'Ambiguous region has no candidate residues' (2623). The parser still accepts `(?)`.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (fix), 86f01c4 (empty region also reported as a mass issue), test b2079fb; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/ProFormaParser_test.cpp, section 'regression: conversion preserves annotations and residue positions', lines 2693-2694 at core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (covers the IndexOverflow case only, not the misattachment with a following residue); PEP(?)TIDE mass issue at 2684
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-033"></a>
### CPP-033: Failed CV mapping loads contaminate later loads

**A CVMappingFile::load that throws partway through a document leaves references, rules and a partial rule inside the reader, and the next load on the same object publishes them into its destination.**  
- **Trigger:** Reuse one CVMappingFile (C++, or pyOpenMS CVMappingFile.load at bind_format.cpp:2882). First load `<CvMapping><CvReference cvName="old" cvIdentifier="OLD"/><CvMappingRule/></CvMapping>`; the missing id throws. Catch the error, then load `<CvMapping/>`: the result contains the stale OLD reference. Partial CvTerms in actual_rule_ also leak into the next rule. No in-tree caller reuses a reader.
- **Consequence:** Silently wrong CV mappings, with stale references or rules or extra terms, after a failed load on a reused reader. SemanticValidator could then enforce rules from the earlier file.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/CVMappingFile.cpp:36-42 `parse_(filename, this); cv_mappings.setCVReferences(cv_references_); cv_mappings.setMappingRules(rules_); cv_references_.clear(); rules_.clear();`. Nothing is cleared before parse_, and actual_rule_ is reset only at a completed </CvMappingRule> (:205-206). XMLFile.cpp:52-55/:120: XMLCleaner_ calls `p_->reset()`, but XMLHandler::reset() is empty (XMLHandler.cpp:37-39) and non-virtual (XMLHandler.h:78 `void reset();`). A missing required attribute throws from attributeAsString_ (XMLHandler.h:388).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Code reading of the load order and the empty non-virtual reset(). The Rust report claims no execution. Core's CVMappingFile_test has no reuse-after-failure section.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): load() now clears cv_references_ and rules_ and resets actual_rule_ = CVMappingRule() before parse_, with a comment explaining that the inherited reset() is empty. Stale state can still sit in the object after a throw, but it no longer reaches the next load's destination.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; CVMappingFile.cpp:36-44 at ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-035"></a>
### CPP-035: Invalid CV mapping enums silently select different rules

**An unrecognised requirementLevel silently becomes MUST, and an unrecognised cvTermsCombinationLogic silently becomes OR, instead of raising a load error.**  
- **Trigger:** A mapping file rule with e.g. requirementLevel="MAYY" or cvTermsCombinationLogic="ANDD" (or empty values), loaded by CVMappingFile::load. Reach: TOPP SemanticValidator and CVInspector with user mapping files, C++ and pyOpenMS. The shipped share/OpenMS/MAPPING files use only valid literals.
- **Consequence:** The validator enforces constraints the file never stated. A misspelled MAY becomes MUST, which gives false validation errors. A misspelled AND or XOR becomes OR, which misses violations. No error is reported.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/CVMappingFile.cpp:103 `CVMappingRule::RequirementLevel level = CVMappingRule::MUST;`, with the unknown-value branch at :121-124 `else { // throw Exception }`. :131 `CVMappingRule::CombinationsLogic logic = CVMappingRule::OR;`, with :149-152 `else { // throw Exception; }`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Code reading of the defaults and the empty else branches. No C++ execution is claimed and no Core test covers it.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Both empty branches now call fatalError(LOAD, ...) naming the invalid requirementLevel or cvTermsCombinationLogic value and the rule id.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; CVMappingFile.cpp:147 and :177 at ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-039"></a>
### CPP-039: SemanticValidator compares descendant units with the measured term

**When a cvParam's unit is a descendant of an allowed unit, SemanticValidator searches the descendants for the measured term instead of the unit, so every legal child unit is reported as not allowed.**  
- **Trigger:** Semantic validation with unit checking on (MzMLValidator.cpp:23, TraMLValidator.cpp:19, MzIdentMLValidator.cpp:20) of a cvParam whose term's has_units names a parent unit, e.g. MS:1000224 'molecular mass' (psi-ms.obo: `has_units UO:0000002 ! mass unit`) with unitAccession UO:0000221 dalton (unit.obo: `is_a: UO:0000002`); also MS:1000207/1000208/1000215/1000222/1000229. Reached via FileInfo -v, TOPP SemanticValidator, MzMLFile/TraMLFile/MzIdentMLFile::isSemanticallyValid.
- **Consequence:** A valid file gets a false 'Unit CV term not allowed' error and is reported as semantically invalid; only validation reports are affected, not loaded data. Most shipped terms list concrete units directly, so the trigger is uncommon.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/VALIDATORS/SemanticValidator.cpp:326-349 (origin/develop 5082181714): inside `if (!term.units.contains(parsed_term.unit_accession))` the descendant walk uses `auto lambda = [&parsed_term, &found_unit] (const std::string& child) { if (child == parsed_term.accession) { found_unit = true; return true; } ...` (:332-334) over `cv_.iterateAllChildren(*it, lambda)` (:343), then `errors_.emplace_back("Unit CV term not allowed: " ...)` (:348). By contrast MzDataValidator.cpp:86-89 correctly compares `child == parsed_term.unit_accession`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Rust report: direct review, no C++ execution. Reachability checked in the shipped CVs (psi-ms.obo MS:1000224 has_units UO:0000002; unit.obo UO:0000221 is_a UO:0000002; ControlledVocabulary builds children from parents). SemanticValidator_test.cpp in Core has no unit-check validation case.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The lambda now compares `child == parsed_term.unit_accession` (Core SemanticValidator.cpp:348), with a comment; the unused `set<std::string> child_terms` was removed.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-040"></a>
### CPP-040: Failed semantic validation contaminates later document paths

**If validate() throws partway through a document, the stale open-tag stack and fulfilled-rule counters survive, so every later validate() on the same object checks paths under a wrong prefix and silently skips the mapping rules.**  
- **Trigger:** One SemanticValidator (or MzMLValidator/TraMLValidator/MzIdentMLValidator) object validates a malformed file that fails after the root element opens (truncated or ill-formed XML, a cvParam without a required name/accession attribute), the caller catches the ParseError, then validates further files with the same object. Reachable from C++ and from pyOpenMS SemanticValidator.validate in a try/except loop; TOPP SemanticValidator and MzMLFile::isSemanticallyValid (MzMLFile.cpp:89) build a fresh validator per file.
- **Consequence:** For later documents every element path looks like /r/cvParam/r/..., no rule matches, MUST rules are never checked and no 'CV term used in invalid element' errors fire; only 'No mapping rule found' warnings are emitted and validate() returns true for invalid documents. The stale prefix persists for all subsequent calls on that object.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/VALIDATORS/SemanticValidator.cpp:96-98 `//initialize errors_.clear(); warnings_.clear();` (open_tags_ and fulfilled_ are not cleared); :115 `open_tags_.push_back(tag);` runs before :121 `getCVTerm_(attributes, parsed_term);`, whose attributeAsString_ throws via XMLHandler.h:388 `fatalError(LOAD, ... "Required attribute '" ...)`; the only cleanup is :220 `fulfilled_.erase(path);` and :222 `open_tags_.pop_back();` in onEndElement. XMLFile.cpp:120 `XMLCleaner_ clean(handler);` calls XMLHandler::reset(), which is non-virtual (XMLHandler.h:78) and empty (XMLHandler.cpp:37-39 `void XMLHandler::reset() { }`). Reachable from pyOpenMS: bind_format.cpp:2891 `.def("validate", [](OpenMS::Internal::SemanticValidator& self, const std::string& filename) {`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream control flow read at origin/develop 5082181714: push before the throwing attribute read, cleanup only in onEndElement, empty non-virtual reset(). The Rust report's two-document scenario (MUST rule at /r/cvParam/@accession) is explicitly not executed ('No executed C++ reproduction is claimed'). No Core regression test: SemanticValidator_test.cpp is unchanged in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..dax/codex/p0-followups. Correction to the earlier classification: upstream pyOpenMS does bind SemanticValidator.validate and locateTerm, not only SemanticValidator_CVTerm.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): SemanticValidator::validate() now also runs open_tags_.clear() and fulfilled_.clear() before parse_, with a comment that the inherited reset() is empty. State is still left dirty after a throw, but it is reset at the start of the next validate(), which covers every derived validator.
- **Commits:** 181dadf [FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation (branch codex/cpp-review-completion; first released in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-045"></a>
### CPP-045: PeptideEvidence misclassifies valid and invalid position limits

**hasValidLimits rejects the legal single-residue N-terminal evidence 0..0 but accepts reversed limits (e.g. 10..2) and negative ones other than -1. In IDFilter's digestion filter, reversed limits become a negative length and a reversed iterator range handed to Boost regex.**  
- **Trigger:** Legal edge case: evidence start=0, end=0. Malformed case: an idXML/mzIdentML evidence with start > end+4 (e.g. start=10 end=2) whose accession is in the FASTA, run through TOPP IDFilter -in_silico_digestion:fasta with a specific enzyme and full or semi specificity (the default), or pyOpenMS PeptideEvidence.hasValidLimits.
- **Consequence:** A 0..0 evidence skips the filter with an 'Invalid limits' warning. A reversed evidence reaches isValidProduct_ with length end-start < -4. tokenize_ then builds boost::sregex_token_iterator(begin+pos-2, begin+end+2), a reversed range, and Boost's find_restart_any scans past the end of the protein string: a heap out-of-bounds read, i.e. undefined behaviour or a crash, from a malformed input file. Other negative coordinates reach no out-of-bounds read on the paths DigestionFilter uses. The earlier P3 classification did not consider the reversed-range path.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/METADATA/PeptideEvidence.cpp:79-85 `return !( getStart() == UNKNOWN_POSITION || getEnd() == UNKNOWN_POSITION || getEnd() == N_TERMINAL_POSITION);` (UNKNOWN_POSITION=-1, N_TERMINAL_POSITION=0 at :15-16). PeptideEvidence.h:88 documents end as 'the position of the last AA', which is inclusive. The only library caller is IDFilter.h:451 `if (!evidence.hasValidLimits())`, which then passes `evidence.getStart(), evidence.getEnd() - evidence.getStart()` to isValidProduct at :459. EnzymaticDigestion.cpp:226-235 does not reject a negative length, and :262 calls `tokenize_(sequence, pos - 2, end + 2)`. tokenize_ (:96-98) clamps start to at least 0 and end to at most size, but never checks end >= start. Setters (PeptideEvidence.cpp:97-110) and the idXML reader (IdXMLFile.cpp:727-759) store start/end unchecked.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The misclassification follows directly from the quoted predicate. The downstream read was observed only in a standalone replica: tokenize_ and the SPEC_FULL branch of isValidProduct_ copied verbatim, with the Trypsin regex `(?<=[KRX])(?!P)` from share/OpenMS/CHEMISTRY/Enzymes.xml, built with Homebrew Boost and -fsanitize=address. Called with pos=10, length=-8 on a 46-residue protein, it reported heap-buffer-overflow READ in boost perl_matcher::find_restart_any (perl_matcher_common.hpp:822) via tokenize_. Probe: /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-kohlbach-Claude-OpenMS-OpenMS4-Exploration/86429cfc-9d6f-4e69-9719-2f10e50d2880/scratchpad/x05probe/probe.cpp. The full chain (idXML -> IDFilter -> OpenMS library) was not executed, and no Core test covers hasValidLimits: grep of src/tests and src/pyOpenMS/tests at ci.5 and p0-followups finds none. The P0 rating rests on this replica plus code reading. If the downstream read is discounted, the rest is a P3 misclassification.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): hasValidLimits now returns `getStart() >= 0 && getEnd() >= 0 && getStart() <= getEnd()` (PeptideEvidence.cpp:86), with a comment on the inclusive contract. DigestionFilter therefore skips reversed or negative limits instead of passing them on. EnzymaticDigestion::isValidProduct_ still does not guard negative lengths in Core (unchanged at :226-235), so the public isValidProduct API (pyOpenMS-bound) can still be misused. A 0..0 evidence now reaches isValidProduct with length 0 because of X-05, and is removed with a warning.
- **Commits:** 181dadf ([FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation), in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute; severity re-checked by a skeptic: P0 to P2

<a id="cpp-052"></a>
### CPP-052: mzML schema selection depends on the first four physical lines

**MzMLFile::isValid picks the indexed or plain mzML schema by searching the first four physical lines for "<indexedmzML", so a valid indexed file whose root starts later is checked against the wrong schema and reported invalid (and a plain file with that text in an early comment gets the indexed schema).**  
- **Trigger:** XMLValidator -in x.mzML (XMLValidator.cpp:135), FileInfo validation (src/openms/source/FORMAT/FileInfo.cpp:701), or C++ MzMLFile::isValid on an indexed mzML with comments, processing instructions or blank lines pushing <indexedmzML past line 4. Typical converter output (declaration then root on line 2) is unaffected.
- **Consequence:** Misleading validation verdict: a schema-valid indexed mzML is reported invalid ('No matching global declaration available for the validation root'); a single-line file is read whole into memory just for the sniff. No effect on loading or data.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzMLFile.cpp:61-67 `bool indexed = false; TextFile file(filename, true, 4); std::string s = StringUtils::concatenate(file); if (StringUtils::hasSubstring(s, "<indexedmzML")) { indexed = true; }`, then :70-77 chooses indexed_schema_location_ or schema_location_. TextFile::load (TextFile.cpp:54-75) counts blank and comment lines toward first_n because skip_empty_lines/comment_symbol keep their defaults.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Rust report probe OpenMS4-R/docs/mzml-schema-selection-probe.json: MzMLFile_4_indexed.mzML with three comment lines inserted after the XML declaration has no needle in its first four trimmed lines, passes mzML_idx_1_10.xsd and fails mzML_1_10.xsd under xmllint (exit 3). The C++ isValid itself (Xerces) was not executed; the four-line predicate is from the code. No Core regression test was added (MzMLFile_test isValid section unchanged).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): isValid parses the document with a RootElementHandler_ that records the document element's name and stops at the root start tag; the indexed schema is chosen iff the local name (prefix stripped) is indexedmzML. TextFile sniffing removed.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-060"></a>
### CPP-060: Negative design indices wrap to large unsigned values

**Negative Label, Fraction or Fraction_Group values in an experimental design are parsed as signed ints and stored in unsigned fields, becoming 4294967295, with no parse error.**  
- **Trigger:** Load an experimental design TSV (one-table or two-table) with Fraction or Label set to -1, via ProteomicsLFQ, ProteinQuantifier, MSstatsConverter, IsobaricWorkflow, Epifany or pyOpenMS. A negative Fraction_Group is caught only indirectly by isValid_, with a message showing the wrapped value.
- **Consequence:** A wrapped fraction counts as an extra fraction and makes the design look fractionated. A wrapped label makes getNumberOfLabels() about 4e9, so ProteinQuantifier.cpp:564 `for (Size c = 1; c <= ed.getNumberOfLabels(); ++c)` runs toward 4e9 iterations, and MSstatsFile.cpp:218 and ProteomicsLFQ.cpp:2456 fail with a misleading labels error. The input is malformed but is handled badly instead of being rejected.
