- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/ExperimentalDesignFile.cpp:232-235 `int label = StringUtils::toInt32(...); int fraction = ...; int fraction_group = ...; parseErrorIf_(!has_sample && (label > 1), ...)`, which -1 passes, then :275-277 `e.fraction_group = fraction_group; e.fraction = fraction; e.label = label;`. The two-table parser at :380-384 does `e.fraction_group = StringUtils::toInt32(...); e.fraction = StringUtils::toInt32(...); e.label = has_label ? StringUtils::toInt32(...) : 1;`. The members are unsigned (ExperimentalDesign.h:470, :476, :485). StringUtils.cpp:252-255 skips only '+' and then `std::from_chars(f, l, ret)` into Int32, which accepts '-1'. ExperimentalDesign.cpp isValid_ :791-812 constrains only fraction_group (consecutive from 1).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Confirmed by reading the parser, the member types, toInt32 and isValid_ at origin/develop. Core has no negative-index test on ci.5 or dax/codex/p0-followups ('must not be negative' appears only in ExperimentalDesignFile.cpp), and the Rust report claims no execution.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Both parsers now read the three columns as signed ints and call parseErrorIf_ for label, fraction or fraction_group < 0 before assignment. In the one-table parser this also happens before the 'Label > 1' multiplex check (ci.5 :243-245 and :394-396).
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5); guard still present on dax/codex/p0-followups (ExperimentalDesignFile.cpp:243-245, :396-398)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-078"></a>
### CPP-078: rasterizeIMFrame casts a non-finite coordinate to Int64

**A NaN m/z or ion-mobility value, or a NaN or infinite range bound, passes the range filter and is converted from double to Int64, which is undefined behaviour.**  
- **Trigger:** MSSpectrum::rasterizeIMFrame (C++ or pyOpenMS MSSpectrum.rasterizeIMFrame) on a spectrum whose m/z or IM float array holds NaN (e.g. a corrupt mzML binary array; the mzML reader does not reject NaN), or called with a NaN bound or min=-inf (inf*0 = NaN for every peak).
- **Consequence:** Undefined float-to-integer conversion (UBSan float-cast-overflow). With usual codegen it is not an out-of-bounds write: x86-64 cvttsd2si gives INT64_MIN, and the index times im_bins times sizeof(float) wraps back to row or column 0; arm64 saturates NaN to 0. The visible effect is wrong intensity added to an edge pixel (with a -inf/NaN bound, every peak lands there). The Rust report's claim of indexing before the buffer is not borne out on these targets.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop 5082181714 src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.cpp:882-889 only reject `min_im >= max_im` / `min_mz >= max_mz` (false for NaN, passes -inf); :917-918 `im_scale = im_bins / im_range` (0 for an infinite range); :931 `if (mz < min_mz || mz > max_mz || im < min_im || im > max_im) continue;` (all false for NaN); :937-938 `Int64 mz_bin = static_cast<Int64>((mz - min_mz) * mz_scale); Int64 im_bin = static_cast<Int64>((im - min_im) * im_scale);`; :941-948 clamp from above only; :951 `pixel_idx = static_cast<Size>(mz_bin) * im_bins + static_cast<Size>(im_bin)`. No in-tree C++ caller; bound in pyOpenMS at bind_spectrum.cpp:591-612.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read upstream MSSpectrum.cpp:857-966. NaN compares false in every filter clause; min_mz=-inf passes :886 and makes mz_scale 0, so (mz+inf)*0 = NaN. Upstream MSSpectrum_rasterizeIMFrame_test.cpp and test_MSSpectrum_rasterizeIMFrame.py have no NaN/inf case; Rust report states source review only; no Core test was added (MSSpectrum_rasterizeIMFrame_test.cpp unchanged between core-v4.0.0-ci.2 and ci.5 and on dax/codex/p0-followups). Malformed data reaching UB with no demonstrated memory corruption: P2.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): rasterizeIMFrame skips peaks with non-finite m/z or IM (std::isfinite) before the range filter, throws InvalidRange when a range or scale is non-finite (before touching the buffer), adds lower clamps to both bin indices, rejects im_bins*mz_bins overflow, and validates the IM array size before zero-filling the output.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-082"></a>
### CPP-082: An empty ion-mobility array makes isSortedByIM report true and sortByIonMobility a silent no-op

**An empty or short but sorted ion-mobility array makes isSortedByIM() return true and sortByIonMobility() do nothing, because neither compares the array length to the peak count.**  
- **Trigger:** An IM frame whose named ion-mobility float array is empty or shorter than the peak list and sorted, e.g. from an mzML IM binaryDataArray that is empty or shorter than defaultArrayLength, fed to IMDataConverter::reshapeIMFrameToMany via PeakPickerIM (PeakPickerIM.cpp:1250), IMDataConverter::splitExperimentByIonMobility (IMDataConverter.cpp:211) or TOPPView (TOPPViewBase.cpp:2010); or direct C++/pyOpenMS calls.
- **Consequence:** reshapeIMFrameToMany iterates only over the IM array, so peaks beyond its length (all peaks for an empty array) are silently dropped instead of raising an error. A short unsorted array does throw (sort() calls checkDataArraySizes_). The mzML decoder may warn about the length mismatch (MzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:190-205), but only when the declared arrayLength differs. A longer sorted array (API misuse only, since the reader clamps) makes the loop read im_frame[i] past the peaks.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.cpp:506-512 `auto [im_data_index, D] = getIMData(); const auto& im_data = getFloatDataArrays()[im_data_index]; return std::is_sorted(im_data.begin(), im_data.end());`; :383-395 sortByIonMobility only calls sort() `if (! std::is_sorted(im_data.begin(), im_data.end()))`; :27 checkDataArraySizes_ exempts empty arrays `if (!arrays[i].empty() && arrays[i].size() != peaks)`; :808-818 IM array found by name only. Consumer IMDataConverter.cpp:105-108 `if (! im_frame.isSortedByIM()) im_frame.sortByIonMobility();` then :136-144 `for (Size i = 0; i < im_data.size(); ++i) { ... last_spec->push_back(im_frame[i]); }`. mzML reader MzMLHandler.cpp:557 `Size copy_length = std::min(default_arr_length, data.size);`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core b6dd412 added MSSpectrum_test.cpp section 'void isSortedByIM() const': it resizes a sorted IM array to size()-1 and size()+1 and expects Exception::Precondition from isSortedByIM() and sortByIonMobility(). By reading, pre-fix code returns true or does nothing there, but no failing run is recorded, and the zero-length case itself is not tested. Malformed input handled silently: P2.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): New requireIMArrayMatchesPeaks_ throws Exception::Precondition in sortByIonMobility and isSortedByIM unless the IM array has exactly one entry per peak.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (fix), b6dd412 (test); both in core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MSSpectrum_test.cpp, section 'void isSortedByIM() const' (size +-1, not size 0)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-097"></a>
### CPP-097: MRMFeature lookup by unknown key mutates the map and returns the first feature

**The non-const MRMFeature::getFeature and getPrecursorFeature default-insert an unknown key mapped to index 0, so a lookup silently returns an unrelated first sub-feature and registers a key that was never added.**  
- **Trigger:** On a non-const MRMFeature: f.addFeature(a, "chromatogram1"); f.getFeature("typo"); returns a, and getFeatureIDs() then lists "typo". With no sub-features, the call throws std::out_of_range instead. Upstream OpenSWATH callers (MRMFeatureFinderScoring.cpp:384-447, EmgScoring.h:67, MRMFeatureAccessOpenMS.cpp:221-266) query only keys the picker added, and pyOpenMS (bind_kernel.cpp:4035/4048) uses the const overloads.
- **Consequence:** A C++ caller with a wrong key gets a silently wrong sub-feature, and its intensities and scores, instead of an error; the map is also mutated. The same call throws on a const receiver.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/MRMFeature.cpp:84 'return features_.at(feature_map_[key]);' and :127 'return precursor_features_.at(precursor_feature_map_[key]);' in the non-const overloads. The const overloads at :89 and :132 use '.at(key)' and throw std::out_of_range.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source review. MRMFeature_test.cpp (Core :94-102, :199-207) looks up only added keys, and no unknown-key test exists in Core ci.5 or dax/codex/p0-followups. The Rust report is source review; its Rust test covers the Rust behaviour only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): A file-local helper, featureIndex_(map, key, OPENMS_PRETTY_FUNCTION), uses find() and throws Exception::ElementNotFound. All four accessors, const and non-const, use it. The same commit also makes addFeature and addPrecursorFeature with a repeated key replace the stored feature in place instead of stranding it.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-098"></a>
### CPP-098: MRMFeature::addFeature strands a feature when a key repeats, while the sibling class throws

**Adding a second feature under a key already in use appends it and moves the key to the new entry, so the earlier feature stays in getFeatures() but no key reaches it any more.**  
- **Trigger:** `MRMFeature f; f.addFeature(a, "x"); f.addFeature(b, "x");` (or the same with addPrecursorFeature): getFeatures().size() is 2 but getFeatureIDs() returns one key. Only direct C++ or pyOpenMS use can do this. The picker keys features by chromatogram nativeID, which is already unique within a group.
- **Consequence:** The feature vector and the key map fall out of step without any warning. Code that checks they match rejects the feature later with an unrelated message (MRMFeatureAccessOpenMS.cpp:260-263 throws IllegalArgument "Feature storage needs to match feature lookup id cache."). Code that indexes by position may pick up the stranded entry. No in-tree tool produces a repeated key.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MRMFeature.cpp:72-73 `features_.push_back(feature); feature_map_[key] = Int(features_.size()) - 1;` (the move overload does the same at :78-79, and addPrecursorFeature at :107-108 and :113-114). By contrast MRMTransitionGroup.h:136-139 addTransition uses `transition_map_.emplace(...)` and throws Exception::InvalidValue on a repeated key.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read at origin/develop 5082181714. Neither the Rust report (source review only, no C++ run) nor Core ran it. MRMFeature_test.cpp is unchanged since the package was extracted (70c8251), and every addFeature/addPrecursorFeature call in it uses a distinct key.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): All four overloads now call map::emplace first. A new key appends as before. A repeated key overwrites the feature at the index already stored, so nothing is stranded (ci.5 MRMFeature.cpp:91, :104, :141, :154). Core deliberately did not throw like addTransition does: there is no hasFeature(), and a repeated nativeID in input data would otherwise abort a whole run. The Rust port throws by default instead.
- **Commits:** core 181dadf on codex/cpp-review-completion; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4 and ci.5
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-099"></a>
### CPP-099: MRMTransitionGroup::isInternallyConsistent cannot report an inconsistent group in a release build

**isInternallyConsistent() returns true for every group in a release build and throws Precondition in a Debug build, so it never actually answers false.**  
- **Trigger:** A group built through the API with a transition but no chromatogram, or with a chromatogram under a different key, then isInternallyConsistent(). In C++ or pyOpenMS (bind_kernel.cpp:363, :420) a release build returns true.
- **Consequence:** An inconsistent group is reported as consistent, and the guard at MRMFeatureFinderScoring.cpp:1445 can never fire in a release build. That in-tree path adds each transition and its chromatogram together under the same key (:1435-1436), so no current tool result changes. Only groups assembled through the API get the misleading answer.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/MRMTransitionGroup.h:291-297 consists of three `OPENMS_PRECONDITION(...)` lines and `return true;`. src/openms/include/OpenMS/CONCEPT/Macros.h:91 `#define OPENMS_PRECONDITION(condition, message)` expands to nothing, and src/openms/CMakeLists.txt:26-30 enables assertions only for CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE Debug. The only real caller is MRMFeatureFinderScoring.cpp:1445 `if (!trgroup_it->second.isInternallyConsistent()) throw Exception::IllegalArgument(...)`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 181dadf extended the section `inline bool isInternallyConsistent() const` in MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp (ci.5 :309-324). It expects false for a transition without a chromatogram and for equal counts with a mismatched key. On the upstream code this returns true in a release build and throws in a Debug build. No failing run on pre-fix code is recorded in the commit or the review JSON.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The function now checks that transition and chromatogram counts match, then that the map sizes match, and returns isMappingConsistent_() (ci.5 MRMTransitionGroup.h:291-305). The PRECONDITIONs were removed so a Debug build returns false rather than throwing. The doc comment no longer claims 'no runtime overhead in release mode'.
- **Commits:** core 181dadf on codex/cpp-review-completion; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4 and ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp, section `inline bool isInternallyConsistent() const`
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-102"></a>
### CPP-102: subset re-keys precursor chromatograms by nativeID and throws on a group the class's own test builds

**subset() copies precursor chromatograms under their nativeID instead of the key they were stored with, so their keys are lost and it throws when two of them share a nativeID, for example when neither has one set.**  
- **Trigger:** Add two precursor chromatograms with unset (equal) nativeIDs under distinct keys, e.g. addPrecursorChromatogram(chrom1, "prec1") and addPrecursorChromatogram(chrom2, "prec2"), then call subset(ids). The copy throws InvalidValue for key "". With keys that differ from the nativeIDs but no clash, the subset silently stores the chromatograms under their nativeIDs, and hasPrecursorChromatogram("prec1") is false. Reachable through C++ and pyOpenMS (bind_kernel.cpp:347, :362, :404, :419). OpenSwathWorkflow.cpp:1395 keys precursors by their nativeID, so MRMFeatureFinderScoring.cpp:320 is not affected.
- **Consequence:** A valid group either cannot be subset (an uncaught InvalidValue aborts the caller), or the subset silently drops the caller's keys, so later lookups by the original key fail. No TOPP workflow path is affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/MRMTransitionGroup.h:355-359 `for (const auto& pc : precursor_chromatograms_) { transition_group_subset.addPrecursorChromatogram(pc, pc.getNativeID()); }`. addPrecursorChromatogram (:231-240) throws Exception::InvalidValue on a repeated key, and its documentation (:229) calls the key 'Unique identifier of the chromatogram, e.g. its nativeID'.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 181dadf extended the section `MRMTransitionGroup subset(std::vector<std::string> tr_ids)` in MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp (ci.5 :300-305). It adds file-scope chrom1/chrom2, whose nativeID is never set (declared at :49-50, no setNativeID anywhere in the file), under "prec1"/"prec2", calls subset, and expects both keys in the result. On the upstream code the second copy throws InvalidValue. No failing run on pre-fix code is recorded. P1 is borderline: this is valid but uncommon API input that no tool produces.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): subset() inverts precursor_chromatogram_map_ into a list of keys indexed by storage position (ci.5 MRMTransitionGroup.h:369-377, `pc_keys.at(pc_entry.second) = pc_entry.first;`). It then copies each precursor chromatogram in storage order under its original key. Order is kept because the picker treats precursor index 0 as the monoisotopic trace. The Rust port instead keeps the upstream re-keying, returns an error, and documents the difference.
- **Commits:** core 181dadf on codex/cpp-review-completion; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4 and ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp, section `MRMTransitionGroup subset(std::vector<std::string> tr_ids)`
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-107"></a>
### CPP-107: Record read length is unchecked in both directions and the read result is never inspected

**Reading a spectrum or chromatogram from an indexed mzML trusts the byte offsets in the file's footer with no checks, so corrupt offsets cause a non-OpenMS exception, an unbounded allocation, or a record built from uninitialised heap bytes that also leaves the stream broken.**  
- **Trigger:** Open an indexed mzML whose <indexList> offsets are corrupt, truncated or hostile, then access records through OnDiscMSExperiment, IndexedMzMLFileLoader, FileInfo, TOPPView indexed loading, or pyOpenMS IndexedMzMLHandler.getSpectrumById / OnDiscMSExperiment.getSpectrum (bind_format.cpp:971, bind_kernel.cpp:1889). The trigger is a decreasing offset pair, an offset far beyond the index, or a range running past EOF.
- **Consequence:** A decreasing pair throws std::bad_array_new_length, which escapes Exception:: handlers. A huge span requests an arbitrary allocation. A range past EOF leaves the buffer tail uninitialised, so std::string(buffer) scans indeterminate bytes (undefined behaviour, but bounded by the terminator at buffer[readl], so no out-of-bounds read). The short read also leaves failbit set, so every later seekg/read on that handler silently reads nothing and yields garbage. The result is bad handling of malformed input, not memory corruption.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:162-167 (getChromatogramById_helper_) and :222-227 (getSpectrumById_helper_): `std::streampos readl = endidx - startidx; char* buffer = new char[readl + std::streampos(1)]; filestream_.seekg(startidx, filestream_.beg); filestream_.read(buffer, readl); buffer[readl] = '\0'; std::string text(buffer);`. There is no ordering or bounds check, and no gcount() or clear(). The offsets come from IndexedMzMLDecoder.cpp:43 `boost::lexical_cast< unsigned long long >(s)`. Only index_offset_ is bounds-checked, at IndexedMzMLDecoder.cpp:95 `if (indexoffset < 0 || indexoffset > length)`. Upstream history shows only refactors (1b0ad582c2 String->std::string).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Code reading only. The Rust report states 'No C++ execution, sanitizer run or upstream fix is claimed'; its 'reproduced' refers to the Rust implementation's own checked-error tests. No Core test exercises corrupt offsets: the 181dadf test changes do not touch IndexedMzMLHandler, and 'Corrupt index' appears in no test on dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): A new anonymous-namespace readRecord_(stream, filename, start, end, index_offset) is used by both helpers (p0-followups IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:34, 223, 278). It throws Exception::ParseError unless 0 <= start <= end <= index_offset_, clears the stream before seeking, reads into a value-initialised std::string, trims the string to gcount() and clears the stream again. parseFooter_ also resets the offsets and flags before reparsing.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3, kept through ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-108"></a>
### CPP-108: Chromatogram out-of-range message reports the spectrum count

**When a chromatogram index is out of range, the error message talks about spectra and quotes the spectrum count, not the chromatogram count, and both helpers call N the 'maximal allowed' id when the largest valid id is N-1.**  
- **Trigger:** Call IndexedMzMLHandler::getChromatogramById / getMSChromatogramById (or OnDiscMSExperiment chromatogram access, C++ or pyOpenMS) with id >= getNrChromatograms().
- **Consequence:** The IllegalArgument is still thrown, but its text points to the wrong range. For example, a chromatogram-only file reports 'maximal allowed is 0', and a DIA file with thousands of spectra reports thousands. No data is affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:132-136 (origin/develop 5082181714): `if (chromToGet >= (int)getNrChromatograms()) { throw Exception::IllegalArgument(..., "id needs to be smaller than the number of spectra, was " + StringUtils::toStr(id) + " maximal allowed is " + StringUtils::toStr(getNrSpectra()) ));`. The spectrum helper at :192-196 also calls getNrSpectra() the 'maximal allowed' id, but the check rejects that value.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read directly from upstream IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:132-136 and :192-196. The Rust report's C++ evidence is 'Source review ... No C++ execution'. No Core test matches on the message text: the inherited-core-review.json entry says nothing greps for it, and a grep of the Core tests for 'number of chromatograms' finds only an unrelated comment. Under the rubric this is a misleading diagnostic (P2), reached only through an out-of-range request.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The chromatogram message now reads 'id needs to be smaller than the number of chromatograms, was X number of chromatograms is N' and uses getNrChromatograms(). The spectrum message changed 'maximal allowed is N' to 'number of spectra is N'.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion; first in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-109"></a>
### CPP-109: Record XML parse errors are discarded, and the handler always feeds the parser ill-formed input for the last record

**The per-record DOM parse has no Xerces error handler, so well-formedness errors are silently dropped and the parser's partial tree gets decoded. For the last record, the parser is always handed trailing list/run end tags after the root element.**  
- **Trigger:** Any indexed-mzML record fetch (IndexedMzMLHandler / OnDiscMSExperiment / MzMLSpectrumDecoder, cached OpenSWATH access, pyOpenMS) where the record's XML is malformed, truncated, or mis-indexed. The trailing-end-tag input occurs on every fetch of the last spectrum or chromatogram, but after the root closes it does not change the DOM.
- **Consequence:** Malformed input either gives a partial or empty spectrum, with only a std::cerr line ('intensity or m/z array is missing, skipping this spectrum'), or fails later with an unrelated ConversionError that hides the XML error. Valid files lose no data, so this is limited to malformed input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSpectrumDecoder.cpp:538-543: `xercesc::MemBufInputSource myxml_buf(..., in.length(), "myxml (in memory)"); ... parser->setLoadExternalDTD(false); parser->parse(myxml_buf);` has no setErrorHandler, and :554-557 rejects only a null root. The last-record range runs to the other list or the index at IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:142-155 and :202-215 (`endidx = index_offset_;` / `endidx = chromatograms_offsets_[0];`). A partial record then takes the silent path at MzMLSpectrumDecoder.cpp:267-271 (`std::cerr << "Error, intensity or m/z array is missing, skipping this spectrum"; return;`). Upstream's own test src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MzMLSpectrumDecoder_test.cpp:392/400 feeds ill-formed '&-' inside <binary> and expects `Exception::ConversionError`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Code reading of upstream :538-557 is backed by the test expectation. Upstream's MzMLSpectrumDecoder_test.cpp:400 pins ConversionError for ill-formed XML, which shows the parse error is masked. Core 5f7d33f changed the same section to expect ParseError, so that test would fail on pre-fix code, but no failing run is recorded. The Rust report is source review only for C++ (its 'Reproduced' refers to Rust behaviour). Silently dropping errors without an ErrorHandler is standard XercesDOMParser behaviour: the scanner has no error reporter and exits on the first fatal error without throwing.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): recordLength_ trims the input to the <spectrum>/<chromatogram> record itself, handling quoted '>' and empty elements, and throws ParseError if the record is not closed inside its byte range. RecordErrorHandler_ is installed on the parser, and any error it captures now throws ParseError('Malformed XML in mzML record: ...') instead of decoding the partial tree.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (RecordErrorHandler_ and recordLength_ trimming, warning only) and 5f7d33f (throw ParseError, test updated); both on codex/cpp-review-completion, first in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MzMLSpectrumDecoder_test.cpp, section '[EXTRA] void domParseSpectrum(std::string& in, OpenMS::Interfaces::SpectrumPtr & sptr)' (the 'Invalid content of <binary>' block) now expects Exception::ParseError (5f7d33f). No dedicated test for last-record trimming or unclosed-record ranges.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-124"></a>
### CPP-124: .ibd array reads are sized from the declared count with no comparison against the file's length

**The .ibd readers size their buffers from the XML-declared element count, capped only at 1e8, and never compare offset + count*width with the .ibd length. A bad count therefore commits the full allocation before fread comes up short.**  
- **Trigger:** An imzML whose IMS:1000103 (external array length) or IMS:1000102 offset points past the end of the .ibd, from a truncated or hostile dataset. Reached through ImzMLFile::load (including FileHandler in TOPP tools), OnDiscImzMLExperiment spectrum access, and pyOpenMS.
- **Consequence:** Each array read transiently commits up to about 0.8 GB (float64 m/z), 1.2 GB (float32 m/z widened to double, or float64/int64 intensity/aux narrowed to float) or 1.6 GB (int64 m/z) of zeroed memory. That can raise std::bad_alloc or push the host into OOM. When the allocation succeeds, the short fread still ends in a clean ParseError.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:37 `static constexpr uint64_t MAX_IBD_ARRAY_ELEMENTS = 100'000'000ULL;`. validateCount_ (:39-51) checks only that cap and Size. readMzArray: validateCount_ :127, seekIbd_ :129, `out.resize(count);` :131, `std::vector<float> tmp(count);` :137, then fread :138. readFloatVector_: :206, :208, `out.resize(count);` :210, `std::vector<double> tmp(count);` :223. git grep for fstat/ftell/fileSize/file_size in the ImzML handlers, ImzMLFile.cpp and OnDiscImzMLExperiment.cpp finds nothing.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream allocation order read at ImzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:127-138 and :206-223, with no length check anywhere in the read path. No Core test declares a range past the end of the .ibd; the review JSON notes both fixtures end exactly at EOF and the aux-length test is filtered before the read. The Rust report is source review only for C++; its listed tests are Rust tests.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): New elementWidth_() and validateRange_() run right after validateCount_ in readMzArray and readFloatVector_, before any resize. They throw ParseError for an unknown type, for offset+bytes overflowing uint64, and for a range past the .ibd length taken from fstat/_fstat64 on the open handle. If fstat fails, the existing short-read check still applies. The header @throws docs were updated.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; the path-stat version described in the review was reworked to fstat before this commit landed)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-130"></a>
### CPP-130: IonImage allocates width * height from unvalidated file-supplied image dimensions

**Ion-image extraction allocates width x height doubles plus a bit mask straight from the imzML header's declared grid size, with no ceiling.**  
- **Trigger:** Load an imzML whose IMS:1000042/IMS:1000043 declare a huge grid (e.g. 100000 x 100000) with ImzMLFile into MSImagingExperiment or OnDiscImzMLExperiment, then call extractIonImage (C++ or pyOpenMS bind_experiment.cpp:703-704). The spectrum list can be tiny. No TOPP tool calls it.
- **Consequence:** About 80 GB of zero-filled doubles plus 1.25 GB of mask bits are requested for 100000 x 100000: std::bad_alloc or an OOM kill instead of a clean InvalidValue. At the UInt32 extreme the element count exceeds vector::max_size and std::length_error is thrown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/IMAGING/IonImage.cpp:21-28 `const Size n = static_cast<Size>(width) * static_cast<Size>(height); intensities_.assign(n, 0.0); mask_.assign(n, false);` with no ceiling; src/openms/include/OpenMS/IMAGING/IonImageExtraction.h:89 `IonImage image(geom.getWidth(), geom.getHeight());`; dimensions come from the file: ImzMLHandler.cpp:699-700 `if (acc == "IMS:1000042") { meta_.max_count_x = parseImsUInt32_(file_, acc, val); return; }` (and IMS:1000043), ImzMLFile.cpp:380-385 `UInt width = meta.max_count_x; ... geom.setDimensions(width, height);`; MSImagingGeometry.cpp:16-20 stores them unchecked.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Allocation path traced at origin/develop from the cvParam parser to IonImage::resize. The Rust report is a source review ('No C++ execution, sanitizer run or upstream fix is claimed'). Core added no over-ceiling test: IonImage_test.cpp, ImzMLFile_test.cpp and MSImagingExperiment_test.cpp at dax/codex/p0-followups have no case for the pixel ceiling, and git log -S"IonImage" -- src/tests over ci.2..p0-followups finds nothing.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): IonImage::resize (and so the constructor and extractIonImage) throws Exception::InvalidValue before allocating when width*height exceeds MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS = 2^31; the header documents the throw. The review table's earlier 4096 x 4096 ceiling does not appear in ci.2..ci.5 history; 181dadf already has 2^31. MSImagingGeometry::setDimensions stays unchecked, and a header just under the ceiling can still request about 17 GB.
- **Commits:** 181dadf [FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation (IonImage.cpp and IonImage.h; first released in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-132"></a>
### CPP-132: ImzMLWriter::store cannot write a metadata-only continuous dataset

**A metadata-only imzML store of a dataset declared continuous always throws InvalidParameter: once the peaks are cleared there is no non-empty reference spectrum, so the shared-axis check fails.**  
- **Trigger:** ImzMLFile::store(MSExperiment) or store(MSImagingExperiment) with PeakFileOptions::setMetadataOnly(true) on an experiment with imzml:imaging_mode=continuous, which every load of a continuous imzML sets. C++ or pyOpenMS only; FileHandler does not store imzML and no TOPP tool reaches it.
- **Consequence:** The store is refused with a misleading 'must share the same m/z axis' message and no file is written, while the same store of a processed dataset succeeds. The only workaround is removing the imaging_mode MetaValue, which writes the file as processed.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLWriter.cpp:485-491 `if (options.getMetadataOnly()) { for (auto& spectrum : exp.getSpectra()) { spectrum.clear(false); } }`. extractMeta_ :342-344 copies imzml:imaging_mode. spectraShareMz_ :707-710 `if (ref >= exp.size() || exp[ref].empty()) { return false; }`. isContinuousMode_ :740-746 `if (meta.imaging_mode == "continuous") { if (!spectraShareMz_(exp)) { throw Exception::InvalidParameter(... "continuous imzML requires all spectra to share the same m/z axis and array length"); }`. store(): applyStoreOptions_ :1402, the work.empty() check :1404 (spectrum count only), isContinuousMode_ :1414.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — The upstream path was read end to end (:485-491 -> :1404 does not catch it -> :707-710 -> :744). Core 181dadf added the section 'void store writes a metadata-only continuous dataset', which calls f.store() with setMetadataOnly(true) outside TEST_EXCEPTION. On pre-fix code that call throws InvalidParameter, but no failing run is recorded in the commit or the docs. The Rust report is source review for C++, and the Rust port reproduces the refusal on purpose in its own test. P1 because it is an exception on a valid, uncommon API call; it is borderline, since the call is exotic and the exception is clean.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): isContinuousMode_ computes no_peaks = std::all_of(spectra empty) and skips the shared-axis check when no spectrum has peaks, keeping the declared mode because both layouts are identical on disk. The ImzMLFile::store doc was updated.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion; first in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/ImzMLFile_test.cpp, section 'void store writes a metadata-only continuous dataset'. The two-axis experiment is still refused with peaks; with setMetadataOnly(true) it stores and reloads as continuous with 2 zero-length index entries.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-141"></a>
### CPP-141: DataValue::operator double() reads the union's double member for a non-numeric value

**Casting a String or list DataValue to double, float or long double reads the union's double member while a pointer is stored. Instead of throwing ConversionError, it returns the pointer's bits as a number, and ImzMLFile::store writes that number as pixel size and image extent.**  
- **Trigger:** ImzMLFile::store(MSExperiment) or buildImagingGeometry (C++ or pyOpenMS) on an experiment whose imzml:pixel_size_x/y or imzml:max_dim_x/y meta value is a String or list, e.g. `exp.setMetaValue("imzml:pixel_size_x", "10")`. The imzML reader always stores doubles (ImzMLFile.cpp:134, :142). A string can also come from an mzML <run> userParam without a numeric xsd type: MzMLHandler.cpp:3426 fromXSDString treats it as a string, and :3447-3449 puts it on the experiment. FileHandler refuses IMZML stores (FileHandler.cpp:1238-1244), so no TOPP tool reaches this. The same DataValue defect has a file-reachable path via the mzML elution-time fallback (MzMLHandler.cpp:1401-1403); that path is tracked under CPP-058.
- **Consequence:** Undefined behaviour: an inactive union member is read. In practice the result is a garbage (typically denormal) double rather than a memory fault. The imzML file is written with a nonsense pixel size and IMS:1000044/45 extent, and no error is raised.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/DATASTRUCTURES/DataValue.cpp:466-478 operator double(): throws only for `value_type_ == EMPTY_VALUE` (:468), converts INT_VALUE (:473-476), otherwise `return data_.dou_;` (:477). operator long double (:452-464, return :463) and operator float (:480-492, return :491) are the same. The union (DataValue.h:409-416) holds `std::string* str_` and list pointers for the non-numeric types. imzML call sites: ImzMLWriter.cpp:360 `meta.pixel_size_x = static_cast<double>(exp.getMetaValue("imzml:pixel_size_x"));` and the same at :364, :368, :372, reached from extractMeta_ (:1413) in store. ImzMLFile.cpp:365-366 (buildImagingGeometry) does the same. DataValue.cpp and ImzMLWriter.cpp are byte-identical to core-v4.0.0-ci.2 (ba6ca52, 277c3aa).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading only. The Rust report states no C++ execution, and the Rust port cannot reproduce it (tagged enum). No class test casts a String or list DataValue to a floating type: DataValue_test.cpp is unchanged in Core, and the ImzMLFile_test sections added in 181dadf do not set string pixel sizes.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): All three floating conversions gained `else if (value_type_ != DOUBLE_VALUE) throw Exception::ConversionError(...)` naming the type and value. The DataValue.h docs now state that only DOUBLE_VALUE and INT_VALUE are accepted and strings are not parsed. The imzML store therefore throws instead of writing garbage.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3, in ci.5; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups), fixed under CPP-058. ImzMLWriter.cpp itself is unchanged.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-157"></a>
### CPP-157: qcML removeAllAttachments omits set-only entries

**removeAllAttachments only visits run IDs, so attachments on sets whose ID is not also a run ID, and on runs that have attachments but no quality parameters, are never removed.**  
- **Trigger:** QCShrinker without -name/-run (QCShrinker.cpp:125-130) on a qcML that has set-level attachments with the removed accessions, for example tables or plots added to a set by QCEmbedder. Also pyOpenMS/C++ QcMLFile.removeAllAttachments.
- **Consequence:** Set-level tables and plots that were meant to be stripped stay in the output, so the file is not shrunk as documented. No scientific values change.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/QcMLFile.cpp:511-517 `void QcMLFile::removeAllAttachments(const std::string& at) { for (... it = runQualityAts_.begin(); it != runQualityAts_.end(); ++it) { removeAttachment(it->first, at); } }`. removeAttachment touches runQualityAts_[r] only `if (existsRun(r))` (:477) and setQualityAts_[r] only `if (existsSet(r))` (:494). existsRun/existsSet look in runQualityQPs_/setQualityQPs_ (:326, :345). QcMLFile.h:119-120 documents removal 'from all runs/sets'.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — docs/port-resumption-2026-09-13/inherited-core-review.json records a harness run against pre-fix Core HEAD (identical to upstream develop): 'a set-only attachment and an attachment-only run both kept the attachment'. The harness is not retained. Core test b2079fb ('void removeAllAttachments(std::string at)') checks the run, set and orphan cases. There is no recorded failing test run on pre-fix code. By the rubric this is a wrong result on valid uncommon input (P1), though practical urgency is low.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): removeAllAttachments now erases matching attachments with std::remove_if directly from every list in runQualityAts_ and setQualityAts_ (QcMLFile.cpp:516-531 at ci.5). It no longer goes through the QP-gated removeAttachment and does not create phantom map entries.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (fix), b2079fb (test), in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/QcMLFile_test.cpp, section 'void removeAllAttachments(std::string at)' (b2079fb)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-176"></a>
### CPP-176: TrafoXML omits unsupported parameter types after a non-fatal diagnostic

**A TrafoXML Param with a type other than int, float or string is logged as a 'Non-fatal error' and dropped, and the model is refitted with that parameter's default instead of raising ParseError.**  
- **Trigger:** Loading a schema-invalid .trafoXML with e.g. `<Param type="bogus" name="interpolation_type" value="linear"/>` through TransformationXMLFile::load (MapRTTransformer -trafo_in, OpenSwathWorkflow, pyOpenMS). OpenMS's own store() never writes such types.
- **Consequence:** The file loads as a different transformation: the dropped value is replaced by the model default (e.g. interpolation_type 'cspline', params_.setDefaults at TransformationModelInterpolated.cpp:217), with only a log line instead of the documented exception.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/TransformationXMLFile.cpp:159-162 `else { error(LOAD,std::string("Unsupported parameter type: '") + type + "'"); }`; XMLHandler::error (HANDLERS/XMLHandler.cpp:71-87) only does `OPENMS_LOG_ERROR << error_message` and returns; load() then calls `transformation.fitModel(model_type_, params_);` (:40). TransformationXMLFile.h:46 documents `@exception Exception::ParseError is thrown if an error occurs during parsing`; TrafoXML_1_1.xsd:36-40 restricts Param/@type to int, float, string (origin/develop 5082181714).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Rust report: source-reviewed, no C++ execution. No test in upstream or Core (TransformationXMLFile_test.cpp has no unsupported-type case on core-v4.0.0-ci.5 or dax/codex/p0-followups).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): error(LOAD, ...) replaced by fatalError(LOAD, ...) naming the type and parameter (Core TransformationXMLFile.cpp:164), so load() throws Exception::ParseError as documented.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-179"></a>
### CPP-179: MSstats unknown summarization method writes zero intensities

**storeLFQ accepts any retention-time summarization method name, and for a name other than manual/max/min/mean/sum every aggregated intensity is written as 0 into an otherwise well-formed MSstats CSV.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ or pyOpenMS call MSstatsFile.storeLFQ(..., retention_time_summarization_method="bogus", ...) (e.g. a typo such as "median"). Not reachable through MSstatsConverter (valid strings enforced) or ProteomicsLFQ (passes "max"); storeISO forces manual.
- **Consequence:** A valid-looking MSstats input with all intensities 0 instead of an error; downstream analysis silently has no signal.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/MSstatsFile.cpp:238 storeLFQ only computes `rt_summarization_manual(retention_time_summarization_method == "manual")`, never validating other names; constructFile_ :178 `MSstatsFile::Intensity intensity(0);`, assigned only in the "max"/"min"/"mean"/"sum" branches (:179-194), then :197-199 `csv_out.addLine(... StringUtils::toStr(intensity) ...)` always writes the line. pyOpenMS src/pyOpenMS/bindings/bind_format.cpp:1172 passes any string to storeLFQ; MSstatsConverter.cpp:115 restricts it with setValidStrings_.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core test expects Exception::IllegalArgument for method "unknown"; pre-fix code writes the CSV (argued from code; no recorded failing run). No executed C++ probe in the Rust report.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): storeLFQ checks the method against manual,max,min,mean,sum with ListUtils::create/ListUtils::contains and throws Exception::IllegalArgument listing the valid methods.
- **Commits:** packages/core 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5), src/openms/source/FORMAT/MSstatsFile.cpp; test in b2079fb (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MSstatsFile_test.cpp section storeLFQ (TEST_EXCEPTION(Exception::IllegalArgument, ... "unknown" ...))
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-183"></a>
### CPP-183: SQLite bound statements leak on bind or step errors

**When binding a blob or stepping the INSERT fails, executeBindStatement throws without finalising the prepared statement, so the statement is leaked.**  
- **Trigger:** Any executeBindStatement call whose sqlite3_bind_blob fails (more data entries than placeholders, SQLITE_RANGE) or whose sqlite3_step does not return SQLITE_DONE (PRIMARY KEY/NOT NULL constraint violation, locked or full database, I/O error), e.g. during sqMass writing in MzMLSqliteHandler (executeBindStatement between BEGIN/END TRANSACTION at MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:899-901, 935, 1269).
- **Consequence:** The unfinalised statement keeps its memory and pins the connection; sqlite3_close_v2 in ~SqliteConnector (:25) then leaves a zombie handle (file descriptor and, inside the aborted transaction, its lock) until process exit. Error-path resource leak only; no corruption or wrong data shown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/SqliteConnector.cpp:160-187 (origin/develop 5082181714), executeBindStatement: after prepareStatement, the bind failure branch (:167-172) and the step failure branch (:177-182) both carry `// TODO this is a mem-leak (missing sqlite3_finalize())` followed by `throw Exception::IllegalArgument(...)`; `sqlite3_finalize(stmt);` is only reached on success (:186). Core's pre-fix file (core-v4.0.0-ci.2) is byte-identical to upstream.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream's own TODO comments at :171 and :181 admit the leak. The Rust report marks it source-reviewed with no C++ execution; the retained probe (oracle/sqlite-connector-s0-probe/result.log) has no bind/step-failure case. Core's SqliteConnector_test.cpp only tests a successful executeBindStatement.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): A local StatementGuard (Core SqliteConnector.cpp:35) finalises the statement in its destructor; executeBindStatement prepares into guard.stmt (:177), so both throws and the success path release it. The TODO comments were removed.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-185"></a>
### CPP-185: SQLite query helpers ignore step errors and can leak statements

**The query helpers never check sqlite3_step's return code, so a busy or failing database reads as 'table/column absent', and countTableRows throws without finalising its statement.**  
- **Trigger:** sqlite3_step returning SQLITE_BUSY, SQLITE_CORRUPT, SQLITE_IOERR etc. while querying an existing table, e.g. another connection holding BEGIN EXCLUSIVE or a damaged .oms/.pqp/.osw file; reached through OMSFileLoad (`if (!db_->tableExists(...)) return;`), TransitionPQPFile, OpenSwathPercolatorScoring, BrukerTimsImagingFile, OSWFile::readTransitions_.
- **Consequence:** tableExists/columnExists return false on an execution error, so readers such as OMSFileLoad silently skip whole sections instead of reporting the error; countTableRows throws SqlOperationFailed with a misleading message and leaks the statement (zombie connection on close).
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/SqliteConnector.cpp:83-89 countTableRows `sqlite3_step(stmt); if (sqlite3_column_type(stmt, 0) == SQLITE_NULL) { throw Exception::SqlOperationFailed(...); } ... sqlite3_finalize(stmt);` (step status ignored, throw before finalize); :103-104 columnExists `sqlite3_step(xcntstmt); while (sqlite3_column_type(xcntstmt, 0) != SQLITE_NULL)`; :123-125 tableExists `sqlite3_step(stmt); bool found = (sqlite3_column_type(stmt, 0) != SQLITE_NULL);` (origin/develop 5082181714).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed adapted C++ probe (oracle/sqlite-connector-s0-probe/result.log, cited in the Rust report): `TRACE locked_count prepare=0 step=5`, `locked_table exists=0 db_error_after_finalize=5`, `locked_column exists=0`, `locked_count count=SqlOperationFailed`, `outstanding_statements_after_locked_count=1`. Pinned connector identical to upstream develop; substitute support headers, not a full SDK build. No Core test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Partial fix. All three helpers now finalise through StatementGuard, so no statement leaks, and countTableRows throws unless step returns SQLITE_ROW (Core SqliteConnector.cpp:111). tableExists (:146 `return sqlite3_step(guard.stmt) == SQLITE_ROW;`, commented 'any status other than SQLITE_ROW means it does not') and columnExists (:128 `while (sqlite3_step(guard.stmt) == SQLITE_ROW)`) still return false on SQLITE_BUSY or other step errors, so the error-as-absence conflation remains in Core.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-195"></a>
### CPP-195: Product and precursor indexes are accidentally built on DATA

**The four indexes named for the PRODUCT and PRECURSOR tables are built on DATA, so DATA carries two redundant indexes while the PRODUCT/PRECURSOR join columns stay unindexed.**  
- **Trigger:** Every sqMass file created by MzMLSqliteHandler::createTables (SqMassFile::store, MSDataSqlConsumer). The cost shows up when reading metadata by index, e.g. OpenSwathWorkflow on sqMass input. There SpectrumAccessSqMass::getSpectrumById calls readSpectra for one index per spectrum, and each call runs a prepareSpectra_ join.
- **Consequence:** Each per-spectrum metadata query scans PRECURSOR and builds a transient automatic index on PRODUCT, so random access costs time linear in the file size and extracting a whole run costs roughly quadratic time. Writes also maintain two redundant DATA indexes. Results are unaffected, and the slowdown has not been measured.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1047-1051 `"CREATE INDEX product_chr_idx ON DATA(CHROMATOGRAM_ID);" "CREATE INDEX product_sp_idx ON DATA(SPECTRUM_ID);" "CREATE INDEX precursor_chr_idx ON DATA(CHROMATOGRAM_ID);" "CREATE INDEX precursor_sp_idx ON DATA(SPECTRUM_ID);"`. These duplicate data_chr_idx/data_sp_idx (:1036-1037). The metadata readers join `LEFT JOIN PRECURSOR ON SPECTRUM.ID = PRECURSOR.SPECTRUM_ID` and `INNER JOIN PRECURSOR ON CHROMATOGRAM.ID = PRECURSOR.CHROMATOGRAM_ID` (:628-629), and those join columns get no index.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream source quoted. A read-only sqlite3 inspection of upstream's fixture src/tests/class_tests/openms/data/SqliteMassFile_1.sqMass shows product_chr_idx, product_sp_idx, precursor_chr_idx and precursor_sp_idx all with tbl_name DATA. EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN of a prepareSpectra_-style join by SPECTRUM.ID shows `SCAN PRECURSOR LEFT-JOIN` and `SEARCH PRODUCT USING AUTOMATIC COVERING INDEX`. This confirms the index layout and the query plan, not a timed slowdown. The Rust report records a source review only, and no Core test asserts index targets.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): createIndices_ builds product_chr_idx/product_sp_idx on PRODUCT(CHROMATOGRAM_ID/SPECTRUM_ID) and precursor_chr_idx/precursor_sp_idx on PRECURSOR(CHROMATOGRAM_ID/SPECTRUM_ID), keeping the names (ci.5 cpp:1141-1145). Existing files keep their old indexes.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion, merged bb8f5b8; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-198"></a>
### CPP-198: Recreating tables on a used handler retains counters from the deleted database

**Calling createTables again on a handler that has already written data starts a new empty file but keeps the old ID counters, so the new file's spectrum and chromatogram IDs do not start at zero.**  
- **Trigger:** On one MzMLSqliteHandler (C++ or pyOpenMS): createTables, write n spectra, createTables again, write more spectra, then read by index (readSpectra({0}), SqMassFile::transform, SpectrumAccessSqMass::getSpectrumById). Upstream's own class test runs this sequence but checks only counts. TOPP writers construct a fresh handler and are not affected.
- **Consequence:** getNrSpectra counts from zero while the IDs start at n, so index-based reads of 0..count-1 throw IllegalArgument ('Illegal spectral indices'). SpectrumAccessSqMass::getSpectrumById(k) with k >= n silently returns the spectrum at position k-n instead of k. Full reads (readExperiment) still work because they map IDs by order.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:939-1023 createTables deletes the file (`std::filesystem::remove(OpenMS::to_path(filename_), ec);` :943), creates tables and indexes, and never assigns spec_id_/chrom_id_. Only the constructor sets them (`spec_id_(0),` `chrom_id_(0),` :271-272). MzMLSqliteHandler.h:223-230 says the ids are 'global for a specific database file'.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream source quoted. The Rust report (CPP-198) records a source review with no C++ run. Upstream MzMLSqliteHandler_test writeSpectra/writeExperiment sections recreate tables on the same handler and assert only getNrSpectra. No Core test checks IDs after recreation.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): After createIndices_() succeeds, createTables sets spec_id_ = 0 and chrom_id_ = 0 (ci.5 cpp:1113-1114); if creation throws, the counters are left alone. The header documents that the new file starts at id zero.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion, merged bb8f5b8; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-202"></a>
### CPP-202: Read accessors create an empty database when the input path is missing

**The sqMass read accessors open the file in read-write-create mode, so reading a missing path creates an empty database file and then fails with a misleading missing-table error.**  
- **Trigger:** Call any of the ten read accessors (readExperiment, readSpectra, getNrSpectra, readSwathWindows, readMS1Spectra, readSpectraForWindow, ...) or SqMassFile::load / SwathFile::loadSqMass with a nonexistent path in a writable directory. TOPP tools check that inputs exist first, so mainly library and pyOpenMS callers hit it.
- **Consequence:** A zero-byte file appears at the input path, and the call throws IllegalArgument 'no such table: SPECTRUM' instead of the documented SqlOperationFailed for an unopenable file. The stray file later passes existence checks. Readers also take write access to existing databases unnecessarily. No results are affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp:26, :61, :89 `SqliteConnector conn(filename_);`, and MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:282 (readExperiment), :366 (getRunID), :396 (readSpectra), :421 (readChromatograms), :440 (getNrSpectra), :457 (getSpectraIndicesbyRT), :499 (getNrChromatograms), all the same. SqliteConnector.h:60 `explicit SqliteConnector(const std::string& filename, const SqlOpenMode mode = SqlOpenMode::READWRITE_OR_CREATE);` and SqliteConnector.cpp:44-45 `case SqlOpenMode::READWRITE_OR_CREATE: flags = SQLITE_OPEN_READWRITE | SQLITE_OPEN_CREATE;`. MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.h:64-65 documents SqlOperationFailed when the file cannot be opened.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Rust report CPP-202: an adapted probe on kim compiled MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp and SqliteConnector.cpp, both identical to origin/develop (diff checked). result.log records `CASE CPP202_after_constructor exists=0`, `CASE CPP202_read IllegalArgument no such table: SPECTRUM`, `CASE CPP202_after_read exists=1 bytes=0` for readMS1Spectra. The other nine read paths share the same constructor call but were only source-reviewed.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): All ten read paths pass SqliteConnector::SqlOpenMode::READ_ONLY (ci.5 MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:312, 396, 426, 451, 470, 487, 529; MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp:39, 73, 98). A missing file now raises SqlOperationFailed and is not created, and both headers document this.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion, merged bb8f5b8; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** none specific to the handlers. SqliteConnector_test.cpp:140 checks only that a READ_ONLY connector on a missing file throws SqlOperationFailed; no test calls a handler read on a missing path.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-203"></a>
### CPP-203: SWATH accessors can return partial success after SQLite step errors

**If a SQLite step fails, the three SWATH accessors see a NULL first column and stop, so the error comes back as a normal empty or truncated list of windows or spectrum indices.**  
- **Trigger:** Read an sqMass file through SwathFile::loadSqMass or SpectrumAccessSqMass (OpenSwathWorkflow with sqMass input) while a step fails before or after the first row. Causes include SQLITE_BUSY from a concurrent writer's lock, SQLITE_CORRUPT or IOERR on a damaged file, or a runtime SQL error such as the Rust test's integer overflow in a generated MSLEVEL column.
- **Consequence:** OpenSwath silently gets missing SWATH windows, MS1 spectra or window spectra, and extracts from incomplete data with no error. The statement's saved error is also dropped, because the finalize result is ignored.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp: readSwathWindows :39-53 `sqlite3_step( stmt );` (:40, :49), `while (sqlite3_column_type( stmt, 0 ) != SQLITE_NULL)` (:42), `sqlite3_finalize(stmt);` (:53). readMS1Spectra repeats this at :70/:72/:75/:79 and readSpectraForWindow at :99/:101/:104/:108. Every step and finalize return code is discarded. The throwing helper already exists at SqliteConnector.cpp:229-257: `SqlState nextRow(...)`, which throws SqlOperationFailed on SQLITE_ERROR, BUSY, MISUSE or any other code. It is not used here. Callers: SwathFile.cpp:353-364 (loadSqMass) and SpectrumAccessSqMass.h:66.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading of upstream only. The Rust report says the step error was triggered only in the Rust test sql_step_error_after_a_valid_row_is_not_mistaken_for_end_of_results, and claims no executed C++ result. MzMLSqliteSwathHandler_test.cpp in Core (ci.5 and p0-followups) covers only successful reads. No Core test exercises a failing step.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): All three accessors now loop with `while ((state = Sql::nextRow(stmt, state)) == SQL_ROW)`, which throws on a failed step. The statement is owned by a StatementGuard (a unique_ptr with sqlite3_finalize as deleter), so no partial vector is returned. The connection opens READ_ONLY. The queries also filter `ISOLATION_TARGET IS NOT NULL` and `SPECTRUM_ID IS NOT NULL`; that filter belongs to CPP-196.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-205"></a>
### CPP-205: MSDataSqlConsumer destructor lets I/O exceptions escape noexcept destruction

**The sqMass consumer's destructor performs the final flush and RUN insert, and both can throw; because the destructor is implicitly noexcept, a write failure at that point calls std::terminate instead of raising a reportable error.**  
- **Trigger:** A SQLite write failure during destruction while records are still buffered or the RUN row is unwritten. Examples: disk full, an unwritable or locked file, or an SQL syntax error from an apostrophe in a native ID, which is spliced unescaped at MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1170/:1405. For example, the last partial batch of up to 500 chromatograms in OpenSwathWorkflow -out_chrom *.sqMass.
- **Consequence:** The process aborts with no clean TOPP error, the handler is not deleted, and the sqMass file is left incomplete.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): MSDataSqlConsumer.cpp:29-43 `MSDataSqlConsumer::~MSDataSqlConsumer() { flush(); ... if (!wrote_any_run_) { handler_->writeRunLevelInformation(peak_meta_, full_meta_); ... } delete handler_; }`, with no try/catch. flush() (:60-75) calls handler_->writeSpectra/writeChromatograms, and both open a SqliteConnector and execute statements that throw (SqliteConnector.cpp:53, MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1058-1065, :1405). The destructor is declared only as `~MSDataSqlConsumer() override;` (h:62), which is implicitly noexcept. No finalize() exists. Callers: OpenSwathWorkflow.cpp:1700 `delete chromatogramConsumer;` and OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher.cpp:163, a stack consumer.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Argument from the code and the C++ noexcept destructor rule. No test injects a write failure, and neither the Rust report nor the Core tests record a termination reproduction.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): A public, idempotent finalize() now flushes and writes run-level information, throwing on failure (h:82-91, cpp:57-69). The destructor calls finalize() inside try/catch and logs std::exception or unknown errors with OPENMS_LOG_ERROR. Callers that must handle errors call finalize() explicitly.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** none for the failure path (SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp and SqMassFile_test.cpp call finalize() on successful writes, but no write failure is injected)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-206"></a>
### CPP-206: MSDataSqlConsumer changes buffered records to the next run ID

**Records consumed under one run that are still in the buffer are written with the new run's RUN_ID after setRunId() or addRun() switches the run.**  
- **Trigger:** Through the C++ API, consume fewer than buffer_size (default 500) spectra or chromatograms under run A, call setRunId(B) or addRun(file, B), then flush or destroy. OpenSwathWorkflow does not reach it: the consumer is created per run (OpenSwathWorkflow.cpp:1635), and addRun (:1663) and setRunId (:1671) run while the buffers are empty. pyOpenMS does not bind addRun or setRunId (bind_misc.cpp:1693-1699).
- **Consequence:** In a multi-run sqMass file, buffered spectra or chromatograms are silently linked to the wrong run.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): MSDataSqlConsumer.cpp:45-53 `void MSDataSqlConsumer::addRun(...) { handler_->setRunId(run_id); MSExperiment meta; ... }` and :55-58 `void MSDataSqlConsumer::setRunId(const UInt64 run_id) { handler_->setRunId(run_id); }`, neither calling flush(). The buffers are written later by flush() (:60-75), and the handler binds its current id at insert time: MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:1167-1169 `INSERT INTO SPECTRUM(ID, RUN_ID, ...) VALUES (" << spec_id_ << "," << run_id_` and :1405 `INSERT INTO CHROMATOGRAM (ID, RUN_ID, NATIVE_ID) VALUES (" << chrom_id_ << "," << run_id_`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Argument from the consumer and handler code. No Core test switches runs with data buffered, and the Rust report marks it unconfirmed with no execution.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): addRun() and setRunId() call flush() before changing the handler's run id (cpp:73-74, 86-87). A comment explains that the buffers do not record their run, and the header documents that buffered records keep the run id they were consumed under.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="x-08"></a>
### X-08: mzXML and mzData readers reserve memory from declared peaksCount/scanCount/count; peaksCount=-1 wraps and requests about 34 GB (OutOfMemory instead of ParseError)

**The mzXML and mzData readers size allocations straight from untrusted count attributes, so a negative or huge count fails the load with an allocation error instead of a ParseError.**  
- **Trigger:** mzXML `<scan ... peaksCount="-1">`: -1 wraps to 4294967295 in UInt peak_count_, so reserve(2147483648) Peak1D, about 34 GB. Also a huge peaksCount such as 2000000000. `<msRun scanCount="-1">` asks reserve for SIZE_MAX, which throws std::length_error. mzData `<spectrumList count="-1">` reserves about 4.3e9 spectra.
- **Consequence:** On hosts that refuse the allocation, the load aborts with OutOfMemory, std::bad_alloc or std::length_error, not a ParseError naming the scan. On hosts that grant it (overcommit, as on dax), the reserve succeeds; for peaksCount=-1 the decode loop then runs on the wrapped count (the separate CPP-170 over-read). Malformed input is handled badly.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop. src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzXMLHandler.cpp:301-302: `spectrum_data_.back().peak_count_ = attributeAsInt_(attributes, s_peakscount_); spectrum_data_.back().spectrum.reserve(spectrum_data_.back().peak_count_ / 2 + 1);` with MzXMLHandler.h:120 `UInt peak_count_;`. MzXMLHandler.cpp:132-134: `Int count = 0; optionalAttributeAsInt_(count, attributes, s_count_); exp_->reserve(count);`. MzDataHandler.cpp:352-353: `UInt count = attributeAsInt_(attributes, s_count); exp_->reserve(count);`.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Core's five-platform CI run at 63e332c failed on Linux x64, Linux arm64 and Windows because MzXMLFile_test expected a ParseError for peaksCount="-1" and got OutOfMemory (docs/cpp-issues-review.md, Validation section). Commit c77ff14 records the same test failing on dax under ulimit -v 12 GB before the change and passing after. The pre-c77ff14 Core reserve lines are identical to upstream :301-302.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): c77ff14 caps the peaksCount, msRun scanCount and mzData spectrumList count reserves at 1e5 (`std::min(Size(1e5), ...)`) and rejects negative peaksCount with fatalError. The p0-followups rework keeps the caps (`reserve(std::min(Size(1e5), static_cast<Size>(std::max(peak_count, 0))))`). Scans with a negative, out-of-range or mismatched peaksCount now load their decoded pairs with a warning.
- **Commits:** c77ff14 (released in ci.3, in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5); reworked on dax/codex/p0-followups by 47b7c76 and b8ce0c0 (with d86a35a wording)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MzXMLFile_test.cpp section '(regression : SAX chunk boundaries and mismatched peak counts)' (ci.5 :650-, count loop {"2","0","-1"} at :676). On p0-followups the section starts at :709, with the negative case at :803-804. No dedicated test for the scanCount or mzData count caps.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-10"></a>
### X-10: MzMLSqliteHandler: a read that throws skips sqlite3_finalize; sqlite3_close_v2 then leaves a zombie connection holding the file open

**When a sqMass read throws, the prepared statement is never finalized, so sqlite3_close_v2 in ~SqliteConnector turns the connection into a zombie that keeps the file open for the rest of the process.**  
- **Trigger:** Any rejected read of a sqMass file through SqMassFile::load, MzMLSqliteHandler::readExperiment, readSpectra/readChromatograms or SpectrumAccessSqMass. Examples: unsupported compression code, a record without two data arrays, data for a missing container, a native-ID mismatch (including X-06's otherwise valid files), or a second RUN row.
- **Consequence:** Each failed read leaks a statement plus a SQLite connection with its file descriptor and cache until the process exits, because the statement pointer is lost and never finalized. On Windows the file stays locked and cannot be deleted or replaced. Long-running hosts (pyOpenMS, TOPPView, services) accumulate handles. This is resource exhaustion after malformed input.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp: `populateContainer_sub_<MSChromatogram>(stmt, chromatograms); sqlite3_finalize(stmt);` at :529-530 and :555-556, and the MSSpectrum equivalents at :576-577 and :602-603. populateContainer_sub_ throws before returning at :142, :147, :170, :192, :215, :235, :248 and :260. ZlibCompression::uncompressData can also throw at :163. readExperiment throws `"More than one run found"` at :305 before `sqlite3_finalize(stmt);` at :332. SqliteConnector.cpp:25 `int rc = sqlite3_close_v2(static_cast<sqlite3*>(db_));`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading, consistent with sqlite3_close_v2's documented zombie behaviour. Several Core review rounds note it (p0-walkthrough, p0-round2/3, p0-upstream). Core tests work around it by giving each variant its own temp file, but no run records an observed leak or lock.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Not fixed; backlog. The reviews suggest reusing the StatementGuard helper that already exists in Core's SqliteConnector.cpp.
- **Commits:** none; code unchanged in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups (MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:559-560, :585-586, :606-607, :632-633). Commit b3ea012 on p0-followups only adds a test comment (MzMLSqliteHandler_test.cpp:830-831).
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-11"></a>
### X-11: MascotXMLHandler: a negative <NumQueries> throws std::length_error instead of ParseError, and query numbers above INT_MAX are truncated by Xerces parseInt

**A negative <NumQueries> throws std::length_error from vector::resize instead of a ParseError, and a query number above INT_MAX is silently truncated (4294967297 reads as 1).**  
- **Trigger:** Mascot XML with `<NumQueries>-1</NumQueries>` loaded through MascotXMLFile::load (IDFileConverter, MascotAdapterOnline, pyOpenMS). Or `<query number="4294967297">` / `<peptide query="4294967297">` on LP64 platforms; on Windows strtol overflows and Xerces throws instead.
- **Consequence:** A negative count aborts with a generic std::length_error ('vector::_M_default_append') and no file or element context. A huge positive count tries to allocate billions of PeptideIdentifications. An out-of-range query number wraps to a valid small index and silently attaches hits, m/z or RT to the wrong identification. Malformed input is handled badly.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MascotXMLHandler.cpp:78-81: `if (tag_ == "NumQueries") { id_data_.resize(StringUtils::toInt32(StringUtils::trim(character_buffer_))); }`. Query numbers are read at :52 `actual_query_ = attributeAsInt_(attributes, s_queries_query_number);` and :56-57 `Int attribute_value = attributeAsInt_(attributes, s_peptide_query); peptide_identification_index_ = attribute_value - 1;`. attributeAsInt_ (XMLHandler.h:393-398) calls StringManager::parseInt, which is StringManager.cpp:109-111 `return xercesc::XMLString::parseInt(...)`: strtol followed by a cast to int. XMLFile.cpp converts only Xerces XMLException/SAXException to ParseError (:96-102, :134-136).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The Core mascot-mzidentml reviewer built a fork-per-case driver against libOpenMS at d95dbd4 (p0-followups). It recorded 'query=4294967297 loads as query 1, and a negative NumQueries throws std::length_error ('vector::_M_default_append')' (scratchpad p0-followups.json; also noted in p0-round2/round3). That was Core's reworked handler, not an upstream build, but the NumQueries resize (Core :170) and the shared StringManager::parseInt path are the same code as upstream.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Not fixed; backlog. Core now rejects query numbers <= 0 and numbers above <NumQueries>, but still calls `id_data_.resize(StringUtils::toInt32(...))` unchecked and still reads query numbers through the truncating parseInt.
- **Commits:** none; the CPP-164 reworks (5575240, ebce908, ca05044 on dax/codex/p0-followups) left both paths unchanged and disclosed them as gaps
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-15"></a>
### X-15: MzXMLHandler: a scan with two <peaks> elements after one <precursorMz> fails the whole load because both payloads are buffered into one base64 string; the schema allows repeated <peaks>

**The mzXML reader concatenates all <peaks> payloads of a scan into one base64 string, so a scan with more than one <peaks> element is decoded wrongly.**  
- **Trigger:** A scan with two <peaks> elements, for example two m/z-int payloads, or an mzXML 3.2 pair such as separate contentType arrays, where the first payload ends in '=' padding (32-bit: pair count not a multiple of 3; 64-bit: any). The 'after one <precursorMz>' qualifier matters only in Core, where CPP-174 decodes early at <precursorMz>; upstream behaves the same with or without it.
- **Consequence:** Upstream, which has no base64 validation, decodes the padding byte as data, so all later values are shifted by one byte. Depending on peaksCount, the scan silently gets garbage peaks, silently loses the second payload's peaks, or reads past the decoded vector in release builds (CPP-170). A debug build asserts. Core, with validating Base64, instead fails the whole load with 'Error during parsing of binary data'. Either way, schema-valid but uncommon input gives a wrong result.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzXMLHandler.cpp:554-561: `if (open_tags_.back() == "peaks") { ... sm_.appendASCII(chars, length, spectrum_data_.back().char_rest_); }` appends every <peaks> payload of a scan into one string. :167-196: each <peaks> start tag overwrites the scan's single precision_/compressionType_. doPopulateSpectraWithData_ :1160-1172 decodes the joined string once (`Base64::decode(spectrum_data.char_rest_, ...)`), with only `assert(data.size() == 2 * spectrum_data.peak_count_)` at :1175. Upstream Base64.h:320-345 (decodeUncompressed_) checks only `in.size() % 4`, and Base64.cpp:171-207 (stringSimdDecoder_) strips only trailing '='. A padding '=' in the middle is decoded as data rather than rejected. The schema allows repeated <peaks> (mzXML_idx_3.1.xsd:490 per the Core review).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The concatenation was observed only on Core. The mzdata-mzxml verifier's probe at aa1d071 and b8ce0c0 threw 'Error during parsing of binary data' (scratchpad p0-round3.json, not_resolved; r2v_probe_old.out). That consequence depends on Core's validating Base64 (181dadf). The silent mis-decode described for upstream follows from reading upstream MzXMLHandler and Base64 and has not been executed.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Not fixed; backlog. The reviewer left it for a separate decision because separating payloads within one scan changes the handler's buffering design.
- **Commits:** none; 6256b0a on dax/codex/p0-followups only restores decoding of a later <peaks> after Core's early decode and does not separate payloads
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

## P3 (86)

<a id="cpp-004"></a>
### CPP-004: trimLeft does nothing when every peak is below cutoff

**IsotopeDistribution::trimLeft keeps the whole distribution when no peak reaches the cutoff, although it should remove every peak, as trimRight does.**  
- **Trigger:** Call trimLeft(cutoff) with a cutoff above every intensity, e.g. peaks [0.1, 0.2] and trimLeft(0.5), from C++ or pyOpenMS (bind_chemistry.cpp:888).
- **Consequence:** All low-intensity peaks come back unchanged, and a following renormalize() rescales them into a wrong isotope pattern. In-tree callers are masked: FeatureFinderIdentificationAlgorithm.cpp:1188/1254, FeatureFinderAlgorithmMetaboIdent.cpp:595 and IsotopeDistribution::merge:244 all call trimRight at the same cutoff right after, which empties the distribution anyway. In FeatureFinderAlgorithmPicked.cpp:372-374 a user-set isotopic_pattern:intensity_percentage_optional (allowed range 0-100) above the largest isotope gives trimmed_left=0, but the pattern is then emptied, so no downstream effect is shown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop (5082181714) src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ISOTOPEDISTRIBUTION/IsotopeDistribution.cpp:210-220: `for (auto iter = distribution_.begin(); iter != distribution_.end(); ++iter) { if (iter->getIntensity() >= cutoff) { distribution_.erase(distribution_.begin(), iter); break; } }`. Nothing is erased if no peak qualifies. trimRight at 194-208 (`distribution_.resize(riter.base() - distribution_.begin())`) empties the container in the same case. The header doc (IsotopeDistribution.h:140-150) gives both functions the same contract.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed in this verification: a C++ probe linked against the pre-fix Core library core-sdk-82ce5b3 on dax. Its IsotopeDistribution.cpp is byte-identical to origin/develop (diffed). Peaks [(100,0.1),(101,0.2)] gave `trimLeft(0.5) size=2 trimRight(0.5) size=0`. The Rust log itself claims no C++ run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): trimLeft now erases up to `std::find_if(... getIntensity() >= cutoff)`, which is end() when no peak qualifies (IsotopeDistribution.cpp:212-216 at ci.5). The fix has no test.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Test:** none (the IsotopeDistribution_test.cpp trimLeft section, 191-196, is unchanged and only covers a C160 distribution where a peak reaches the cutoff)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-013"></a>
### CPP-013: Feature finding does not guard zero normalization intensity

**When the summed intensity of all input mass traces is zero, every hypothesis score is computed as 0/0, and the resulting features carry NaN overall quality.**  
- **Trigger:** FeatureFindingMetabo::run called directly (C++ or pyOpenMS) with non-empty mass traces that all have zero intensity, or whose signed intensities cancel to zero. The TOPP FeatureFinderMetabo chain (noise-thresholded MassTraceDetection, EPD, or estimateFWHM with smoothing switched off when EPD is disabled) does not produce such input.
- **Consequence:** NaN overall quality on features from zero-signal input. With all scores NaN the comparator is consistently false, so there is no crash and no feature loss, but the order in which hypotheses are accepted becomes arbitrary. No effect on real data is shown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FEATUREFINDER/FeatureFindingMetabo.cpp:764 `tmp_hypo.setScore((candidates[0]->getIntensity(use_smoothed_intensities_)) / total_intensity);`, the same division at :778 and :843 (`... * best_so_far) / total_intensity`). total_intensity is summed at :911-915 with no zero check. The score is published at :1057 `f.setOverallQuality(feat_hypos[hypo_idx].getScore());`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading of the unconditional division and the unguarded sum. The Rust report claims no C++ execution. FeatureFindingMetabo_test.cpp is unchanged in Core.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): An anonymous intensityShare_(intensity, total) returns intensity/total when total > 0, else 0.0. All three score divisions use it, so zero-signal input scores 0 instead of NaN. Hypothesis order for all-equal scores stays arbitrary.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-014"></a>
### CPP-014: Addition can reduce the cached maximum residue count

**MassDecomposition::operator+ can report a maximum residue count lower than a count it stores.**  
- **Trigger:** MassDecomposition("A1") + MassDecomposition("B10 C5"), where the right operand adds several new keys with descending counts, then getNumberOfMaxAA() or operator==(string).
- **Consequence:** getNumberOfMaxAA() returns 5 for a decomposition that holds B10, and += returns 10 for the same inputs. The value is a bookkeeping cache. No library or TOPP code calls operator+ or getNumberOfMaxAA (MetaProSIP.cpp:32 only includes the header), and pyOpenMS binds getNumberOfMaxAA (bind_chemistry.cpp:965) but not operator+. No downstream effect is shown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/MASSDECOMPOSITION/MassDecomposition.cpp:183-189, operator+: `d.decomp_.insert(*it); if (it->second > number_of_max_aa_) { d.number_of_max_aa_ = it->second; }`. It compares against the left operand's maximum but writes the result's. operator+= (75-81) compares against its own running maximum. operator==(string) (105) also compares number_of_max_aa_.
