- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed in this verification: a C++ probe against the pre-fix Core library core-sdk-82ce5b3, whose MassDecomposition.cpp is byte-identical to origin/develop, printed `(A1 + B10 C5).max=5 toString=A1 B10 C5 ; += max=10`.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): operator+ now compares against d.number_of_max_aa_ (MassDecomposition.cpp:188 at ci.5), the same as operator+=. The fix has no test.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Test:** none (MassDecomposition_test.cpp operator+ section, 115-133, is unchanged and uses ascending counts that never trigger the bug)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-018"></a>
### CPP-018: Centroid inspection leaves reader options changed after failure

**If MzMLFile::getCentroidInfo throws (missing or malformed file), the reader object keeps FillData(true) instead of the caller's setting.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ API only: set options FillData(false) on an MzMLFile, call getCentroidInfo on a missing or malformed mzML, catch the exception, then reuse the same object for another load. The only in-tree caller, SearchEngineBase::getRawfileName (SearchEngineBase.cpp:41-43), uses a fresh local MzMLFile; not bound in pyOpenMS.
- **Consequence:** Later loads on the reused reader decode full peak data where the caller asked for none: extra memory and time, no wrong values.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzMLFile.cpp:235-236 `bool oldoption = options_.getFillData(); options_.setFillData(true);`, :268 `transform(filename, &c, true, true); // no first pass`, :270-271 `// restore old state options_.setFillData(oldoption);` with no try/catch; transform (:178-190) calls safeParse_, which rethrows any BaseException as ParseError (:113-128).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The restore statement sits only on the normal return path after a call that can throw; no Core test calls getCentroidInfo (git grep in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups src/tests finds none), and the Rust log claims no C++ run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): transform is wrapped in try/catch(...) that restores FillData to the saved value before rethrowing.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-019"></a>
### CPP-019: Declared processing count includes records that are not written

**The mzML writer declares a dataProcessingList count that includes every float data array, but it writes a dataProcessing element only for arrays with their own history, so the declared count is higher than the number of elements actually written.**  
- **Trigger:** Store any mzML (MzMLFile::store, MzMLHandler writeTo, and so most TOPP tools and pyOpenMS) with spectra carrying float data arrays without their own processing history, for example numpress or ion-mobility arrays, or PeakPicker FWHM/meta arrays. This input is common.
- **Consequence:** The count attribute contradicts the file, for example count="787" over one element. The XSD does not enforce the equality, and the OpenMS reader ignores this count, so no data or result changes. It is a conformance and interoperability detail.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:5160-5168 counts every float data array unconditionally (`for (Size m = 0; m < exp[s].getFloatDataArrays().size(); ++m) { ++num_bi_dps; }`, :5166). :5170 then writes `<dataProcessingList count="` << (std::max)((Size)1, dps.size() + num_bi_dps). An array record is emitted only under :5192 `if (!exp[s].getFloatDataArrays()[m].getDataProcessing().empty())`. Upstream's own reference src/tests/topp/PeakPickerIterative_1_output.mzML:59 has `<dataProcessingList count="787">` over a single `<dataProcessing id="dp_sp_0">`, and FileFilter_49_output.mzML:43 has count="51" over one element.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The defective output appears in C++-written reference files on upstream develop: PeakPickerIterative_1_output.mzML:59 (count 787, one dataProcessing element), FileFilter_49_output.mzML:43 (count 51, one element) and PeakPickerHiRes_output.mzML:79 (count 3, one element). docs/cpp-issues-review.md records that the installed TOPP regression suite failed 13 tests against the corrected writer at ci.3 because these references held the old counts. test-data commit 43b02b1 updated 14 of them, citing 'count="787" over a single dataProcessing element'.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The header writer builds an array_processing list with one entry per float, integer or string array (spectrum or chromatogram) that has a non-empty history. It writes exactly those records and declares dps.size() + array_processing.size().
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (ci.3: count only arrays with a non-empty history), superseded by 63e332c (ci.3: count derived from the emitted array_processing registry). References were updated in test-data 43b02b1 for ci.4. Present at core-v4.0.0-ci.5 MzMLHandler.cpp:5226-5228.
- **Test:** No Core class test asserts the count. It is covered by the installed TOPP regression comparisons against references updated in test-data 43b02b1, e.g. TOPP_FileFilter_49_out (test-data/topp/CMakeLists.txt:859) and TOPP_PeakPickerIterative_1_out1 (:2568). MSDataWritingConsumer_test 'regression: stored spectrum and chromatogram array histories are distinct' (63e332c) covers the emitted records but not the count.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-022"></a>
### CPP-022: Legacy binary xref parsing removes the wrong prefix length

**For the legacy `xref_analog: binary-data-type:` spelling the OBO parser strips a fixed 22 characters, so it stores "a-type:MS:1000523" instead of "MS:1000523" in CVTerm::xref_binary.**  
- **Trigger:** ControlledVocabulary::loadFromOBO on an OBO file containing `xref_analog: binary-data-type: MS:1000523`, i.e. a legacy or custom PSI-MS OBO. Reach: CVInspector -cv_files, C++ and pyOpenMS. The bundled psi-ms.obo uses `xref: binary-data-type` (37 lines) and no file in the upstream tree uses the xref_analog form.
- **Consequence:** A corrupted allowed-binary-type accession. MzMLValidator.cpp:138 would reject valid binary arrays, and CVInspector prints the wrong accession. Nothing is affected with bundled vocabularies.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/ControlledVocabulary.cpp:465 `else if (StringUtils::hasPrefix(line_wo_spaces, "xref:binary-data-type") || StringUtils::hasPrefix(line_wo_spaces, "xref_analog:binary-data-type"))`, then :474-475 `//trim prefix line_wo_spaces = StringUtils::substr(line_wo_spaces, 22);`. 22 is the length of "xref:binary-data-type:" only.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The fixed substr offset against the longer prefix is visible in the source. No C++ run and no Core test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The parser picks the prefix that actually matched ("xref_analog:binary-data-type:" or "xref:binary-data-type:") and strips its length.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; ControlledVocabulary.cpp:494-497 at ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-023"></a>
### CPP-023: Vocabulary printing splits output between two streams

**Printing a ControlledVocabulary to a stream sends the is_a parent lines to std::cout instead of the requested stream.**  
- **Trigger:** `std::ostringstream ss; ss << cv;` (operator declared at ControlledVocabulary.h:30/255) for any vocabulary with parent terms.
- **Consequence:** The requested stream lacks every is_a line, and those lines appear on the process console instead. Diagnostic output only; the earlier review found no library or TOPP caller.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/ControlledVocabulary.cpp:616-629 `std::ostream& operator<<(std::ostream& os, const ControlledVocabulary& cv)`: term, id and name lines go to `os`, but :623-626 `for (const auto & parent_term : it.second.parents) { cout << "is_a: '" << parent_term << "'\n"; }`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — `cout` instead of `os` is visible in the source. No run and no Core test for operator<<.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The is_a lines are written to `os`.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; ControlledVocabulary.cpp:647 at ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-024"></a>
### CPP-024: CV parameter rendering does not escape every XML attribute

**CVTerm::toXMLString escapes name and value but concatenates the accession, cvRef and unitAccession raw, so XML metacharacters in them yield malformed XML.**  
- **Trigger:** Calling either toXMLString overload with a ref such as "MS&custom", or with a CVTerm whose id or unit holds &, < or a quote. pyOpenMS exposes CVTerm.id read-write and toXMLString. In-tree writers (MzIdentMLHandler) pass cv_.name() and accessions from bundled OBO files, which contain no metacharacters.
- **Consequence:** The emitted cvParam is not well-formed XML. No output from real data is affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/ControlledVocabulary.cpp:110 and :122 `std::string s = "<cvParam accession=\"" + id + "\" cvRef=\"" + ref + "\" name=\"" + Internal::XMLHandler::writeXMLEscape(name);` (id and ref unescaped), and :129-130 `std::string un = *(this->units.begin()); s += "\" unitAccession=\"" + un + ...` (unit unescaped). Only name and value are escaped.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The unescaped concatenation is visible in the source. The existing ControlledVocabulary_test toXMLString sections use plain accessions only. No run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): accession, cvRef and unitAccession go through writeXMLEscape. The same commit also writes the value's own unit (from DataValue::getUnit/getUnitType) instead of the term's first allowed unit, which was a separate defect, and omits the unit when its ontology is unknown.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; ControlledVocabulary.cpp:111-112, :125-126 at ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-025"></a>
### CPP-025: A later processing method can omit its required action term

**Once any processing method in a dataProcessing block has an action, every later method with no recognised action is written without the fallback data-transformation term, so that method violates the mzML mapping's MUST rule.**  
- **Trigger:** Write mzML where one processing history contains a DataProcessing with actions followed by one with an empty action set. This happens, for example, when reading ProteoWizard output whose filter step has only userParams and writing it again with any TOPP tool. Reachable from every mzML writer (MzMLFile::store, PlainMSDataWritingConsumer, pyOpenMS).
- **Consequence:** The later processingMethod has no MS:1000452-derived cvParam, so semantic validation against ms-mapping.xml fails. Round-tripping through OpenMS keeps the empty action set, so no data or scientific result changes.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:3848 `bool written = false;` is declared once, before `for (Size i = 0; i < dps.size(); ++i)` at :3849. Every action branch sets `written = true;`. The fallback at :3939-3942 is `if (!written) { os << ... accession="MS:1000543" name="data processing action" ... }`. share/OpenMS/MAPPING/ms-mapping.xml:99 is rule processingmethod_must (MUST, scope processingMethod, MS:1000452 data transformation). Observed in upstream data: src/tests/topp/THIRDPARTY/Chy_test_in.mzML, written by OpenMS 3.4.0-pre FileFilter. Its :80 processingMethod pm_1 has `peak picking`, and :84-88 pm_2 holds only userParams (`num masses in window`) with no action term.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The Rust report and the earlier classification are code reading only. In this review, a read-only scan of the mzML test data on upstream develop found 6 OpenMS-written files that show the defective output. Their writer is identified by softwareRef ids so_dp_sp_0_pm_N and '+hh:mm' completion times. Example: THIRDPARTY/Chy_test_in.mzML:80-89, where a method without an action follows 'peak picking' and has no MS:1000543. Others are SiriusExport_1_input.mzML and AssayGeneratorMetabo_input.mzML. No Core test or report records a failure run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): `bool written = false;` moved inside the per-method loop, with the comment 'per method: the fallback action below depends on this method alone'.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (core-v4.0.0-ci.3). The per-method flag survived the 23944b6 revert and is present at core-v4.0.0-ci.5 MzMLHandler.cpp:3900-3901 and on dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-026"></a>
### CPP-026: Processing step order is always written as zero

**Every processingMethod in a multi-step processing history is written with order="0", so the output carries no ordering keys for its consecutive steps.**  
- **Trigger:** Any mzML written with more than one processingMethod in a dataProcessing element. That covers nearly every TOPP tool output, because the tool appends its own step to the history it read.
- **Consequence:** External consumers that order steps by the attribute cannot recover the sequence. The XSD permits duplicate values, and the OpenMS reader uses document order, so the output validates and OpenMS round-trips it correctly. No demonstrated effect on data.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:3852 inside the per-method loop: `os << "\t\t\t<processingMethod order=\"0\" softwareRef=\"so_" << id << "_pm_" << i << "\">\n";` (and :3842 for the forced placeholder). share/OpenMS/SCHEMAS/mzML_1_10.xsd:460-462: order is a required xs:nonNegativeInteger, 'This attributes allows a series of consecutive steps to be placed in the correct order.' Upstream reference src/tests/topp/FileFilter_49_output.mzML:45 and :49 show two consecutive steps in dp_sp_0, both `order="0"`.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The output is observed in C++-written references. docs/cpp-issues-review.md and commit 23944b6 record that writing the index at ci.3 failed 89 installed TOPP regression tests, because 210 of 267 reference mzML files (252 per the review table) record 0 for every step. Upstream develop FileFilter_49_output.mzML:45/49 shows it directly. No external consumer failure is shown.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (reverted): Open in Core. order is back to 0 with an in-code note at ci.5 MzMLHandler.cpp:3902-3904: 'order stays 0 for every method (CPP-026, open) ... writing the index belongs with a coordinated update of those references.' The reference outputs need a coordinated update before the fix can return.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 wrote the index (core-v4.0.0-ci.3). 23944b6 reverted it for core-v4.0.0-ci.4 (still reverted at ci.5 and on dax/codex/p0-followups).
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-027"></a>
### CPP-027: mzML writing discards processing completion seconds

**The mzML writer stores a processing step's completion time only to the minute, so its seconds are silently lost and do not come back on reload.**  
- **Trigger:** Write any DataProcessing with a valid completion time that has non-zero seconds to mzML. Every TOPP tool records DateTime::now() (TOPPBase.cpp:558), so almost every mzML output is affected.
- **Consequence:** Provenance timestamps lose their seconds, so a round trip changes DataProcessing::getCompletionTime. The value is also not a valid xsd:dateTime. No peak data or scientific result is affected. The effect is limited to metadata precision.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandler.cpp:3947 `value=\"" << dps[i]->getCompletionTime().toString("yyyy-MM-dd+hh:mm")`. DateTime.cpp:157-161 formats only `%04d-%02d-%02d+%02d:%02d` (no seconds). The reader at MzMLHandler.cpp:3246-3248 calls `setCompletionTime(asDateTime_(value))`, and DateTime.cpp:287-290 parses the legacy '+' form with five fields, so seconds come back as 00. psi-ms.obo:5315 declares MS:1000747 `has_value_type xsd:dateTime`. The upstream header DataProcessing.h:111-114 documents no precision limit. Upstream reference FileFilter_49_output.mzML:51 contains `value="1999-12-31+23:59"`, while TOPPBase.cpp:547 sets `1999-12-31 23:59:59` in test mode.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The Rust report records an executed CLI-2 run against the Core SDK at 4fdec46 (tagged ci.1-ci.3, same writer line). SpectraFilterWindowMower wrote the test-mode time 1999-12-31 23:59:59 as '1999-12-31+23:59'. The retained output at oracle/topp-cli-lifecycle/cli2/window_mower_1/cwd/SpectraFilterWindowMower_1.tmp.mzML:119 shows it. Upstream develop references show the same thing, e.g. FileFilter_49_output.mzML:51.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): The writer is unchanged (ci.5 MzMLHandler.cpp:4001 still uses "yyyy-MM-dd+hh:mm"). DataProcessing.h:112-113 gained '@note mzML records this value to the minute, so a round trip through that format drops the seconds.'
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (core-v4.0.0-ci.3, retained in ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-036"></a>
### CPP-036: XML compression sniffing reads uninitialized short-file bytes

**For an empty or one-byte input file the compression sniffer copies uninitialized stack bytes into the magic-prefix string and branches on them.**  
- **Trigger:** Any XMLFile::parse_-based loader (mzML, featureXML, consensusXML, idXML, TraML, mzIdentML, the semantic validators) given a 0-byte or 1-byte file.
- **Consequence:** Technically undefined behaviour (indeterminate char values read by the std::string constructor and the comparisons); MSan or Valgrind would report it. There is no out-of-bounds access: tmp_bz[2] is always NUL and bz[1] is read only after bz[0] matched, so index 1 is at most size(). Stray bytes can only choose between CompressedInputSource and LocalFileInputSource, and both end in a parse error for such a file, at worst with a misleading decompression message.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/XMLFile.cpp:144-148 `std::ifstream file(filename.c_str()); char tmp_bz[3]; file.read(tmp_bz, 2); tmp_bz[2] = '\0'; bz =std::string(tmp_bz);` and :160 `if ((bz[0] == 'B' && bz[1] == 'Z') || (bz[0] == g1 && bz[1] == g2) || (bz[0] == 'P' && bz[1] == 'K'))`. The short read is never checked (no gcount).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream lines read at origin/develop. The Rust report: 'No C++ execution or sanitizer result is claimed.' No Core XMLFile test for short files: XMLFile_test.cpp is unchanged in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..dax/codex/p0-followups (the File_test.cpp change in 181dadf covers File::localPath).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The buffer is zero-initialized, bz is built from only file.gcount() bytes, and the magic-prefix test is guarded by bz.size() == 2, so files shorter than two bytes always take the plain LocalFileInputSource path.
- **Commits:** 181dadf [FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation (first released in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-037"></a>
### CPP-037: ProForma combination discards a formula's charge contribution

**When the diff formulas of the modifications on one residue add up to a net charge, combineOnOneResidue_ checks the charged mass for agreement but interns the charge-free formula text, so the combined modification loses charge x 1.00728 Da.**  
- **Trigger:** ProForma::toAASequence (or the mass and spectrum paths built on it) on a residue that carries two or more resolved modifications whose ResidueModification diff formulas have a nonzero summed charge and a declared diff mass equal to that charged weight. ProForma text cannot produce this: charged Formula tags are rejected at :1628/:1639. Unimod compositions and PSI-MOD DiffFormula strings are neutral, and OpenMS's own definition-record writer drops charge (ResidueModification.cpp:845 uses toString()). It takes hand-built ResidueModification objects or a hand-edited definition record: ResidueModification.cpp:889 parses the diff formula with EmpiricalFormula, which accepts a charge suffix, and ModificationDefinitionIO::registerFrom (ModificationDefinitionIO.cpp:191) registers it without a charge check. That record route is not traced end to end.
- **Consequence:** The combined modification's mass is short by charge * PROTON_MASS_U, and no warning is logged, because the agreement check passed. This is a silent mass error, but only for modification records that nothing OpenMS ships or writes produces.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/CHEMISTRY/ProForma.cpp:1736-1740 `if (all_have_formulas && !formula_sum.isEmpty() && std::fabs(formula_sum.getMonoWeight() - mass_sum) <= 1e-3) { FormulaTag ft; ft.formula_string = formula_sum.toString(); return resolveFormulaTag_(ft, residue, ResidueModification::ANYWHERE); }`. The check counts charge: EmpiricalFormula.cpp:49 `double weight = Constants::PROTON_MASS_U * charge_;`. The re-interned text does not carry it: EmpiricalFormula.cpp:258-266, toString() emits only element symbols and counts. resolveFormulaTag_ then re-parses the neutral text and passes the `ef.getCharge() != 0` check at :1639. Caller: ProForma.cpp:2322.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading only. The Rust report gives an API-constructed trigger (records with charges +1 and 0 and matching masses) and claims no C++ execution. No Core test covers a charged formula sum: ProFormaParser_test sections added in b2079fb/86f01c4 test charged Formula tags only at parse level (e.g. :1788, :2479-2503).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): combineOnOneResidue_ computes formula_agrees and takes the formula route only when `formula_agrees && formula_sum.getCharge() == 0` (ci.5 ProForma.cpp:1736-1741). A charged sum falls through to the mass-only modification, and the disagreement warning is limited to `all_have_formulas && !formula_agrees` (:1748).
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3, contained in ci.5; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-044"></a>
### CPP-044: Unmapped CV lookup depends on earlier validation calls

**locateTerm on a path with no mapping rule throws std::out_of_range on a fresh validator but returns false once an earlier validate() has visited that element, because validation inserts empty rule lists into the mapping index.**  
- **Trigger:** Construct a SemanticValidator, call locateTerm for an unmapped path (e.g. /r/cvParam/@accession) -> std::out_of_range; validate a document containing <r/>; the same locateTerm call now returns false. C++ or pyOpenMS on the same object. Validation alone also grows rules_ by one empty entry per distinct element path.
- **Consequence:** History-dependent API contract (exception vs false) and a mapping index that grows across validated documents. No wrong validation verdict; the throwing half is what made CPP-028 surface as std::out_of_range during mzML writing.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/VALIDATORS/SemanticValidator.cpp:538 (locateTerm, const) `const vector<CVMappingRule>& rules = rules_.at(path);` throws std::out_of_range for an unmapped path; :147 (onEndElement) `vector<CVMappingRule>& rules = rules_[path];` and :277 (handleTerm_) `vector<CVMappingRule>& rules = rules_[path];` insert an empty entry for every element path seen. rules_ is otherwise built only from mapping rules (:37-40). Both validate and locateTerm are bound in pyOpenMS (bind_format.cpp:2891, :2904).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream lines read at origin/develop. The Rust report says 'No C++ execution is claimed'. No Core test calls locateTerm on an unmapped path before and after validate(); SemanticValidator_test.cpp is unchanged in ci.2..p0-followups. The MzMLFile_test change in 1beb468 covers the CPP-028 software path, not this call-order behaviour.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): All three lookups are now non-inserting find() calls, and locateTerm returns false for an unmapped path, so the answer no longer depends on earlier validate() calls and the rule index stays read-only.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 [FIX] Address the first tranche of the Rust port's C++ findings (locateTerm uses rules_.find and returns false) and 181dadf [FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation (onEndElement and handleTerm_ use rules_.find with a local empty vector); both first released in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-048"></a>
### CPP-048: Zero centroid-inspection limit depends on the first spectrum type

**getCentroidInfo(file, 0) wraps its unsigned counter to SIZE_MAX and scans the whole file if the first spectrum is centroid or profile, but stops after one spectrum if the first is unknown.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ call MzMLFile::getCentroidInfo(filename, 0) on any mzML. The only in-tree caller (SearchEngineBase.cpp:43) uses the default 10; not exposed in pyOpenMS.
- **Consequence:** A zero limit gives either a full-file scan (time) or a one-spectrum result depending on the first spectrum's type; no memory-safety or scientific effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzMLFile.cpp:238 `Size first_n_spectra_only_remaining = first_n_spectra_only;`, :246 and :250 `--first_n_spectra_only_remaining;` in the CENTROID/PROFILE cases (UNKNOWN at :252-254 does not decrement), :261 `if (first_n_spectra_only_remaining == 0)` ends parsing; zero is not checked before parsing. Header MzMLFile.h:183 documents only 'Only inspect this many spectra (UNKNOWN spectra do not count)'.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Control flow plus `typedef size_t Size` establishes defined unsigned wraparound. Rust log calls it source evidence, not an executed reproduction; no Core test references getCentroidInfo.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): getCentroidInfo returns an empty map immediately when first_n_spectra_only == 0, before touching options or parsing.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-049"></a>
### CPP-049: Indexed mzML output writes a constant placeholder checksum

**Every indexed mzML that OpenMS writes contains the literal checksum "0" instead of the SHA-1 digest the indexed mzML schema describes.**  
- **Trigger:** Any indexed mzML output: MzMLFile::store with the default write-index option, MSDataWritingConsumer, any TOPP tool or pyOpenMS writing mzML.
- **Consequence:** Consumers cannot verify file integrity from the footer, and a checksum-validating tool would reject or flag the files. The schema type does not enforce the digest, OpenMS never reads the value, and no failure in another tool has been shown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:129-132 in writeFooter_: `// TODO calculate checksum here:` `// SHA-1 checksum from beginning of file to end of 'fileChecksum' open tag.` `std::string sha1_checksum = "0";` `os << sha1_checksum << "</fileChecksum>\n";`. share/OpenMS/SCHEMAS/mzML_idx_1_10.xsd:1185-1187 declares fileChecksum as xs:string, 'SHA-1 checksum from beginning of file to end of 'fileChecksum' open tag.' No OpenMS reader checks it (MzMLHandler.cpp:815 only collects the characters). On upstream develop, 171 files under src/tests/topp carry `<fileChecksum>0</fileChecksum>`, e.g. FileFilter_49_output.mzML:3816.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The writer line is a hard-coded constant. Its output is observed in 171 C++-written reference mzML files on upstream develop that contain <fileChecksum>0</fileChecksum>, found by read-only git grep in this review; 6 files with real digests come from other tools. The Rust report claims no C++ execution. The Core review notes a real digest would change 194 Core reference documents.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Left open. A real SHA-1 would change 194 reference documents whose FuzzyDiff whitelist does not cover fileChecksum, so it needs its own change that updates those outputs.
- **Commits:** none (still `std::string sha1_checksum = "0";` at core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups MzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:132)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-050"></a>
### CPP-050: Empty indexed mzML declares zero indices but emits a dummy index

**Writing an empty experiment as indexed mzML declares indexList count="0" but then writes one fabricated 'dummy' index with offset -1.**  
- **Trigger:** Store an MSExperiment with no spectra and no chromatograms while indexing is on (the default). For example, a TOPP filter whose output ends up empty, as in TOPP_FileFilter_52. This is valid but uncommon.
- **Consequence:** The declared count contradicts the one index element that follows. OpenMS's own indexed decoder rejects the 'dummy' name either way, so on-disc access to the empty file fails whatever the count says. Ordinary loading is unaffected, and there is nothing to index. The effect is conformance only.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:91 `int indexlists = (int) !spectra_offsets.empty() + (int) !chromatograms_offsets.empty();`, :97 `os << "<indexList count=\"" << indexlists << "\">\n";`, then :116-123 `if (indexlists == 0) { ... os << "\t<index name=\"dummy\">\n"; os << "\t\t<offset idRef=\"dummy\">-1</offset>\n"; ...}`. mzML_idx_1_10.xsd:1119-1129: index is required (default minOccurs 1), and count is 'Number of indices in this list.' IndexedMzMLDecoder.cpp:317-332 accepts only 'spectrum' or 'chromatogram' and otherwise returns -1. Upstream reference src/tests/topp/FileFilter_52_output.mzML:48-51 shows `<indexList count="0">` followed by the dummy index.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The output is observed in upstream's C++-written reference FileFilter_52_output.mzML:48 (count="0" plus a dummy index). docs/cpp-issues-review.md records one installed TOPP regression failure against the corrected count at ci.3. test-data 43b02b1 changed that reference from `<indexList count="0">` to count="1".
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): MzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:98 writes `(indexlists == 0 ? 1 : indexlists)` with the comment that the count includes the dummy index. The dummy index is kept because the schema requires at least one index and one offset.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3, kept through ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups). References were updated in test-data 43b02b1.
- **Test:** TOPP_FileFilter_52_out1 (test-data/topp/CMakeLists.txt:871) compares against FileFilter_52_output.mzML as updated in test-data 43b02b1. The MzMLFile_test empty-map section checks only for the indexedmzML wrapper, not the count.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-051"></a>
### CPP-051: mzML semantic validation reuses parameter groups from earlier documents

**A reused MzMLValidator keeps referenceableParamGroup definitions from earlier documents, so a later document's dangling group reference can be satisfied, and repeated group IDs accumulate terms from both files.**  
- **Trigger:** Direct C++ reuse of one OpenMS::Internal::MzMLValidator for two mzML files: the second references a group it does not define (terms of the first file's group are applied), or both define the same group id (e.g. a common 'CommonInstrumentParams'), which appends the second file's terms to the first's. No shipped caller reuses it: MzMLFile::isSemanticallyValid (MzMLFile.cpp:89) and MzMLHandler.cpp:4767 build fresh validators, MSDataWritingConsumer.cpp:30 uses its validator only for term lookup, and pyOpenMS binds only the SemanticValidator base class.
- **Consequence:** Wrong semantic-validation verdict for the later file: an invalid file can pass (missing required term supplied by the stale group), or a valid file can get a false 'number of term repeats' error when a doubled group term is not repeatable.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/VALIDATORS/MzMLValidator.cpp:37-40 `if (!open_tags_.empty()) { parent_tag = open_tags_.back(); }` has no reset branch; :50 `const std::vector<CVTerm> & terms = param_groups_[attributeAsString_(attributes, "ref")];` and :83 `param_groups_[current_id_].push_back(parsed_term);`. Members at MzMLValidator.h:66-74 (param_groups_, current_id_, binary_data_array_, binary_data_type_). The inherited SemanticValidator.cpp:96-98 validate() clears only errors_ and warnings_.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream member lifetime and callbacks read at origin/develop. The Rust report says the two-document scenario 'is derived from that control flow; no executed C++ reproduction or full SDK build is claimed'. MzMLValidator_test.cpp is unchanged in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..dax/codex/p0-followups. Graded P3 because only an Internal:: C++ class reused by hand reaches it; the effect on a verdict would otherwise be a P2 misleading diagnostic.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): MzMLValidator::onStartElement gains an else branch: when open_tags_ is empty (document element), it clears param_groups_, current_id_, binary_data_array_ and binary_data_type_. This relies on the CPP-040 fix clearing open_tags_ in validate(), so it also holds after an aborted parse.
- **Commits:** 181dadf [FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation (first released in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-057"></a>
### CPP-057: Spectrum equality ignores ion-mobility format and peak type

**Two spectra or spectrum settings that differ only in ion-mobility format or IM peak type (profile vs centroided) compare equal.**  
- **Trigger:** Copy a SpectrumSettings or MSSpectrum, call setIMPeakType(IM_PROFILE) on one and IM_CENTROIDED on the other (or change setIMFormat), then compare with == in C++ or pyOpenMS __eq__.
- **Consequence:** Equality-based checks, such as round-trip test comparisons and MSExperiment equality, cannot see a lost or changed IM representation. No production consumer that makes a wrong decision from it was found.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/METADATA/SpectrumSettings.cpp:23-39 `return MetaInfoInterface::operator==(rhs) && type_ == rhs.type_ && native_id_ == rhs.native_id_ && comment_ == rhs.comment_ && instrument_settings_ == ... && data_processing_ ...` with no im_type_ or im_peak_type_ (declared at SpectrumSettings.h:170-171 `IMFormat im_type_ = IMFormat::UNKNOWN; IMPeakType im_peak_type_ = IMPeakType::UNKNOWN;`). MSSpectrum.cpp:514-529 delegates to `SpectrumSettings::operator==(rhs)` and does not compare them either. The std::hash (SpectrumSettings.h:192ff) also omits them, so hash and equality agree.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The comparison list is quoted in full. The Rust report says source review only. In Core, SpectrumSettings_test has no range change, and MSSpectrum_test's only change (b6dd412, isSortedByIM precondition) does not test equality. The existing setIMPeakType sections (MSSpectrum_test.cpp:1480ff) do not compare spectra.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): SpectrumSettings::operator== now also compares im_type_ and im_peak_type_, and std::hash<SpectrumSettings> hashes getIMFormat() and getIMPeakType() to keep hash and equality consistent (SpectrumSettings.cpp/.h).
- **Commits:** 181dadf ([FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation), in core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-062"></a>
### CPP-062: SpectrumRangeManager::byMSLevel(0) can only throw

**byMSLevel's default argument 0 names the global ranges, but those live in the base object and never get a map entry, so calling byMSLevel() with no argument always throws.**  
- **Trigger:** Any C++ caller that uses the default, e.g. `exp.spectrumRanges().byMSLevel()` on a populated manager.
- **Consequence:** A clean Exception::InvalidValue instead of the global ranges. No wrong data. Every in-tree caller (FeatureFinderAlgorithmPicked.cpp:242-245, MultiplexClustering.cpp:83-86, FileInfo.cpp:247/470/599, ImageCreator.cpp:273-286) passes an explicit level. The pyOpenMS binding (bind_kernel.cpp:3205) requires ms_level, so the default is only a misleading C++ API trap.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop 5082181714 src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/SpectrumRangeManager.h:94 `const BaseType& byMSLevel(UInt ms_level = 0) const` looks the level up only in ms_level_ranges_ (:96) and otherwise throws at :100 `throw Exception::InvalidValue(... "No ranges for this MS level" ...)`. extend/extendRT/extendMZ/extendUnsafe (:84, :126, :137, :148) all do `ms_level == 0 ? BaseType::extend...(...) : ms_level_ranges_[ms_level]...`, so key 0 is never inserted. The upstream class test SpectrumRangeManager_test.cpp:109-112 expects it: `// byMSLevel(0) therefore has no entry and throws` / `TEST_EXCEPTION(Exception::InvalidValue, srm.byMSLevel(0))`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — From the header source. The upstream class test at :112 confirms the throw for an explicit 0, which is the default value, but it treats the throw as intended rather than recording a failure. The Rust report claims no C++ run. The Core change only removes the default, so no test fails on the old code.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): SpectrumRangeManager.h:99 is now `const BaseType& byMSLevel(UInt ms_level) const`, with no default. The doc says level 0 never has an entry, that the global ranges live in the BaseType subobject, and that the level is mandatory. Calling with 0 still throws, as before.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (first tagged in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Test:** none (SpectrumRangeManager_test.cpp:112 still asserts byMSLevel(0) throws, and its section title still spells the old `ms_level = 0` signature; nothing checks that the default is gone)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-063"></a>
### CPP-063: MSExperiment::updateRanges registers no per-level entry for MS level 0

**Spectra at MS level 0 get no per-level range entry, so spectrumRanges().getMSLevels() omits 0 while MSExperiment::getMSLevels() reports it, and spectrumRanges().byMSLevel(0), which is also the default argument, always throws.**  
- **Trigger:** An MSExperiment containing a spectrum with MS level 0, for example PDA/absorption scans loaded by ThermoRawFile (ThermoRawFile.cpp:602), followed by updateRanges() and per-level range queries. An unannotated spectrum defaults to level 1 (MSSpectrum.h:752 `UInt ms_level_ = 1;`), so level 0 has to be set explicitly.
- **Consequence:** The per-level range reports silently leave out level 0: FileInfo.cpp:243-247 and :467-470 print ranges for spectrumRanges().getMSLevels() only, and extractRangesExp_ at :597-599 fills per_ms_level the same way. The two level lists disagree for the same run. The level-0 data are still in the global spectrum ranges, and no processing result changes. This follows from SpectrumRangeManager's design of using 0 as the key for the global ranges, so it is an inconsistency rather than a computational error.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSExperiment.cpp:695 `spectrum_ranges_.extendUnsafe(*it);` then :698 `spectrum_ranges_.extendUnsafe(*it, it->getMSLevel());`. src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/SpectrumRangeManager.h:148 `ms_level == 0 ? BaseType::extendUnsafe(spectrum.getRange()) : ms_level_ranges_[ms_level].extendUnsafe(...)`, so level 0 extends the global ranges again and creates no per-level entry. getMSLevels() at :108-116 lists only ms_level_ranges_ keys, and byMSLevel(UInt ms_level = 0) at :94-100 throws 'No ranges for this MS level' for 0. MSExperiment::getMSLevels() at MSExperiment.cpp:753-764 inserts every spectrum's level, 0 included. Level 0 does occur: ThermoRawFile.cpp:602 `spectrum.setMSLevel(0);` for PDA absorption scans.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream code read at 5082181714. The Rust report says source review, and there is no Core test with a level-0 spectrum. MSExperiment_test.cpp is unchanged in ci.2..p0-followups, and git grep finds no setMSLevel(0) in those tests.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): Behaviour is unchanged. MSExperiment.h:1094 at ci.5 adds an @note to updateRanges() saying a level-0 spectrum extends only the global ranges, getMSLevels() omits 0 and byMSLevel(0) throws. getMSLevels() is documented as including 0. MSExperiment.cpp gets a comment at the extendUnsafe call. SpectrumRangeManager.h byMSLevel is documented as 'must not be 0' and loses its `= 0` default, a small API change.
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (codex/cpp-review-completion; also on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-064"></a>
### CPP-064: RangeBase accepts NaN and infinity and breaks its own emptiness invariant

**RangeBase stores NaN (and infinite) bounds unchecked, so after setMin(NaN) a range is neither empty nor valid and contains() is false for every value.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ or pyOpenMS code passing NaN or +-inf to RangeBase/RangeRT/RangeMZ/RangeIntensity/RangeMobility setMin/setMax, the value constructors, extendLeftRight, minSpanIfSingular, scaleBy or shift. No in-tree TOPP path found passing a non-finite bound (IMDataConverter.cpp:37's NaN FAIMS placeholder does not reach a range setter, per the earlier caller audit).
- **Consequence:** isEmpty() and min<=max are both false, getMin() returns NaN instead of throwing, and contains/clampTo/pushInto give meaningless answers; infinite bounds make center()/getSpan() infinite and scaleBy produce NaN. extend(NaN) is a harmless no-op, because std::min/std::max keep the current bound. No downstream effect is demonstrated.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/RangeManager.h:43 `RangeBase(const double single): min_(single), max_(single)`, :49-51 `RangeBase(const double min, const double max): min_(min), max_(max) { if (min_ > max_) throw ... }` (NaN passes), :113-117 `void setMin(const double min) { min_ = min; if (max_ < min) max_ = min; }`, :120-124 setMax likewise, :88-91 `isEmpty() { return min_ > max_; }`, :94-97 contains; :166-171 extendLeftRight, :178-181 minSpanIfSingular, :235-242 scaleBy, :246-251 shift accept non-finite arguments. Bound in pyOpenMS bind_kernel.cpp:2775-2776 (RangeBase.setMin/setMax) and the per-dimension classes.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read RangeManager.h at origin/develop. The Core review ledger reports a standalone clang++ mock of the logic printing 'isEmpty=0 valid=0 contains(0)=0', but that is a mock rather than the real header, and no Rust-report probe exists. No RangeManager_test section passes NaN or infinity (ci.2..ci.5 changed only a pointer initialiser in 9b56872).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): New RangeBase::requireFinite_ throws Exception::InvalidRange for non-finite values in both value constructors, setMin/setMax, extendLeftRight, minSpanIfSingular, scaleBy and shift; extend() is left unchecked and documented as ignoring NaN.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-065"></a>
### CPP-065: RangeUtils energy and isolation predicates contradict their own notes for MS1 spectra

**The notes on IsInCollisionEnergyRange, IsInIsolationWindowSizeRange and IsInIsolationWindow say MS1 spectra (and spectra without a collision energy) give true, but the code returns false regardless of reverse.**  
- **Trigger:** A developer relying on the documented behaviour when using these predicates, e.g. with remove_if/erase_if.
- **Consequence:** Documentation only. The in-tree callers (FileFilter.cpp:900, :986, :991) want the coded behaviour, which keeps MS1 spectra and spectra without a collision energy, so no wrong filtering results.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/RangeUtils.h:517 `For spectra that do not have a collision energy, the predicate will return true.`, :518 `This predicate will return always true for spectra with getMSLevel() = 1.`, but :542 `if (s.getMSLevel() == 1) return false;` and :557 `if (!hasCollisionEnergy) return false;` (both before `if (reverse_)` at :559); :570 note 'return always true' vs :595 `return false`; :615 note vs :640 `return false`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Compared the notes with the operator() bodies in upstream RangeUtils.h:515-660 and checked the callers via git grep. Upstream RangeUtils_test.cpp covers only MS2 spectra.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): Only documentation changed: the three @note blocks now say the predicates return false for MS1 spectra (and for spectra without a collision energy), that reverse does not invert this early return, and that remove_if() therefore keeps them; the misleading inline comment was reworded. Code is unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-067"></a>
### CPP-067: BinnedSpectrum::getBinIntensity mutates the spectrum it reads

**Reading an absent bin with getBinIntensity inserts a stored zero, so a read-only query changes nonZeros() and makes a spectrum compare unequal to an identical copy.**  
- **Trigger:** Construct a BinnedSpectrum, copy it, call getBinIntensity(mz) on one copy for an m/z whose bin is empty, then compare with ==. Reachable from pyOpenMS (bind_kernel.cpp:218 getBinIntensity) and the C++ API. The only in-tree caller is OpenNuXL.cpp:3239, which is compiled only under DEBUG_OpenNuXL.
- **Consequence:** operator==/!= and nonZeros() give wrong answers after a read. Each miss also inserts an entry into the sparse vector. Similarity scores (sparse dot products) are unaffected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/BinnedSpectrum.h:114 'float getBinIntensity(double mz);' (non-const); src/openms/source/KERNEL/BinnedSpectrum.cpp:191-194 'return bins_->coeffRef(getBinIndex(mz));'; operator== :139 'if (bins_->nonZeros() != rhs.bins_->nonZeros()) return false;'. Eigen SparseVector::coeffRef inserts an explicit entry for an absent index (atWithInsertion), and nonZeros() counts stored entries.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading plus the Eigen coeffRef/atWithInsertion semantics, which were checked in the installed Eigen headers per the ledger. No test reads an absent bin and then checks equality. The Core BinnedSpectrum() test added in 181dadf checks only a default-constructed spectrum, where nonZeros() stays 0.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): getBinIntensity is const (BinnedSpectrum.h ci.5 :113) and uses bins_->coeff() (cpp ci.5 :201-217). It returns 0 for empty bins, no bin layout, non-positive or non-finite m/z, and out-of-range indices. The default constructor now allocates bins.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** none (BinnedSpectrum_test.cpp BinnedSpectrum() section, 181dadf, covers the default-constructed/zero-bin-size path only)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-068"></a>
### CPP-068: BinnedSpectrum::operator== ignores the bin offset

**BinnedSpectrum::operator== ignores offset_, so two spectra on different bin grids compare equal when their bin indices and intensities coincide.**  
- **Trigger:** Two BinnedSpectrum objects with the same bin size, unit, spread and precursors but different offsets, where the peaks land on the same indices: e.g. both from empty spectra, or bin_size 1.0 with a peak at m/z 100.3 and offsets 0.0 and 0.4 (both index 100).
- **Consequence:** operator== returns true although bin i covers a different m/z interval, which is inconsistent with isCompatible(). No production code uses BinnedSpectrum::operator== (only pyOpenMS ==/!= at bind_kernel.cpp:216-217), so there is no demonstrated downstream effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/BinnedSpectrum.cpp:128-129 'if (std::tie(unit_ppm_, bin_size_, bin_spread_, precursors_) != std::tie(rhs.unit_ppm_, rhs.bin_size_, rhs.bin_spread_, rhs.precursors_))' omits offset_. isCompatible :163-164 compares 'std::tie(a.unit_ppm_, a.bin_size_, a.offset_)', and getBinIndex :187 bins with 'floor(mz / bin_size_ + offset_)'.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The omission is directly visible in the develop source. The existing operator== test compares only a copy, which shares its offset, and no test compares spectra that differ only in offset.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): offset_ is added to both std::tie tuples in operator== (BinnedSpectrum.cpp ci.5 :136-139), with a comment that the offset is part of the bin layout, consistent with isCompatible().
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-070"></a>
### CPP-070: FeatureHandle::asMutable casts away constness of a possibly const object

**asMutable() is a const method that const_casts *this, so writing through its result on a FeatureHandle that is itself a const object is undefined behaviour with no compile-time protection.**  
- **Trigger:** Only when a genuinely const FeatureHandle object calls asMutable() and a setter, e.g. 'const FeatureHandle h(...); h.asMutable().setRT(1);'. All in-tree uses (ConsensusMapNormalizerAlgorithmMedian.cpp:134/149, ...Quantile.cpp:169, ...Threshold.cpp:103, MapAlignmentAlgorithmPoseClustering.cpp:81, MapAlignmentTransformer.cpp:167) modify std::set elements, which are non-const objects reached through const iterators; that use is well defined and is the intended one. The upstream test (FeatureHandle_test.cpp:139-140) uses a const reference to a non-const handle.
- **Consequence:** No observed or in-tree effect. It is a deliberate const escape hatch: UB is reachable only through API misuse, and the header carries a stale TODO asking for a const_cast that the code already uses.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/FeatureHandle.h:148-153 'inline FeatureHandle::FeatureHandleMutable_& FeatureHandle::asMutable() const { ... // TODO use const_cast\n return static_cast<FeatureHandleMutable_&>(const_cast<FeatureHandle&>(*this)); }'. Doc :70-84 states it exists to modify handles stored in a ConsensusFeature's std::set.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Language-rule argument from the header. No execution. The Rust report is source review only, and the item was unranked in the earlier classification.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): The stale TODO is replaced by a comment explaining the const_cast (std::set exposes const iterators) and noting that FeatureHandleMutable_ hides only setMapIndex/setUniqueId. An @note says writing through the result on a genuinely const FeatureHandle is UB. The cast itself is unchanged. The commit also fixes an IndexLess test typo (lhs->rhs setUniqueId) and adds IndexLess cases, which are unrelated to the cast.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** none for the cast (FeatureHandle_test.cpp change in 181dadf covers IndexLess only)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-071"></a>
### CPP-071: DRange default constructor contradicts its documentation

**DRange's default constructor is documented as producing an all-zero range but actually produces the empty sentinel (min = +max, max = -max).**  
- **Trigger:** A C++ or pyOpenMS caller who default-constructs DRange and relies on the documented all-zero coordinates.
- **Consequence:** Documentation contradicts behaviour; a caller trusting the comment would get an empty range with extreme bounds instead of zeros. No upstream caller shown to be affected; DRange_test.cpp:46-49 only checks the pointer is non-null.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/DATASTRUCTURES/DRange.h:69-77 `@brief Default constructor. Creates a range with all coordinates zero.` then `DRange() : Base() {}`; DIntervalBase.h:48-57 `Creates an empty interval with corners at infinity.` `min_(PositionType::maxPositive()), max_(PositionType::minNegative())`. The all-zero range is DIntervalBase<D>::zero (DIntervalBase.h:259-260,364-365).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read upstream DRange.h:67-98 and DIntervalBase.h:41-90,259-260,364-365, and DRange_test.cpp:46-49. The Rust report records source review only; no execution. Not in ranked.json/cpp-findings.json.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): Constructor doc rewritten (DRange.h 69-75 at ci.5): creates the empty sentinel of the base class, isEmpty() is true; use DRange<D>::zero for an all-zero range. Behaviour unchanged.
- **Commits:** Core 181dadf '[FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation' (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-073"></a>
### CPP-073: DRange::extend comment contradicts the collapse it performs

**The @param text of DRange::extend says an inverted min/max is not fixed, but the body collapses such a dimension to its centre point, and the class test asserts that collapse.**  
- **Trigger:** Reading the @param documentation of DRange<D>::extend with a negative addition large enough to invert a dimension.
- **Consequence:** Stale, self-contradictory documentation. The behaviour is correct and tested, so there is no functional effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/DATASTRUCTURES/DRange.h:310 '@param[in] addition Additive for each dimension (can be negative). Resulting invalid min/max are not fixed automatically!' contradicts both :305 '...may result in min==max (but never min>max)' and the body at :318-321 `if (min_[i] > max_[i]) min_[i] = max_[i] = (min_[i] + max_[i]) / 2;`. The upstream class test DRange_test.cpp:427-432 asserts the collapse (`r.extend({-200.0, 0.0});` then min == max == 1.0).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Header source reading. The existing upstream test DRange_test.cpp:427-432 pins the collapse the comment denies. The Rust report claims no C++ execution.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): The @param text was rewritten (ci.5 DRange.h:317-318): a dimension that would end up inverted is collapsed to its centre point, so min <= max always holds afterwards.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3, in ci.5)
- **Test:** none new. The existing DRange_test.cpp extend section already asserts the collapse.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-074"></a>
### CPP-074: DIntervalBase default constructor documented as infinite corners

**The DIntervalBase default constructor is documented as placing the corners at infinity, but it uses the finite numeric_limits extrema, so code that tests the corners for infinity never matches.**  
- **Trigger:** A developer relies on the header documentation and checks a default-constructed interval or range with std::isinf or against infinity() instead of isEmpty().
- **Consequence:** Documentation mismatch only; library behaviour is unaffected. No in-tree code is known to depend on the documented infinity.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/DATASTRUCTURES/DIntervalBase.h:51 'Creates an empty interval with corners at infinity.' while :53-55 `DIntervalBase() : min_(PositionType::maxPositive()), max_(PositionType::minNegative())`. DPosition.h:332-340: minNegative() is `std::numeric_limits<CoordinateType>::lowest()`, maxPositive() is max(), both finite. The same header family has a related stale doc: DRange.h:72 'Creates a range with all coordinates zero.' for `DRange() : Base()`, which actually yields the empty sentinel.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Header source reading. The Rust report claims no C++ execution.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): Rewrote the constructor doc (DIntervalBase.h 48-54): minPosition() is maxPositive() and maxPosition() is minNegative(), the finite max()/lowest() extrema, not infinity. The same commit also corrected DRange's default-constructor doc to 'Creates an empty range', pointing to DRange<D>::zero for all-zero coordinates.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.3, in ci.5)
- **Test:** none (documentation only)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-075"></a>
### CPP-075: Class tests with unreachable or duplicated assertions

**Three class tests are wrong: the IndexLess test sets lhs's unique id twice and never tests the equal-map-index branch, StandardTypes_test repeats two typedefs instead of covering Chromatogram, and a BinnedSpectrum section title omits the unit_ppm parameter.**  
- **Trigger:** Running or reading the FeatureHandle, StandardTypes and BinnedSpectrum class tests; nothing in product use.
- **Consequence:** Coverage gaps and a wrong section title only; the product code behind them (IndexLess, the Chromatogram typedef, the BinnedSpectrum constructor) is correct. No assertion is actually unreachable despite the title.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/FeatureHandle_test.cpp:187-190 `lhs.setMapIndex(2); lhs.setUniqueId(77); rhs.setMapIndex(4); lhs.setUniqueId(29);` (rhs id never set, equal-map-index branch of IndexLess at FeatureHandle.h:158-160 never exercised); StandardTypes_test.cpp:48-51 `GOOD_TYPEDEF(PeakSpectrum) GOOD_TYPEDEF(PeakMap) GOOD_TYPEDEF(PeakSpectrum) GOOD_TYPEDEF(PeakMap)` while StandardTypes.h:42 `typedef MSChromatogram Chromatogram;` is never covered; BinnedSpectrum_test.cpp:47 `START_SECTION((BinnedSpectrum(const PeakSpectrum & ps, float size, UInt spread, float offset)))` vs BinnedSpectrum.h:96 `BinnedSpectrum(const PeakSpectrum& ps, float size, bool unit_ppm, UInt spread, float offset);`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Plain from reading the test sources at origin/develop. The corrected tests would pass on pre-fix product code, so no failure run exists or is possible.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): FeatureHandle_test sets rhs.setUniqueId(29) and adds equal-map-index and irreflexivity cases; StandardTypes_test replaces the duplicated pair with GOOD_TYPEDEF(Chromatogram); the BinnedSpectrum section title now includes bool unit_ppm.
- **Commits:** 181dadf in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (FeatureHandle_test.cpp, StandardTypes_test.cpp, BinnedSpectrum_test.cpp)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/FeatureHandle_test.cpp section [FeatureHandle::IndexLess] operator(); StandardTypes_test.cpp GOOD_TYPEDEF(Chromatogram); BinnedSpectrum_test.cpp constructor section
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-076"></a>
### CPP-076: rasterizeIMFrame clears the caller's image before the check that can throw

**rasterizeIMFrame zeroes the caller's output buffer before checking that the ion-mobility array has one entry per peak, so a call that then throws Precondition has already wiped the caller's image.**  
- **Trigger:** A non-empty spectrum whose IM float data array length differs from its peak count, passed to C++ MSSpectrum::rasterizeIMFrame or pyOpenMS bind_spectrum.cpp:591-612.
- **Consequence:** A weak exception guarantee: the call still fails cleanly with Precondition, but a caller that catches it and keeps displaying the buffer shows a blank frame. There is no crash, no wrong result and no data loss beyond the caller's scratch image, and zero-initialization at the start is documented behaviour.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.cpp:895-898 `const Size total_pixels = im_bins * mz_bins; std::fill(output, output + total_pixels, 0.0f);`, :901-904 `if (this->empty()) return;`, then :907-911 `if (im_data.size() != this->size()) { throw Exception::Precondition(...`. The throw comes after the caller's buffer has been zeroed. MSSpectrum.h:705 documents 'The output buffer is zero-initialized at the start of this method.'
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream code read at 5082181714. The Rust report says source review. MSSpectrum_rasterizeIMFrame_test.cpp is unchanged in ci.2..dax/codex/p0-followups and has no mismatched-IM-array case, so neither the defect nor the fix is tested.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): MSSpectrum.cpp:948 at ci.5 runs `if (!this->empty() && im_data.size() != this->size()) throw Exception::Precondition` right after getIMData(). The overflow and finite-range checks follow, and only then std::fill and the early return for an empty spectrum, so every throw happens before the buffer is touched.
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (codex/cpp-review-completion; also on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-079"></a>
### CPP-079: sortByPositionPresorted handles an incomplete chunk list differently in its two branches

**sortByPositionPresorted never validates its chunk list, so a list that stops short of size() (or a single partial chunk marked sorted) leaves peaks unsorted, and whether it does depends on whether data arrays are attached.**  
- **Trigger:** A C++ caller passing a hand-built std::vector<Chunk> that does not tile the spectrum, e.g. built before the last peaks were appended, with at least one float/string/integer data array attached (or a single partial chunk flagged is_sorted in either branch).
- **Consequence:** The spectrum is silently left partly unsorted in the data-array branch but fully sorted in the no-array branch. No in-tree path builds such a list (Chunks::add always starts at the previous end and TSG adds a chunk after every block). A related, unclaimed case, a chunk ending past size() or a spectrum that shrank after Chunks::add, would hand past-the-end iterators to stable_sort/inplace_merge (memory safety via API misuse); Core's fix closes that too.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSSpectrum.cpp:399 `if (chunks.empty()) return;`, :401 `if (chunks.size() == 1 && chunks[0].is_sorted) { return; }`, :405-408 no data arrays -> `std::stable_sort(ContainerType::begin(), ContainerType::end(), PeakType::PositionLess());` (chunks ignored), :417-437 data arrays -> sorts/merges only `[chunks[first].start, chunks[last].end)`, :440 `selectUnchecked(select_indices)`. No check that chunks tile [0, size()). Producer MSSpectrum.h:80-83 `Chunks::add` always tiles; only caller TheoreticalSpectrumGenerator.cpp:222; not bound in pyOpenMS.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read MSSpectrum.cpp:397-442 and MSSpectrum.h:58-91 at origin/develop; grep shows the only production caller is TheoreticalSpectrumGenerator.cpp:222, and every block there ends with chunks.add. Upstream MSSpectrum_test.cpp:876-907 builds tiling chunks only. The Rust report is source review; no Core test passes a non-tiling list (MSSpectrum_test sortByPositionPresorted section unchanged ci.2..ci.5). API misuse with no demonstrated downstream effect: P3.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): sortByPositionPresorted throws Exception::Precondition unless the chunks start at 0, end at size(), are non-reversed and contiguous; the single-chunk shortcut also requires isSorted(); Chunks::add throws if the spectrum shrank below the previous chunk end.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-085"></a>
### CPP-085: setSumSimilarUnion has external linkage at global namespace scope

**The file-local merge helper ::setSumSimilarUnion has external linkage, so a global function with the same signature elsewhere in the program would be an ODR violation that the linker does not report.**  
- **Trigger:** Link another object file into the same program (for example a static build or a plugin in the same link unit) that defines a global `OpenMS::MSChromatogram::Iterator setSumSimilarUnion(Iterator, Iterator, Iterator, Iterator, Iterator)`. No such definition exists in the tree: git grep finds the name only in MSChromatogram.cpp.
- **Consequence:** Latent only: which definition mergePeaks calls would be unspecified if a clash existed. The report's claim that the symbol is exported from the shared libOpenMS does not hold for default builds, because the visibility preset is hidden. No observable effect today.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSChromatogram.cpp:17 `using namespace OpenMS;` and :477 `OpenMS::MSChromatogram::Iterator setSumSimilarUnion(OpenMS::MSChromatogram::Iterator first1, ...` at global namespace scope, with no static and no anonymous namespace. Its only use is :552 in mergePeaks. CMakeLists.txt:366-367 sets CMAKE_CXX_VISIBILITY_PRESET to "hidden" and cmake/add_library_macros.cmake:129 sets CXX_VISIBILITY_PRESET hidden on the library target.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — A linkage hygiene issue established by reading the code at origin/develop. There is no reproduction, no Core test and no C++ run in the Rust report.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The helper is now `static`, with a comment explaining why internal linkage is required, and its input ranges are ConstIterator (ci.5 MSChromatogram.cpp:482) so mergePeaks can take a const reference.
- **Commits:** core 181dadf on codex/cpp-review-completion; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4 and ci.5
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-086"></a>
### CPP-086: mergePeaks takes a non-const reference to a chromatogram it only reads

**mergePeaks takes a non-const reference to the source chromatogram and documents it as [in,out], although it only reads it, so a caller holding a const chromatogram cannot call it without copying.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ code such as `void f(const MSExperiment& e, MSChromatogram& a) { a.mergePeaks(e.getChromatograms()[0]); }`, which does not compile. There are no library, TOPP or pyOpenMS callers at develop; git grep finds only the class test MSChromatogram_test.cpp:1250.
- **Consequence:** Only at compile time: callers must copy or const_cast. Results are never wrong, nothing crashes, no data is lost.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/MSChromatogram.h:479 `void mergePeaks(MSChromatogram& other, bool add_meta=false);` with :476 `@param[in,out] other`; body src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSChromatogram.cpp:548-564 only reads `other` (:552 `setSumSimilarUnion(begin(), end(), other.begin(), other.end(), temp.begin())`, :562 `ls.push_back(other.getMZ())`). The helper at :477 takes non-const `MSChromatogram::Iterator`, which is why the parameter could not simply be made const.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream code read at origin/develop 5082181714. Nobody compiled the const call: the Rust report says source review only, and ranked.json says no test or probe. The fix commit changes the signature but adds no test; MSChromatogram_test.cpp is unchanged in ci.2..dax/codex/p0-followups. Nearby, and not part of this item: upstream mergePeaks also leaves the float/string/integer data arrays and the cached ranges stale. The same Core commit fixed that too.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): MSChromatogram.h:484 at ci.5 is `void mergePeaks(const MSChromatogram& other, bool add_meta=false);` with `@param[in] other ... it is only read`. MSChromatogram.cpp:557 matches it. setSumSimilarUnion now takes ConstIterator inputs and is `static`.
- **Commits:** 181dadf ([FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation), in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (branch codex/cpp-review-completion, also on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-087"></a>
### CPP-087: The chromatogram stream operator prints an always-empty settings block

**Streaming a chromatogram (or its settings) prints a settings section that only ever contains its two delimiter lines, with no native ID, type, precursor, product or meta values.**  
- **Trigger:** Any operator<< on an MSChromatogram or ChromatogramSettings, e.g. `std::cout << chrom` on a chromatogram with a native ID and product m/z.
- **Consequence:** Debug output suggests the chromatogram has no settings. No computed result or written file is affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/METADATA/ChromatogramSettings.cpp:149-154 at origin/develop 5082181714: `std::ostream & operator<<(std::ostream & os, const ChromatogramSettings & /*spec*/) { os << "-- CHROMATOGRAMSETTINGS BEGIN --" << std::endl; os << "-- CHROMATOGRAMSETTINGS END --" << std::endl; return os; }`, streamed from src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSChromatogram.cpp:19-24 `os << static_cast<const ChromatogramSettings&>(chrom);`. The sibling SpectrumSettings.cpp:185-190 is just as empty, so this is a house pattern.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The whole function body is quoted above, so the behaviour is certain from the source. The Rust report says it was found by source review with no C++ run. No Core test checks the new output: ChromatogramSettings_test and MSChromatogram_test do not change in ci.2..ci.5 or on dax/codex/p0-followups, and no test greps for the printed fields.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The parameter is now named, and the block prints native ID, chromatogram type (via ChromatogramNames), precursor m/z, product m/z and each meta value between the unchanged delimiters (ChromatogramSettings.cpp:149-168).
- **Commits:** 181dadf ([FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation), in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; no later rework on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-088"></a>
### CPP-088: updateRanges warns that ranges were already up to date on its very first call

**In debug builds, updateRanges() on a chromatogram with no cached range logs a spurious 'ranges were already up-to-date' warning whenever the new extrema are all 0, because an empty range and a range of 0 both read as 0.**  
- **Trigger:** A Debug (OPENMS_ASSERTIONS) build and `MSChromatogram c; c.updateRanges();`, or a first updateRanges() on a chromatogram whose peaks are all at RT 0 with intensity 0. The title's 'very first call' overstates it: a first call on an ordinary non-zero chromatogram does not warn. The same pattern sits in MSExperiment::updateRanges (MSExperiment.cpp:667-739), but there it can fire only on an experiment whose spectra and chromatograms are all empty, because an entirely empty experiment returns early at :682-685.
- **Consequence:** A misleading log line in debug builds only. The ranges are computed correctly, and release builds are unaffected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSChromatogram.cpp:517-546 (the whole function is inside #ifdef OPENMS_ASSERTIONS). :520 `double rt_min = RangeRT::isEmpty() ? 0 : getMinRT();` (the other three are computed the same way), recomputed the same way after clearRanges(), then :540-543 `if (rt_min_new == rt_min && rt_max_new == rt_max && int_min_new == int_min && int_max_new == int_max) { OPENMS_LOG_WARN << "Update ranges was called but ranges were already up-to-date"`. Nothing checks whether a range was empty. OPENMS_ASSERTIONS is on only when CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE is Debug (src/openms/CMakeLists.txt:26-30), or under !NDEBUG on MSVC.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream code read at 5082181714. Nobody ran it: the Rust report says source review, and no Core test asserts that the warning is absent. MSChromatogram_test.cpp is unchanged in ci.2..p0-followups. Core fixed only the MSChromatogram instance, not the MSExperiment::updateRanges one.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): MSChromatogram.cpp:528 at ci.5 captures `const bool had_ranges = !RangeRT::isEmpty() && !RangeIntensity::isEmpty();` before clearRanges(), with a comment explaining why. :549 warns only if `had_ranges && ...` the numeric extrema are unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (codex/cpp-review-completion; also on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-093"></a>
### CPP-093: ConsensusFeature::Ratio default constructor leaves ratio_value_ indeterminate

**A default-constructed ConsensusFeature::Ratio (or one created by vector resize) has an indeterminate ratio_value_, which is garbage if read or stored.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ code that default-constructs a Ratio, or grows a ratio vector with resize(), without setting ratio_value_, then reads it or stores the ConsensusMap to .oms. No in-tree producer of ratios exists: addRatio/setRatios are called only from pyOpenMS lambdas (bind_kernel.cpp:3826-3828), and the Ratio type itself is not registered there. The OMS loader's resize path fills a local copy that is discarded.
- **Consequence:** A read of an indeterminate double (undefined behaviour in C++23; in practice a garbage value) that OMSFileStore would write into FEAT_ConsensusRatio. No demonstrated in-tree effect. Tracing it exposed separate real defects: ratios stored under the last handle's id and dropped on load.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/ConsensusFeature.h:94-96 'Ratio() { }', :100-102 'virtual ~Ratio() { }', :107 'double ratio_value_;' (no initialiser). OMSFileStore.cpp:1563 'query_ratio->bind(":ratio_value", ratio.ratio_value_);' writes whatever is stored. OMSFileLoad.cpp:1036 'consensus.push_back(feature);' comes before :1044-1049 'feature.getRatios().resize(ratio_index + 1); ... ratio.ratio_value_ = ...', so the loader's resize only affects a discarded local copy.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading only. Upstream OMSFile_test sets every ratio_value_ explicitly, and so does the Core round-trip test added in 181dadf. No test reads a default-constructed Ratio.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): ConsensusFeature.h (ci.5 :94-106): 'Ratio() = default;', non-virtual defaulted destructor, and 'double ratio_value_ = 0.0;'.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5); the same commit also fixes the related OMS store/load ratio defects (OMSFileStore.cpp binds parent_id; OMSFileLoad.cpp push_back after the ratio loop)
- **Test:** none for the default value (OMSFile_test.cpp store/load ConsensusMap sections, 181dadf, cover the separate ratio round-trip defect and set every ratio_value_)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-094"></a>
### CPP-094: ConsensusFeature::setRatios takes a non-const lvalue reference for a copy-in setter

**The setter only copies its argument but takes a non-const lvalue reference, so a const vector or a temporary cannot be passed.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ code calling cf.setRatios(buildRatios()) or passing a const std::vector<Ratio>&; both fail to compile.
- **Consequence:** Compile-time inconvenience only; nothing misbehaves at runtime. The one upstream caller (pyOpenMS lambda bind_kernel.cpp:3827) passes a non-const lvalue and works.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/ConsensusFeature.h:265 'void setRatios(std::vector<Ratio>& rs);' and src/openms/source/KERNEL/ConsensusFeature.cpp:321-324 'void ConsensusFeature::setRatios(std::vector<ConsensusFeature::Ratio>& rs) { ratios_ = rs; }'
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Compile-time property read from the declaration and definition. The Rust report is source review only; OMSFile_test.cpp:183 passes a named non-const vector, which compiles with either signature, so no test tells them apart.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): ConsensusFeature.h:264 and ConsensusFeature.cpp:321 now take 'const std::vector<Ratio>&'. The same commit also defaults Ratio::ratio_value_ to 0.0 and makes ~Ratio non-virtual.
- **Commits:** 181dadf [FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-095"></a>
### CPP-095: getAnnotationState reports MULTIPLE_SAME when only one identification actually has hits

**A feature with one identification that has hits plus one or more hit-less identifications is reported as FEATURE_ID_MULTIPLE_SAME ('multiple IDs (identical)') although only one ID exists. This contradicts the function's own treatment of hit-less identifications as no ID.**  
- **Trigger:** getAnnotationState() in legacy-ID mode (id_matches_ empty) on peptides_ = {identification with hits, identification without hits}, e.g. ids.resize(2); ids[0].setHits({hit}). Hit-less identifications can come from featureXML.
- **Consequence:** The feature is miscategorised. In-tree effects: FeatureMap::getAnnotationStatistics counts (logged by IDMapper.cpp:1020) and pyOpenMS users. ProteinQuantifier.cpp:978 and ConsensusMapArrowExport.cpp:1403 test only for DIVERGENT and are unaffected. The effect is cosmetic or statistical.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/BaseFeature.cpp:154-171 'if (peptides_.size() == 1 && !peptides_[0].getHits().empty()) return FEATURE_ID_SINGLE; ... if (!peptides_[i].getHits().empty()) { ... seqs.insert(...) } ... if (seqs.size() == 1) return AnnotationState::FEATURE_ID_MULTIPLE_SAME;'. Names at :16-17 are 'single ID' and 'multiple IDs (identical)'. An all-hit-less list falls through to FEATURE_ID_NONE (:177).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading only. The upstream BaseFeature_test getAnnotationState section never combines a hit-bearing identification with a hit-less one. The Rust port keeps the behaviour and asserts it in its own test, which is not a C++ observation.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): No logic change. A code comment at BaseFeature.cpp (ci.5 :190-194) says the state counts attached identifications rather than sequences, so more than one attached identification yields MULTIPLE_*. The behaviour was kept because tools branch on the existing states.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (comment only; core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-101"></a>
### CPP-101: getLibraryIntensity clamps entries the caller already had in the output vector

**getLibraryIntensity() sets to zero any negative value the caller already had in the output vector, not just the values it appended.**  
- **Trigger:** `std::vector<double> r{-5.0}; group.getLibraryIntensity(r);` sets r[0] to 0.0. This needs a direct C++ caller passing a non-empty vector.
- **Consequence:** The caller's pre-existing negative entries are silently changed to zero. No in-tree caller passes a non-empty vector: MRMFeatureAccessOpenMS.h:194 passes the vector through and has no production user. The pyOpenMS binding (bind_kernel.cpp:361, :418) takes the vector by value and discards the result. So no tool or script output is affected.
