- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/MRMTransitionGroup.h:321-324 appends to the caller's vector, then :325 `for (Size i = 0; i < result.size(); i++)` with :328-330 `if (result[i] < 0.0) { result[i] = 0.0; }` clamps the whole vector, including entries that were already there.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 181dadf extended the section `void getLibraryIntensity(std::vector<double> & result) const` in MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp: a vector pre-filled with -5.0 must keep TEST_REAL_SIMILAR(prefilled[0], -5.0). On the upstream code that entry becomes 0. No failing run on pre-fix code is recorded.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The function saves result.size() before appending (`const Size appended_from = result.size();`, ci.5 MRMTransitionGroup.h:331) and clamps only from that index. A comment says why.
- **Commits:** core 181dadf on codex/cpp-review-completion; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4 and ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp, section `void getLibraryIntensity(std::vector<double> & result) const`
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-103"></a>
### CPP-103: subsetDependent indexes chromatogram_map_.at() without the guard subset uses

**subsetDependent() on a selected transition with no chromatogram fails with a bare std::out_of_range from map::at, which gives no native ID, file or function.**  
- **Trigger:** A group with addTransition(t, "t1") (t.getNativeID() == "t1") and no chromatogram under "t1", then subsetDependent({"t1"}). The in-tree caller MRMFeatureFinderScoring.cpp:345-346 receives groups built with a transition and its chromatogram together (:1435-1436), so only hand-built, inconsistent groups reach this.
- **Consequence:** Invalid input is rejected, but with an unhelpful message: an STL out_of_range instead of an OpenMS exception naming the missing native ID.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/KERNEL/MRMTransitionGroup.h:396-397 `transition_group_subset.addTransition(*tr_it, tr_it->getNativeID()); transition_group_subset.addChromatogram(chromatograms_[chromatogram_map_.at(tr_it->getNativeID())], tr_it->getNativeID());` has no hasChromatogram guard. subset() guards the same step at :348 `if (this->hasChromatogram(tr.getNativeID()))`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 181dadf rewrote the section `MRMTransitionGroup subsetDependent(std::vector<std::string> tr_ids)` in MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp. It includes `TEST_EXCEPTION(Exception::ElementNotFound, mrmtrgroup_nochrom.subsetDependent(single_id))` on a group with a transition and no chromatogram. On the upstream code std::out_of_range is thrown and this check fails. No failing run on pre-fix code is recorded.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): subsetDependent() now checks hasChromatogram() first and throws Exception::ElementNotFound(__FILE__, __LINE__, OPENMS_PRETTY_FUNCTION, nativeID) (ci.5 MRMTransitionGroup.h:414-419). Core chose a clear error over subset()'s silent skip, because a skip would leave transitions and chromatograms out of step and fail later without context.
- **Commits:** core 181dadf on codex/cpp-review-completion; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4 and ci.5
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp, section `MRMTransitionGroup subsetDependent(std::vector<std::string> tr_ids)`
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-104"></a>
### CPP-104: The subsetDependent class-test section tests subset, leaving subsetDependent with no coverage

**The subsetDependent class-test section calls subset instead, so subsetDependent (used by MRMFeatureFinderScoring for identifying transitions) has no class-test coverage.**  
- **Trigger:** Running MRMTransitionGroup_test; the section reports a pass without ever calling subsetDependent.
- **Consequence:** Test coverage gap only: subsetDependent's whole-feature copy, its handling of precursor chromatograms and its unguarded chromatogram lookup are untested. No product misbehaviour follows from the test defect itself.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp:328 `START_SECTION ( MRMTransitionGroup subsetDependent(std::vector<std::string> tr_ids))` but :346 `mrmtrgroupsub = mrmtrgroup.subset(transition_ids);` on a group with no chromatograms or features. The real subsetDependent (MRMTransitionGroup.h:387-407) copies features whole and calls `chromatogram_map_.at(tr_it->getNativeID())` unguarded at :397; it is used in production by MRMFeatureFinderScoring.cpp:345-346.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Visible from the test source at origin/develop (line 346). No execution recorded.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The section now adds chromatograms and an MRMFeature to the fixture, calls subsetDependent, checks a single-transition subset keeps the whole feature (2 feature ids), and expects Exception::ElementNotFound for a selected transition without a chromatogram (that last case depends on the product change to MRMTransitionGroup.h in the same commit).
- **Commits:** 181dadf in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/MRMTransitionGroup_test.cpp section MRMTransitionGroup subsetDependent(std::vector<std::string> tr_ids)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-112"></a>
### CPP-112: FeatureMap::swap and ConsensusMap::swap do not swap the meta values

**FeatureMap::swap and ConsensusMap::swap exchange everything except the inherited meta values, so after a swap each map keeps its own meta values, including a FeatureMap's spectra_data primary-run path, next to the other map's data.**  
- **Trigger:** Calling fm1.swap(fm2) or cm1.swap(cm2) on maps with different meta values, from C++ or pyOpenMS FeatureMap.swap (bind_kernel.cpp:3632). In-tree callers are unaffected: MapAlignerIdentification.cpp:439 swaps maps with identical meta values, FeatureDeconvolution.cpp:974 and MetaboliteFeatureDeconvolution.cpp:1041 swap with a copy (cons_map_tmp(cons_map) at :930/:986), and FeatureGroupingAlgorithmUnlabeled.cpp:65/70 relies on the meta values staying put.
- **Consequence:** A user who swaps and then stores a FeatureMap can write the wrong spectra_data run path or other map-level annotation. No TOPP tool is affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/FeatureMap.cpp:323-342 swaps features/ranges, DocumentIdentifier, UniqueIdInterface, UniqueIdIndexer, protein/unassigned IDs, data_processing_, id_data_, with no MetaInfoInterface::swap. ConsensusMap.cpp:393-421 has the same omission. Both operator== functions compare meta values (FeatureMap.cpp:151, ConsensusMap.cpp:566), and clear(true) clears them (FeatureMap.cpp:472).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source review. The Core swap test sections (FeatureMap_test.cpp:483-510, ConsensusMap_test.cpp:461-487 on dax/codex/p0-followups) set no meta values. The Rust report is source review; its Rust tests copy the omission in Rust.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Both swap() bodies now call MetaInfoInterface::swap(from). FeatureGroupingAlgorithmUnlabeled::group copies input[0]'s MetaInfoInterface back into out after its swap, keeping the caller's meta values as before.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** none (swap test sections do not check meta values)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-117"></a>
### CPP-117: MSExperiment::getPrimaryMSRunPath joins using the unstripped file:/// path

**setPrimaryMSRunPath(s, exp) in FeatureMap, ConsensusMap and ProteinIdentification checks File::exists on a file:// URI, which never resolves, so the experiment's own recorded mzML run path is never used and the caller's fallback list is always stored.**  
- **Trigger:** Any setPrimaryMSRunPath(s, exp) call where the experiment has a single SourceFile ending in mzML with a file:// path, for example FeatureFinderIdentificationAlgorithm.cpp:646, SimpleSearchEngineAlgorithm.cpp:894, CometAdapter.cpp:884, MassTraceExtractor.cpp:234/306 and the pyOpenMS bindings bind_kernel.cpp:2721/3450/3658. The branch matters only when that mzML exists on disk, for example an mzML written by FileConverter with rewrite_source_file=true (FileConverter.cpp:707), whose sourceFile names the original mzML.
- **Consequence:** Provenance metadata only: spectra_data records the tool's own input file instead of the primary run the experiment points to. Scores, quantities and identifications do not change. The defect is in the consumers' existence check, not in how getPrimaryMSRunPath joins the path. The Rust report's proposed fix, building the location from actual_path, changed the path written to output and broke references, as Core recorded.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSExperiment.cpp:922-924 `std::string actual_path = StringUtils::hasPrefix(path, "file:///") ? StringUtils::substr(path, 8) : path;` is used only to choose the separator, then `std::string ms_run_location = path + sep + filename;`. Source-file paths are URIs: FileHandler.cpp:1039 `std::string("file://") + path_to_file` and MzMLHandler.cpp:1080-1107. The consumers pass the URI to File::exists, which is `return fs::exists(to_path(file));` (File.cpp:110-113; to_path in PathUtils.h:38-52 does not strip a scheme): FeatureMap.cpp:440 `... StringUtils::hasSuffix(ms_path[0], "mzML") && File::exists(ms_path[0])`, ConsensusMap.cpp:543 is identical, and ProteinIdentification.cpp:489 `if ((filetype == FileTypes::MZML) && File::exists(ms_path[0]))`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream code read at 5082181714. The Rust report says source review. Core's File_test.cpp section 'static std::string localPath(const std::string& file)' tests only the new helper, and no test calls setPrimaryMSRunPath(s, exp) with a URI source file. The review document (cpp-issues-review.md:45) records that the first Core fix changed getPrimaryMSRunPath's return value and broke NucleicAcidSearchEngine references. That was a failure of the fix, not an observed run of this defect.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): New File::localPath() (File.cpp:409 at ci.5) strips file:// and removes the slash before a Windows drive letter. FeatureMap.cpp:445-447, ConsensusMap.cpp and ProteinIdentification.cpp now test File::exists(File::localPath(ms_path[0])) and store the local path. getPrimaryMSRunPath still returns the URI form, and actual_path now uses localPath only to choose the separator.
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (codex/cpp-review-completion; also on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/File_test.cpp section 'static std::string localPath(const std::string& file)' (covers the helper only; not the setPrimaryMSRunPath path)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-119"></a>
### CPP-119: FeatureMap::getPrimaryMSRunPath does not clear its output argument

**When the map has no spectra_data meta value, FeatureMap::getPrimaryMSRunPath leaves a non-empty output list untouched, so a reused list returns the previous map's paths with no warning.**  
- **Trigger:** A C++ caller that reuses one StringList across several FeatureMaps, one of which lacks spectra_data. Every in-tree caller declares a fresh list, and the pyOpenMS binding (bind_kernel.cpp:3659) builds a new vector. The sibling functions have different semantics again: ConsensusMap::getPrimaryMSRunPath (ConsensusMap.cpp:553-560) and MSExperiment::getPrimaryMSRunPath append, and ProteinIdentification's assigns only when the value is present.
- **Consequence:** Stale run paths are attributed to the wrong map, but only on that reuse pattern, which no in-tree or pyOpenMS path follows.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/KERNEL/FeatureMap.cpp:452-464 'void FeatureMap::getPrimaryMSRunPath(StringList& toFill) const { if (this->metaValueExists("spectra_data")) { toFill = this->getMetaValue("spectra_data"); } if (toFill.empty()) { ... toFill.push_back("UNKNOWN"); } }'
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source review. No class test reuses a non-empty list. The Rust report is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): toFill.clear() on entry to FeatureMap::getPrimaryMSRunPath, with a comment that the argument is an output, not an accumulator. The ConsensusMap and MSExperiment variants are unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-121"></a>
### CPP-121: rasterizeRTMZ's SUM aggregation is not reproducible across thread counts

**With SUM aggregation, rasterizeRTMZ adds float32 intensities in an order that depends on the thread count and on OpenMP's dynamic scheduling, so pixel values can differ in their last bits between machines, thread settings and repeated runs.**  
- **Trigger:** An OpenMP build with at least 2 threads, at least 4 matching spectra, and a raster of at most about 524k pixels, so that max_by_memory = 4 MiB / (pixels*4) is at least 2. The documented 800x600 example qualifies with 2 threads. MAX aggregation is unaffected.
- **Consequence:** Differences are at float32 rounding level only. Images are not bit-reproducible, which breaks exact-equality comparisons, but no scientifically meaningful change has been shown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/KERNEL/MSExperiment.cpp:344-351 picks the thread count as `std::min({max_threads, max_by_memory, max_by_sqrt})`. :355-398 is the serial branch, writing `output[...] += spec[peak_idx].getIntensity()` at :379. :409 is `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(dynamic) num_threads(num_threads)`, with per-thread sums at :460 `local_buffer[pixel_idx] += spec[peak_idx].getIntensity();` and the merge at :499 `output[i] += thread_buf[i];`. The header note at MSExperiment.h:457-458 mentions per-thread buffers but not the nondeterminism.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream code read at 5082181714. Nobody ran it: the Rust report says source review, and MSExperiment_rasterizeRTMZ_test.cpp has no thread-count comparison and is unchanged in ci.2..p0-followups. schedule(dynamic) makes run-to-run variation possible even at a fixed thread count.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): MSExperiment.h:460 at ci.5 adds an @note that SUM pixel values depend on the thread count and OpenMP distribution and can differ by float rounding between machines, settings and runs, and that MAX is thread-independent. MSExperiment.cpp gets a matching comment at the SUM merge. The code is unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf, in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (codex/cpp-review-completion; also on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-125"></a>
### CPP-125: ImzMLMeta::mz_data_type and int_data_type stay empty on an index-only load

**After an index-only load, ImzMLMeta::mz_data_type and int_data_type are empty strings, even though every spectrum's XML declared the array types.**  
- **Trigger:** ImzMLFile::loadSpectraIndex, OnDiscImzMLExperiment::open followed by getImzMLMeta(), or the pyOpenMS equivalents, on any imzML with external arrays.
- **Consequence:** The dataset-level type summary is blank, so a caller cannot learn the element width from the metadata without decoding a spectrum. The per-spectrum index entries still carry mz_type/int_type, and no in-tree code reads the summary after an index-only load, so no downstream wrong result is shown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLHandler.cpp:198 `if (handler_.ibd_ && handler_.decode_ibd_ && (ims->mz_meta.is_ext || ims->int_meta.is_ext))` encloses :249-252 `if (handler_.meta_.mz_data_type.empty()) handler_.meta_.mz_data_type = dtStr_(ims->mz_meta.dt); ...`, the only assignment (git grep). ImzMLFile.cpp:484-487 loadSpectraIndex calls `loadImpl_(filename, nullptr, dummy, &meta, &index, true, ibd_path)` and :563 `handler.connectDecodeConsumer(consumer, append_to_map, !index_only);`, so decode_ibd_ is false. Header ImzMLHandlerHelper.h:66-70 documents the fields as a 'dataset-level summary' with no caveat.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream control flow read at ImzMLHandler.cpp:198/249-252 and ImzMLFile.cpp:487/563. No test reads these fields after loadSpectraIndex: checkMatchesIndexMeta_ in ImzMLFile_test.cpp compares imaging_mode, counts, uuid, checksums, scan and polarity, not the data types. The Rust report is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): dtStr_ moved to the file's anonymous namespace. The first non-UNKNOWN m/z and intensity types are now recorded in ImzMLHandler::onEndElement("spectrum") from cur_mz_meta_/cur_int_meta_, so both full and index-only parses fill them. The assignment in the decode branch was removed and the header comment updated.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-126"></a>
### CPP-126: An unnamed external auxiliary array vanishes from the index with no trace

**On the index-only/on-disc path, an external auxiliary array with no array-identity CV term is dropped from ImzMLSpectrumIndex::aux with no warning and no count.**  
- **Trigger:** An imzML spectrum with an external binaryDataArray that has neither MS:1000786 nor a child of MS:1000513 (non-conformant input), loaded through ImzMLFile::loadSpectraIndex or OnDiscImzMLExperiment::open/getIndex (C++ or pyOpenMS).
- **Consequence:** The file declares more arrays than the index lists, and an on-disc consumer cannot tell. The array has no name and cannot be decoded anyway, so no wrong data results. A full in-memory load does log the warning.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLHandler.cpp:258 `if (handler_.ibd_ && handler_.decode_ibd_ && !ims->aux_meta.empty())` guards the only warning at :264-268 (`OPENMS_LOG_WARN << "Skipping external auxiliary array without MS:1000513 / MS:1000786 identity at pixel ("`). The index build at :374-379 does a bare `if (aux.name.empty()) { continue; }`. ImzMLSpectrumIndex (src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLHandlerHelper.h:102-139) has no counter for dropped arrays. OnDiscImzMLExperiment.cpp:232 opens through loadSpectraIndex (decode = false via ImzMLFile.cpp:563).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream guard and bare skip read at ImzMLHandler.cpp:258-268 and :374-379, plus the struct at ImzMLHandlerHelper.h:102-139. No Core test covers unnamed_aux; a grep of the Core tests finds nothing. The Rust report is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Added `uint32_t unnamed_aux {0}` to ImzMLSpectrumIndex, documented in the header. The index-build loop, which runs for both full and index-only loads, increments it and emits the existing warning once per dropped array. The duplicate warning in the decode-guarded loop was replaced with a comment. aux still lists only named arrays.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-127"></a>
### CPP-127: verifyIbdUuid_ carries two contradictory doc comments, one describing behaviour the function does not have

**The anonymous-namespace helper verifyIbdUuid_ has two stacked comments, the first of which says a UUID mismatch is rejected, while the code (and the second comment) only logs a warning and continues loading.**  
- **Trigger:** A developer reading ImzMLFile.cpp above verifyIbdUuid_; no runtime trigger.
- **Consequence:** Misleading source comment only: a maintainer may believe a mismatched .imzML/.ibd pair is refused. Runtime behaviour is unaffected and the function is internal (not public API).
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/ImzMLFile.cpp:77-81 first comment: `A mismatch means the .imzML and .ibd do not belong together ... — reject loudly instead of silently decoding garbage offsets.`; :82-87 second comment: `This is reported as a loud WARNING rather than a hard error so that legacy / non-conformant datasets ... still load`; the function body :115-121 only does `OPENMS_LOG_WARN << ... "may not belong together."; return;`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Both comment blocks and the warn-and-return body are visible verbatim at origin/develop ImzMLFile.cpp:77-123. Nothing to execute.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Deleted the superseded first comment block (the 'reject loudly' text), leaving the advisory-warning comment that matches the code. The review table records this as 'documented' because the change is comment-only; for a comment defect it removes the defect.
- **Commits:** packages/core 181dadf '[FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation' (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-131"></a>
### CPP-131: MSImagingRegion::fromMask computes the far corner in wrapping UInt arithmetic

**MSImagingRegion::fromMask wraps the far corner when the origin lies within width-1 (or height-1) of UINT_MAX, producing an inverted bounding box that contains no pixel, instead of rejecting the input.**  
- **Trigger:** MSImagingRegion::fromMask(id, name, origin_x, origin_y, w, h, mask) from C++ or pyOpenMS with origin_x > UINT_MAX - (w-1) or origin_y > UINT_MAX - (h-1), e.g. origin_x = 4294967295, w = 2. No file reader builds regions.
- **Consequence:** The region is silently inert: contains() is false everywhere, intersects() reports no overlap so MSImagingGeometry::addRegion accepts it, and getRegionPixels/getRegionSpectrumIndices return nothing. There is no out-of-bounds access: contains() returns before indexing, and getBBoxWidth() wraps back to w. The coordinates involved are non-physical.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/IMAGING/MSImagingRegion.cpp:52-53 `reg.max_x_ = origin_x + width - 1; reg.max_y_ = origin_y + height - 1;` in UInt; the only earlier checks (:37 width/height != 0, :42 mask size and non-empty) do not bound the origin. rectangle() at :17 rejects `max_x < min_x || max_y < min_y`; contains() at :99 `if (x < min_x_ || x > max_x_ || y < min_y_ || y > max_y_) { return false; }`. Bound in pyOpenMS at bind_experiment.cpp:524-551.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Arithmetic and consumers read at origin/develop. The Rust report is a source review ('No C++ execution ... is claimed'). The MSImagingRegion_test.cpp fromMask section covers only zero-size and bad-mask exceptions; the file is unchanged in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): After the mask check, fromMask throws Exception::InvalidValue ('mask extends past the coordinate range') if origin_x > UINT_MAX - (width-1) or origin_y > UINT_MAX - (height-1). Not covered, upstream and in Core: a region that ends exactly at UINT_MAX is still allowed, and the mask path of intersects() loops `for (UInt x = lo_x; x <= hi_x; x++)` (Core MSImagingRegion.cpp:133-134), which never terminates when hi_x == UINT_MAX and no shared pixel is found. That is a separate latent issue.
- **Commits:** 181dadf [FIX,TEST] Complete inherited Core review fixes for validation (first released in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-133"></a>
### CPP-133: ImzMLWriter::store leaks the ProgressLogger recursion depth on every throw

**If ImzMLWriter::store throws after startProgress, endProgress is never called, so the process-wide static ProgressLogger recursion depth stays raised.**  
- **Trigger:** Any ImzMLFile::store failure after the progress range starts, such as an unwritable .ibd or .imzML path, a short fwrite, or a failed fflush, in a long-lived process such as a pyOpenMS session or GUI host.
- **Consequence:** Later CMD progress output in the same process is indented two spaces deeper per leaked level. Nothing is suppressed: there is no depth cap, contrary to the Rust report. No data is affected, so the effect is cosmetic.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLWriter.cpp:1437 `logger.startProgress(0, work.size() + 2, "storing imzML file");`, with the only `logger.endProgress();` at :1519. Throws in between skip it: :1443 UnableToCreateFile (.ibd), :656 UUID header ParseError, the array writers, :1508 fflush ParseError, :1189 UnableToCreateFile (.imzML). src/openms/source/CONCEPT/ProgressLogger.cpp:237-238 `current_logger_->startProgress(begin, end, label, recursion_depth_); ++recursion_depth_;` and :266-268 decrement only in endProgress. ProgressLogger.h:105 `static int recursion_depth_;`. CMD output indents by it at ProgressLogger.cpp:41,61,80.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream store() control flow and ProgressLogger's static depth read directly. No Core test: the review JSON says a mid-write I/O failure cannot be triggered portably in the class-test framework. The Rust report is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Everything after startProgress is wrapped in try { ... } catch (...) { logger.endProgress(); if (ibd_created) File::remove(ibd_path); throw; }, with the normal endProgress() after the try. The same change removes a partial .ibd (CPP-134).
- **Commits:** 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-134"></a>
### CPP-134: A failed ImzMLWriter::store leaves a truncated .ibd with no .imzML

**If ImzMLWriter::store fails after it has opened the .ibd, the partly written .ibd stays on disk with no matching .imzML, or next to an older .imzML at the same path.**  
- **Trigger:** ImzMLFile::store (MSExperiment or MSImagingExperiment overload, C++ or pyOpenMS) fails mid-write: disk full or short fwrite, an array over 100M elements, a failed fflush, or an .imzML path that cannot be opened while the .ibd path can.
- **Consequence:** The store does throw, so the failure is reported. But a truncated .ibd survives. When an existing dataset path is overwritten, the old .imzML now sits next to the new partial .ibd. A later load can pair them, reading wrong or short data, with at most a UUID warning, and none if the UUID was carried over from a loaded dataset.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLWriter.cpp:1440 `UniqueFile_ ibd(fopen(ibd_path.c_str(), "wb"));`. The .ibd is streamed first, and the XML is written last at :1517 `writeImzMLXml_(imzml_path, work, meta, plans, ...)`. store() (:1396-1520) has no try/catch. The UniqueFile_ destructor (:782-788) only calls `fclose(fp);`. Throws after the .ibd exists: ImzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:41 `if (count > MAX_IBD_ARRAY_ELEMENTS)` (called at :304), a short fwrite at :314, the fflush check at ImzMLWriter.cpp:1506-1508, and UnableToCreateFile in writeImzMLXml_ at :1189. ensureUuidBytes_ (:635) reuses meta.uuid, and ImzMLFile.cpp:117 only warns on a UUID mismatch. The file is byte-identical to core-v4.0.0-ci.2.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading of origin/develop only. The Rust report entry is a source review with no C++ execution. The Core review (docs/port-resumption-2026-09-13/inherited-core-review.json) says no class test was added. The existing store-failure tests in ImzMLFile_test.cpp (MissingInformation, InvalidParameter) throw before the .ibd is opened.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): store() now wraps the write in try/catch. An ibd_created flag is set right after a successful fopen. The catch calls logger.endProgress(), then File::remove(ibd_path) if this call created the file, then rethrows. ImzMLFile.h store() documents the cleanup and that an earlier .ibd at the same path is overwritten and not restored.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-135"></a>
### CPP-135: ImzMLFile_1_Example_Continuous.imzML is not schema-valid mzML 1.1.0, so ImzMLFile::isValid would reject its own reference fixture

**The continuous imzML reference fixture is not schema-valid mzML 1.1.0 (cvParams lack the required cvRef and softwareList comes after scanSettingsList), so validating it with ImzMLFile::isValid's schema would fail.**  
- **Trigger:** Schema-validating the unmodified fixture; no class test does so, and the reader runs without schema validation.
- **Consequence:** Test data only: all reader tests pass against it and no user path is affected; a future isValid test on the fixture, or a strict single-pass reader, would reject it.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/tests/class_tests/openms/data/ImzMLFile_1_Example_Continuous.imzML, sha256 a358a80751cfd014dc86d5a18ee04c21b98063d3523c0f03f29c375f49c6b0cc: 109 `<cvParam` elements, 0 with cvRef; scanSettingsList at line 35 before softwareList at line 57. share/OpenMS/SCHEMAS/mzML_1_10.xsd:167 `<xs:attribute name="cvRef" type="xs:IDREF" use="required">`. ImzMLFile.cpp:209 registers `/SCHEMAS/mzML_1_10.xsd`; the only isValid section, ImzMLFile_test.cpp:921-931, validates a file the test has just written.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — I validated the upstream fixture (sha256 matched) against the upstream mzML_1_10.xsd with /usr/bin/xmllint (libxml 2.9.13): 'fails to validate', ten 'cvRef is required but missing' errors (lines 17-32) plus 'scanSettingsList: This element is not expected' at line 35. xmllint stops checking the remaining content after the sequence error, which explains the Rust report's eleven diagnostics (its count of ten is consistent, not a miscount of the 109). Xerces via ImzMLFile::isValid itself was not run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Left open: the review defers adding cvRef to the 109 cvParams and reordering softwareList as a fixture-only change, since no test validates the fixture.
- **Commits:** none; fixture unchanged (same sha256) at core-v4.0.0-ci.2, core-v4.0.0-ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-137"></a>
### CPP-137: ImzMLFile's two buildImagingGeometry overloads, documented as the same source of truth, disagree on the pixel-size condition and on the skip-reason ordering

**The two ImzMLFile::buildImagingGeometry overloads disagree: the MSExperiment overload accepts a non-positive pixel size and warns about off-plane spectra with x or y < 1, where the index overload ignores such a pixel size and skips those spectra silently.**  
- **Trigger:** Call the static ImzMLFile::buildImagingGeometry(const MSExperiment&, MSImagingGeometry&) (C++ or pyOpenMS) on an experiment whose imzml:pixel_size_x/y MetaValues were set to 0 or less by hand, or which has a spectrum with imzml:z != 1 and x or y < 1.
- **Consequence:** Pixel size 0 (or negative) instead of the 1.0 default on the MetaValue path, and a spurious non-conformant-coordinate warning for off-plane spectra. ImzMLFile::load only sets the pixel-size MetaValues when > 0 (ImzMLFile.cpp:132-139), so experiments loaded from files never diverge; the pixel map itself is identical on both paths.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/ImzMLFile.cpp: MSExperiment overload tests `if (x_imz < 1 || y_imz < 1)` with a warning (:277-285) before `if (z_imz != 1) { continue; }` (:292-295), and at :363-367 `if (exp.metaValueExists("imzml:pixel_size_x") && exp.metaValueExists("imzml:pixel_size_y")) { ... geom.setPixelSize(px, py, "micrometer"); }` with no sign check. Index overload: `if (e.z != 1) ... continue;` first (:397-400) and `if (meta.pixel_size_x > 0 && meta.pixel_size_y > 0)` (:472). Header ImzMLFile.h:155 calls the index overload 'the source-of-truth path'.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Both overloads read side by side at origin/develop; no Core class test exercises a non-positive pixel size or an off-plane pixel with x/y < 1, and no executed C++ probe exists.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The MSExperiment overload now skips spectra with imzml:z != 1 before the coordinate check and calls setPixelSize only when px > 0 && py > 0, matching the index overload.
- **Commits:** packages/core 5f7d33f '[FIX,TEST] Land the review's deferred cross-file follow-ups' (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5); 181dadf had first added a header @note documenting the divergence, which 5f7d33f removed together with the code fix
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-138"></a>
### CPP-138: ClassTest::isRealSimilar reports any two infinities as similar, including +inf against -inf

**TEST_REAL_SIMILAR / isRealSimilar accepts +inf against -inf (either order), and also 1.0 against -inf, as similar.**  
- **Trigger:** A class test comparing a value with an infinity of the opposite sign, e.g. TEST_REAL_SIMILAR(x, -inf) where x evaluates to +inf.
- **Consequence:** A class test can pass silently when a result has the wrong infinite sign (e.g. TransformationModel_test.cpp:123-135 compares weightDatum against both inf and -inf). Only the test harness is affected; no false pass in the upstream suite is demonstrated.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/testframework/source/CONCEPT/ClassTest.cpp:364 `bool isRealSimilar(long double number_1, long double number_2)`; only NaN is rejected at :374 `if (std::isnan(number_1))` and :379 `if (std::isnan(number_2))`. :440 `ratio = number_1 / number_2;` gives NaN for (inf,-inf), so :441 `if (ratio < 0.)`, :458 `if (ratio < 1.)` and :467 `if (ratio > ratio_max_allowed)` are all false and :483-484 `fuzzy_message = "ratio of numbers is small"; return true;`.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — I compiled the verbatim upstream function body (ClassTest.cpp:364-489 from origin/develop, with the globals at :33-38 redeclared) with conda-forge GCC 14.4 on x86_64 (dax, temp dir removed): isRealSimilar(inf,-inf)=1 and (-inf,inf)=1 with 'ratio of numbers is small', (1,-inf)=1, while (1,inf)=0 and (-inf,1)=0. The Rust port's oracle transcription pins the same quirk (the_oracle_reproduces_the_upstream_infinity_quirk). Not run through the full upstream test binary.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): An infinity branch after the NaN guards: an infinity is similar only to the same infinity; opposite infinities or an infinite against a finite value return false with a specific fuzzy_message and absdiff set to inf. Equal-infinity assertions (AbsoluteQuantitation_test, MRMFeatureFilter_test, FeatureDistance_test) keep passing.
- **Commits:** 181dadf in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (src/testframework/source/CONCEPT/ClassTest.cpp)
- **Test:** none (no ClassTest_test section for infinities was added)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-139"></a>
### CPP-139: ClassTest::isRealSimilar is asymmetric — the quotient underflows to -0.0 and defeats its own opposite-sign branch

**isRealSimilar decides the opposite-sign case from the quotient, which underflows to -0.0 for widely different magnitudes, so it accepts opposite-sign pairs in one argument order only.**  
- **Trigger:** TEST_REAL_SIMILAR(a, b) with opposite signs and |a/b| below the double range, e.g. (1e-300, -1e300), or a finite value against -inf such as (1.0, -inf).
- **Consequence:** isRealSimilar(1e-300,-1e300) is true while (-1e300,1e-300) is false; a class test with such values can pass silently. Test harness only; no upstream assertion with such values is known.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/testframework/source/CONCEPT/ClassTest.cpp:36 `double ratio = 1.;` (global double), :440 `ratio = number_1 / number_2;` and :441 `if (ratio < 0.)`. For (1e-300, -1e300) the quotient stored in ratio is -0.0, so :441 is false; :458 `if (ratio < 1.)` is true and :460 `ratio = 1. / ratio;` gives -inf; :467 `if (ratio > ratio_max_allowed)` is false, so :483-484 returns true. The comment at :462 `// by now, we are sure that ratio >= 1` is false on this path.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed the verbatim upstream ClassTest.cpp:364-489 body on x86_64 with GCC 14.4 (dax temp dir, removed): (1e-300,-1e300)=1 'ratio of numbers is small', (-1e300,1e-300)=0 'numbers have different signs and difference is not small', (1e-300,1e300)=0. The Rust report also records compiling and running the transcribed control flow with the same results.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Replaced `if (ratio < 0.)` with `if (std::signbit(number_1) != std::signbit(number_2))`, with a comment explaining the -0.0 underflow; at that point both operands are non-zero and (after the CPP-138 branch) finite.
- **Commits:** 181dadf in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5 (src/testframework/source/CONCEPT/ClassTest.cpp)
- **Test:** none (no ClassTest_test section added)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-140"></a>
### CPP-140: ImzMLHandler's non-external peak fill is dead code: MzMLHandler throws first on a mixed spectrum

**The intercept consumer's fallback for an imzML spectrum with only one external peak array can never run: MzMLHandler rejects the spectrum first, with a message about differing m/z and intensity lengths.**  
- **Trigger:** Loading an imzML file (ImzMLFile::load, FileHandler, pyOpenMS) where one of a spectrum's m/z or intensity binaryDataArrays has IMS:1000101 (external data) and the other is inline base64. imzML 1.1 does not allow this.
- **Consequence:** Load fails with a clean ParseError ('Error during parsing of binary data: ... The length of m/z and integer values ... differ (mz-size: N, int-size: 0)'). The message does not name the missing external-data flag, and the fallback code is dead. No wrong data is produced and nothing crashes.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLHandler.cpp:198 `if (handler_.ibd_ && handler_.decode_ibd_ && (ims->mz_meta.is_ext || ims->int_meta.is_ext))`. Inline fallbacks at :212-219 `mz_vec[i] = s[i].getMZ();` and :225-232 `int_vec[i] = s[i].getIntensity();`. Only declared lengths are zeroed: :550-553 `bin_data_.back().size = 0;` and :608-611 `default_array_length_ = 0;`. MzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:189-195 resets bindata.size to the decoded count. MzMLHandler.cpp:410-412 `if (mz_size != int_size) { fatalError(LOAD, "The length of m/z and integer values of spectrum '...' differ ..."`. That error is caught in the batch loop (:224-231) and rethrown as ParseError (:243) before consumer_->consumeSpectrum (:264). The file is identical to core-v4.0.0-ci.2.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Code reading only. The Rust report is a source review with no C++ execution, and the Core review confirmed the argument by reading. There is no single-external-array fixture in ImzMLFile_test.cpp, and 5f7d33f added no test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): ImzMLHandler::onEndElement now throws a ParseError when cur_mz_meta_.is_ext != cur_int_meta_.is_ext. The message names IMS:1000101 and says imzML 1.1 requires both peak arrays in the .ibd. The inline fallbacks in the intercept consumer were removed, and both arrays are always read from the .ibd.
- **Commits:** 5f7d33f (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-142"></a>
### CPP-142: ImzMLWriter::store treats one misaligned FloatDataArray as skippable or fatal depending on an unrelated PeakFileOptions flag

**When a per-peak data array's length does not match the peak count, ImzMLWriter::store either skips the array with a warning or aborts with Exception::Precondition. Which one happens depends on whether sorting or m/z/intensity filtering touches the spectrum.**  
- **Trigger:** Storing to imzML a spectrum whose non-empty FloatDataArray (or Integer/String array) length differs from its peak count, when the spectrum is unsorted under the default PeakFileOptions or an m/z or intensity range trims a peak.
- **Consequence:** The same experiment either stores with a warning (sorted spectrum, no trimming) or the whole store aborts with Precondition. Both outcomes are clean, with no corruption. The defect is that the policy is inconsistent.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/ImzMLWriter.cpp:1402 `applyStoreOptions_(work, options);` runs first. It calls `spectrum.sortByPosition();` (:456, :480) and `spectrum.select(kept);` (:470). The later skip at :521-528 reads `// Per-peak contract ... if (fda.size() != spec.size()) { OPENMS_LOG_WARN << "Skipping FloatDataArray '"...`. MSSpectrum.cpp:444-460: sortByPosition calls sort() whenever data arrays exist. MSSpectrum.h:369 `checkDataArraySizes_();` and MSSpectrum.cpp:66 in selectUnchecked both run the check, and MSSpectrum.cpp:27-31 throws `Exception::Precondition` for a non-empty mis-sized array. PeakFileOptions.h:241 `bool sort_spectra_by_mz_ = true;`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — 181dadf adds ImzMLFile_test.cpp section 'void store skips a misaligned FloatDataArray whatever the PeakFileOptions say'. It stores an unsorted 2-peak spectrum with a 1-value array under three option sets: untouched, default sort, and m/z trim. On the pre-fix code, which is byte-identical to develop, the sort and trim stores would throw Precondition, but no recorded failing run exists.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): New dropMisalignedDataArrays_(MSExperiment&) removes every non-empty float, integer or string array whose length differs from the peak count, with a warning. applyStoreOptions_ calls it after the spectrum-level filters and before any sortByPosition or select. The write-time check stays as a guard.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/ImzMLFile_test.cpp, section 'void store skips a misaligned FloatDataArray whatever the PeakFileOptions say' (181dadf)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-149"></a>
### CPP-149: MzTab-M assay custom metadata is written under ms_run

**The mzTab-M writer emits an assay's custom parameters under ms_run[n]-custom[m], attributing them to the run with the same index instead of to assay[n].**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabMFile::store with MzTabMMetaData::assay[n].custom non-empty. No upstream code fills it (MzTabM.cpp:321 is only a comment), and pyOpenMS does not expose MzTabM metadata (bind_format.cpp:1279-1288 binds only the constructor and exportFeatureMapToMzTabM), so only direct C++ API users reach it.
- **Consequence:** Assay-level custom metadata is written under the wrong key and appears to describe an unrelated ms_run; no current OpenMS output is affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTabMFile.cpp:228-232, inside `for (const auto& assay : md.assay)`: `std::string s = "MTD\tms_run[" + StringUtils::toStr(assay.first) + "]-custom[" + StringUtils::toStr(custom.first) + "]\t" + custom.second.toCellString();`. The neighbouring assay lines use "MTD\tassay[" (:225, :236).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read on origin/develop. No Core or upstream test writes assay custom parameters. The Rust report entry is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The key prefix is now "MTD\tassay[" + n + "]-custom[" + m + "]", with a comment explaining why.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5), MzTabMFile.cpp:228-234; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-150"></a>
### CPP-150: MzTab-M column-unit families share an incorrect output key

**The mzTab-M writer writes feature and evidence column units under the small-molecule colunit key, so the three families cannot be told apart.**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabMFile::store with colunit_small_molecule_feature or colunit_small_molecule_evidence non-empty. Only possible from C++, since nothing upstream fills them and pyOpenMS does not expose mzTab-M metadata.
- **Consequence:** Feature and evidence unit definitions are written as if they belonged to small-molecule columns. No OpenMS output is affected. The key is also spelled with an underscore rather than mzTab-M 2.0's 'colunit-small_molecule...', which the Core fix keeps.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTabMFile.cpp:347-363: the loops over md.colunit_small_molecule, md.colunit_small_molecule_feature and md.colunit_small_molecule_evidence all build `"MTD\tcolunit_small_molecule\t" + ....toCellString()` (lines 349, 355, 361). The vectors are declared at MzTabM.h:121-123 and are referenced nowhere else upstream.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read on origin/develop. No Core or upstream test sets these vectors. The Rust report entry is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The feature and evidence loops now write colunit_small_molecule_feature and colunit_small_molecule_evidence. The file's non-spec underscore after 'colunit' is kept, so this is only a partial spec fix.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5), MzTabMFile.cpp:355-367; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups (lines 357-365).
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-152"></a>
### CPP-152: pepXML end_scan mismatch check reads start_scan twice

**The pepXML reader reads start_scan into both locals, so its 'merged spectrum queries not supported' message never fires.**  
- **Trigger:** PepXMLFile::load of a spectrum_query with start_scan != end_scan (e.g. start_scan="1" end_scan="2"), via IDFileConverter, CometAdapter, MSFraggerAdapter or pyOpenMS PepXMLFile.load.
- **Consequence:** The merged query is still parsed using the start scan only, which the code comment says is intended. The user just gets no log message saying so. Results do not change.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/PepXMLFile.cpp:934-938 in readRTMZCharge_: `scannr_ = attributeAsInt_(attributes, "start_scan"); Size endscan = attributeAsInt_(attributes, "start_scan"); if (scannr_ != endscan) { error(LOAD, "endscan not equal to startscan. Merged spectrum queries not supported. Parsing start scan nr. only."); }`
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read on origin/develop. XMLHandler::error only logs, so the lost message is non-fatal. No test uses start_scan != end_scan; the only end_scan in Core's PepXMLFile_test.cpp (181dadf section) writes equal values. The Rust report entry is source review only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): endscan is initialised to start_scan and overwritten from "end_scan" via optionalAttributeAsInt_, so a missing end_scan does not make the file unreadable, and the comparison now reports merged queries.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5), PepXMLFile.cpp readRTMZCharge_; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-165"></a>
### CPP-165: Mascot RT failure test treats zero as failure and NaN as success

**Mascot XML's RT look-up check tests the double's truth value, so a failed look-up (NaN) gets no scan_regex hint, while a valid RT of 0 gets a false 'Could not extract RT value' error.**  
- **Trigger:** A Mascot XML <pep_scan_title> that matches none of the scan_regex reference formats, which leaves the RT NaN, or a title that yields RT 0.
- **Consequence:** Only log output is wrong. The targeted hint to adjust scan_regex is never printed on a real miss, though the file-level count warning and the all-missing MissingInformation throw still fire. A spurious error is printed once per file for RT 0. Stored RT values are unaffected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MascotXMLHandler.cpp:117 `if (!id_data_[peptide_identification_index_].getRT())` guards the 'Could not extract RT value ... Try adjusting the 'scan_regex' parameter.' error (:119-129). :104 calls `setRT(meta.rt)`. SpectrumMetaDataLookup.h:148 `rt(std::numeric_limits<double>::quiet_NaN())`. SpectrumMetaDataLookup.cpp:73-154 returns without touching meta when no reference format matches. PeptideIdentification.cpp:28 sets rt_ to NaN, and :64 defines hasRT(). The sibling <StringTitle> branch at :365 already uses hasRT(). XMLHandler.cpp:71-76 error() is non-fatal. Mitigation: MascotXMLFile.cpp:72-81 warns '<n> (of <m>) peptide identifications have no retention time value', and :84-88 throws MissingInformation when a lookup is set and no ID has an RT.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Source reading of origin/develop; MascotXMLHandler.cpp is identical to core-v4.0.0-ci.2. The Rust report says 'confirmed by source review; not executed'. No Core test checks this message. The p0-followups Mascot tests check hasRT() on stray-element cases, not this diagnostic. Severity is lowered from the earlier P2 because MascotXMLFile::load already warns about missing RTs.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The check now reads `if (!id_data_[peptide_identification_index_].hasRT())`, with a comment that a failed look-up leaves NaN and 0 is a valid RT.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-175"></a>
### CPP-175: SVOutStream probe stream remains poisoned after non-newline manipulator

**After a manipulator that writes a character other than a newline (such as std::ends), SVOutStream never recognizes std::endl again, so every following line starts with a separator.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ code writes a field, then std::ends (or a custom character-writing manipulator), then std::endl, then more fields. The probe buffer holds "\0\n" and never equals "\n" again.
- **Consequence:** Every later line of the TSV/CSV output gets a leading separator, shifting columns. No OpenMS code does this: the only std::ends in src is a comment in extern GTE (MinimumVolumeBox3.h:905), and SVOutStream is not bound in pyOpenMS. So it is reachable only through direct C++ use, with no demonstrated effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/SVOutStream.cpp:108-113 `fp(ss_); if (ss_.str() == "\n") { newline_ = true; ss_.str(""); }`: the probe stringstream (SVOutStream.h:188 `std::stringstream ss_;`) is cleared only when it equals "\n". Separator insertion depends on newline_ at SVOutStream.cpp:67-70 `if (!newline_) { (ostream&) *this << sep_; }` and SVOutStream.h:109 and :119-120.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The Rust report and ranked.json record source review only. The Core review ledger mentions a standalone copy of the probe logic, run without an OpenMS build (old logic: a\0\n\tb\n\tc\td\n\te), which is a simulation, not a run of the class. No Core test covers it: SVOutStream_test.cpp is unchanged from core-v4.0.0-ci.2 through dax/codex/p0-followups.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): Before each probe the buffer is emptied and its error state cleared (`ss_.str(""); ss_.clear();`), and the clear inside the match is removed, so every std::endl is detected no matter what earlier manipulators wrote.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4, ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-182"></a>
### CPP-182: SqliteConnector does not close a handle when opening fails

**When sqlite3_open_v2 fails, SqliteConnector throws without closing the connection handle that SQLite still allocated, so the handle leaks.**  
- **Trigger:** Construct a SqliteConnector on a missing file in READ_ONLY/READWRITE mode or on an inaccessible path, for example through OSWFile, TransitionPQPFile, MzMLSqliteHandler/SqMassFile (including pyOpenMS SqMassFile.load), OpenSwathPercolatorScoring or BrukerTimsImagingFile.
- **Consequence:** A few kilobytes of SQLite connection memory leak per failed open. The exception itself is clean and no file descriptor stays open, so the effect matters only for long-running processes that fail to open repeatedly. No demonstrated effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/SqliteConnector.cpp:48-54 `sqlite3* db = nullptr; int rc = sqlite3_open_v2(filename.c_str(), &db, flags, nullptr); db_ = db; if (rc) { throw Exception::SqlOperationFailed(... "Could not open sqlite db '" + filename ...); }`. The handle is never closed, and the destructor (:23-30) does not run because the constructor (:18-21) throws.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The Rust report is source review with no C++ run or sanitizer. The Core SqliteConnector_test '[EXTRA] (open modes: existing DB read-only and missing-file errors)' section checks only the exception, not the leak, and no leak-checker result is recorded.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): On a failed sqlite3_open_v2 the handle is passed to sqlite3_close_v2 and db_ set to nullptr before throwing; db_ is assigned only after a successful open.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4, ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-184"></a>
### CPP-184: SQLite table-name helpers interpolate names as SQL syntax

**tableExists, columnExists and countTableRows paste the table name into SQL unescaped, so names with an apostrophe or space fail and injection-shaped names change the query.**  
- **Trigger:** Direct C++ calls with a non-constant table name: tableExists("odd'name") on an existing table throws IllegalArgument; tableExists("ABSENT' OR 1=1 --") returns true; countTableRows/columnExists("odd table") fail to prepare. All upstream library callers (OMSFileLoad, TransitionPQPFile, OpenSwathPercolatorScoring, BrukerTimsImagingFile, OSWFile.cpp:2639) pass fixed internal names; SqliteConnector is not bound in pyOpenMS.
- **Consequence:** Latent API hardening issue: wrong existence answer or spurious exception for unusual names, not reachable from file content or any TOPP tool.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/SqliteConnector.cpp:81 `std::string select_runs = "SELECT count(*) FROM " + table_name + ";";`, :100 `prepareStatement(db, &xcntstmt, "PRAGMA table_info(" + tablename + ")");`, :121 `prepareStatement(db, &stmt, "SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='" + tablename + "';");` (origin/develop 5082181714).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed adapted C++ probe recorded in the Rust report and retained at OpenMS4-Exploration/oracle/sqlite-connector-s0-probe/result.log: `table_apostrophe exists=IllegalArgument near "name": syntax error`, `table_injected_expression exists=1`, `count_spaced_name ... syntax error`, `column_spaced_name ... syntax error`. The probe compiled the pinned connector (bc9cc12514, identical to upstream develop) with substitute support headers against SQLite 3.45.1, not a full SDK build.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): tableExists binds the name as a value (`name=?` with sqlite3_bind_text, Core SqliteConnector.cpp:143-146); countTableRows and columnExists wrap the name with quoteIdentifier (:42, doubling embedded double quotes) at :107 and :125.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-186"></a>
### CPP-186: SQLite string extraction truncates embedded NUL bytes

**Reading a TEXT cell into std::string stops at the first embedded NUL byte, dropping the rest of the value.**  
- **Trigger:** A SQLite TEXT value containing a NUL byte (e.g. bytes 'a', 0x00, 'b') read through Internal::SqliteHelper::extractValue<std::string>/extractString, used by TransitionPQPFile, MzMLSqliteHandler and OSW readers.
- **Consequence:** The string is truncated to 'a'. Fields read this way (native IDs, sequences, names, annotations) do not realistically contain NUL, so no downstream effect is demonstrated.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/SqliteConnector.cpp:222 extractValue<std::string>: `*dst = std::string(reinterpret_cast<const char*>(sqlite3_column_text(stmt, pos)));` (C-string constructor, no sqlite3_column_bytes length) (origin/develop 5082181714).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Rust report: source-reviewed candidate, no C++ execution; the retained SQLite probe has no NUL case. No Core test exercises a TEXT value containing NUL.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): extractValue<std::string> now builds the string from the text pointer and sqlite3_column_bytes (Core SqliteConnector.cpp:236), keeping embedded NULs.
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-187"></a>
### CPP-187: SQLite integer-to-string extraction narrows to 32 bits

**extractValueIntStr reads a 64-bit SQLite INTEGER through sqlite3_column_int, so values outside the 32-bit range are converted to a wrong decimal string.**  
- **Trigger:** An INTEGER cell above 2^31-1 or below -2^31 (e.g. 4294967297) read through extractValueIntStr; upstream only uses it for PQP precursor and fragment charge columns.
- **Consequence:** The string holds the truncated low 32 bits. Real charge values never approach that range, so no realistic effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/SqliteConnector.cpp:261-265 extractValueIntStr: `if (sqlite3_column_type(stmt, pos) == SQLITE_INTEGER) { *dst = StringUtils::toStr(sqlite3_column_int(stmt, pos));` (origin/develop 5082181714); callers are TransitionPQPFile.cpp precursor_charge/fragment_charge columns.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Rust report: source-reviewed candidate, no C++ execution; the retained probe has no case for it. No Core test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): extractValueIntStr now formats sqlite3_column_int64 (Core SqliteConnector.cpp:279).
- **Commits:** 1beb468 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

### CPP-188: MSDataWritingConsumer class test is disabled and calls a nonexistent constructor

**The class test for the streaming mzML writer is commented out of the build and could not compile, because it default-constructs an abstract class that has only a filename constructor; the writer therefore has no executed unit test.**

- **Consequence:** Test-coverage gap only; no product misbehavior. It left writer defects such as CPP-172 uncaught.

The class is abstract and has no default constructor: MSDataWritingConsumer.h:76 `explicit MSDataWritingConsumer(const std::string& filename);`, :154 `virtual void processSpectrum_(SpectrumType & s) = 0;`, :161 `virtual void processChromatogram_(ChromatogramType & c) = 0;`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read from the upstream test file, the CMake registration and the header. No compile attempt is recorded anywhere, and the Rust report says no compiler reproduction is claimed.

- **Commits:** 181dadf (test rewritten against the current API with PlainMSDataWritingConsumer and temp files), b6dd412 (registered in executables.cmake), 63e332c (array-history sections added); core-v4.0.0-ci.5

- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-189"></a>
### CPP-189: SqliteConnector row-count documentation names the wrong exception

**The installed header says countTableRows throws SqlOperationFailed for an unknown table, but it actually throws IllegalArgument.**  
- **Trigger:** countTableRows("UNKNOWN") on a database without that table, by a C++ caller relying on the header's documented exception.
- **Consequence:** A caller catching only SqlOperationFailed misses the real exception. No upstream library caller catches it this way (OSWFile.cpp:2639 lets it propagate), so no demonstrated effect beyond the wrong documentation.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/SqliteConnector.h:74-76 `/// @throws Exception::SqlOperationFailed if table is unknown` on countTableRows, but SqliteConnector.cpp:82 calls prepareStatement, which for an unknown table throws `Exception::IllegalArgument(... sqlite3_errmsg(db))` at :148-152; upstream's own src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SqliteConnector_test.cpp:105-106 asserts `TEST_EXCEPTION(Exception::IllegalArgument, conn.countTableRows("UNKNOWN"))`. The two exceptions are siblings under BaseException (Exception.h:556, :632).
- **Evidence:** reproduced — Executed adapted C++ probe (oracle/sqlite-connector-s0-probe/result.log): `CASE count_unknown count=IllegalArgument no such table: UNKNOWN`; the upstream class test at SqliteConnector_test.cpp:106 also asserts IllegalArgument, contradicting the header.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): Header corrected (Core SqliteConnector.h:79-82): documents IllegalArgument for an unknown table (rejected at prepare) and SqlOperationFailed when the prepared query yields no row or a NULL count. Behaviour unchanged.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (in core-v4.0.0-ci.3..ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SqliteConnector_test.cpp, section countTableRows (:106, pre-existing, asserts IllegalArgument)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-194"></a>
### CPP-194: Metadata failures leave committed DATA rows and advanced writer counters

**When a sqMass metadata insert fails, the DATA blobs written just before it stay committed and the spectrum/chromatogram ID counters stay advanced, leaving orphan rows and gaps in the IDs.**  
- **Trigger:** Any failure of the metadata batch after DATA insertion. Examples: a native ID with an apostrophe (CPP-193), a duplicate primary key from writing into an already populated database, or a disk-full or constraint error. Also a failure midway through writeExperiment after RUN and chromatograms were committed.
- **Consequence:** The write throws, but the file keeps orphan DATA rows and possibly RUN and chromatogram rows without their spectra. Counters on the handler have advanced, so a retry on the same handler writes IDs that do not start at 0 and are not contiguous, which breaks position-based reads (readSpectra with 0..count-1, SqMassFile::transform). Nothing is lost silently; the failure handling is wrong.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp: writeSpectra advances `spec_id_++;` in the loop (:1261) and commits DATA with `conn.executeBindStatement(prepare_statement, data);` (:1269, :1283) before `conn.executeStatement("BEGIN TRANSACTION");` (:1286). writeChromatograms is the same (`chrom_id_++;` :1472, binds :1480/:1494, BEGIN :1497). writeRunLevelInformation commits RUN in its own transaction (:899-901) and inserts RUN_EXTRA in autocommit (:935). writeExperiment (:877-885) calls the three public writers one after another, each on its own connection.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Transaction boundaries and counter increments read from upstream source at the lines quoted. The Rust report (CPP-194) records a pinned source review with no C++ run. No Core test injects a failing metadata insert.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The bodies moved into protected writeRunLevelInformation_/writeSpectra_/writeChromatograms_(SqliteConnector&, ...), which neither begin nor end a transaction. writeExperiment and each public writer run in one transaction covering DATA, metadata and run rows. On any exception, rollbackOpenTransaction issues ROLLBACK and restores spec_id_/chrom_id_ to their starting values (ci.5 cpp:954, 972, 1180, 1450). The header documents the all-or-nothing contract.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion, merged bb8f5b8; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-197"></a>
### CPP-197: SWATH window docs promise distinct centers but query deduplicates full bounds tuples

**The readSwathWindows documentation promises one window per distinct centre, but the query returns one window per distinct (centre, lower, upper) tuple, so equal centres with different widths yield several windows.**  
- **Trigger:** MS2 spectra in a sqMass file with the same isolation target but different lower/upper offsets (e.g. 500 with offsets 10 and 20), read via readSwathWindows / SwathFile::loadSqMass.
- **Consequence:** Callers get two SwathMap entries with the same centre. readSpectraForWindow uses only the centre, so both entries receive the same spectra, and OpenSwath may process those spectra twice for transitions that fall inside both bounds. The tuple behaviour itself is reasonable; the defect is the contract text.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.h:57-58 `Returns one @c SwathMap entry per distinct precursor isolation centre present at MS level 2`, while MzMLSqliteSwathHandler.cpp:30-33 selects `"DISTINCT(ISOLATION_TARGET)," "ISOLATION_TARGET - ISOLATION_LOWER," "ISOLATION_TARGET + ISOLATION_UPPER "`, where DISTINCT applies to the whole (centre, lower, upper) row.
- **Evidence:** reproduced — The Rust probe on kim used pinned sources identical to origin/develop for this handler. result.log records `CASE CPP197_same_center_distinct_bounds count=2` with windows 490-510 and 480-520 at centre 500. Whether duplicate processing in OpenSwath follows was not executed.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): The header now says one entry per distinct combination of centre, lower and upper bound, and that two windows with the same centre but different widths are two entries for which readSpectraForWindow returns the same spectra. The SQL keeps tuple DISTINCT and additionally skips rows with `ISOLATION_TARGET IS NOT NULL` failing.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion, merged bb8f5b8; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-201"></a>
### CPP-201: Full metadata recovery promise omits auxiliary-array loss

**The write_full_meta documentation promises complete recovery of the input file, but float, integer and string data arrays (e.g. ion mobility arrays) are stored neither in RUN_EXTRA nor in DATA and are silently dropped.**  
- **Trigger:** Store an MSExperiment with auxiliary data arrays (e.g. mzML with ion mobility float arrays) to sqMass with write_full_meta=true, then load it.
- **Consequence:** The reloaded experiment lacks the auxiliary arrays with no warning, contrary to the documented promise. The format was never designed to hold them, so the defect is the contract text rather than the storage code.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteHandler.h:116 `@param[in] write_full_meta Whether to write a complete mzML meta data structure into the RUN_EXTRA field (allows complete recovery of the input file)`. MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:913-914 `MSSpectrum s = exp.getSpectra()[k]; s.clear(false);` (chromatograms :919-920), and MSSpectrum.cpp:208-210 `float_data_arrays_.clear(); string_data_arrays_.clear(); integer_data_arrays_.clear();` runs regardless of the flag. writeSpectra/writeChromatograms store only m/z-or-RT and intensity blobs, and no data-array accessor appears in the writer.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Upstream header and source quoted. The Rust report classifies it as an unconfirmed documentation/contract candidate from source review, with no C++ run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): @param write_full_meta now says settings and spectrum/chromatogram metadata are recovered, but only m/z (or RT) and intensity arrays are stored, so float, integer and string data arrays such as ion mobility arrays are lost and lossy compression limits precision. The class description adds the same note. The storage behaviour is unchanged.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (codex/cpp-review-completion, merged bb8f5b8; released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-209"></a>
### CPP-209: SpectrumAccessSqMass metadata index stays zero for every spectrum

**The metadata returned by SpectrumAccessSqMass has index 0 for every spectrum, although SpectrumMeta::index is documented as the zero-based position in the spectrum list.**  
- **Trigger:** getSpectrumMetaById(k) for k >= 1, or getAllSpectra with two or more spectra, on any SpectrumAccessSqMass.
- **Consequence:** External code that reads SpectrumMeta::index gets 0. No reader of the field was found at develop (git grep over OPENSWATH sources, openswathalgo, topp and pyOpenMS bindings), and other accessors such as SpectrumAccessOpenMS also leave it 0, so no downstream effect is shown.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): SpectrumAccessSqMass.cpp:114-117 `OpenSwath::SpectrumMeta m; m.id = spectrum.getNativeID(); m.RT = spectrum.getRT(); m.ms_level = spectrum.getMSLevel();`, and the same at :153-156 in getAllSpectra. index is never set. src/openswathalgo/include/OpenMS/OPENSWATHALGO/DATAACCESS/DataStructures.h:158-159 `/// the zero-based, consecutive index of the spectrum in the SpectrumList. size_t index;` and :168-170 `OSSpectrumMeta() : index(0)`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 181dadf added assertions that fail on pre-fix code by construction: `TEST_EQUAL(full.getSpectrumMetaById(1).index, 1)` (SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp:154), `TEST_EQUAL(spectra_meta[1].index, 1)` (:230) and `TEST_EQUAL(spectra_meta[k].index, k)` (:301). They were not present at ci.2, and no pre-fix failure run is recorded.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): getSpectrumMetaById sets m.index = id (the view position) and getAllSpectra sets m.index = k. A DataStructures.h note says not every ISpectrumAccess implementation fills the field.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf, released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp: section getSpectrumMetaById (lines 153-154, 162) and section getAllSpectra (lines 229-230, 301)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-210"></a>
### CPP-210: SpectrumAccessSqMass class test repeats one out-of-range index instead of testing 50

**The SpectrumAccessSqMass subset-constructor test repeats the [1] out-of-range case instead of testing index 50, and has no negative-index case.**  
- **Trigger:** Running SpectrumAccessSqMass_test; the intended [50] assertion never runs.
- **Consequence:** Test coverage gap only; the [50] case would pass on current code. The missing negative case is why the absent lower-bound check (CPP-207) went unnoticed.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp:101-103 `std::vector<int> indices2; indices2.push_back(50); TEST_EXCEPTION(Exception::IllegalArgument, new SpectrumAccessSqMass(*ptr, indices))` passes `indices` ([1]) instead of `indices2`; no negative-index case. The constructor checks only the upper bound: SpectrumAccessSqMass.cpp:43 `if (indices[k] >= (int)sp.sidx_.size()) throw Exception::IllegalArgument(...)`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Test typo visible at origin/develop line 103. No execution; the corrected [50] case would not fail pre-fix, only the added [-1] case does (that failure belongs to CPP-207).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The test now passes indices2 ([50]) at line 104 and adds indices3 = [-1] expecting Exception::IllegalArgument at lines 106-108; unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups.
