<a id="cpp-211"></a>
### CPP-211: SpectrumAccessSqMass installed example omits required handler run ID

**The Doxygen usage example for SpectrumAccessSqMass constructs MzMLSqliteHandler with one argument, but the only constructor needs a filename and a run_id, so the example does not compile as written.**  
- **Trigger:** Copying the documented example into code.
- **Consequence:** Documentation only: a compile error for anyone who copies the snippet. No runtime effect. The in-tree equivalent (SwathFile.cpp:357) passes `(file, 0)`.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/ANALYSIS/OPENSWATH/DATAACCESS/SpectrumAccessSqMass.h:67 (SpectrumAccessSqMass_example) `OpenMS::Internal::MzMLSqliteHandler handler(file);`, while src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzMLSqliteHandler.h:59 declares only `MzMLSqliteHandler(const std::string& filename, const UInt64 run_id);` with no default argument and no other constructor.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Compared the example text with the public constructor declaration at origin/develop. Doxygen @code blocks are not compiled and no compiler run was done. The snippet also uses an undeclared sql_mass_reader, so it is illustrative rather than self-contained.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): The example now reads `OpenMS::Internal::MzMLSqliteHandler handler(file, 0); // the run id is only used when writing`.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (SpectrumAccessSqMass.h), released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-213"></a>
### CPP-213: SqMassFile store documentation hides unconditional replacement of an existing database

**The public SqMassFile::store documentation says it creates the file and tables if necessary, but store always deletes an existing file first and replaces it non-atomically, which the documentation does not say.**  
- **Trigger:** Call SqMassFile::store (C++ or pyOpenMS) on a path that already holds a sqMass database, expecting the tables to be reused or the old data kept, or expecting the old file to survive a failed write.
- **Consequence:** The existing database is removed before writing starts; if the write fails, the old file is gone and a partial database may remain. Overwriting on store is the usual convention, so the defect is the misleading wording and missing warning, not the behavior. No demonstrated effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/SqMassFile.h:95-97 says store "Writes the spectra, chromatograms and experimental metadata of @p map to @p filename, creating the file (and the required SQLite tables) if necessary." SqMassFile.cpp:33 calls `sql_mass.createTables();` unconditionally, and MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:941-943 `// delete file if present ... std::filesystem::remove(OpenMS::to_path(filename_), ec);`. Only the internal MzMLSqliteHandler.h:187-188 warns "calling this on an existing file will delete the file!".
- **Evidence:** code-reading — The Rust report calls it an unconfirmed documentation candidate from source review, with no C++ write run. The Core ledger cites the same source lines. The documentation fix has no test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): store() docs now say a new database is written, with an @warning: an existing file is deleted before writing starts, nothing is appended or kept, the replacement is not atomic, and a failed write leaves the old file gone and possibly a partial database. Behavior is unchanged.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (SqMassFile.h docs; in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, ci.4, ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-215"></a>
### CPP-215: MSDataSqlConsumer accepts negative signed buffer size before unsigned allocation

**A negative buffer_size wraps to SIZE_MAX, so the constructor fails with an uninformative std::length_error from vector::reserve instead of rejecting the argument, and it leaks the already-allocated handler.**  
- **Trigger:** MSDataSqlConsumer(path, run_id, -1) from C++ or pyOpenMS. No TOPP caller passes a negative value: OpenSwathBase.cpp:359 hard-codes 500, and OpenSwathMzMLFileCacher.cpp:163 passes a bool in that position.
- **Consequence:** The constructor throws std::length_error with a misleading message and leaks the small handler object (see CPP-204). The throw comes before createTables, so no file is touched, and there is no crash, hang or OOM.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): MSDataSqlConsumer.cpp:16-23 `MSDataSqlConsumer(..., int flush_after, ...) : ... handler_(new OpenMS::Internal::MzMLSqliteHandler(filename, run_id) ), flush_after_(flush_after), ... { spectra_.reserve(flush_after_); chromatograms_.reserve(flush_after_);`, with MSDataSqlConsumer.h:96 `size_t flush_after_;`. No validation exists, and the header doc (h:50) does not define negative values. pyOpenMS exposes the signed parameter: bind_misc.cpp:1694 `.def(nb::init<std::string, size_t, int, bool, bool, double>())`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core test SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp '[EXTRA] reading spectra written by MSDataSqlConsumer' line 367 `TEST_EXCEPTION(Exception::IllegalArgument, new MSDataSqlConsumer(tmp_filename, 0, -1))` would fail on pre-fix code, which throws std::length_error, but no failing run is recorded. The Rust report marks it unconfirmed with no allocation probe.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The constructor throws Exception::IllegalArgument ('Buffer size must not be negative') for flush_after < 0 before reserve() and createTables() (cpp:24-30). The header documents the exception and that 0 means immediate writes, and the handler is now a unique_ptr, so nothing leaks.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (core-v4.0.0-ci.5); unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp, section '[EXTRA] reading spectra written by MSDataSqlConsumer' (line 367)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-216"></a>
### CPP-216: SpectrumAccessSqMass zero-width RT documentation differs from delegated first-at-or-after behavior

**The SpectrumAccessSqMass (and base ISpectrumAccess) documentation says getSpectraByRT with deltaRT = 0 matches spectra at exactly RT, but it actually returns the first spectrum at or after RT, even if its RT is different.**  
- **Trigger:** getSpectraByRT(0.3, 0.0) on SqliteMassFile_1.sqMass (spectra at RT 0.2961 and 0.4738) returns index 1 (RT 0.4738).
- **Consequence:** Documentation mismatch only. The behaviour is intentional, and getMultipleSpectra depends on it for its nearest-spectrum search. A caller who trusts the doc gets a spectrum at a different RT rather than an empty result. No wrong in-tree result.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/include/OpenMS/ANALYSIS/OPENSWATH/DATAACCESS/SpectrumAccessSqMass.h:171 `@param[in] deltaRT Half-width of the RT window; matches spectra in [RT - deltaRT, RT + deltaRT]`, while SpectrumAccessSqMass.cpp:168-169 allows deltaRT >= 0 and delegates to MzMLSqliteHandler.cpp:463-478 (`if (deltaRT > 0.0) ... BETWEEN ... else ... WHERE RETENTION_TIME >= RT` plus `if (deltaRT <= 0.0) {select_sql += " LIMIT 1";}`). The base-interface doc at ISpectrumAccess.h:55 says the same ('within RT +/- deltaRT'). ISpectrumAccess.cpp:24/60 relies on getSpectraByRT(RT, 0.0) returning the first spectrum at or after RT. Only the handler header (MzMLSqliteHandler.h:140) documents the <= 0 behaviour.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 181dadf added a getSpectraByRT section (SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp:170-193 at ci.5, absent at ci.2). It asserts that getSpectraByRT(0.3, 0.0) returns one index equal to 1, that (0.5, 0.0) returns none, and that the subset view returns 0. The behaviour code was not changed, so the test pins the pre-existing behaviour that contradicts the old doc. No executed failure applies, since this is a documentation defect.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): The deltaRT doc now says a positive value matches [RT-deltaRT, RT+deltaRT], while zero returns only the first spectrum at or after RT (spectra assumed stored in RT order), or nothing, and that getMultipleSpectra relies on this. The base ISpectrumAccess::getSpectraByRT doc was updated the same way.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 181dadf (SpectrumAccessSqMass.h deltaRT param; ISpectrumAccess.h getSpectraByRT and getMultipleSpectra docs), released in core-v4.0.0-ci.5, unchanged on dax/codex/p0-followups
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SpectrumAccessSqMass_test.cpp, section std::vector<std::size_t> getSpectraByRT(double RT, double deltaRT) const (lines 179-191)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-217"></a>
### CPP-217: OpenSwath getMultipleSpectra count parameter can return more than requested

**getMultipleSpectra returns 2*floor(n/2)+1 spectra (fewer at map edges), so an even request returns one spectrum more than the documented sequence length, and n <= 1 (including 0 or negative) returns one spectrum.**  
- **Trigger:** OpenSwath scoring with add_up_spectra set to an even value (allowed, minimum 1), or spectrum_merge_method_type=dynamic where ceil(peak width * fraction) is even; direct API calls with n=0, 2 or negative.
- **Consequence:** Scoring merges or passes on one extra spectrum (add_up_spectra=2 behaves exactly like 3), contrary to the header and OpenSwathScoring documentation; the debug log reports the requested count. The symmetric window is intentional per the code comment, so the defect is the documented contract, with no demonstrated effect on scores beyond the window width.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop 5082181714 src/openswathalgo/source/OPENSWATHALGO/DATAACCESS/ISpectrumAccess.cpp:39 `all_spectra.push_back(getSpectrumById(closest_idx));` then :42 `for (int i = 1; i <= nr_spectra_to_fetch / 2; i++) // cast to int is intended!` pushing closest_idx - i (:44-47) and closest_idx + i (:48-51); same in the drift overload :75-88. Header ISpectrumAccess.h:71 documents `nr_spectra_to_fetch = # spectra around target RT to fetch (length of the spectrum sequence)`; OpenSwathScoring.h:265 and :304 say `Array of length "nr_spectra_to_add" returned`. MRMFeatureFinderScoring.cpp:104 `defaults_.setMinInt("add_up_spectra", 1);` allows even values, and OpenSwathScoring.cpp:209 (dynamic mode) computes `n_merge_spectra = std::max(1, static_cast<int>(std::ceil(peakWidth * merge_spectra_by_peak_width_fraction_)))`, which is often even.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read both overloads and their callers at origin/develop. No execution; the upstream OpenSwathScoring_test only requests 1 or 3 spectra (lines 122-277), and the Rust report says no execution was done. No Core test exercises the count.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): Behaviour unchanged. ISpectrumAccess.h now documents that the sequence is the closest spectrum plus up to n/2 neighbours per side in alternating order, unsorted, so odd n yields at most n spectra, even n at most n+1, and any value below 2 only the closest; OpenSwathScoring.h says 'up to nr_spectra_to_add spectra (one more for an even value)'.
- **Commits:** 181dadf (ISpectrumAccess.h doc of getMultipleSpectra and getSpectraByRT) and 5f7d33f (OpenSwathScoring.h fetchSpectrumSwath docs), both in core-v4.0.0-ci.2..core-v4.0.0-ci.5
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

<a id="cpp-221"></a>
### CPP-221: Gumbel result declares eval without defining it

**GumbelDistributionFitter::GumbelDistributionFitResult::eval is declared in the public header but never defined, so any client that calls it fails to link.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ client code: `GumbelDistributionFitter::GumbelDistributionFitResult r(0.0, 1.0); r.eval(0.0);`
- **Consequence:** Undefined-symbol link error at build time. There is no runtime misbehaviour, and no in-tree, TOPP or pyOpenMS caller. The nested struct also lacks an export annotation for Windows DLL clients.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/MATH/STATISTICS/GumbelDistributionFitter.h:51 `double eval(double x) const;` inside `struct GumbelDistributionFitResult` (:38, with no OPENMS_DLLAPI). GumbelDistributionFitter.cpp defines only log_eval_no_normalize (:23-28), the constructor/destructor (:30-35), setInitialParameters (:37-40) and fit (:96-122). A git grep for `GumbelDistributionFitResult::eval` over origin/develop src finds no definition and no caller, and pyOpenMS has no Gumbel fitter binding.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 56f5f29 adds GumbelDistributionFitter_test.cpp section `(double GumbelDistributionFitResult::eval(double x) const)`, which calls eval. Against the old library it would not link, but no failing build is recorded, and the Rust report says the link failure was inferred, not run.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): GumbelDistributionFitter.cpp now defines eval as `z = exp((a - x) / b); return (z * exp(-z)) / b;`, and the header marks the struct `OPENMS_DLLAPI GumbelDistributionFitResult` and documents the density.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 56f5f29 (first tagged in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/GumbelDistributionFitter_test.cpp section '(double GumbelDistributionFitResult::eval(double x) const)': checks eval(2.0)=exp(-1)/0.5 and eval(3.0)=0.236409903186 = exp(log_eval_no_normalize(3.0)) for a=2, b=0.5
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-222"></a>
### CPP-222: Gumbel least-squares fitter advertises an undefined weighted fit

**GumbelDistributionFitter declares and documents a weighted-histogram fitWeighted that was never implemented, so calling it fails to link.**  
- **Trigger:** C++ client code calling `GumbelDistributionFitter().fitWeighted(x, w)` with any vectors.
- **Consequence:** Undefined-symbol link error. The documented weighted-histogram fit does not exist. No in-tree, TOPP or pyOpenMS code uses it, and the same-named method on GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter is a different class and operation.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/include/OpenMS/MATH/STATISTICS/GumbelDistributionFitter.h:72-81 `@brief Fits a gumbel distribution to the given data x values. Fills a weighted histogram first and generates y values. ... GumbelDistributionFitResult fitWeighted(const std::vector<double> & x, const std::vector<double> & w);`. git grep for fitWeighted in origin/develop src/openms, topp, utils and pyOpenMS finds only this declaration, the GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter.h:72 declaration, its definition GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter.cpp:74 (a different class), and the PosteriorErrorProbabilityModel.cpp:160 call on that other class. GumbelDistributionFitter.cpp has no definition.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Declaration and missing definition confirmed with git grep on origin/develop. No link probe was run, and Core removed the declaration without a test.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The undefined fitWeighted declaration and its Doxygen block are removed from GumbelDistributionFitter.h. GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter::fitWeighted remains the weighted (maximum-likelihood) fit. The histogram contract was dropped, not implemented.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 56f5f29 (first tagged in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-223"></a>
### CPP-223: Distribution-fitter documentation names nonexistent gnuplot members

**The class docs of GaussFitter and both Gumbel fitters promise a getGnuplotFormula() member that none of them has, and IDDecoyProbability's debug-only code calls it.**  
- **Trigger:** A developer following the Doxygen and calling fitter.getGnuplotFormula(), or building IDDecoyProbability.cpp with IDDECOYPROBABILITY_DEBUG defined.
- **Consequence:** A compile error for such client code or debug builds. There is no effect on normal builds, outputs or results.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop GaussFitter.h:29-30 `The complete Gaussian formula with the fitted parameters can be transformed into a gnuplot formula using getGnuplotFormula after fitting.`; GumbelDistributionFitter.h:28-29 and GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter.h:26-27 `The formula with the fitted parameters can be transformed into a gnuplot formula using getGnuplotFormula() after fitting.` None of the three classes declares getGnuplotFormula (the only real ones are on TraceFitter/EGHTraceFitter/GaussTraceFitter). Debug-only callers in src/openms/source/ANALYSIS/ID/IDDecoyProbability.cpp: :193/:195 `gdf.getGnuplotFormula()` on a GammaDistributionFitter, and :319/:321/:328 `gf.getGnuplotFormula()` on a GaussFitter, both under `#ifdef IDDECOYPROBABILITY_DEBUG`, whose define is commented out at :14.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Header and source search on origin/develop. No compile with the debug macro was attempted, either upstream or in Core.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): The getGnuplotFormula promise is removed from GaussFitter.h, GumbelDistributionFitter.h and GumbelMaxLikelihoodFitter.h. The Gamma debug block in IDDecoyProbability.cpp now builds its formula from result_gamma.b and .p. The review table says the debug output was fixed, but only partly: the GaussFitter debug block still calls gf.getGnuplotFormula() (dax/codex/p0-followups IDDecoyProbability.cpp:323, :325, :332), so a debug-macro build would still fail to compile.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 56f5f29 (first tagged in core-v4.0.0-ci.3, present in ci.5 and dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-225"></a>
### CPP-225: Memory-usage delta overwrites its minus sign

**SysInfo::MemUsage::diff_str_ writes a minus sign for a memory decrease and then overwrites the whole string with the absolute value, so a shrinking working set is reported as growth.**  
- **Trigger:** Any MemUsage::delta call where mem_after < mem_before (or peak after < peak before), e.g. mem_before=4096 KiB, mem_after=1024 KiB gives '3 MB' instead of '-3 MB'; reached in PeptideIndexing (PeptideIndexer and search adapters with reindexing) whenever the working set shrinks across Aho-Corasick indexing.
- **Consequence:** Informational stdout line shows a positive delta for a decrease; no computed result or file output is affected.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/SYSTEM/SysInfo.cpp:326-331 `std::string s; if (mem_after < mem_before) { s +=std::string("-"); } s =StringUtils::toStr(std::abs(((long long)mem_after - (long long)mem_before) / 1024)) + " MB";` — the assignment discards the sign. Used by MemUsage::delta at :301 and :304; printed by PeptideIndexing.cpp:578-579 `mu.after(); std::cout << mu.delta("Aho-Corasick")`.
- **Evidence:** test-backed — Core 56f5f29 adds SysInfo_test.cpp section '[EXTRA] MemUsage::delta keeps the sign of a decrease' (mem_before=4096, mem_after=1024, requires substring '-3 MB'); by source the pre-fix/develop code yields '... 3 MB (working set delta)' so the check would fail, but no recorded failing run on pre-fix code was found in commits, docs or the Rust report (which also calls it source-only).
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): SysInfo.cpp diff_str_ appends the magnitude (`s += StringUtils::toStr(...) + " MB"`) so the previously added '-' is kept.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 56f5f29 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; no later change on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Test:** src/tests/class_tests/openms/source/SysInfo_test.cpp, section '[EXTRA] MemUsage::delta keeps the sign of a decrease' (checks '-3 MB' for a decrease and ': 3 MB' for growth)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="cpp-226"></a>
### CPP-226: Update notification announces the local version instead of the offered update

**When the update server reports a newer release, the notice prints the locally running version as the one that is available instead of the version the server offers.**  
- **Trigger:** A TOPP tool run (build with ENABLE_UPDATE_CHECK, not -test, OPENMS_DISABLE_UPDATE_CHECK unset or OFF, first run or >24 h since the tool's .ver timestamp) where the REST response parses to a version newer than VersionInfo::getVersionStruct().
- **Consequence:** The user reads e.g. 'Version 3.5.0 of FeatureFinderCentroided is available' naming the version already installed, so the notice is confusing and does not tell which release to get; nothing else depends on it.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/SYSTEM/UpdateCheck.cpp:141-143 `if (VersionInfo::getVersionStruct() < server_version) { OPENMS_LOG_INFO << "Version " + version + " of " + tool_name + " is available at www.OpenMS.de" << endl;` where `version` is the caller's local version: TOPPBase.cpp:121 `version_ = VersionInfo::getVersion();` passed at :393 `UpdateCheck::run(tool_name_, version_, debug_level_);`. ENABLE_UPDATE_CHECK defaults ON (CMakeLists.txt:418).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Wrong variable in the message is plain from source. No C++ test covers the notice (Core UpdateCheck_test.cpp has one run() section, not a fake-server response); the Rust report says no C++ execution was done.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (fixed): UpdateCheck.cpp builds `offered` from server_version.version_major.minor.patch plus '-' pre_release_identifier when present, and logs 'Version ' + offered + ' of ' + tool_name + ' is available at www.OpenMS.de'.
- **Commits:** OpenMS4 Core 56f5f29 (in core-v4.0.0-ci.5; no later change on dax/codex/p0-followups)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-14"></a>
### X-14: The mzTab writer spells the column-unit key colunit-PSM; the mzTab 1.0 specification uses colunit-psm

**The mzTab 1.0 writer emits the PSM column-unit key as "colunit-PSM", but the specification (and OpenMS's own reader) uses "colunit-psm".**  
- **Trigger:** MzTabFile::store with MzTabMetaData::colunit_psm non-empty. Upstream only a direct C++ caller can reach it, since no code fills the vector and loading a colunit line throws. In Core, since the reader fix, a pyOpenMS load->store round trip of a third-party file with a colunit-psm line also reaches it.
- **Consequence:** A case-sensitive mzTab reader does not see the PSM column units. Upstream the line is malformed anyway (key and value are joined without a tab, so the output fails the cells.size() < 3 check on reload), so the spelling alone has no demonstrated effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): src/openms/source/FORMAT/MzTabFile.cpp:1995-1999: `// colunit-PSM ... std::string s =std::string("MTD\tcolunit-PSM") + md.colunit_psm[i];`. The reader at :695 compares the lower-case `meta_key_fields[1] == "psm"`, and the other writer keys at 1984/1991/2005 are lower case. The writer also omits the tab between key and value, and nothing upstream fills colunit_psm (MzTab.h:175; the only other reference is the reader at :699, which throws for spec keys, see CPP-148).
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read from origin/develop MzTabFile.cpp:1998 and the reader at :695. No test anywhere checks the written key spelling. The Core test that loads 'MTD\t colunit-PSM \t...' (dax/codex/p0-followups MzTabFile_test.cpp ~399-406) exercises only the case-insensitive reader. No execution evidence of an external reader rejecting it.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (open): Not fixed. Core worked around it only on the read side: the colunit section name is lower-cased so its own "colunit-PSM" output reads back, and the writer now inserts the tab. The writer key is still upper case.
- **Commits:** none. core-v4.0.0-ci.5 MzTabFile.cpp:2022 and dax/codex/p0-followups:2181 still write "MTD\tcolunit-PSM\t". 181dadf only added the missing tab and made the reader lower-case the section, and b34c9a2 keeps that with the comment 'the writer spells colunit-PSM'. Listed as open in docs/project-state.md:190 and docs/reviews/2026-09-14-p0-upstream-patch-sets.md:127.
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter could not refute

<a id="x-16"></a>
### X-16: mzData writer writes MS1SPECTRUM and MSNSPECTRUM as MassScan/Zoom and the reader maps them back to MASSSPECTRUM, so the spectrum type is lost in a round trip

**Spectra whose scan mode is MS1 spectrum or MSn spectrum are written to mzData as MassScan/Zoom and read back as the generic mass-spectrum mode, so the scan mode silently changes in a round trip while the MS level survives.**  
- **Trigger:** MzDataFile::store of an MSExperiment whose spectra carry ScanMode MS1SPECTRUM or MSNSPECTRUM (the normal case for data loaded from mzML, where MzMLHandler.cpp:1581-1587 sets them from MS:1000579/MS:1000580), then MzDataFile::load.
- **Consequence:** getScanMode() returns MASSSPECTRUM instead of MS1SPECTRUM/MSNSPECTRUM; a later mzML store writes the generic MS:1000294 'mass spectrum' term (MzMLHandler.cpp:5303-5305) instead of MS1/MSn spectrum. No peaks, RT or MS level are lost, and mzData has no dedicated MS1/MSn ScanMode value, so this is a legacy-format limitation with no demonstrated downstream effect.
- **Upstream evidence** (origin/develop 5082181714 (15 Sep)): origin/develop src/openms/source/FORMAT/HANDLERS/MzDataHandler.cpp:882-893 `case InstrumentSettings::ScanMode::MASSSPECTRUM: case ...MS1SPECTRUM: case ...MSNSPECTRUM: if (iset.getZoomScan()) ... value="Zoom" ... else ... value="MassScan"` (no warning, unlike the default branch at :931-933); reader :1089-1097 `if (value == "Zoom") {... setScanMode(ScanMode::MASSSPECTRUM);} else if (value == "MassScan") { setScanMode(ScanMode::MASSSPECTRUM); }`. MS level is kept: writer :862 `<spectrumInstrument msLevel=`, reader :388 `spec_.setMSLevel(...)`.
- **Evidence:** code-reading — Read upstream writer and reader directly; no test or executed round trip covers the scan-mode loss. The Core documentation commit b3eb212 describes the same mapping from source reading only.
- **Fix in OpenMS4 Core** (documented): Adds an @note to the MzDataFile class documentation (src/openms/include/OpenMS/FORMAT/MzDataFile.h) that MS1SPECTRUM/MSNSPECTRUM are stored as MassScan/Zoom and loaded as MASSSPECTRUM, that the MS level is kept, and that mzML should be used to keep the scan mode. Code unchanged, per the CPP-171 decision.
- **Commits:** packages/core b3eb212 '[DOC] Note that mzData does not keep the MS1/MSn scan mode' on branch dax/codex/p0-followups (not in core-v4.0.0-ci.5)
- **Confirmed by:** prover, refuter and judge

## Not confirmed (13)

| ID | Finding | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CPP-054 | Shipped indexed mzML schema leaves index references unchecked | The behaviour is real: in upstream share/OpenMS/SCHEMAS/mzML_idx_1_10.xsd:1192-1199, KEY_ID_IDX and FKNID live inside the indexedmzML element. Their selectors look for a nested './/dx:indexedmzML', and the keyref field is '@id', but OffsetType (:1147) only defines idRef. Both constraints therefore select nothing, and a dangling idRef passes validation. I compared this with the official HUPO-PSI schemas mzML1.1.0_idx.xsd and mzML1.1.2_idx.xsd (github HUPO-PSI/mzML, schema/schema_1.1). They contain the same key and keyref character for character. OpenMS ships the standard's normative schema unchanged, so it validates exactly as the standard defines; the flaw belongs to the PSI schema. Fixing it only in OpenMS would make OpenMS stricter than the standard. Core's attempt also broke OpenMS's own empty-file output (CPP-050), which is why it was reverted. The OpenMS reader does not depend on these constraints, so no data is read wrongly. This should be reported to HUPO-PSI; it is not an OpenMS defect. |
| CPP-069 | BinnedSpectrum left-boundary guard relies on unsigned wraparound | The guard is still at origin/develop BinnedSpectrum.cpp:117 ('if (static_cast<int>(idx - j - 1) >= 0)'), but the reported failure cannot happen. Under C++20/23 the size_t-to-int conversion is modular. At a real left boundary (idx <= j), idx-j-1 wraps to 2^64-k with k=j+1-idx. That converts to -k, which the guard correctly rejects, whenever k <= 2^31, and k > 2^31 would need bin_spread_ > 2^31. The report's trigger, idx >= 2^31, is not a left-boundary case. There idx-j-1 is a real non-negative index, so a sign change after truncation can only wrongly reject a spread bin, never admit an out-of-range one. That regime is broken anyway: SparseVectorType is Eigen::SparseVector<float,0,int> (BinnedSpectrum.h:86), so coeffRef(idx) and coeffRef(idx+j+1) already truncate to int. It also needs m/z divided by bin size to reach about 2.1e9. The Core change to 'if (idx > j)' is a cleanup with no behavioural difference in reachable cases. |
| CPP-083 | mergePeaks leaves the inherited range cache too narrow, undocumented | The code is as described: MSChromatogram.cpp:548-565 assigns the merged peaks and never touches the range cache. That matches OpenMS's documented general contract: RangeManager.h:899 says 'call updateRanges() before to ensure the range is accurate'. Other container mutators (push_back, insert, resize) leave the cache stale the same way, and select()'s @note (MSChromatogram.h:439-440) states the same policy for a sibling mutator. mergePeaks has no caller in the library, TOPP or pyOpenMS; the only use is MSChromatogram_test.cpp:1250. So no consumer reads a stale range after a merge. Stale ranges follow the documented contract, and at most a courtesy @note is missing. Core's switch to recomputing ranges is an improvement, not a fix for an upstream defect. |
| CPP-092 | updateIDReferences / updateAllIDReferences lose identification matches on a throwing translation | The code is still in origin/develop (BaseFeature.cpp:247-259, Feature.cpp:209-216), but it has no observable effect. RefTranslator::translate throws (IdentificationData.cpp:1392-1398) only for a reference missing from the translation map. Every caller first merges the source map's id_data_ (FeatureMap copy constructor :103-107, operator= :130-135, operator+= :198-203, and the matching ConsensusMap code), so the throw needs an already inconsistent map. No upstream caller catches the exception and continues. None of the enclosing operations is strongly exception-safe either. operator= has already replaced data_ and cleared id_data_ before translating. operator+= has already appended features, protein IDs and data processing. A copy constructor that throws destroys the object. An atomic commit per feature therefore cannot leave the caller with a consistent map. Worse, it would keep untranslated references into the source's IdentificationData instead of dropping them. The header (BaseFeature.h:196-203) promises no strong guarantee, and the basic guarantee holds. This is not a defect under the rubric. |
| CPP-100 | IDScoresAsMetaValue writes the transition_names meta value twice | The duplicate is still present at origin/develop 5082181714: MRMFeature.cpp:142 and :152 both call setMetaValue(id + "transition_names", idscores.ind_transition_names). The second call writes the same value to the same key, so nothing observable changes. The written keys (:142-188) cover the ind_* members of OpenSwath_Ind_Scores, so no score was lost to a copy-paste slip. Prover and refuter agree. This is a redundant line, not a defect. |
| CPP-110 | getMSChromatogramById(int) recomputes ranges twice | The redundant call is present upstream: getMSChromatogramById(int) at IndexedMzMLHandler.cpp:278-284 calls the out-parameter overload, which already ends with c.updateRanges() at :301, and then calls c.updateRanges() again. The spectrum counterpart at :246-251 does not repeat the call. updateRanges is idempotent, so outputs, diagnostics and data are unchanged. The only cost is one extra linear min/max pass, which is negligible next to the DOM parse and base64 decode the same call already does. Prover and refuter agree this is a code-hygiene nit and not a defect under the rubric, so it is not confirmed as a defect (severity none). Core 181dadf removed the duplicate anyway. |
| CPP-118 | UniqueIdIndexer::resolveUniqueIdConflicts can loop forever | Upstream origin/develop UniqueIdIndexer.h resolveUniqueIdConflicts has an unbounded `while (uniqueid_to_index_.contains(unique_id))` redraw loop, but it cannot run forever. Each setUniqueId() takes the next output of the process-wide std::mt19937_64 (UniqueIdGenerator.cpp:23-37, guarded by an omp critical section). Nothing reseeds the generator inside the loop; setSeed (:66) would have to be called concurrently from another thread, which is API misuse. The set of stored ids is finite, so consecutive generator outputs cannot stay inside it. In the identical-seed case (TOPP -test runs, merging maps written under the same seed), each used id is replayed at most once, so the loop ends within N+1 redraws and total work stays linear. The refuter's simulation (max redraws N+1) and Core's later bound of 'ids in use + 64' both agree with this. So the claimed infinite loop is not a defect. What remains is cosmetic: the @return doc says 'number of invalid (=replaced) elements', but the value counts redraws and leaves out elements that only got an id via ensureUniqueId. That only changes the 'Replaced N invalid uniqueID's' log line (FeatureMap/ConsensusMap), at most a P3 wording issue and not the reported defect. Evidence: code-reading. |
| CPP-122 | rasterizeRTMZ's two negative-bin guards are dead code that mask an unchecked precondition | The item says the negative-bin guards are dead code that mask a problem. That is wrong, and the prover agrees. On sorted input they cannot fire, because RTBegin/MZBegin are lower bounds. On unsorted input they are exactly the checks that keep `static_cast<Size>(mz_bin) * rt_bins + rt_bin` inside the buffer (MSExperiment.cpp single- and multi-threaded paths). Removing them would turn wrong output into a heap out-of-bounds write. What is left is that sortedness is not checked. MSExperiment.h documents it as required 'for optimal performance and correct results', and leaving it unchecked follows the usual OpenMS pattern for binary-search APIs such as RTBegin and MZBegin; checking it would add an O(n) pass. Wrong output on input that breaks a documented precondition, with memory safety intact, is not a defect under the rubric. |
| CPP-128 | The declared .ibd checksums are parsed and mirrored but never verified on any read path | The facts hold at origin/develop. ImzMLHandler.cpp:703-704 stores IMS:1000091/1000090 in meta_, and ImzMLFile.cpp:152-158 only mirrors them as MetaValues. Digests are computed only in ImzMLWriter.cpp:1515-1516. But no documented contract is broken. ImzMLFile.h lists checksums only among the exposed MetaValues. Its 'IBD integrity check' section promises just the 16-byte UUID comparison, and only as a warning. The imzML spec does not require readers to verify the declared digests, and OpenMS's mzML reader does not verify fileChecksum either. The main consequence claimed in the Rust report is wrong: a truncated .ibd fails fread and throws Exception::ParseError via throwReadError_ (ImzMLHandlerHelper.cpp:30-33, 138-140 and the other array reads). Only same-length in-place corruption goes undetected, and no wrong result on valid input is shown. This is a missing optional verification feature (a feature request), not a defect. Core's own response was documentation only (181dadf). |
| CPP-129 | OnDiscImzMLExperiment::open re-derives the .ibd path in a branch that cannot be taken | I re-read origin/develop (5082181714) and both sides are right: the observation is accurate, but it is not a defect. OnDiscImzMLExperiment::open (OnDiscImzMLExperiment.cpp:232) calls ImzMLFile::loadSpectraIndex, which calls loadImpl_. At ImzMLFile.cpp:568-570, loadImpl_ always sets handler.getImzMLMeta().ibd_file_path to the override, or to inferIbdPath_(filename) when there is no override. inferIbdPath_ (:633-643) never returns an empty string. At :620 loadImpl_ copies the meta into out_meta. Searching src/openms for ibd_file_path and for `meta_ =` shows that ImzMLHandler only reads the field (ImzMLHandler.cpp:202, :260) and never resets or overwrites it. So the ternary at OnDiscImzMLExperiment.cpp:238 gives the same path on both branches. The `if (ibd_path_.empty())` fallback at :239-246 can never run, and its suffix logic matches inferIbdPath_ exactly anyway. No input or ordinary API call changes which .ibd gets opened. Open() therefore reads the same file whose index and UUID loadImpl_ already checked. What remains is duplicated dead code, a maintainability note that falls below P3, so the severity is none. Core cleaned this up in 181dadf, but it does not change behaviour. |
| CPP-136 | ImzMLFile_2_Example_Processed.imzML declares mzML version="1.1" instead of 1.1.0 and is ISO-8859-1 with Latin-1 bytes | The upstream fixture src/tests/class_tests/openms/data/ImzMLFile_2_Example_Processed.imzML matches the report (sha256 066671590f...). It declares encoding="ISO-8859-1" and version="1.1". That is well-formed XML: the Latin-1 bytes match the declared encoding. It is also schema-valid, because mzML_1_10.xsd types the mzML version attribute as plain xs:string with no pattern. Upstream VersionInfo.cpp returns major.minor with patch 0 when there is no second dot, so "1.1" compares equal to 1.1.0 and passes the MzMLHandler minimum-version check; Xerces transcodes ISO-8859-1. OpenMS reads the file correctly, and the only failures reported are in the stricter Rust port reader. The file is real-world example input, and keeping it unchanged tests OpenMS's tolerance of such files. Nothing in OpenMS is wrong. |
| CPP-229 | Download collision-suffix counter lacks an overflow guard | Upstream origin/develop src/openms/source/SYSTEM/Network.cpp:43-46 (saveFileName_) matches what both sides quote: `int i = 0; while (fs::exists(dest / (basename + "." + std::to_string(i)))) ++i;` with no upper bound. The loop stops at the first missing suffix, so its cost grows only with files that really exist and it does not hang. The only claimed failure is signed int overflow, which needs basename plus every basename.0 through basename.2147483647 to exist in one directory. That is about 2^31 directory entries, and the loop would make 2^31 stat calls before it overflowed. No realistic input or ordinary API use gets there, and no effect has been shown (the Rust report itself calls it an unconfirmed extreme-input candidate; evidence is code-reading only). This is a hardening note, not a defect under the rubric. Core's 10000-collision cap (56f5f29) is a reasonable guard, but it fixes no reachable failure. Separate from this item, the gap between the exists() check and opening the file with ofstream is a check-then-use race, but nobody claimed it and it is not judged here. |
| X-07 | Streaming mzML output writes a later spectrum without processing history with the first spectrum's history | The defect as titled does not exist in upstream develop. It was introduced by OpenMS4 Core's first_spectrum_data_processing_ fallback (181dadf). How upstream behaves: MSDataWritingConsumer::consumeSpectrum (MSDataWritingConsumer.cpp:71-96) writes the header from a dummy experiment that holds only the first spectrum, so dps_ has a single entry, filled by writeHeader_'s cmpPtrContainer loop (MzMLHandler.cpp:5059-5072). MzMLHandler::writeSpectrum_ (:5258-5272) checks `spec.getDataProcessing() != dps[0]`. A later spectrum with an empty history fails that check and does not match in the search loop, so dp_ref_num stays s and the writer emits dataProcessingRef="dp_sp_<s>". The dataProcessingList (:5170-5181) declares only dp_sp_0. On reload, processing_[data_processing_ref] (:918-920) default-inserts an empty vector, so the spectrum comes back with an empty history, as it went in. The first spectrum's history is never attached to it. What upstream really has is the dangling IDREF, plus provenance loss for a later non-empty history that differs from the first. That is CPP-172, a separate item, and it is not what X-07 claims. Evidence: code-reading of upstream origin/develop 5082181714. |
